# World City Travel Guide: Europe, Asia & the Americas

## Abu Dhabi, United Arab Emirates

*The UAE's capital of grand mosques, world-class museums and desert adventures.*

- **Country:** United Arab Emirates
- **Region:** Middle East
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.6 million (city); ~3.8 million (emirate)
- **Language(s):** Arabic (English widely used)
- **Currency:** UAE Dirham (AED)
- **Main airport:** Zayed International Airport (AUH)
- **Best time to visit:** November–March
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Abu Dhabi is the capital of the United Arab Emirates and the largest of its seven emirates, built on an island in the Persian Gulf. Oil wealth transformed a small pearl-diving and fishing settlement into a modern city of skyscrapers, boulevards and cultural institutions within a few decades. Compared with neighbouring Dubai, Abu Dhabi feels calmer, greener and more focused on culture and heritage. Saadiyat Island is developing into a major cultural district with world-renowned museums. The emirate also includes the vast Empty Quarter desert and the oasis city of Al Ain.

### Top Attractions

- **Sheikh Zayed Grand Mosque:** One of the largest mosques in the world, with 82 white marble domes, huge chandeliers and one of the world's largest hand-knotted carpets; entry is free but requires modest dress and online booking.
- **Louvre Abu Dhabi:** A museum designed by Jean Nouvel, known for its "rain of light" dome, presenting art from across civilisations.
- **Qasr Al Watan:** The Presidential Palace, open to visitors, with grand halls and exhibitions on Arab governance and science.
- **Yas Island:** Home to Ferrari World, Warner Bros. World, Yas Waterworld and the Yas Marina Formula 1 circuit.
- **Corniche:** An 8-kilometre waterfront promenade with beaches, parks and cycling paths.
- **Qasr Al Hosn:** The oldest stone building in the city, a former fort and royal residence, now a museum.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Corniche and city centre offer convenient hotels near the beach and business districts. Saadiyat Island has luxury resorts and cultural attractions, while Yas Island is ideal for theme parks and families. Al Maryah and Al Reem Islands are modern districts with shopping malls and waterfront dining.

### Food & Drink

Emirati cuisine includes machboos (spiced rice with meat or fish), harees (wheat and meat porridge) and luqaimat (sweet dumplings with date syrup). Arabic coffee (gahwa) with dates is a symbol of hospitality. The city has an enormous range of international restaurants, especially Indian, Lebanese and Pakistani. Alcohol is served mainly in hotel restaurants and licensed venues.

### When to Visit

The best time to visit is from November to March, when temperatures are pleasant and outdoor activities are enjoyable. Summers are extremely hot and humid, often exceeding 40°C. The Formula 1 Abu Dhabi Grand Prix takes place in late November or December.

### Getting Around

Taxis are affordable and the most common way to get around, and ride-hailing apps are widely used. Public buses are available, but the city is spread out and not very walkable in the heat. The drive to Dubai takes around 1.5 hours.

### Day Trips

Al Ain, the "Garden City", has oases, forts, a zoo and Jebel Hafeet mountain. Desert safaris offer dune bashing, camel rides and dinners under the stars. The Liwa Oasis on the edge of the Empty Quarter has some of the world's highest sand dunes.

### Practical Tips

- Dress modestly in public areas, especially at religious sites and government buildings.
- Public displays of affection and drinking alcohol in public are not allowed.
- During Ramadan, avoid eating or drinking in public during daylight hours.
- Carry water and use sun protection, even in winter.

## Agra, India

*Home of the Taj Mahal, the world's most famous monument to love.*

- **Country:** India
- **Region:** South Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.8 million
- **Language(s):** Hindi
- **Currency:** Indian Rupee (INR)
- **Main airport:** Agra Airport (AGR) with limited flights; most visitors arrive via Delhi (DEL)
- **Best time to visit:** October–March
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Agra is a city in the northern Indian state of Uttar Pradesh, located on the banks of the Yamuna River. It was the capital of the Mughal Empire during the 16th and 17th centuries, when emperors built magnificent monuments. The city's star attraction is the Taj Mahal, built by Emperor Shah Jahan in memory of his wife Mumtaz Mahal. Agra has three UNESCO World Heritage Sites: the Taj Mahal, Agra Fort and nearby Fatehpur Sikri. Together with Delhi and Jaipur, it forms India's popular "Golden Triangle" route.

### Top Attractions

- **Taj Mahal:** A white marble mausoleum completed in the mid-17th century, renowned for its symmetry and inlaid decorations; visit at sunrise for soft light and fewer crowds.
- **Agra Fort:** A massive red sandstone fortress and palace complex where Shah Jahan was imprisoned by his son, with views of the Taj.
- **Fatehpur Sikri:** A deserted Mughal city built by Emperor Akbar about 40 km from Agra, with palaces and the grand Jama Masjid.
- **Itmad-ud-Daulah's Tomb:** Often called the "Baby Taj", a delicate marble tomb that inspired the Taj Mahal.
- **Mehtab Bagh:** A garden across the river offering sunset views of the Taj Mahal.
- **Akbar's Tomb at Sikandra:** The mausoleum of Emperor Akbar, surrounded by gardens and deer.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Most visitors stay in the Taj Ganj area close to the Taj Mahal's eastern and southern gates, which has many budget and mid-range hotels with rooftop views. Luxury hotels are found along Fatehabad Road. The Sadar Bazaar area is a lively market district.

### Food & Drink

Agra is famous for petha, a translucent sweet made from ash gourd, available in many flavours. Mughlai cuisine includes rich curries, kebabs and biryanis. Street foods like bedai (fried bread) with spicy potato curry and jalebi are popular breakfasts. Many rooftop restaurants in Taj Ganj offer views of the monument.

### When to Visit

October to March offers the most comfortable weather for sightseeing. Summers (April–June) are extremely hot, while the monsoon (July–September) brings rain and humidity. Winter mornings can be foggy, sometimes obscuring the Taj Mahal at sunrise.

### Getting Around

Auto-rickshaws, cycle-rickshaws and taxis are the main ways to get around. Electric vehicles are required near the Taj Mahal to reduce pollution. The Gatimaan Express and other trains connect Agra with Delhi in about two hours, and the Yamuna Expressway makes road travel fast.

### Day Trips

Fatehpur Sikri is the most popular side trip. Mathura and Vrindavan, sacred Hindu towns associated with Lord Krishna, are about an hour away. Keoladeo National Park in Bharatpur is a renowned bird sanctuary.

### Practical Tips

- The Taj Mahal is closed on Fridays.
- Buy tickets online and bring only essentials; large bags, food and tripods are not allowed.
- Hire licensed guides only and be cautious of touts.
- Foreign visitors pay higher entrance fees than Indian citizens.

## Almaty, Kazakhstan

*Kazakhstan's green cultural capital at the foot of the snow-capped Tian Shan mountains.*

- **Country:** Kazakhstan
- **Region:** Central Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~2.2 million
- **Language(s):** Kazakh and Russian
- **Currency:** Kazakhstani Tenge (KZT)
- **Main airport:** Almaty International Airport (ALA)
- **Best time to visit:** May–June and September–October; December–March for skiing
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Almaty is Kazakhstan's largest city and its former capital, set at the foot of the Trans-Ili Alatau range of the Tian Shan mountains. Its name is linked to apples, and the region is believed to be the ancestral home of the domestic apple. The city is leafy and spacious, with Soviet-era architecture, wide avenues, parks and a growing café and nightlife scene. Almaty is the gateway to spectacular mountain landscapes, canyons and lakes. It is one of Central Asia's most cosmopolitan cities.

### Top Attractions

- **Medeu and Shymbulak:** A high-altitude ice-skating rink and a nearby ski resort reached by cable car, just 30 minutes from the city.
- **Kok-Tobe:** A hill with a cable car, amusement rides and panoramic views over the city.
- **Zenkov Cathedral:** A colourful wooden Orthodox cathedral in Panfilov Park, built without nails.
- **Green Bazaar:** A bustling market selling dried fruits, nuts, spices, horse meat and local cheeses.
- **Central State Museum of Kazakhstan:** Exhibitions on the country's history, from nomadic cultures to independence.
- **Big Almaty Lake:** A turquoise alpine lake in the mountains, about an hour's drive from the city.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The city centre around Panfilov Park, Arbat (Zhibek Zholy pedestrian street) and Abylai Khan Avenue is convenient. The Golden Square area has upscale hotels and restaurants. Areas to the south, closer to the mountains, are greener and quieter.

### Food & Drink

Kazakh cuisine is meat-based and rooted in nomadic traditions: beshbarmak (boiled meat with noodles) is the national dish. Baursak (fried dough), plov (rice with meat) and manty (steamed dumplings) are common. Kumis (fermented mare's milk) and shubat (fermented camel's milk) are traditional drinks. Almaty also has good Korean, Uyghur and international restaurants.

### When to Visit

Late spring and early autumn offer pleasant weather and colourful landscapes. Summers are warm and ideal for hiking. Winters are cold and snowy, great for skiing at Shymbulak.

### Getting Around

Almaty has a metro line, buses and trolleybuses, but taxis and ride-hailing apps like Yandex Go are cheap and convenient. The city is laid out in a grid, making navigation easy. The mountains are clearly visible to the south, helping with orientation.

### Day Trips

Charyn Canyon, often compared to a smaller Grand Canyon, is about three hours away. Kolsai and Kaindy lakes, including a lake with a submerged forest, are popular multi-day trips. Altyn-Emel National Park features the "Singing Dune".

### Practical Tips

- Carry your passport, as police may ask for identification.
- Big Almaty Lake is a water reservoir; swimming is not allowed.
- Learn a few words of Russian, which is widely spoken.
- Check border zone permit requirements for some mountain areas.

## Amman, Jordan

*A hilly, welcoming capital of Roman ruins and the gateway to Petra and Wadi Rum.*

- **Country:** Jordan
- **Region:** Middle East
- **Population (approx.):** ~4 million (Greater Amman)
- **Language(s):** Arabic
- **Currency:** Jordanian Dinar (JOD)
- **Main airport:** Queen Alia International Airport (AMM)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and September–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Amman, the capital of Jordan, is built on a series of hills (jebels) and is one of the oldest continuously inhabited cities in the world. Known in antiquity as Philadelphia, it was part of the Roman Decapolis, and ruins from that era still stand in the centre. The modern city is a mix of old souks, whitewashed buildings, trendy cafés and upscale districts. Jordanians are known for their hospitality. Amman is the starting point for exploring Petra, Wadi Rum, the Dead Sea and Jerash.

### Top Attractions

- **Amman Citadel (Jabal al-Qal'a):** A hilltop site with the Temple of Hercules, an Umayyad palace and views over the city.
- **Roman Theatre:** A 2nd-century theatre carved into the hillside, seating around 6,000 people.
- **Rainbow Street:** A lively street in Jabal Amman with cafés, restaurants and shops.
- **King Abdullah I Mosque:** A blue-domed mosque open to non-Muslim visitors.
- **Jordan Museum:** Home to some of the Dead Sea Scrolls and the ancient Ain Ghazal statues.
- **Downtown souks:** Markets for spices, gold, clothes and sweets.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Jabal Amman and Jabal al-Weibdeh are charming, walkable areas with cafés and galleries. Downtown (Al-Balad) is busy and budget-friendly. Abdoun and Sweifieh are upscale, modern districts.

### Food & Drink

Jordan's national dish is mansaf, lamb cooked in fermented yoghurt sauce and served over rice. Falafel and hummus from Hashem restaurant downtown are legendary. Knafeh, a sweet cheese pastry soaked in syrup, is a favourite dessert. Tea with sage and Arabic coffee with cardamom are served everywhere.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are the best seasons. Summers are hot but less humid than the Gulf, and winters can be cold, with occasional snow. Ramadan changes opening hours of restaurants.

### Getting Around

Taxis are the most common way to get around; ride-hailing apps like Uber and Careem are widely used. The city is hilly and spread out, so walking between areas can be tiring. JETT buses connect Amman with Petra and Aqaba.

### Day Trips

Jerash, one of the best-preserved Roman cities in the world, is under an hour away. The Dead Sea, the lowest point on Earth, offers floating in extremely salty water. Petra, Wadi Rum and Madaba (famous for Byzantine mosaics) are other key destinations.

### Practical Tips

- The Jordan Pass waives the tourist visa fee for many nationalities (if you stay at least three nights) and includes entry to Petra and many sites.
- Dress modestly, especially outside upscale areas.
- Friday is the main day of rest, when many shops close.
- Check current travel advisories for the region before your trip.

## Amsterdam, Netherlands

*A city of canals, gabled houses, world-class museums and bicycles.*

- **Country:** Netherlands
- **Region:** Western Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~930,000 (city); ~2.5 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Dutch (English very widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Amsterdam Airport Schiphol (AMS)
- **Best time to visit:** April–May and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Amsterdam is the capital of the Netherlands and one of Europe's most distinctive cities, built around a UNESCO-listed ring of 17th-century canals. The city grew rich during the Dutch Golden Age, when its merchants and the Dutch East India Company dominated global trade, and that wealth is still visible in the narrow, ornate canal houses. Today Amsterdam is known for its liberal culture, creative scene and relaxed pace of life. It is compact enough to explore on foot or by bike, and most major sights lie within or close to the canal belt. Visitors come for art, history, nightlife and the simple pleasure of wandering along the water.

### Top Attractions

- **Rijksmuseum:** The national museum of the Netherlands holds masterpieces of the Dutch Golden Age, including Rembrandt's The Night Watch and several works by Vermeer.
- **Van Gogh Museum:** Home to the world's largest collection of Vincent van Gogh's paintings and letters, it traces his artistic development from dark early works to vivid late canvases.
- **Anne Frank House:** The canal-side building where Anne Frank and her family hid during the Second World War is now a moving museum; tickets are sold online only and sell out weeks ahead.
- **Canal Ring (Grachtengordel):** The Herengracht, Keizersgracht and Prinsengracht are best explored on a canal cruise or on foot, especially at dusk when bridges are lit.
- **Jordaan:** A former working-class district of narrow streets, hidden courtyards (hofjes), independent shops, brown cafés and weekend markets.
- **Vondelpark:** The city's most popular park, perfect for cycling, picnics and people-watching on summer afternoons.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Centrum and canal belt are the most convenient bases, though hotels there are expensive and small. De Pijp is lively and full of restaurants around the Albert Cuyp Market, while Oud-West and Amsterdam-Noord (reached by free ferry behind Centraal Station) offer a more local and creative atmosphere. The Museum Quarter is quieter and ideal for culture-focused travellers.

### Food & Drink

Dutch classics include raw herring with onions, bitterballen (fried meat-ragout balls) served with mustard, stroopwafels and Dutch apple pie. Amsterdam's colonial history means Indonesian food is excellent here; try a rijsttafel, a feast of many small dishes. Brown cafés (bruine kroegen) serve local beer and jenever, the Dutch predecessor of gin. Food halls such as Foodhallen in Oud-West are good for casual meals.

### When to Visit

Spring brings tulip season, with nearby Keukenhof gardens open from late March to mid-May, and King's Day on 27 April turns the city orange. Summer is busy and pleasant but crowded; autumn is quieter and often mild. Winters are grey and damp, but the Amsterdam Light Festival brightens the canals from December to January.

### Getting Around

Trains connect Schiphol Airport to Amsterdam Centraal in about 15–20 minutes. Within the city, trams, metro, buses and ferries are run by GVB, and contactless bank cards can be used to tap in and out. Cycling is the local way to move around, but beginners should be careful of tram tracks and fast local riders.

### Day Trips

Popular day trips include the windmills of Zaanse Schans, the historic fishing villages of Volendam and Marken, and the cities of Haarlem, Utrecht and Delft, all under an hour by train. In spring, Keukenhof and the flower fields around Lisse are a highlight.

### Practical Tips

- Book the Anne Frank House, Van Gogh Museum and Rijksmuseum online well in advance.
- Never walk in the red-painted bike lanes; cyclists have priority and move fast.
- Consider staying in Haarlem or Utrecht to save money on accommodation.
- Tourist tax and hotel prices are high, so budget accordingly, especially in summer.

## Antigua Guatemala, Guatemala

*A cobblestoned colonial gem surrounded by volcanoes.*

- **Country:** Guatemala
- **Region:** Central America
- **Population (approx.):** ~50,000
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Guatemalan Quetzal (GTQ)
- **Main airport:** La Aurora International Airport (GUA) in Guatemala City, about 1 hour away
- **Best time to visit:** November–April
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Antigua Guatemala was the capital of the Spanish colonial Kingdom of Guatemala, which stretched across much of Central America, for over two centuries. After devastating earthquakes in 1773, the capital was moved to present-day Guatemala City, leaving Antigua's baroque churches and convents in picturesque ruins. The town's grid of cobbled streets, pastel-coloured houses and colonial architecture make it a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Three volcanoes – Agua, Fuego and Acatenango – dominate the skyline, and Fuego erupts regularly. Antigua is a popular base for Spanish-language schools, coffee tours and volcano hikes.

### Top Attractions

- **Santa Catalina Arch:** A bright yellow arch with a clock tower framing Volcán de Agua, the town's most photographed landmark.
- **Central Park (Parque Central):** The main square, surrounded by the cathedral, the Palace of the Captains General and colonial arcades.
- **La Merced Church:** An ornate yellow-and-white baroque church with a large fountain in its cloister.
- **Convent ruins:** Atmospheric ruins such as Las Capuchinas, Santo Domingo and San Francisco, partly damaged by earthquakes.
- **Cerro de la Cruz:** A hilltop viewpoint with a cross overlooking the town and Volcán de Agua.
- **Acatenango volcano hike:** A challenging overnight trek to camp opposite the erupting Volcán de Fuego.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Most hotels and guesthouses are within the central grid, a short walk from Central Park. Quieter areas lie on the outskirts, near coffee farms. Boutique hotels in restored colonial mansions offer atmospheric stays.

### Food & Drink

Guatemalan dishes include pepián (a rich meat and vegetable stew), kak'ik (a spicy turkey soup) and tamales. Antigua has excellent cafés serving local coffee, one of the country's main exports. The local market offers cheap food and fresh produce. Chocolate workshops explain Mayan cacao traditions.

### When to Visit

The dry season from November to April offers sunny days and clear volcano views. Semana Santa (Holy Week) is spectacular, with processions over colourful sawdust carpets (alfombras), but accommodation fills up fast. The rainy season (May–October) brings afternoon showers.

### Getting Around

Antigua is small and walkable, but cobblestones can be uneven. Tuk-tuks and taxis are available for short trips. Shuttles connect Antigua with Guatemala City, Lake Atitlán and other destinations.

### Day Trips

Lake Atitlán, surrounded by volcanoes and indigenous villages, is about 2.5–3 hours away. The Chichicastenango market is famous for its colourful textiles. Pacaya volcano offers an easier hike, and coffee farms around Antigua offer tours.

### Practical Tips

- Hire a reputable guide for volcano hikes and bring warm clothes for Acatenango.
- Book Holy Week accommodation months in advance.
- Walk carefully on uneven cobblestones.
- Use registered shuttles and avoid travelling at night between cities.

## Arequipa, Peru

*Peru's "White City" of volcanic stone, set beneath snow-capped volcanoes.*

- **Country:** Peru
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.1 million
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Peruvian Sol (PEN)
- **Main airport:** Rodríguez Ballón International Airport (AQP)
- **Best time to visit:** April–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Arequipa, in southern Peru, is the country's second-largest city and is known as the "White City" because many of its colonial buildings are made of sillar, a white volcanic stone. The historic centre is a UNESCO World Heritage Site, with elegant churches, mansions and plazas. The city is framed by three volcanoes: El Misti, Chachani and Pichu Pichu. Arequipa has a strong regional identity and a proud culinary tradition. It is the gateway to the Colca Canyon, one of the deepest canyons in the world.

### Top Attractions

- **Plaza de Armas:** One of Peru's most beautiful main squares, with the cathedral and arcades.
- **Santa Catalina Monastery:** A vast, colourful convent that functioned like a small city within the city for over 400 years.
- **Museo Santuarios Andinos:** Home to "Juanita", the Inca Ice Maiden, a mummy discovered on Mount Ampato.
- **Yanahuara viewpoint:** Sillar arches framing views of El Misti.
- **San Camilo Market:** A traditional market with fruit juices and local foods.
- **Mundo Alpaca:** A free exhibition on alpaca wool and weaving.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The historic centre is the best base. Yanahuara and Cayma are quieter residential districts nearby.

### Food & Drink

Arequipa's picanterías serve rocoto relleno (stuffed spicy peppers), chupe de camarones (shrimp chowder), adobo arequipeño and queso helado, a local ice cream. Chicha de jora, a fermented corn drink, is traditional.

### When to Visit

The dry season from April to November is best, with sunny days. The rainy season is mild but affects trips to Colca Canyon.

### Getting Around

The centre is walkable. Taxis are cheap. Buses connect Arequipa with Puno, Cusco and Lima.

### Day Trips

Colca Canyon, home to Andean condors, is a popular two-day trip. Hikers climb El Misti or Chachani with guides.

### Practical Tips

- Adjust gradually to the altitude (about 2,300 m).
- Wear sun protection.
- Book Colca tours with reputable agencies.
- Try a traditional picantería for lunch.

## Athens, Greece

*The cradle of Western civilisation, where ancient ruins meet a buzzing modern capital.*

- **Country:** Greece
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~640,000 (city); ~3.7 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Greek
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Athens International Airport Eleftherios Venizelos (ATH)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Athens is one of the oldest continuously inhabited cities in the world and the birthplace of democracy, philosophy and theatre. Its skyline is dominated by the Acropolis, a rocky hill crowned by the Parthenon, which has watched over the city for nearly 2,500 years. Around the ancient monuments lies a sprawling, energetic modern capital with rooftop bars, street art, lively markets and excellent food. Athens is also the main gateway to the Greek islands through the port of Piraeus. Many travellers spend two or three days here before heading to the Aegean, but the city rewards a longer stay.

### Top Attractions

- **Acropolis and Parthenon:** The most important ancient site in Greece, including the Parthenon, the Erechtheion with its Caryatid porch, and the Temple of Athena Nike; arrive at opening time to avoid heat and crowds.
- **Acropolis Museum:** A modern museum at the foot of the hill displaying original sculptures from the Acropolis, with a top-floor gallery aligned with the Parthenon.
- **Ancient Agora:** The heart of ancient Athenian public life, featuring the remarkably preserved Temple of Hephaestus and the reconstructed Stoa of Attalos.
- **Plaka and Anafiotika:** The old neighbourhood beneath the Acropolis, with neoclassical houses, tavernas and the island-like whitewashed lanes of Anafiotika.
- **National Archaeological Museum:** One of the world's great museums of ancient art, home to the Mask of Agamemnon and the Antikythera Mechanism.
- **Lycabettus Hill:** The highest point in central Athens, reached on foot or by funicular, offers the best sunset views over the city and the sea.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Plaka and Monastiraki are central and atmospheric, ideal for first-time visitors. Koukaki, just south of the Acropolis, is quieter and popular with travellers who want a local feel. Psyrri and Exarchia are known for nightlife, street art and alternative culture, while Kolonaki is upscale, with boutiques and cafés.

### Food & Drink

Greek cuisine in Athens ranges from simple street food to refined modern restaurants. Souvlaki and gyros are cheap and delicious, while tavernas serve moussaka, grilled fish, horiatiki (Greek salad) and meze with ouzo or local wine. The Central Market (Varvakios Agora) is a vivid place to see meat, fish and spice stalls. For dessert, try loukoumades, small fried dough balls with honey.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn offer warm, sunny weather ideal for sightseeing. July and August can be extremely hot, sometimes over 40°C, and archaeological sites may close at midday during heatwaves. Winters are mild and quiet, with lower prices, though some days are rainy.

### Getting Around

The metro connects the airport to the city centre in about 40 minutes and serves most key sights, including Acropoli, Monastiraki and Syntagma stations. Central Athens is walkable, and a pedestrian promenade links many ancient sites. Taxis are relatively affordable, and ride-hailing apps work well.

### Day Trips

The Temple of Poseidon at Cape Sounion is famous for sunsets over the Aegean. Delphi, the ancient sanctuary of Apollo, is about three hours away, and the island of Aegina can be reached by ferry from Piraeus in around an hour. The Peloponnese sites of Mycenae, Epidaurus and Nafplio make a great full-day tour.

### Practical Tips

- A combined ticket covers the Acropolis and several other ancient sites and is valid for several days.
- Carry water and wear good shoes; marble paths on the Acropolis are slippery.
- Dinner in Athens is late; locals rarely eat before 9 pm.
- Many museums offer free entry on certain days in the low season.

## Austin, United States (Texas)

*The "Live Music Capital of the World", with barbecue, tech and a laid-back vibe.*

- **Country:** United States (Texas)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~980,000 (city); ~2.5 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Austin–Bergstrom International Airport (AUS)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and October–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Austin, the capital of Texas, is known for its live music scene, creative culture and motto "Keep Austin Weird". The city has grown rapidly into a major technology hub, attracting companies and young professionals. Austin hosts major events such as South by Southwest (SXSW) and the Austin City Limits Music Festival. It combines Texan traditions with a liberal, outdoorsy lifestyle. The Colorado River and nearby Hill Country provide swimming, kayaking and hiking.

### Top Attractions

- **Texas State Capitol:** A grand granite capitol building taller than the US Capitol, with free tours.
- **Sixth Street and Rainey Street:** Entertainment districts with bars and live music.
- **Lady Bird Lake:** A river reservoir for kayaking, paddleboarding and walking trails.
- **Congress Avenue Bridge bats:** The largest urban bat colony in North America emerges at dusk from spring to autumn.
- **Barton Springs Pool:** A spring-fed swimming pool at a constant temperature of around 20°C.
- **LBJ Presidential Library:** A museum on President Lyndon B. Johnson and the civil rights era.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Downtown is central to nightlife and sights. South Congress (SoCo) has boutiques, food trucks and music venues. East Austin is trendy, with bars and restaurants.

### Food & Drink

Austin is famous for Texas barbecue, especially brisket; Franklin Barbecue is known for long queues. Tex-Mex, breakfast tacos and food trucks are everywhere.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal. Summers are very hot. SXSW takes place in March.

### Getting Around

Austin is car-oriented, but downtown is walkable. Buses, scooters and ride-hailing apps are common.

### Day Trips

The Texas Hill Country, with wineries and small towns like Fredericksburg, is nearby. San Antonio and its River Walk are about 1.5 hours away.

### Practical Tips

- Book accommodation early during SXSW and ACL.
- Arrive early for popular barbecue spots.
- Stay hydrated in summer.
- Check event calendars for live music.

## Baku, Azerbaijan

*The "City of Winds", mixing a medieval walled city with futuristic skyscrapers on the Caspian Sea.*

- **Country:** Azerbaijan
- **Region:** Caucasus
- **Population (approx.):** ~2.3 million
- **Language(s):** Azerbaijani
- **Currency:** Azerbaijani Manat (AZN)
- **Main airport:** Heydar Aliyev International Airport (GYD)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Baku, the capital of Azerbaijan, lies on the western shore of the Caspian Sea. Its fortunes were transformed by oil, first in a boom in the late 19th century and again after independence. The walled old city, Icherisheher, is a UNESCO World Heritage Site, while the skyline features bold modern architecture such as the Flame Towers. Baku is known for its strong winds, seaside boulevard and hospitality. It blends Persian, Russian, Turkic and European influences.

### Top Attractions

- **Icherisheher (Old City):** Narrow lanes, caravanserais, mosques and the Palace of the Shirvanshahs within medieval walls.
- **Maiden Tower:** A mysterious 12th-century tower with a museum and views over the Old City.
- **Flame Towers:** Three flame-shaped skyscrapers that light up at night with LED displays.
- **Heydar Aliyev Center:** A flowing, futuristic building designed by Zaha Hadid, hosting exhibitions.
- **Baku Boulevard:** A long seaside promenade with parks, cafés and the Carpet Museum.
- **Yanar Dag and Ateshgah:** A hillside with natural gas flames burning continuously and a fire temple used by Zoroastrians and Hindus.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Staying in or near the Old City and Fountain Square is best for sightseeing. The boulevard area offers sea views and luxury hotels. Nizami Street is the main shopping and pedestrian street.

### Food & Drink

Azerbaijani cuisine includes plov (saffron rice with meat and dried fruit), dolma, kebabs and qutab (thin stuffed flatbreads). Tea culture is strong, often served with jam and sweets. Pakhlava and shekerbura are traditional pastries. Local wines and brandies are available.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are the most pleasant seasons. Summers are hot and windy, while winters are cold and windy. The Formula 1 Azerbaijan Grand Prix takes place on a street circuit around the Old City.

### Getting Around

Baku has a metro, buses and cheap taxis; use ride-hailing apps like Bolt or Uber. The Old City and boulevard are walkable. The airport express bus connects with the city centre.

### Day Trips

Gobustan National Park has prehistoric rock carvings and mud volcanoes. The Absheron Peninsula has fire temples and beaches. Sheki, with its Khan's Palace, is a longer trip into the Caucasus foothills.

### Practical Tips

- Apply for an e-visa online before travelling if required.
- Check current border and travel conditions if planning to visit neighbouring countries.
- Dress modestly when visiting mosques.
- Bring a jacket for the strong winds.

## Bali (Denpasar), Indonesia

*The "Island of the Gods", famous for temples, rice terraces, surf and spiritual culture.*

- **Country:** Indonesia
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~730,000 (Denpasar); ~4.4 million (Bali province)
- **Language(s):** Indonesian and Balinese
- **Currency:** Indonesian Rupiah (IDR)
- **Main airport:** I Gusti Ngurah Rai International Airport (DPS)
- **Best time to visit:** April–October (dry season)
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Bali is an Indonesian island east of Java, and Denpasar is its capital and main urban centre. Unlike most of Indonesia, Bali is predominantly Hindu, and daily life revolves around temples, offerings and ceremonies. The island offers a remarkable variety of experiences, from surfing and beach clubs to yoga retreats, volcano hikes and rice terraces. Areas like Ubud, Seminyak, Canggu and Uluwatu each have distinct atmospheres. Tourism has grown enormously, so choosing the right base is important.

### Top Attractions

- **Ubud:** The cultural heart of Bali, with art markets, the Sacred Monkey Forest Sanctuary, traditional dance performances and nearby rice terraces.
- **Tegallalang and Jatiluwih rice terraces:** Stunning terraced landscapes; Jatiluwih is part of a UNESCO-listed subak irrigation system.
- **Uluwatu Temple:** A clifftop temple with sunset Kecak fire dances.
- **Tanah Lot:** A temple on a rock in the sea, especially popular at sunset.
- **Mount Batur:** An active volcano with a popular sunrise trek.
- **Besakih Temple:** The "Mother Temple", Bali's most important Hindu temple, on the slopes of Mount Agung.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Seminyak is upscale, with boutiques and beach clubs, while Canggu is popular with surfers and digital nomads. Ubud suits culture lovers and wellness seekers, Uluwatu offers dramatic cliffs and surf, and Sanur and Nusa Dua are quieter and family-friendly.

### Food & Drink

Balinese dishes include babi guling (suckling pig), bebek betutu (slow-cooked duck), sate lilit and lawar. Nasi campur, rice with a variety of small dishes, is everywhere. Warungs (small local eateries) offer cheap, tasty meals. Bali also has a large café culture with smoothie bowls and specialty coffee.

### When to Visit

The dry season from April to October is best for beaches and hiking. The wet season (November–March) brings heavy showers, but fewer crowds. Nyepi, the Balinese Day of Silence, shuts down the entire island, including the airport, for 24 hours.

### Getting Around

Traffic is heavy, and there is limited public transport. Private drivers, taxis and ride-hailing apps such as Grab and Gojek are common. Many visitors rent scooters, but this is risky without experience and an appropriate licence.

### Day Trips

The Nusa Islands (Nusa Penida, Lembongan) are reached by fast boat and feature dramatic cliffs and snorkelling. The Gili Islands and Lombok are further by boat. Munduk and the north coast offer waterfalls and quieter villages.

### Practical Tips

- Bali charges a tourist levy for foreign visitors; pay it online before arrival.
- Dress respectfully at temples, wearing a sarong and sash.
- Do not step on offerings placed on the ground.
- Drink bottled or filtered water.

## Banff, Canada (Alberta)

*A mountain town in Canada's first national park, surrounded by turquoise lakes.*

- **Country:** Canada (Alberta)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~8,300 (town)
- **Language(s):** English (French also official in Canada)
- **Currency:** Canadian Dollar (CAD)
- **Main airport:** Calgary International Airport (YYC), about 1.5 hours away
- **Best time to visit:** June–September; December–March for skiing
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Banff is a resort town in Banff National Park, Canada's first national park, established in 1885 in the Canadian Rockies. The park is part of the Canadian Rocky Mountain Parks UNESCO World Heritage Site. Banff is famous for glacier-fed turquoise lakes, dramatic peaks, hot springs and wildlife such as elk, bears and bighorn sheep. It is a year-round destination for hiking, skiing and scenic drives. The town has shops, restaurants and hotels, including the historic Fairmont Banff Springs.

### Top Attractions

- **Lake Louise:** A turquoise lake beneath the Victoria Glacier.
- **Moraine Lake:** A stunning lake in the Valley of the Ten Peaks, accessible by shuttle.
- **Banff Gondola:** A gondola up Sulphur Mountain.
- **Banff Upper Hot Springs:** Natural hot spring pools.
- **Icefields Parkway:** One of the world's most scenic drives.
- **Johnston Canyon:** A canyon walk to waterfalls.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Staying in Banff town is convenient. Canmore, just outside the park, is cheaper.

### Food & Drink

Banff offers Canadian dishes like Alberta beef, bison burgers and poutine.

### When to Visit

Summer is ideal for hiking. Winter brings skiing at Sunshine Village and Lake Louise.

### Getting Around

Roam Transit buses and Parks Canada shuttles are available. Private vehicles cannot drive to Moraine Lake.

### Day Trips

Jasper, Yoho National Park and Kootenay National Park are nearby.

### Practical Tips

- Buy a Parks Canada pass.
- Book shuttles to Moraine Lake and Lake Louise.
- Carry bear spray on hikes.
- Book accommodation early.

## Bangkok, Thailand

*A dazzling, chaotic capital of golden temples, street food and river life.*

- **Country:** Thailand
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~10.5 million (city); ~17 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Thai
- **Currency:** Thai Baht (THB)
- **Main airport:** Suvarnabhumi Airport (BKK); Don Mueang International Airport (DMK)
- **Best time to visit:** November–February
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Bangkok, the capital of Thailand, is one of the world's most visited cities. Its Thai name, Krung Thep, means "City of Angels", and the full ceremonial name is one of the longest place names in the world. The city combines ornate temples and royal palaces with shiny skyscrapers, floating markets, rooftop bars and legendary street food. Bangkok is chaotic, hot and exciting, but also efficient and welcoming. It is the gateway to Thailand's beaches, islands and northern mountains.

### Top Attractions

- **Grand Palace and Wat Phra Kaew:** The former royal residence and the Temple of the Emerald Buddha, Thailand's most sacred temple.
- **Wat Pho:** Home to a 46-metre reclining Buddha and a traditional Thai massage school.
- **Wat Arun:** The Temple of Dawn on the Chao Phraya River, decorated with porcelain.
- **Chatuchak Weekend Market:** One of the world's largest markets, with thousands of stalls.
- **Chao Phraya River:** Ride the river express boats to see temples and riverside life.
- **Chinatown (Yaowarat):** A bustling district famous for street food at night.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Sukhumvit is modern, with hotels, restaurants and nightlife, and well served by the BTS Skytrain. Silom and Sathorn are business districts with rooftop bars, while Riverside offers luxury hotels. The old town (Rattanakosin) and Banglamphu, including Khao San Road, are close to the main temples.

### Food & Drink

Bangkok is one of the world's great food cities. Try pad thai, tom yum goong, green curry, som tam (papaya salad) and mango sticky rice. Street food stalls are everywhere, and some have Michelin recognition. Night markets and food courts in malls offer affordable options.

### When to Visit

The cool, dry season from November to February is the most pleasant. March to May is extremely hot, and the rainy season runs from June to October. Songkran, the Thai New Year in mid-April, is celebrated with a huge water fight.

### Getting Around

The BTS Skytrain and MRT subway avoid traffic jams. River boats and canal boats are cheap and scenic. Taxis and ride-hailing apps (Grab, Bolt) are affordable, and the Airport Rail Link connects Suvarnabhumi with the city.

### Day Trips

Ayutthaya, the former capital with temple ruins, is about 1.5 hours away. Floating markets such as Damnoen Saduak and the Maeklong Railway Market are popular. Kanchanaburi has the Bridge on the River Kwai and waterfalls.

### Practical Tips

- Dress modestly at temples (cover shoulders and knees).
- Beware of scams, such as people claiming the Grand Palace is closed.
- Show respect for the monarchy; criticising the royal family is illegal.
- Drink bottled water and stay hydrated.

## Barcelona, Spain

*Gaudí's masterpieces, Mediterranean beaches and a vibrant Catalan culture.*

- **Country:** Spain
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.65 million (city); ~5.6 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Catalan and Spanish
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Josep Tarradellas Barcelona–El Prat Airport (BCN)
- **Best time to visit:** May–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Barcelona, the capital of Catalonia, is one of Europe's most visited cities and a unique blend of Gothic history, modernist architecture and seaside lifestyle. The city is famous for the work of Antoni Gaudí, whose extraordinary buildings define its skyline. It has a strong Catalan identity, with its own language, festivals and cuisine. Barcelona combines a walkable old town, a grid-planned 19th-century district called the Eixample, and several kilometres of urban beaches. Its food, nightlife and football club FC Barcelona draw visitors all year round.

### Top Attractions

- **Sagrada Família:** Gaudí's monumental basilica, under construction since 1882, is the city's defining landmark; its forest-like interior and stained glass are unforgettable.
- **Park Güell:** A whimsical hillside park designed by Gaudí, with colourful mosaic benches, gingerbread-like pavilions and views over the city.
- **Gothic Quarter (Barri Gòtic):** A maze of medieval streets around Barcelona Cathedral, with Roman remains, hidden squares and small bars.
- **Casa Batlló and Casa Milà (La Pedrera):** Two of Gaudí's most famous houses on Passeig de Gràcia, known for their organic shapes and rooftop terraces.
- **La Rambla and La Boqueria:** The famous boulevard leads to the Boqueria market, a riot of fruit, ham and seafood stalls, though the area is very touristy.
- **Montjuïc:** A hill with gardens, the Olympic stadium, the Joan Miró Foundation, the National Art Museum of Catalonia and a castle overlooking the port.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Eixample is central, elegant and well connected, while El Born is trendy, with boutiques, cocktail bars and the Picasso Museum. Gràcia has a village-like atmosphere with lively squares, and Barceloneta is the traditional beach neighbourhood. Poblenou is a former industrial area turned creative district, close to quieter beaches.

### Food & Drink

Catalan cuisine includes pa amb tomàquet (bread rubbed with tomato and olive oil), escalivada, fideuà and crema catalana. Tapas bars are everywhere, and seafood paella is best eaten near the sea at lunchtime. Vermouth (vermut) before lunch on weekends is a local ritual. Lunch is usually eaten between 2 and 4 pm, and dinner rarely before 9 pm.

### When to Visit

Late spring and early autumn offer warm weather and swimmable sea without the peak summer crowds. July and August are hot, humid and very busy. The Festa de la Mercè in late September features human towers (castells), fire runs and concerts, while Sant Joan on 23 June is celebrated with fireworks and beach parties.

### Getting Around

The metro is fast and covers the city well, and the Aerobús and train link the airport to the centre. The city is walkable, and the Bicing bike-share is for residents, but many private rental shops exist. Taxis are plentiful and reasonably priced.

### Day Trips

Montserrat, a mountain monastery with dramatic rock formations, is about an hour away by train and cable car. Girona offers a beautiful medieval old town, and the seaside town of Sitges is famous for beaches and festivals. The Dalí Theatre-Museum in Figueres is a must for fans of surrealism.

### Practical Tips

- Book Sagrada Família, Park Güell and Casa Batlló tickets online in advance with a timed slot.
- Pickpocketing is common on La Rambla, the metro and beaches; keep valuables secure.
- Respect residents: many neighbourhoods are sensitive about mass tourism.
- Visit the Sagrada Família in the late afternoon for the best light through the stained glass.

## Bariloche, Argentina

*Argentina's alpine lake district of chocolate shops, forests and ski slopes.*

- **Country:** Argentina
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~135,000
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Argentine Peso (ARS)
- **Main airport:** San Carlos de Bariloche International Airport (BRC)
- **Best time to visit:** December–March for summer; July–September for skiing
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

San Carlos de Bariloche sits on the southern shore of Nahuel Huapi Lake in the foothills of the Andes, in Argentina's Patagonian Lake District. Founded by European immigrants, especially from Switzerland, Germany and Italy, it has an alpine-style architecture of stone and wood. The town is the gateway to Nahuel Huapi National Park, Argentina's oldest national park. Bariloche is famous for chocolate, craft beer, hiking in summer and skiing in winter. It is one of Argentina's most popular domestic destinations.

### Top Attractions

- **Cerro Campanario:** A chairlift to a viewpoint rated among the best in the world, overlooking lakes and mountains.
- **Circuito Chico:** A scenic 60-kilometre loop drive or bike ride past lakes, forests and the Llao Llao Hotel.
- **Cerro Catedral:** One of South America's largest ski resorts, with summer hiking and mountain biking.
- **Centro Cívico:** The town's main square with stone buildings in alpine style.
- **Isla Victoria and Bosque de Arrayanes:** A boat trip to an island and a forest of cinnamon-coloured myrtle trees.
- **Refugio Frey:** A popular hike to a mountain hut surrounded by granite spires.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The town centre is convenient for restaurants and shops. Hotels and cabins along the road to Llao Llao offer lake views and nature.

### Food & Drink

Bariloche is famous for chocolate shops such as Rapa Nui and Mamuschka. Patagonian lamb (cordero patagónico), trout and smoked meats are specialities. The region has many craft breweries.

### When to Visit

Summer (December–March) is best for hiking and lakes. Winter (July–September) brings skiing. Autumn offers beautiful colours.

### Getting Around

Local buses reach major sights, but renting a car or bike is convenient. Flights connect Bariloche with Buenos Aires.

### Day Trips

The Route of the Seven Lakes leads to San Martín de los Andes. El Bolsón is a hippie town with a craft market. Cerro Tronador offers glaciers.

### Practical Tips

- Book ski season accommodation early.
- Weather changes quickly in the mountains.
- Carry cash; exchange rates in Argentina fluctuate.
- Try chocolate from several shops.

## Beijing, China

*China's imperial capital, home to the Forbidden City and the Great Wall.*

- **Country:** China
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~21.8 million
- **Language(s):** Mandarin Chinese
- **Currency:** Chinese Yuan Renminbi (CNY)
- **Main airport:** Beijing Capital International (PEK); Beijing Daxing International (PKX)
- **Best time to visit:** April–May and September–October
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Beijing, the capital of China, has been the political heart of the country for most of the past eight centuries. It was the seat of the Ming and Qing dynasties, which left behind palaces, temples and gardens of extraordinary scale. The city blends ancient traditions with modern development, from narrow hutong alleys to futuristic architecture. Beijing is the best base for visiting the Great Wall. It hosted the Summer Olympics in 2008 and the Winter Olympics in 2022.

### Top Attractions

- **Forbidden City (Palace Museum):** The vast imperial palace complex with nearly 1,000 buildings; tickets must be booked online with a passport.
- **Great Wall of China:** Sections such as Mutianyu and Badaling are within easy reach, while Jinshanling and Jiankou offer wilder hiking.
- **Temple of Heaven:** A beautiful complex where emperors prayed for good harvests.
- **Summer Palace:** A royal garden with lakes, pavilions and the Long Corridor.
- **Tiananmen Square:** One of the largest public squares in the world; security checks and advance registration may be required.
- **Hutongs:** Traditional alleyways, best explored by bike or rickshaw, especially around Nanluoguxiang and Shichahai.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Dongcheng is central and close to the Forbidden City and hutongs. Chaoyang is modern, with the Central Business District, embassies and nightlife in Sanlitun. Xicheng is historic, with lakes and hutongs.

### Food & Drink

Peking duck, served with pancakes, spring onions and sweet sauce, is Beijing's most famous dish. Other favourites include zhajiangmian (noodles with fermented soybean paste), jiaozi (dumplings) and jianbing (a savoury crepe for breakfast). Hot pot and food from across China are widely available. Street food snacks are found in markets.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are the most pleasant seasons. Summers are hot and humid, and winters are cold and dry. Avoid national holidays like the Golden Week in early October, when crowds are enormous.

### Getting Around

Beijing has an extensive subway system. Taxis are available, and ride-hailing apps like DiDi work well. Airport express trains connect both airports with the city. Mobile payment apps like Alipay and WeChat Pay are essential; both now accept many foreign cards.

### Day Trips

The Great Wall is the most popular day trip. The Ming Tombs are often combined with the Great Wall. Tianjin is 30 minutes away by high-speed train.

### Practical Tips

- Carry your passport at all times; it is needed for tickets and security checks.
- Set up Alipay or WeChat Pay before travelling.
- Many foreign websites and apps are blocked; plan for internet access.
- Book popular attractions several days in advance.

## Belgrade, Serbia

*A gritty, energetic Balkan capital famed for its fortress and legendary nightlife.*

- **Country:** Serbia
- **Region:** Southeastern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.2 million (city); ~1.7 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Serbian
- **Currency:** Serbian Dinar (RSD)
- **Main airport:** Belgrade Nikola Tesla Airport (BEG)
- **Best time to visit:** May–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Belgrade sits at the confluence of the Sava and Danube rivers and is one of the oldest cities in Europe, having been destroyed and rebuilt dozens of times over its long history. As the capital of Serbia, it mixes Ottoman, Austro-Hungarian, socialist and modern architecture in a chaotic but charming way. The city is famous for its hospitality, café culture and energetic nightlife, including floating river clubs known as splavovi. Prices are lower than in Western Europe, making it popular with budget travellers. Belgrade is increasingly a hub for digital nomads and creative industries.

### Top Attractions

- **Belgrade Fortress and Kalemegdan Park:** A vast fortress complex above the river confluence with walls, gates, museums and superb sunset views.
- **Knez Mihailova Street:** The main pedestrian street, lined with 19th-century buildings, shops and cafés, linking the fortress with Republic Square.
- **Church of Saint Sava:** One of the largest Orthodox churches in the world, with a spectacular gold-mosaic interior.
- **Skadarlija:** A cobbled bohemian quarter with traditional kafanas (taverns), live folk music and hearty Serbian food.
- **Museum of Yugoslavia and House of Flowers:** The mausoleum of Josip Broz Tito and exhibitions on the history of Yugoslavia.
- **Zemun:** A former Austro-Hungarian town on the Danube with fish restaurants, riverside walks and the Gardoš Tower.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Stari Grad (Old Town) is the most convenient base, close to Knez Mihailova and the fortress. Dorćol has trendy cafés and bars, while Savamala is a former warehouse district known for nightlife. Vračar is residential and pleasant, near the Church of Saint Sava.

### Food & Drink

Serbian food is meat-heavy and generous: try ćevapi (grilled minced-meat sausages), pljeskavica (a large spiced patty), sarma (stuffed cabbage leaves) and kajmak, a rich dairy spread. Burek is the classic breakfast, often with drinking yoghurt. Rakija, a fruit brandy, is offered as a welcome drink. Kafanas provide an authentic dining experience with live music.

### When to Visit

Spring and early autumn are ideal for walking and river activities. Summers are hot, but the river islands and beach at Ada Ciganlija offer relief, and the floating clubs are at their best. Winters are cold, though the city's cafés and restaurants remain lively.

### Getting Around

Buses, trams and trolleybuses cover the city, and central areas are walkable. Taxis are affordable, but use official taxis or apps such as CarGo or Yandex to avoid overcharging. The airport is about 30 minutes from the centre by bus or taxi.

### Day Trips

Novi Sad, Serbia's second city with the Petrovaradin Fortress, is about an hour away by high-speed train. The monasteries of Fruška Gora and the wine town of Sremski Karlovci make a good combined trip. The Golubac Fortress and Iron Gates gorge on the Danube are further but spectacular.

### Practical Tips

- Cash is still widely used, although cards are accepted in most restaurants.
- Smoking is still common in some cafés and bars.
- Nightlife starts late; clubs rarely fill up before midnight.
- Carry your passport, as police may ask for identification.

## Bengaluru, India

*India's "Silicon Valley", a garden city of pubs, palaces and a mild climate.*

- **Country:** India
- **Region:** South Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~13 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Kannada (English widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Indian Rupee (INR)
- **Main airport:** Kempegowda International Airport (BLR)
- **Best time to visit:** October–February
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Bengaluru (Bangalore), the capital of Karnataka in southern India, is the country's leading technology hub. Its elevated location on the Deccan Plateau gives it a pleasant climate year-round compared with most Indian cities. Known as the "Garden City" for its parks and tree-lined avenues, Bengaluru has grown rapidly into a sprawling metropolis. It has a cosmopolitan, young population and India's best craft beer scene. The city is also a gateway to Mysuru, Hampi and the hills of Coorg.

### Top Attractions

- **Bangalore Palace:** A Tudor-style palace inspired by Windsor Castle, with ornate interiors.
- **Lalbagh Botanical Garden:** A historic garden with a glasshouse and flower shows around Independence Day and Republic Day.
- **Cubbon Park:** A large green space in the centre, surrounded by colonial buildings.
- **Tipu Sultan's Summer Palace:** A teak palace of the 18th-century ruler of Mysore.
- **ISKCON Temple:** A large, modern Hindu temple complex dedicated to Krishna.
- **Vidhana Soudha:** The grand neo-Dravidian building housing the state legislature, beautifully lit on Sundays and holidays.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

MG Road and Brigade Road are central shopping and nightlife areas. Indiranagar and Koramangala are trendy, with restaurants, cafés and breweries. Malleswaram and Basavanagudi are traditional, with temples and South Indian eateries.

### Food & Drink

Bengaluru is famous for South Indian breakfasts like masala dosa, idli, vada and filter coffee, served at institutions such as MTR and Vidyarthi Bhavan. Bisi bele bath and ragi mudde are local dishes. The city has dozens of microbreweries. Street food lanes such as VV Puram Food Street are popular in the evenings.

### When to Visit

The weather is pleasant most of the year, but October to February is especially comfortable. The monsoon brings rain from June to September. Summers are warm but milder than in northern India.

### Getting Around

The Namma Metro network is expanding and helps avoid notorious traffic jams. Auto-rickshaws and ride-hailing apps like Uber, Ola and Rapido are widely used. The airport is far from the centre, so allow at least an hour.

### Day Trips

Mysuru, with its spectacular palace, is about three hours away. Nandi Hills is popular for sunrise views. Hampi, Coorg (Kodagu) and the temples of Belur and Halebidu make longer trips.

### Practical Tips

- Traffic is heavy; plan travel outside rush hours.
- Pubs and bars often close relatively early.
- Carry cash for small vendors, although UPI mobile payments dominate.
- Dress modestly in temples and remove shoes.

## Bergen, Norway

*Norway's colourful harbour city and the gateway to the western fjords.*

- **Country:** Norway
- **Region:** Northern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~290,000
- **Language(s):** Norwegian
- **Currency:** Norwegian Krone (NOK)
- **Main airport:** Bergen Airport Flesland (BGO)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Bergen is Norway's second-largest city, surrounded by seven mountains and located on the country's rugged west coast. It was once a major trading post of the Hanseatic League, and its colourful wooden warehouses on the Bryggen wharf are a UNESCO World Heritage Site. The city has a lively student population, a strong music and art scene and a reputation as one of the rainiest cities in Europe. Bergen is the main starting point for trips into the fjords, including the Sognefjord and Hardangerfjord. Despite its small size, it offers a rich mix of culture and nature.

### Top Attractions

- **Bryggen:** The historic Hanseatic wharf with narrow wooden alleys, craft shops and the Hanseatic Museum.
- **Mount Fløyen:** A funicular (Fløibanen) climbs to a viewpoint above the city, with hiking trails and forest lakes at the top.
- **Mount Ulriken:** The highest of Bergen's mountains, reached by cable car, with spectacular views and a popular ridge hike to Fløyen.
- **Fish Market (Fisketorget):** A harbour-side market selling seafood, fish soup and snacks, especially lively in summer.
- **KODE Art Museums:** A group of museums with works by Edvard Munch and Norwegian artists, and exhibitions dedicated to composer Edvard Grieg.
- **Troldhaugen:** The former home of Edvard Grieg, with a museum and concert hall overlooking a lake.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The area around Bryggen and Vågen harbour is central and scenic. Nordnes is a quiet peninsula with old wooden houses and the Bergen Aquarium, while Sandviken offers charming streets further along the harbour. Most visitors stay within walking distance of the centre.

### Food & Drink

Seafood dominates Bergen's cuisine, especially salmon, shrimp, cod and the creamy Bergen fish soup (Bergensk fiskesuppe). Skillingsboller, large cinnamon buns, are a local favourite. Eating out is expensive, so many travellers mix restaurant meals with market snacks and supermarket picnics.

### When to Visit

Summer offers long days, mild temperatures and the best conditions for fjord trips and hiking. Rain is common year-round, so a waterproof jacket is essential. Winter is dark and wet in the city but magical in December with the world's largest gingerbread town exhibition.

### Getting Around

The city centre is compact and walkable, and a light rail line (Bybanen) connects the centre with the airport in about 45 minutes. Ferries and express boats run to fjord destinations. The Bergen Line railway to Oslo is one of the most scenic train journeys in Europe.

### Day Trips

The Norway in a Nutshell route combines the Bergen Line, the Flåm Railway and a cruise on the narrow Nærøyfjord in one day. Hardangerfjord is known for orchards, waterfalls and the Trolltunga hike. Boat trips to the Osterfjord and Mostraumen are shorter alternatives.

### Practical Tips

- Pack rain gear regardless of the season.
- Buy the Bergen Card for discounts on museums and public transport.
- Book Norway in a Nutshell tickets in advance in summer.
- Alcohol is expensive and sold in supermarkets only until evening; stronger drinks are sold in state-run Vinmonopolet shops.

## Berlin, Germany

*A creative, historic capital where 20th-century history is written on every street.*

- **Country:** Germany
- **Region:** Central Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~3.8 million (city); ~6 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** German
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Berlin Brandenburg Airport (BER)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Berlin is Germany's capital and largest city, a place shaped by Prussian grandeur, the tragedy of the Nazi era, the division of the Cold War and the euphoria of reunification in 1990. Today it is one of Europe's most creative and tolerant cities, known for its art, music, clubs and alternative culture. The city is spread out and green, with many lakes, parks and distinct neighbourhoods, each with its own character. History is everywhere, from the remains of the Berlin Wall to thoughtful memorials. Berlin is relatively affordable for a Western European capital and rewards visitors who explore beyond the main sights.

### Top Attractions

- **Brandenburg Gate:** The neoclassical gate is the symbol of German reunification and stands at the end of Unter den Linden boulevard.
- **Reichstag Building:** The German parliament features a glass dome by Norman Foster; entry to the dome is free but must be booked online.
- **Museum Island:** A UNESCO-listed complex of five museums, including the Pergamon Museum and the Neues Museum with the bust of Nefertiti.
- **East Side Gallery:** The longest surviving section of the Berlin Wall, covered with murals by international artists.
- **Memorial to the Murdered Jews of Europe:** A field of 2,711 concrete slabs near the Brandenburg Gate, with an information centre beneath.
- **Berlin Wall Memorial (Bernauer Strasse):** The best place to understand how the Wall divided the city, with a preserved border strip and viewing tower.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Mitte is central and full of major sights. Kreuzberg and Neukölln are multicultural and known for nightlife, street food and alternative culture. Prenzlauer Berg is leafy and family-friendly, with cafés and restored buildings, while Charlottenburg in the west is elegant and home to Charlottenburg Palace.

### Food & Drink

Berlin's signature snack is currywurst, a sliced sausage with curry ketchup, while the döner kebab was popularised here by Turkish immigrants. The city has an outstanding international food scene, including many vegetarian and vegan restaurants. Beer gardens and weekend markets like Markthalle Neun are popular. Breakfast and brunch culture is strong, often served until late afternoon.

### When to Visit

Late spring and summer are the best time, with long evenings, open-air cinemas, lake swimming and beer gardens. Winters are cold and grey, but Christmas markets bring festive charm in December. Major events include the Berlin International Film Festival (Berlinale) in February and the Berlin Marathon in September.

### Getting Around

Berlin has an extensive U-Bahn, S-Bahn, tram and bus network, and the airport is connected by train in about 30 minutes. Tickets must be validated before travel. The city is flat and bike-friendly, with many rental options.

### Day Trips

Potsdam, with the Sanssouci Palace and gardens, is under an hour away. The Sachsenhausen Concentration Camp Memorial offers a sobering history lesson. The Spreewald, a network of canals and forests, is popular for boat and kayak trips.

### Practical Tips

- Some shops and cafés still accept cash only, although cards are increasingly common.
- Most shops are closed on Sundays, except at major stations.
- Famous clubs like Berghain have strict and unpredictable door policies.
- A Welcome Card combines public transport and museum discounts.

## Bogotá, Colombia

*Colombia's high-altitude capital of gold, graffiti and colonial charm.*

- **Country:** Colombia
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~7.9 million (city); ~11 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Colombian Peso (COP)
- **Main airport:** El Dorado International Airport (BOG)
- **Best time to visit:** December–March and July–August
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Bogotá, the capital of Colombia, sits on a plateau in the Andes at around 2,640 metres above sea level. Founded by the Spanish in 1538, it has a historic centre, La Candelaria, with colonial houses, churches and museums. Bogotá has transformed in recent decades into a dynamic, cultural city with a strong arts scene, cycling culture and excellent restaurants. The climate is cool year-round, often described as "eternal autumn". The city is the gateway to Colombia's diverse regions.

### Top Attractions

- **Gold Museum (Museo del Oro):** One of the world's largest collections of pre-Hispanic gold.
- **La Candelaria:** The historic centre, with colourful houses, street art and museums.
- **Monserrate:** A mountain with a church and views over the city, reached by cable car or funicular.
- **Botero Museum:** A free museum showing works by Fernando Botero.
- **Plaza de Bolívar:** The main square with the cathedral and government buildings.
- **Graffiti tours:** Guided tours of the city's vibrant street art.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

La Candelaria is historic but quiet at night. Chapinero and Zona G are trendy, with restaurants, while Zona T and Parque 93 are upscale nightlife areas. Usaquén has a Sunday flea market.

### Food & Drink

Ajiaco, a chicken and potato soup, is Bogotá's signature dish. Arepas, empanadas and tamales are common. Colombian coffee is excellent. Andrés Carne de Res, outside the city, is a famous party restaurant.

### When to Visit

Bogotá has a mild climate year-round, with rainy seasons in April–May and October–November. Ciclovía closes main roads to cars on Sundays for cyclists.

### Getting Around

TransMilenio buses are extensive but crowded. Taxis and ride-hailing apps are common. The first metro line is under construction.

### Day Trips

The Salt Cathedral of Zipaquirá is an underground church in a salt mine. Villa de Leyva is a colonial town. Guatavita Lake is linked to the El Dorado legend.

### Practical Tips

- Allow time to adjust to the altitude.
- Use ride-hailing apps rather than hailing taxis on the street.
- Bring layers for the changeable weather.
- Keep valuables secure.

## Bologna, Italy

*Italy's culinary capital, with medieval towers and endless porticoes.*

- **Country:** Italy
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~390,000 (city); ~1 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Italian
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Bologna Guglielmo Marconi Airport (BLQ)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Bologna, the capital of the Emilia-Romagna region, is nicknamed "la dotta, la grassa, la rossa" – the learned, the fat and the red. It is home to the oldest university in the Western world, founded in 1088, has a legendary food culture, and features red-brick buildings and historically left-wing politics. The city's nearly 40 kilometres of porticoes, covered walkways, are a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Bologna is less crowded than Florence or Venice, making it a relaxed and authentic Italian city break. It is also an excellent base for exploring the food towns of the region.

### Top Attractions

- **Piazza Maggiore:** The main square, surrounded by the Basilica of San Petronio, Palazzo d'Accursio and the Fountain of Neptune.
- **Two Towers (Asinelli and Garisenda):** Medieval leaning towers that symbolise Bologna; access to Asinelli has been restricted during stabilisation works, so check before visiting.
- **Archiginnasio:** The historic seat of the university, with an anatomical theatre carved in wood and walls covered with coats of arms.
- **Portico di San Luca:** The world's longest portico climbs about 3.8 km to the Sanctuary of the Madonna di San Luca, with views over the hills.
- **Quadrilatero:** A medieval market district full of delis, fresh pasta shops and wine bars.
- **Basilica of Santo Stefano:** A complex of interconnected churches known as the Seven Churches, among the oldest in the city.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The historic centre is compact, and staying near Piazza Maggiore puts everything within walking distance. The university quarter around Via Zamboni is lively and student-filled, while Santo Stefano is elegant and calm. The Bolognina area north of the station is more local and affordable.

### Food & Drink

Bologna gave the world mortadella, tortellini in broth and tagliatelle al ragù, the true version of what outsiders call "spaghetti bolognese". Lasagne verdi and crescentine (fried dough) with cured meats are also local favourites. The region produces Parmigiano Reggiano, Prosciutto di Parma and traditional balsamic vinegar of Modena. Aperitivo with a glass of Lambrusco or Pignoletto is a daily ritual.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are the most pleasant seasons for walking and eating. Summers are hot and humid, and some restaurants close in August. Winters are foggy and cold but perfect for hearty pasta dishes.

### Getting Around

The centre is best explored on foot, with buses for longer trips. The Marconi Express monorail connects the airport and central station in about 7 minutes. Bologna is a major rail hub, with fast trains to Florence (around 35 minutes), Milan and Venice.

### Day Trips

Modena (balsamic vinegar and Ferrari), Parma (ham and cheese) and Ravenna (Byzantine mosaics) are all easy train trips. Food tours visiting Parmigiano Reggiano dairies are very popular. Florence is close enough for a day trip by high-speed train.

### Practical Tips

- Many restaurants close on Sunday evenings or Mondays; check opening hours.
- Book food tours to cheese and balsamic producers in advance.
- The historic centre has limited traffic zones (ZTL) where driving is restricted.
- Walk under the porticoes to stay dry in rain and cool in summer.

## Bordeaux, France

*An elegant riverside city at the heart of the world's most famous wine region.*

- **Country:** France
- **Region:** Western Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~260,000 (city); ~1 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** French
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Bordeaux–Mérignac Airport (BOD)
- **Best time to visit:** May–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Bordeaux is a graceful port city on the Garonne River in southwestern France, renowned for its 18th-century architecture and its wine. Much of the city centre, known as the Port of the Moon, is a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Over the past two decades, Bordeaux has transformed its riverfront, cleaned its limestone façades and introduced trams, becoming one of France's most liveable cities. It is the gateway to famous wine appellations such as Saint-Émilion, Médoc and Pessac-Léognan. The city also offers excellent museums, restaurants and shopping.

### Top Attractions

- **Place de la Bourse and the Water Mirror:** An elegant 18th-century square facing a shallow reflecting pool, the most photographed spot in the city.
- **La Cité du Vin:** A striking modern museum dedicated to wine cultures worldwide, including a tasting at the panoramic top floor.
- **Saint-André Cathedral and Pey-Berland Tower:** A Gothic cathedral with a separate bell tower offering views over the rooftops.
- **Rue Sainte-Catherine:** One of Europe's longest pedestrian shopping streets.
- **Grand Théâtre:** A neoclassical opera house considered one of the finest in France.
- **Bassins des Lumières:** A former submarine base turned into a large digital art centre with immersive projections.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The historic centre around Saint-Pierre and the Golden Triangle is elegant and convenient. Chartrons, the former wine merchants' district, has antique shops, cafés and a Sunday market. Saint-Michel is multicultural and lively, while Bastide on the right bank offers views back to the old city.

### Food & Drink

Bordeaux cuisine includes entrecôte à la bordelaise (steak in red wine sauce), oysters from nearby Arcachon Bay and canelés, small caramelised pastries flavoured with rum and vanilla. The Marché des Capucins is the city's main food market. Wine bars offer tastings of local reds, dry whites and sweet Sauternes.

### When to Visit

Spring and early autumn are ideal, and the grape harvest usually takes place in September and October. The Bordeaux Wine Festival is held in June in some years. Summers are warm and sunny, but August can be quiet as locals go on holiday.

### Getting Around

The tram network is modern and efficient, and the centre is pleasant to explore on foot or by bike. The high-speed TGV connects Bordeaux with Paris in about 2 hours. Buses and shuttles link the airport to the city centre.

### Day Trips

The medieval village of Saint-Émilion, surrounded by vineyards, is a UNESCO site and easy to reach by train. The Médoc wine route passes famous châteaux such as Margaux and Pauillac. Arcachon Bay and the Dune of Pilat, the tallest sand dune in Europe, are about an hour away.

### Practical Tips

- Many châteaux require advance booking for tours and tastings.
- Rent a bike and ride along the riverside quays.
- Oysters with white wine at the Marché des Capucins is a classic Saturday morning experience.
- Consider the Bordeaux CityPass for transport and museum entry.

## Boston, United States (Massachusetts)

*A historic city of revolutionary history, universities and sports passion.*

- **Country:** United States (Massachusetts)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~650,000 (city); ~4.9 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Logan International Airport (BOS)
- **Best time to visit:** May–October
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Boston, the capital of Massachusetts, is one of the oldest cities in the United States, founded in 1630 by Puritan settlers. It played a central role in the American Revolution, with events such as the Boston Tea Party and the battles of Lexington and Concord. Boston is a leading centre of education and research, home to Harvard and MIT in neighbouring Cambridge. The city is compact and walkable, with brick streets, historic neighbourhoods and waterfront views. Bostonians are passionate about sports, especially the Red Sox.

### Top Attractions

- **Freedom Trail:** A 4-kilometre walking trail linking 16 historic sites.
- **Boston Common and Public Garden:** America's oldest public park and a garden with swan boats.
- **Fenway Park:** The oldest Major League Baseball stadium, home of the Red Sox.
- **Museum of Fine Arts:** One of the largest art museums in the US.
- **Harvard University:** Tours of the campus in Cambridge.
- **Faneuil Hall and Quincy Market:** A historic market with food stalls.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Back Bay is elegant, with brownstones and shopping on Newbury Street. Beacon Hill is historic, with gas lamps, and the North End is Boston's Little Italy. Seaport is modern.

### Food & Drink

New England clam chowder, lobster rolls and oysters are must-tries. Cannoli from the North End are famous. Boston cream pie originated here.

### When to Visit

Summer and autumn are ideal, with fall foliage in October. Winters are cold and snowy.

### Getting Around

The "T" subway system covers the city. The city is walkable.

### Day Trips

Salem, Cape Cod, Plymouth and Lexington/Concord are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Book hotels early during university graduations.
- Walk the Freedom Trail with a guide.
- Try a lobster roll.
- Check the Red Sox schedule.

## Bratislava, Slovakia

*A compact Danube capital with a charming old town and a hilltop castle.*

- **Country:** Slovakia
- **Region:** Central Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~480,000
- **Language(s):** Slovak
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** M. R. Štefánik Airport (BTS); Vienna International Airport (VIE) is about 1 hour away
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Bratislava, the capital of Slovakia, lies on the Danube River close to the borders of Austria and Hungary. For centuries it was known as Pressburg or Pozsony and served as the coronation city of Hungarian kings. Its small, pedestrian-friendly old town is full of pastel buildings, quirky bronze statues and cafés. Bratislava is often visited as a day trip from Vienna, but staying overnight lets you enjoy its relaxed atmosphere and lower prices. The city combines historic charm with bold socialist-era architecture.

### Top Attractions

- **Bratislava Castle:** A rectangular white castle with four corner towers sits on a hill above the Danube, housing a museum and offering wide views.
- **Old Town (Staré Mesto):** Cobbled streets, the Main Square, the Old Town Hall and statues such as Čumil, a bronze worker peering out of a manhole.
- **St. Martin's Cathedral:** The Gothic coronation church of Hungarian kings, topped by a gilded replica of the crown.
- **Blue Church (Church of St. Elizabeth):** An Art Nouveau church painted entirely in pale blue, one of the city's most photographed buildings.
- **UFO Tower (Most SNP):** A bridge with a flying-saucer-shaped observation deck and restaurant high above the Danube.
- **Devín Castle:** Dramatic ruins on a cliff at the confluence of the Morava and Danube rivers, a short bus or boat ride from the centre.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Staying in or near the Old Town is best for first-time visitors. The riverside Eurovea and Sky Park areas are modern, with shopping and new apartments. Petržalka, across the river, is a huge socialist housing estate offering cheaper accommodation.

### Food & Drink

Slovak cuisine is hearty; the national dish is bryndzové halušky, potato dumplings with sheep's cheese and bacon. Kapustnica, a sauerkraut soup, is popular in winter. The Small Carpathians wine region produces good white wines, and local beers are inexpensive. Bratislava rožky, crescent-shaped pastries filled with walnut or poppy seed, make a good souvenir.

### When to Visit
