Late spring through early autumn is ideal for walking and riverside cafés. Summer brings open-air concerts and festivals. December's Christmas markets on the Main Square are charming and less crowded than those in Vienna.

### Getting Around

The Old Town is compact and walkable. Trams and buses cover the rest of the city, and tickets must be validated. Frequent trains, buses and summer boats connect Bratislava with Vienna in about an hour.

### Day Trips

The Small Carpathian wine towns of Pezinok and Modra are easy to reach. Čachtice Castle and Trenčín further north offer medieval history. Vienna and Győr in Hungary are both close enough for a day trip.

### Practical Tips

- One or two days are enough to see the main sights.
- Take the Danube boat from Vienna for a scenic arrival in summer.
- Many restaurants in the Old Town are geared towards tourists; walk a few streets away for better value.
- The Bratislava City Card includes public transport and a walking tour.

## Bruges, Belgium

*A fairy-tale medieval city of canals, belfries and chocolate shops.*

- **Country:** Belgium
- **Region:** Western Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~120,000
- **Language(s):** Dutch (Flemish)
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Brussels Airport (BRU), about 1.5 hours by train
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September; December for Christmas markets
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Bruges is one of Europe's best-preserved medieval cities, a UNESCO World Heritage Site in the Flanders region of Belgium. In the Middle Ages it was one of the richest trading cities in Europe thanks to the cloth trade, before its access to the sea silted up and the city fell into a long sleep. That decline froze its streets in time, leaving cobbled lanes, canals and brick gables almost untouched. Today Bruges is a romantic destination famous for chocolate, beer, lace and Flemish Primitive art. It is small and very popular, so staying overnight lets you enjoy it after day-trippers leave.

### Top Attractions

- **Markt and Belfry:** The central square is dominated by the 83-metre Belfry; climb its 366 steps for views across the rooftops.
- **Burg Square:** Home to the Gothic City Hall and the Basilica of the Holy Blood, which houses a revered relic.
- **Canal boat tour:** Short boat trips through the canals offer the classic views of Bruges.
- **Groeninge Museum:** A collection of Flemish Primitive masterpieces by Jan van Eyck and Hans Memling.
- **Begijnhof and Minnewater:** A tranquil 13th-century beguinage with white houses and a lake known as the Lake of Love.
- **Church of Our Lady:** Its brick spire is one of the tallest in Belgium, and it holds Michelangelo's Madonna and Child.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Almost all visitors stay inside the old town ring, which is small and walkable. The area around Markt is central but busy, while the St. Anna quarter to the east is quieter and more residential. Guesthouses and B&Bs are plentiful.

### Food & Drink

Bruges is famous for Belgian chocolate, with dozens of chocolatiers in the centre. Moules-frites (mussels and fries), Flemish beef stew (stoofvlees) and waffles are local staples. The De Halve Maan brewery produces the local Brugse Zot beer and even pumps beer through an underground pipeline. Frites from a stand near the Markt are a classic snack.

### When to Visit

Spring and early autumn offer pleasant weather and fewer crowds than summer. Weekends and summer holidays can be extremely busy. December brings Christmas markets and an ice-skating rink, creating a festive atmosphere.

### Getting Around

Bruges is best explored on foot or by bike. The train station is a 15–20 minute walk from the Markt, with frequent buses. Direct trains connect Bruges with Brussels, Ghent and Brussels Airport.

### Day Trips

Ghent, a lively university city with its own impressive medieval centre and the Ghent Altarpiece, is just 25 minutes away. The seaside town of Knokke-Heist and the fishing village of Damme, reachable by bike along a canal, are other options. The First World War battlefields around Ypres (Ieper) are about an hour away.

### Practical Tips

- Visit early in the morning or in the evening to enjoy the city without crowds.
- Buy chocolate from smaller artisan shops rather than chains.
- Rent a bike to ride along the canals to Damme.
- Wear comfortable shoes; cobblestones are uneven.

## Brussels, Belgium

*The capital of Europe, famous for the Grand-Place, comics, beer and chocolate.*

- **Country:** Belgium
- **Region:** Western Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.2 million (Brussels-Capital Region)
- **Language(s):** French and Dutch
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Brussels Airport (BRU); Brussels South Charleroi Airport (CRL)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Brussels is the capital of Belgium and the de facto capital of the European Union, hosting the European Commission, Council and Parliament. It is a bilingual, multicultural city where French and Dutch are both official. Beyond its political role, Brussels is known for its stunning Grand-Place, Art Nouveau architecture, comic-strip culture and exceptional food and beer. The city can seem chaotic at first, but it rewards visitors with a relaxed, quirky charm. It is also an excellent base for day trips to Bruges, Ghent and Antwerp.

### Top Attractions

- **Grand-Place:** One of the most beautiful squares in the world, surrounded by ornate guildhalls and the Gothic Town Hall; every two years it is covered in a flower carpet.
- **Manneken Pis:** A tiny bronze statue of a urinating boy, often dressed in costumes, and a beloved symbol of the city's humour.
- **Atomium:** A giant model of an iron crystal built for the 1958 World's Fair, with exhibitions and a panoramic viewing sphere.
- **Royal Museums of Fine Arts and Magritte Museum:** Collections ranging from Flemish masters to Belgian surrealism.
- **Comic Strip Route and Belgian Comic Strip Center:** Murals across the city and a museum celebrating Tintin, the Smurfs and other Belgian comics.
- **European Quarter and Parlamentarium:** A free visitor centre explaining how the European Parliament works.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The area around the Grand-Place and Sainte-Catherine is central and lively. Ixelles and Saint-Gilles are popular for Art Nouveau buildings, bars and restaurants, while the Sablon is elegant, with antique shops and chocolatiers. The Marolles district has a daily flea market on Place du Jeu de Balle.

### Food & Drink

Brussels is a paradise for food lovers: try moules-frites, waffles (the lighter Brussels waffle or the sweeter Liège waffle), stoemp and carbonnade flamande, a beef and beer stew. Frites are served with a wide choice of sauces from friteries. The city has hundreds of Belgian beers, from Trappist ales to sour lambics and gueuzes brewed nearby. Chocolate houses such as Pierre Marcolini, Neuhaus and Wittamer are world-famous.

### When to Visit

Spring and early autumn are the most pleasant times, though rain is possible year-round. The Flower Carpet takes place in August of even-numbered years. Winter brings the Winter Wonders Christmas market and light shows on the Grand-Place.

### Getting Around

Metro, trams and buses are run by STIB/MIVB, and contactless cards can be used. The centre is walkable, though hilly between the lower and upper town. Trains from the airport reach Brussels Central in about 20 minutes, and Eurostar and high-speed trains link Brussels with Paris, London, Amsterdam and Cologne.

### Day Trips

Bruges, Ghent, Antwerp and Leuven are all within an hour by train. The Waterloo battlefield, site of Napoleon's defeat in 1815, lies just south of the city. Dinant and the Ardennes offer castles and nature.

### Practical Tips

- Avoid overpriced restaurants on Rue des Bouchers near the Grand-Place.
- Visit Delirium Café or a traditional estaminet to sample Belgian beers.
- Watch your belongings around major stations.
- The Brussels Card offers entry to many museums.

## Bucharest, Romania

*A city of grand boulevards, belle époque villas and monumental communist architecture.*

- **Country:** Romania
- **Region:** Southeastern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.7 million (city); ~2.3 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Romanian
- **Currency:** Romanian Leu (RON)
- **Main airport:** Henri Coandă International Airport (OTP)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Bucharest is the capital and largest city of Romania, once nicknamed the "Little Paris of the East" for its elegant boulevards and belle époque buildings. Its history is layered, from Ottoman-era inns and Orthodox churches to the enormous projects of dictator Nicolae Ceaușescu. After the 1989 revolution, the city has become a dynamic, creative capital with a thriving café and nightlife scene. Bucharest is affordable compared with most European capitals. It is also a gateway to Transylvania and the Carpathian Mountains.

### Top Attractions

- **Palace of the Parliament:** One of the largest administrative buildings in the world, built under Ceaușescu; guided tours require identification and advance booking.
- **Old Town (Lipscani):** A historic district of narrow streets packed with bars, restaurants and churches.
- **Stavropoleos Monastery:** A small, exquisite 18th-century church with ornate carvings and a peaceful courtyard.
- **Romanian Athenaeum:** A beautiful domed concert hall and home of the George Enescu Philharmonic.
- **Village Museum:** An open-air museum in Herăstrău (King Michael I) Park with traditional houses, churches and windmills from across Romania.
- **Calea Victoriei:** The city's most elegant avenue, lined with palaces, museums and the Revolution Square.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Old Town and University area are central but can be noisy at night. Cotroceni and the areas around Dorobanți and Floreasca are leafy and upscale. Piața Romană and the area around Calea Victoriei offer a convenient balance of location and atmosphere.

### Food & Drink

Romanian food includes sarmale (cabbage rolls with minced meat), mici (grilled skinless sausages), ciorbă (sour soups) and mămăligă (polenta). Papanași, fried doughnuts with sour cream and jam, are a must-try dessert. Caru' cu Bere, a historic beer hall, is famous for its décor. Romanian wines, such as Fetească Neagră, are increasingly well regarded.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are pleasant and colourful. Summers can be very hot, while winters are cold and sometimes snowy. The George Enescu Festival, a major classical music event, takes place in late summer of odd-numbered years.

### Getting Around

Bucharest has a metro network and many buses and trams. The airport is about 30–45 minutes from the centre by train, bus or taxi. Use ride-hailing apps such as Bolt or Uber to avoid taxi scams.

### Day Trips

Snagov Monastery, said to be Vlad the Impaler's burial place, lies on a lake north of the city. Mogoșoaia Palace is a beautiful Brâncovenesc-style palace nearby. Longer trips reach Brașov, Bran Castle and Peleș Castle in the Carpathians, about 2.5–3 hours away.

### Practical Tips

- Book the Palace of the Parliament tour in advance and bring your passport.
- Traffic is heavy; the metro is often the fastest option.
- Stray dogs are now rare, but be cautious in outlying areas.
- Tipping around 10% is customary in restaurants.

## Budapest, Hungary

*The "Pearl of the Danube", famed for thermal baths, grand architecture and ruin bars.*

- **Country:** Hungary
- **Region:** Central Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.7 million (city); ~3 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Hungarian
- **Currency:** Hungarian Forint (HUF)
- **Main airport:** Budapest Ferenc Liszt International Airport (BUD)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Budapest, the capital of Hungary, is one of Europe's most beautiful cities, split by the Danube into hilly Buda and flat, bustling Pest. The city was officially formed in 1873 by uniting Buda, Óbuda and Pest, and its golden age during the Austro-Hungarian Empire produced grand boulevards and landmarks. Budapest is famous for its natural hot springs, which feed historic bathhouses. It combines imperial architecture, a dramatic riverside panorama and a vibrant nightlife. Budapest is excellent value compared with Western European capitals.

### Top Attractions

- **Hungarian Parliament Building:** A Gothic Revival masterpiece on the Danube and one of the largest parliament buildings in the world; guided tours show the Holy Crown.
- **Buda Castle and Castle District:** The royal palace houses the Hungarian National Gallery, while the medieval district includes Matthias Church and Fisherman's Bastion.
- **Széchenyi and Gellért Baths:** Iconic thermal baths; Széchenyi is known for its huge outdoor pools and Gellért for its Art Nouveau interiors.
- **St. Stephen's Basilica:** The largest church in Budapest, with a dome offering panoramic views.
- **Ruin bars:** Bars in abandoned buildings of the Jewish Quarter, led by Szimpla Kert, the most famous.
- **Danube cruise and Chain Bridge:** Evening river cruises reveal the illuminated Parliament and castle.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

District V (Belváros-Lipótváros) is central and close to the Parliament and river. District VII, the Jewish Quarter, is the nightlife hub. District VI around Andrássy Avenue is elegant, while Buda offers quieter streets and views.

### Food & Drink

Hungarian cuisine is rich and paprika-flavoured: try goulash (gulyás) soup, chicken paprikash, lángos (fried dough with sour cream and cheese) and stuffed peppers. The Great Market Hall is a good place for local products. Chimney cake (kürtőskalács) and Dobos torte are popular sweets. Hungary produces famous wines, including sweet Tokaji, and pálinka, a fruit brandy.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are the best times for sightseeing. Summer brings festivals such as Sziget, one of Europe's largest music festivals, held on an island in the Danube in August. Winter is cold, but Christmas markets and steaming outdoor baths are memorable.

### Getting Around

Budapest has an extensive metro, tram and bus network; tram line 2 along the Danube is especially scenic. The 100E bus connects the airport with the city centre. Tickets must be validated, and inspectors are strict.

### Day Trips

The Danube Bend towns of Szentendre, Visegrád and Esztergom are reachable by train, bus or boat. Lake Balaton, Central Europe's largest lake, is about 1.5 hours away. The Gödöllő Royal Palace was a favourite residence of Empress Elisabeth (Sisi).

### Practical Tips

- Check prices before ordering in tourist-area restaurants.
- Bring flip-flops and a towel to the baths, or rent them on site.
- Some places accept euros, but the exchange rate is poor; pay in forints.
- Validate public transport tickets to avoid fines.

## Buenos Aires, Argentina

*The "Paris of South America", a city of tango, steak and passionate nightlife.*

- **Country:** Argentina
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~3.1 million (city); ~15 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Argentine Peso (ARS)
- **Main airport:** Ministro Pistarini International (EZE); Aeroparque Jorge Newbery (AEP)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and September–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Buenos Aires, the capital of Argentina, sits on the Río de la Plata estuary. Its European-style architecture, grand boulevards and café culture reflect waves of Spanish and Italian immigration. The city is famous for tango, football, steak and late-night living. Each neighbourhood (barrio) has its own character, from colourful La Boca to elegant Recoleta. Buenos Aires is a cultural powerhouse with theatres, bookshops and museums.

### Top Attractions

- **Recoleta Cemetery:** An elaborate cemetery with mausoleums, including Eva Perón's tomb.
- **Plaza de Mayo and Casa Rosada:** The political heart of Argentina, with the pink presidential palace.
- **La Boca and Caminito:** A colourful street with tango dancers, next to the Boca Juniors stadium.
- **San Telmo:** A historic area with a Sunday antiques market.
- **Teatro Colón:** One of the world's greatest opera houses.
- **El Ateneo Grand Splendid:** A bookshop in a former theatre.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Palermo is trendy, with restaurants and nightlife. Recoleta is elegant, and San Telmo is bohemian.

### Food & Drink

Argentine steak at parrillas, empanadas, choripán and dulce de leche are staples. Malbec wine is the national favourite. Dinner is late, often after 10 pm.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are best. Summers are hot and humid.

### Getting Around

The Subte (subway), buses and taxis serve the city. The SUBE card works on public transport.

### Day Trips

Tigre and the Paraná Delta, Colonia del Sacramento in Uruguay and estancias (ranches) are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Exchange rates fluctuate; check current options for paying by card or cash.
- Watch belongings in crowded areas.
- Take a tango class or show.
- Dinner starts late.

## Busan, South Korea

*South Korea's vibrant port city of beaches, seafood and hillside temples.*

- **Country:** South Korea
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~3.3 million
- **Language(s):** Korean
- **Currency:** South Korean Won (KRW)
- **Main airport:** Gimhae International Airport (PUS)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Busan is South Korea's second-largest city and largest port, on the southeastern tip of the Korean Peninsula. It is surrounded by mountains and sea, giving it beaches, hiking trails and dramatic coastal scenery. During the Korean War, Busan served as a temporary capital and refuge for millions. Today it is famous for its seafood markets, colourful hillside villages and the Busan International Film Festival. The city is more relaxed than Seoul and easy to reach by high-speed train.

### Top Attractions

- **Haeundae Beach:** Korea's most famous beach, lined with hotels and restaurants.
- **Gamcheon Culture Village:** A colourful hillside village of painted houses, murals and art installations.
- **Jagalchi Fish Market:** Korea's largest fish market, where you can choose seafood and have it cooked upstairs.
- **Haedong Yonggungsa Temple:** A picturesque Buddhist temple set on seaside rocks.
- **Taejongdae:** A coastal park with cliffs, lighthouses and views.
- **Gwangalli Beach:** Popular for views of the illuminated Gwangan Bridge at night.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Haeundae is ideal for beaches and resorts. Seomyeon is a busy shopping and nightlife district, and Nampo-dong is close to the markets and old town. Gwangalli offers cafés and bridge views.

### Food & Drink

Busan is famous for seafood, including raw fish (hoe), grilled clams and eomuk (fish cakes). Dwaeji gukbap, a pork and rice soup, is a local speciality. Milmyeon, cold wheat noodles, originated here. Ssiat hotteok, sweet pancakes filled with seeds, is a popular street snack.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are pleasant, while summer is hot, humid and the beach season. The Busan International Film Festival is held in October. Winters are mild compared with Seoul.

### Getting Around

Busan has an efficient subway system. KTX trains connect Busan with Seoul in about 2.5 hours. The T-money card works on buses and subways.

### Day Trips

Gyeongju, the ancient capital of the Silla kingdom, is under an hour away by train. The Geoje and Tongyeong areas offer islands and coastal scenery. Ferries connect Busan with Fukuoka in Japan.

### Practical Tips

- Download Naver Map or KakaoMap, as Google Maps is limited in Korea.
- Use a T-money card for public transport.
- Tipping is not customary.
- Many restaurants serve dishes for a minimum of two people.

## Cancún, Mexico

*Mexico's Caribbean resort capital of turquoise water, white sand and nightlife.*

- **Country:** Mexico
- **Region:** North America (Caribbean coast)
- **Population (approx.):** ~950,000
- **Language(s):** Spanish (English widely spoken in tourist areas)
- **Currency:** Mexican Peso (MXN); US Dollars widely accepted
- **Main airport:** Cancún International Airport (CUN)
- **Best time to visit:** December–April
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Cancún, on the northeastern tip of the Yucatán Peninsula, was developed in the 1970s as a planned resort. It is now one of the world's most popular beach destinations, known for its Hotel Zone, a 22-kilometre strip of beaches and resorts. Cancún is also a gateway to the Riviera Maya, Mayan ruins and cenotes (natural sinkholes). The city has lively nightlife and all-inclusive resorts. Beyond the Hotel Zone, downtown Cancún offers a more local experience.

### Top Attractions

- **Hotel Zone beaches:** Playa Delfines, Playa Chac Mool and others with turquoise water.
- **Isla Mujeres:** An island with beaches and snorkelling, reached by ferry.
- **Underwater Museum of Art (MUSA):** Underwater sculptures for snorkelling and diving.
- **Chichén Itzá:** A famous Mayan site and one of the New Seven Wonders of the World.
- **Cenotes:** Natural swimming holes along the Ruta de los Cenotes.
- **Mayan Museum of Cancún:** Artefacts and the San Miguelito ruins.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Hotel Zone is resort-focused. Downtown is more affordable, and Playa del Carmen is south.

### Food & Drink

Yucatecan dishes like cochinita pibil and sopa de lima are popular. Seafood tacos and ceviche are everywhere.

### When to Visit

December to April is the dry season. Hurricane season runs from June to November. Sargassum seaweed can affect beaches in summer.

### Getting Around

Buses run along the Hotel Zone. ADO buses connect with other towns. Taxis are expensive.

### Day Trips

Chichén Itzá, Tulum, Isla Mujeres and Holbox are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Use reef-safe sunscreen.
- Check sargassum reports.
- Agree taxi fares in advance.
- Pay in pesos for better rates.

## Cartagena, Colombia

*A romantic walled city of colourful colonial streets on the Caribbean.*

- **Country:** Colombia
- **Region:** South America (Caribbean coast)
- **Population (approx.):** ~1 million
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Colombian Peso (COP)
- **Main airport:** Rafael Núñez International Airport (CTG)
- **Best time to visit:** December–April
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Cartagena de Indias, on Colombia's Caribbean coast, was one of the most important ports of the Spanish Empire, through which gold and silver from the Americas were shipped to Spain. To defend it from pirates and rival powers, the Spanish built massive walls and fortresses, now part of a UNESCO World Heritage Site. The old walled city is a maze of colourful colonial houses with flower-draped balconies, plazas and churches. Cartagena is also known for its Afro-Caribbean culture, lively music and nearby islands. It is Colombia's most visited destination.

### Top Attractions

- **Walled City (Ciudad Amurallada):** Colonial streets, plazas and churches, best explored on foot in the early morning or evening.
- **Castillo San Felipe de Barajas:** The largest Spanish fortress in the Americas, with tunnels and views.
- **City walls:** Walk along the walls at sunset, with bars like Café del Mar offering sea views.
- **Getsemaní:** A vibrant neighbourhood with street art, bars and salsa, centred on Plaza de la Trinidad.
- **Rosario Islands:** A coral archipelago with beaches and snorkelling, reached by boat.
- **Palace of the Inquisition:** A museum on the history of the Inquisition and the city.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Walled City is atmospheric, with boutique hotels in colonial mansions. Getsemaní is lively and more affordable, and Bocagrande has high-rise hotels and beaches.

### Food & Drink

Caribbean-Colombian cuisine includes fried fish with coconut rice and patacones (fried plantains), arepas de huevo and ceviche. Street vendors sell tropical fruits. Cartagena has many excellent restaurants, from street food to fine dining.

### When to Visit

December to April is the dry season, though it is hot and humid year-round. October and November are the rainiest months. Holidays attract large crowds.

### Getting Around

The old town is walkable. Taxis are inexpensive but agree fares first. Boats to the islands depart from the Muelle de la Bodeguita.

### Day Trips

The Rosario Islands, Playa Blanca on Barú and the mud volcano El Totumo are popular. Palenque de San Basilio, the first free African town in the Americas, is a UNESCO cultural site.

### Practical Tips

- Carry water and sun protection.
- Negotiate prices with street vendors.
- Book island tours with reputable operators.
- Beware of overcharging in tourist areas.

## Cebu, Philippines

*The Philippines' oldest city and a gateway to islands, reefs and waterfalls.*

- **Country:** Philippines
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~960,000 (city); ~3 million (Metro Cebu)
- **Language(s):** Cebuano (Filipino and English widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Philippine Peso (PHP)
- **Main airport:** Mactan–Cebu International Airport (CEB)
- **Best time to visit:** December–May
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Cebu City, on the island of Cebu in the Visayas region, is the oldest city in the Philippines, where Ferdinand Magellan arrived in 1521. It is the country's second-most important economic centre and a major transport hub. The city has Spanish colonial landmarks, a strong Catholic heritage and lively festivals. Nearby Mactan Island has beach resorts, while the rest of Cebu offers waterfalls, diving and whale sharks. English is widely spoken, making travel easy.

### Top Attractions

- **Magellan's Cross:** A cross planted by Spanish explorers in 1521, housed in a small chapel.
- **Basilica Minore del Santo Niño:** The oldest Roman Catholic church in the Philippines, home to a revered statue of the Christ Child.
- **Fort San Pedro:** A Spanish-era triangular fort in the old city.
- **Temple of Leah:** A Roman-inspired temple in the hills with city views.
- **Taoist Temple:** A colourful temple in the Beverly Hills area.
- **Mactan Island:** Beach resorts, island hopping and diving.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Cebu IT Park and Cebu Business Park are modern areas with hotels and restaurants. Mactan Island is best for beaches and resorts. The downtown area is historic but busy.

### Food & Drink

Cebu is famous for lechon, whole roasted pig with crispy skin, often considered the best in the Philippines. Seafood, puso (rice cooked in woven palm leaves) and dried mangoes are local favourites. Larsian is a popular barbecue food area. Filipino dishes like adobo and sinigang are everywhere.

### When to Visit

The dry season from December to May is best. The Sinulog Festival in January is one of the country's biggest festivals. The rainy season brings typhoons from June to November.

### Getting Around

Taxis, ride-hailing apps (Grab) and jeepneys are common. Traffic can be heavy. Ferries connect Cebu with Bohol and other islands.

### Day Trips

Moalboal offers the sardine run and turtles. Kawasan Falls is famous for canyoneering. Bohol, with the Chocolate Hills and tarsiers, is a ferry ride away.

### Practical Tips

- Avoid Oslob whale shark tours if you are concerned about animal welfare.
- Book ferries in advance during holidays.
- Carry cash for small shops and rural areas.
- Check typhoon forecasts during the rainy season.

## Charleston, United States (South Carolina)

*A gracious Southern city of pastel houses, cobblestones and Lowcountry cuisine.*

- **Country:** United States (South Carolina)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~155,000 (city); ~850,000 (metro)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Charleston International Airport (CHS)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and September–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Charleston, founded in 1670, is one of the oldest cities in the United States and a jewel of the American South. Its historic district features antebellum mansions, cobblestone streets, church steeples and gardens. The city's wealth was built on plantations and the slave trade, and museums address this history. Charleston is known for Southern hospitality and an acclaimed food scene. It regularly ranks among the best small cities in the US.

### Top Attractions

- **Historic District and The Battery:** Pastel houses on Rainbow Row and a waterfront promenade.
- **Fort Sumter:** Where the first shots of the Civil War were fired in 1861.
- **International African American Museum:** A museum on the site of a former slave-trade wharf.
- **Charleston City Market:** Historic market with sweetgrass baskets.
- **Plantations:** Magnolia, Middleton Place and McLeod Plantation, with tours on the lives of the enslaved.
- **King Street:** A shopping and dining street.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The historic district is best. Mount Pleasant and the beaches of Folly and Sullivan's Island are nearby.

### Food & Drink

Lowcountry cuisine includes shrimp and grits, she-crab soup, Hoppin' John and fried green tomatoes.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal. Summers are hot and humid, with hurricane risk.

### Getting Around

The historic district is walkable. Free DASH trolleys serve downtown.

### Day Trips

Beaches, plantations and Savannah (2 hours) are nearby.

### Practical Tips

- Book popular restaurants in advance.
- Visit plantations that present the history of slavery.
- Wear comfortable shoes.
- Check hurricane forecasts.

## Chengdu, China

*The laid-back home of giant pandas, teahouses and fiery Sichuan cuisine.*

- **Country:** China
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~21 million (municipality)
- **Language(s):** Mandarin Chinese (Sichuanese dialect)
- **Currency:** Chinese Yuan Renminbi (CNY)
- **Main airport:** Chengdu Tianfu International (TFU); Chengdu Shuangliu International (CTU)
- **Best time to visit:** March–June and September–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Chengdu, the capital of Sichuan Province in southwestern China, is known for its relaxed lifestyle, spicy food and giant pandas. The city has over 2,000 years of history and was the capital of the Shu kingdom during the Three Kingdoms period. Chengdu is a UNESCO City of Gastronomy. Locals love teahouses, mahjong and leisurely afternoons in parks. The city is also a gateway to Tibet, Jiuzhaigou and the mountains of western Sichuan.

### Top Attractions

- **Chengdu Research Base of Giant Panda Breeding:** The best place to see giant pandas, especially in the morning when they are active.
- **Wenshu Monastery:** A peaceful Buddhist temple with a vegetarian restaurant and teahouse.
- **Jinli Ancient Street:** A restored street with snacks, crafts and traditional architecture.
- **Kuanzhai Alley:** Historic lanes with courtyard houses, teahouses and shops.
- **People's Park:** A lively park with teahouses, dancing and a matchmaking corner.
- **Sichuan opera:** Performances featuring the famous "face-changing" art.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Chunxi Road and Taikoo Li are central shopping areas. Kuanzhai Alley and Wenshu are historic. The southern districts are modern, with business hotels.

### Food & Drink

Sichuan cuisine is famous for the numbing heat of Sichuan pepper and chilli. Try hot pot, mapo tofu, kung pao chicken, dan dan noodles and twice-cooked pork. Street snacks include chuan chuan (skewers dipped in broth). Teahouses serve jasmine and green teas.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are pleasant. Summers are hot and humid, and winters are cool and grey. Avoid the Golden Week holiday in early October.

### Getting Around

Chengdu has an extensive metro network. DiDi and taxis are widely available. High-speed trains connect Chengdu with Chongqing, Xi'an and other cities.

### Day Trips

Leshan Giant Buddha, the world's largest stone Buddha, is about an hour away by train. Mount Emei is a sacred Buddhist mountain. Dujiangyan, an ancient irrigation system, and Mount Qingcheng are UNESCO sites.

### Practical Tips

- Visit the panda base early in the morning.
- Ask for "wei la" (mild spice) if you are not used to spicy food.
- Set up mobile payment before arrival.
- Carry your passport for train tickets and hotels.

## Chiang Mai, Thailand

*Northern Thailand's cultural capital of temples, markets and mountain adventures.*

- **Country:** Thailand
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~130,000 (city); ~1.2 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Thai (Northern Thai dialect)
- **Currency:** Thai Baht (THB)
- **Main airport:** Chiang Mai International Airport (CNX)
- **Best time to visit:** November–February
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Chiang Mai is the largest city in northern Thailand, founded in 1296 as the capital of the Lanna Kingdom. Its old city is surrounded by a moat and remnants of ancient walls, containing more than 30 temples. The city is known for its relaxed pace, crafts, night markets and cooking classes. Chiang Mai is surrounded by mountains, making it a base for trekking, elephant sanctuaries and visits to hill-tribe villages. It is also a popular hub for digital nomads.

### Top Attractions

- **Wat Phra That Doi Suthep:** A golden temple on a mountain overlooking the city.
- **Old City temples:** Wat Chedi Luang, Wat Phra Singh and many others.
- **Sunday Walking Street:** A large night market along Ratchadamnoen Road.
- **Night Bazaar:** A market selling handicrafts and souvenirs.
- **Elephant sanctuaries:** Ethical sanctuaries where visitors observe elephants without riding them.
- **Doi Inthanon National Park:** Thailand's highest mountain, with waterfalls and pagodas.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Old City is central and walkable. Nimmanhaemin is trendy, with cafés and co-working spaces. The Riverside area offers quieter hotels.

### Food & Drink

Khao soi, a curry noodle soup with crispy noodles, is Chiang Mai's signature dish. Sai oua (northern sausage), nam prik ong (a chilli dip) and gaeng hang lay (pork curry) are local dishes. Cooking classes are very popular. Markets offer cheap, delicious street food.

### When to Visit

The cool season from November to February is best. Loy Krathong and Yi Peng lantern festivals in November are spectacular. The burning season (February–April) causes poor air quality.

### Getting Around

Songthaews (red shared trucks) and ride-hailing apps are common. Renting a scooter is popular but requires caution. The Old City is walkable.

### Day Trips

Chiang Rai's White Temple and Blue Temple are about three hours away. Pai is a mountain town popular with backpackers. Doi Inthanon and elephant sanctuaries are nearby.

### Practical Tips

- Choose elephant sanctuaries that do not allow riding.
- Check air quality forecasts during the burning season.
- Dress modestly at temples.
- Book Yi Peng lantern events in advance.

## Chicago, United States (Illinois)

*The "Windy City" of bold architecture, deep-dish pizza, blues and lakefront beaches.*

- **Country:** United States (Illinois)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~2.7 million (city); ~9.4 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** O'Hare International (ORD); Midway International (MDW)
- **Best time to visit:** May–October
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Chicago, on the shores of Lake Michigan, is the third-largest city in the United States. After the Great Chicago Fire of 1871, the city rebuilt with innovative architecture and became the birthplace of the skyscraper. It is known for its world-class museums, blues and jazz, comedy and sports teams. Chicago has miles of lakefront parks and beaches. The city is a Midwestern hub with a friendly, down-to-earth atmosphere.

### Top Attractions

- **Chicago Architecture Center river cruise:** The best way to see the city's famous buildings.
- **Millennium Park and Cloud Gate ("The Bean"):** A park with public art and concerts.
- **Art Institute of Chicago:** One of the world's great art museums, with works like Nighthawks and A Sunday on La Grande Jatte.
- **Willis Tower Skydeck and 360 Chicago:** Observation decks with glass ledges.
- **Navy Pier:** A pier with rides, restaurants and boat tours.
- **Wrigley Field:** The historic home of the Chicago Cubs.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Loop is the downtown core. River North and the Magnificent Mile have hotels and shopping, while Wicker Park, Logan Square and West Loop are trendy.

### Food & Drink

Deep-dish pizza, Chicago-style hot dogs and Italian beef sandwiches are local classics. The West Loop's Restaurant Row has acclaimed dining.

### When to Visit

Summer is ideal for festivals like Lollapalooza. Winters are very cold and windy.

### Getting Around

The "L" trains and buses cover the city. Divvy bikes are popular along the lakefront.

### Day Trips

Milwaukee, Indiana Dunes National Park and Oak Park (Frank Lloyd Wright) are nearby.

### Practical Tips

- Take the architecture river cruise.
- Dress warmly in winter.
- Use the Ventra card or app for transit.
- Book museum tickets in advance.

## Cologne, Germany

*A lively Rhine city crowned by one of the world's greatest Gothic cathedrals.*

- **Country:** Germany
- **Region:** Central Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.1 million
- **Language(s):** German
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Cologne Bonn Airport (CGN); Düsseldorf Airport (DUS) is nearby
- **Best time to visit:** May–September; Carnival in February
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Cologne (Köln) is Germany's fourth-largest city and one of its oldest, founded by the Romans nearly 2,000 years ago. Its twin-spired cathedral, a UNESCO World Heritage Site, dominates the skyline beside the Rhine. Although heavily bombed in the Second World War, Cologne has rebuilt itself into a cheerful, open-minded city known for its carnival, beer culture and art museums. Locals are famously sociable, and the city has a strong LGBTQ+ scene. Cologne is well connected and an easy stop on a Rhine or Germany itinerary.

### Top Attractions

- **Cologne Cathedral (Kölner Dom):** A monumental Gothic cathedral that took over 600 years to complete; climb 533 steps to the top of the south tower.
- **Museum Ludwig:** A major collection of modern art, including Pop Art and one of Europe's largest Picasso collections.
- **Old Town (Altstadt):** Colourful houses along the Rhine promenade and small squares packed with breweries.
- **Hohenzollern Bridge:** A railway bridge covered with thousands of love locks, offering views of the cathedral.
- **Chocolate Museum:** A popular museum on the Rhine covering the history of chocolate, with a chocolate fountain.
- **Romano-Germanic Museum:** Roman artefacts including the Dionysus mosaic.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Altstadt and area near the cathedral are convenient for sightseeing. The Belgian Quarter is trendy, with independent shops and bars, while Ehrenfeld is multicultural and creative. Deutz, across the river, offers views and trade-fair hotels.

### Food & Drink

Cologne's beer is Kölsch, a light top-fermented beer served in small 0.2-litre glasses called Stangen; waiters keep refilling until you cover the glass with a coaster. Traditional brewhouses serve dishes such as Halve Hahn (a rye roll with cheese, not chicken), Himmel und Ääd (mashed potatoes with apples and black pudding) and sauerbraten. Reibekuchen, potato pancakes, are popular at markets.

### When to Visit

Summer is ideal for Rhine walks and beer gardens. Cologne Carnival reaches its peak between Weiberfastnacht and Rose Monday, usually in February, with parades and costumes. December brings some of Germany's best Christmas markets.

### Getting Around

Cologne Hauptbahnhof is right next to the cathedral and is one of Germany's busiest rail hubs. Trams and buses cover the city, and the airport is connected by S-Bahn in about 15 minutes. The old town is walkable.

### Day Trips

Bonn, the former West German capital and Beethoven's birthplace, is just 20–30 minutes away. Aachen, with Charlemagne's cathedral, and Düsseldorf are also close. Rhine cruises through the Middle Rhine Valley past castles are reachable from Koblenz.

### Practical Tips

- Say "Kölsch" at a brewhouse, not "beer", and put your coaster on the glass when you are done.
- Book hotels well ahead during Carnival and major trade fairs.
- The KölnCard gives transport and museum discounts.
- Many museums are closed on Mondays.

## Colombo, Sri Lanka

*Sri Lanka's bustling commercial capital of colonial buildings and ocean views.*

- **Country:** Sri Lanka
- **Region:** South Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~750,000 (city); ~5.6 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Sinhala and Tamil (English widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Sri Lankan Rupee (LKR)
- **Main airport:** Bandaranaike International Airport (CMB)
- **Best time to visit:** December–March (west coast)
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Colombo is Sri Lanka's largest city and commercial capital, on the west coast of the island. Its natural harbour made it an important trading port for Arab, Portuguese, Dutch and British traders. The city blends colonial architecture, Buddhist temples, markets and modern developments. Many travellers pass through quickly, but Colombo has excellent restaurants, museums and seaside promenades. It is the main gateway to Sri Lanka's beaches, tea country and ancient cities.

### Top Attractions

- **Galle Face Green:** An oceanside promenade popular at sunset, with street food stalls.
- **Gangaramaya Temple:** An eclectic Buddhist temple with a museum.
- **Pettah Market:** A bustling market district.
- **National Museum of Colombo:** Sri Lanka's largest museum, with royal regalia and artefacts.
- **Seema Malaka:** A temple on a lake designed by Geoffrey Bawa.
- **Independence Memorial Hall:** A monument commemorating Sri Lanka's independence.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Colombo 3 (Kollupitiya) and Colombo 7 (Cinnamon Gardens) are upscale. Fort is the historic business district, and Mount Lavinia offers beachfront hotels.

### Food & Drink

Rice and curry, kottu roti (chopped flatbread stir-fried), hoppers (bowl-shaped pancakes) and string hoppers are staples. Seafood is excellent, and crab curry is a speciality. Ceylon tea is served everywhere.

### When to Visit

The west coast is best from December to March. The southwest monsoon brings rain from May to September. Sri Lanka has different weather patterns on each coast.

### Getting Around

Tuk-tuks and ride-hailing apps (PickMe, Uber) are common. Trains connect Colombo with Kandy and the south coast. Traffic can be heavy.

### Day Trips

Galle, a Dutch fort town, is about two hours away. Negombo is a beach town near the airport. Kandy and the hill country are reachable by train.

### Practical Tips

- Apply for the Electronic Travel Authorization (ETA) before arrival.
- Dress modestly at temples.
- Negotiate tuk-tuk fares or use metered ones.
- Carry cash for rural areas.

## Copenhagen, Denmark

*A stylish, bike-friendly capital of design, hygge and New Nordic cuisine.*

- **Country:** Denmark
- **Region:** Northern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~660,000 (city); ~2.1 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Danish (English widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Danish Krone (DKK)
- **Main airport:** Copenhagen Airport, Kastrup (CPH)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Copenhagen, the capital of Denmark, regularly ranks among the world's most liveable cities. It combines colourful harbourfront houses, royal palaces and cutting-edge architecture with a relaxed, human-scale atmosphere. The city is a global leader in cycling, sustainability and gastronomy, having been home to Noma and other highly acclaimed restaurants. Danes are known for "hygge", a sense of cosy wellbeing. Copenhagen is expensive, but its compact size makes it easy to enjoy on foot, by bike and even by swimming in the clean harbour.

### Top Attractions

- **Nyhavn:** A 17th-century canal lined with brightly painted townhouses, bars and old wooden ships.
- **Tivoli Gardens:** One of the world's oldest amusement parks, opened in 1843, with gardens, rides and concerts.
- **The Little Mermaid:** A small bronze statue inspired by Hans Christian Andersen's fairy tale, located on the waterfront.
- **Christiansborg Palace:** Home to the Danish parliament and royal reception rooms, with a free tower offering views.
- **Rosenborg Castle and Amalienborg Palace:** Rosenborg displays the Crown Jewels, while Amalienborg is the royal residence with a daily changing of the guard.
- **Freetown Christiania:** A self-governing alternative community with colourful houses and art; photography is restricted in some areas.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Indre By (the inner city) is central and close to most sights. Vesterbro, the former meatpacking district, is trendy with bars and restaurants, and Nørrebro is multicultural and creative. Christianshavn has canals and a laid-back vibe, while Østerbro is residential and upmarket.

### Food & Drink

Smørrebrød, open-faced rye sandwiches with toppings like herring, roast beef or shrimp, is the classic Danish lunch. Danish pastries (wienerbrød), cinnamon buns and hot dogs from street stands are popular. Copenhagen has many Michelin-starred restaurants, but street food markets like Reffen offer cheaper options. Craft beer and natural wine bars are everywhere.

### When to Visit

Summer brings long days, harbour swimming and outdoor festivals such as Copenhagen Jazz Festival in July. Spring and early autumn are cooler but pleasant. Winter is dark and cold, though Tivoli's Christmas season and cosy cafés create a hygge atmosphere.

### Getting Around

The metro runs 24 hours and connects the airport with the city centre in about 15 minutes. Cycling is the best way to get around, with dedicated lanes everywhere. Harbour buses (boats) are part of the public transport system.

### Day Trips

Kronborg Castle in Helsingør, the setting of Shakespeare's Hamlet, and the Louisiana Museum of Modern Art in Humlebæk are both easy train trips. Roskilde has a Viking Ship Museum and a cathedral with royal tombs. Malmö in Sweden is just 35 minutes away across the Øresund Bridge.

### Practical Tips

- The Copenhagen Card includes transport and entry to many attractions.
- Follow cycling etiquette and signal when turning or stopping.
- Tap water is excellent; carry a reusable bottle.
- Card payments are accepted almost everywhere; cash is rarely needed.

## Cuenca, Ecuador

*A colonial Andean city of blue domes, rivers and Panama hats.*

- **Country:** Ecuador
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~600,000
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Mariscal Lamar Airport (CUE)
- **Best time to visit:** June–September
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Cuenca, in Ecuador's southern highlands, is one of the most beautiful colonial cities in South America. Its historic centre, a UNESCO World Heritage Site, features cobblestone streets, colonial houses and the striking blue-domed New Cathedral. The city is built along four rivers. Cuenca is famous for the production of toquilla straw hats, known worldwide as Panama hats. It is popular with retirees from abroad because of its mild climate and low cost of living.

### Top Attractions

- **New Cathedral (Catedral de la Inmaculada Concepción):** A massive cathedral with blue domes.
- **Parque Calderón:** The main square.
- **Tomebamba River:** Riverside walks.
- **Pumapungo Museum:** Inca ruins and ethnographic exhibits.
- **Panama hat workshops:** Learn how toquilla straw hats are woven.
- **Mirador de Turi:** A viewpoint over the city.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The historic centre is the best base.

### Food & Drink

Local dishes include cuy (guinea pig), hornado (roast pork) and mote pillo (hominy with eggs). Markets like Mercado 10 de Agosto are good for local food.

### When to Visit

Cuenca has a spring-like climate year-round. June to September is drier.

### Getting Around

The centre is walkable. A tram crosses the city.

### Day Trips

Cajas National Park offers lakes and hiking. Ingapirca is Ecuador's largest Inca site.

### Practical Tips

- Adjust to the altitude (around 2,500 m).
- Ecuador uses the US dollar.
- Carry small bills.
- Bring layers.

## Cusco, Peru

*The ancient Inca capital and gateway to Machu Picchu.*

- **Country:** Peru
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~450,000
- **Language(s):** Spanish and Quechua
- **Currency:** Peruvian Sol (PEN)
- **Main airport:** Alejandro Velasco Astete International Airport (CUZ)
- **Best time to visit:** April–October (dry season)
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Cusco, high in the Andes of southern Peru, was the capital of the Inca Empire, the largest empire in pre-Columbian America. The Spanish built colonial churches and palaces on top of Inca foundations, creating a unique blend of cultures. The city is a UNESCO World Heritage Site and the main base for visiting Machu Picchu and the Sacred Valley. At around 3,400 metres above sea level, altitude is a factor for visitors. Quechua culture remains strong in the region.

### Top Attractions

- **Plaza de Armas:** The main square with the cathedral and the Church of La Compañía.
- **Qorikancha:** The Inca Temple of the Sun, with a Dominican convent built on top.
- **Sacsayhuamán:** A massive Inca fortress with huge stone walls above the city.
- **San Blas:** A bohemian neighbourhood of artisans.
- **San Pedro Market:** A market for food and crafts.
- **Machu Picchu:** The iconic Inca citadel, reached by train or the Inca Trail.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The historic centre and San Blas are convenient.

### Food & Drink

Andean cuisine includes alpaca, cuy (guinea pig), quinoa and lomo saltado. Coca tea helps with altitude.

### When to Visit

April to October is the dry season. Inti Raymi, the Inca Festival of the Sun, takes place on 24 June.

### Getting Around

The centre is walkable but hilly. Taxis are cheap. Trains to Machu Picchu depart from Poroy or Ollantaytambo.

### Day Trips

The Sacred Valley, Rainbow Mountain and Moray are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Rest on arrival to acclimatise to the altitude.
- Book Machu Picchu tickets and Inca Trail permits well in advance.
- Buy the Boleto Turístico for multiple sites.
- Bring layers.

## Da Nang, Vietnam

*A modern beach city between the mountains and the sea in central Vietnam.*

- **Country:** Vietnam
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.2 million
- **Language(s):** Vietnamese
- **Currency:** Vietnamese Dong (VND)
- **Main airport:** Da Nang International Airport (DAD)
- **Best time to visit:** February–August
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Da Nang is a fast-growing coastal city in central Vietnam, located between the ancient capital of Hue and the historic town of Hoi An. It has long sandy beaches, modern bridges and a relaxed atmosphere. The city is a gateway to UNESCO sites and the scenic Hai Van Pass. Da Nang is clean, well organised and popular with families and digital nomads. It combines urban life with beaches and mountains.

### Top Attractions

- **My Khe Beach:** A long sandy beach with resorts and seafood restaurants.
- **Marble Mountains:** Limestone hills with caves, temples and viewpoints.
- **Dragon Bridge:** A bridge shaped like a dragon that breathes fire and water on weekend nights.
- **Ba Na Hills and the Golden Bridge:** A mountain resort with a bridge held by giant stone hands.
- **Son Tra Peninsula:** A nature reserve with the Lady Buddha statue and monkeys.
- **Han Market:** A local market for food and souvenirs.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

My Khe Beach area is popular for hotels. The city centre along the Han River has restaurants and nightlife. Son Tra is quieter.

### Food & Drink

Mi Quang (turmeric noodles), banh xeo (crispy pancakes) and fresh seafood are local favourites. Bun cha ca (fish cake noodle soup) is a Da Nang speciality. Coffee culture is strong.

### When to Visit

February to August is dry and sunny. The rainy season (September–December) brings typhoons. The Da Nang International Fireworks Festival takes place in summer.

### Getting Around

Grab and taxis are affordable. Motorbike rental is common. The airport is close to the city centre.

### Day Trips

Hoi An is 30–45 minutes away. Hue, the former imperial capital, is reached via the Hai Van Pass. My Son Sanctuary has ancient Cham temples.

### Practical Tips

- Check typhoon forecasts in autumn.
- Visit Ba Na Hills early to avoid crowds.
- Carry cash for small vendors.
- Negotiate prices in markets.

## Delhi, India

*India's sprawling capital of Mughal monuments, bustling bazaars and political power.*

- **Country:** India
- **Region:** South Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~33 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Hindi and English
- **Currency:** Indian Rupee (INR)
- **Main airport:** Indira Gandhi International Airport (DEL)
- **Best time to visit:** October–March
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Delhi, India's capital territory, is one of the oldest continuously inhabited cities in the world. It has been the capital of numerous empires, including the Delhi Sultanate, the Mughals and the British Raj. Old Delhi is a maze of narrow lanes, markets and mosques, while New Delhi, designed by the British, features wide boulevards and government buildings. The city is chaotic and intense but rich in history and food. It is the starting point of the Golden Triangle with Agra and Jaipur.

### Top Attractions

- **Red Fort:** A Mughal fortress and UNESCO World Heritage Site built by Shah Jahan.
- **Qutub Minar:** A 73-metre minaret from the early 13th century.
- **Humayun's Tomb:** A garden tomb that inspired the Taj Mahal.
- **Jama Masjid:** One of India's largest mosques.
- **India Gate:** A war memorial on the ceremonial Kartavya Path.
- **Lotus Temple and Akshardham:** A lotus-shaped Bahá'í house of worship and a vast Hindu temple complex.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Connaught Place is central. Paharganj is a budget backpacker area near the station, and South Delhi neighbourhoods like Hauz Khas and Defence Colony are upscale. Old Delhi is historic.

### Food & Drink

Delhi is famous for street food like chaat, parathas in Paranthe Wali Gali, butter chicken and kebabs. Karim's near Jama Masjid serves Mughlai food. Chole bhature is a popular breakfast. Try lassi and masala chai.

### When to Visit

October to March is the most pleasant. Summers are extremely hot, and the monsoon brings humidity. Winter smog can be severe, especially in November.

### Getting Around

The Delhi Metro is efficient and connects the airport. Auto-rickshaws and ride-hailing apps are widely used. Traffic is heavy.

### Day Trips

Agra and the Taj Mahal are a popular day trip by train. Jaipur is about 4–5 hours away. Rishikesh and Haridwar are further north.

### Practical Tips

- Check air quality in winter.
- Dress modestly at religious sites.
- Beware of touts at railway stations.
- Use prepaid taxis or apps from the airport.

## Denver, United States (Colorado)

*The "Mile High City", a gateway to the Rocky Mountains with craft beer and sunshine.*

- **Country:** United States (Colorado)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~720,000 (city); ~3 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Denver International Airport (DEN)
- **Best time to visit:** June–September; December–March for skiing
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Denver, the capital of Colorado, sits exactly one mile (1,609 metres) above sea level at the eastern edge of the Rocky Mountains. Founded during the 1858 gold rush, it grew into the largest city in the Mountain West. Denver enjoys around 300 days of sunshine a year and a strong outdoor culture, with skiing, hiking and cycling close by. The city has a thriving craft beer scene, art districts and professional sports teams. It is the main gateway to Colorado's national parks and ski resorts.

### Top Attractions

- **Red Rocks Park and Amphitheatre:** A natural open-air amphitheatre between giant red sandstone formations, famous for concerts.
- **Denver Art Museum:** A striking building with Native American and Western art collections.
- **Union Station:** A restored historic railway station with restaurants and bars.
- **Larimer Square and LoDo:** Historic Victorian buildings, restaurants and nightlife.
- **Colorado State Capitol:** Its steps mark the elevation of one mile above sea level.
- **RiNo Art District:** Street art, galleries and breweries.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

LoDo (Lower Downtown) is lively and central. RiNo is trendy, while Capitol Hill is eclectic, and Cherry Creek is upscale.

### Food & Drink

Denver's food scene includes Southwestern and Mexican influences, green chile, bison burgers and Rocky Mountain oysters. Craft breweries are everywhere.

### When to Visit

Summer is ideal for hiking and concerts; winter for skiing. Weather can change quickly.

### Getting Around

The A Line train connects the airport with Union Station. Light rail, buses and bike-share are available.

### Day Trips

Rocky Mountain National Park, Boulder, Colorado Springs (Garden of the Gods) and ski resorts are nearby.

### Practical Tips

- Drink water and take it easy on alcohol at altitude.
- Use sunscreen; UV is strong.
- Check road conditions in winter.
- Book Red Rocks concert tickets early.

## Doha, Qatar

*A futuristic Gulf capital of striking museums, souks and desert adventures.*

- **Country:** Qatar
- **Region:** Middle East
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.2 million (city); ~2.7 million (Qatar)
- **Language(s):** Arabic (English widely used)
- **Currency:** Qatari Riyal (QAR)
- **Main airport:** Hamad International Airport (DOH)
- **Best time to visit:** November–March
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Doha, the capital of Qatar, sits on the eastern coast of the Qatar peninsula on the Persian Gulf. In just a few decades, natural gas wealth has transformed it from a pearl-fishing town into a modern city of skyscrapers and cultural landmarks. Doha hosted the FIFA World Cup in 2022, which brought major investment in transport and infrastructure. The city is known for its world-class museums, restored traditional souks and luxury hotels. Hamad International Airport, one of the world's best, makes Doha a popular stopover destination.

### Top Attractions

- **Museum of Islamic Art:** A landmark building by I. M. Pei housing one of the world's finest collections of Islamic art, with a waterfront park.
- **National Museum of Qatar:** Designed by Jean Nouvel to resemble a desert rose crystal, it tells the story of Qatar's people and land.
- **Souq Waqif:** A restored traditional market with spices, textiles, falcons, restaurants and shisha cafés.
- **Corniche:** A seven-kilometre waterfront promenade with views of the West Bay skyline.
- **Katara Cultural Village:** A cultural district with an amphitheatre, galleries, mosques and a beach.
- **The Pearl-Qatar:** An artificial island with marinas, luxury apartments, shops and restaurants.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

West Bay is the business district with high-rise hotels and beaches. The area around Souq Waqif and Msheireb Downtown is historic yet modern and walkable. The Pearl and Lusail offer upscale waterfront living and newer attractions.

### Food & Drink

Qatari dishes include machboos (spiced rice with meat), harees and balaleet (sweet vermicelli with eggs). Karak tea, a sweet spiced milk tea, is a local favourite sold everywhere. Souq Waqif offers Middle Eastern, Persian and Indian restaurants. Alcohol is only served in licensed hotel venues.

### When to Visit

November to March offers pleasant temperatures for outdoor sightseeing. Summers are extremely hot and humid, often above 40°C. Ramadan changes opening hours, but evenings are festive.

### Getting Around

The Doha Metro is modern and connects the airport, West Bay, Souq Waqif and Lusail. Taxis and ride-hailing apps (Uber, Careem) are widely used. Walking is pleasant only in the cooler months.

### Day Trips

Desert safaris to Khor Al Adaid (the Inland Sea), where dunes meet the sea, are popular. The Zubarah Fort, a UNESCO site, is in the north. Mangroves at Al Thakira can be explored by kayak.

### Practical Tips

- Dress modestly in public places, covering shoulders and knees.
- Many nationalities receive visa-free entry; check requirements before travelling.
- Friday is the weekly holy day, with limited morning opening hours.
- Use sun protection and carry water.

## Dubai, United Arab Emirates

*A glittering city of record-breaking skyscrapers, luxury shopping and desert adventures.*

- **Country:** United Arab Emirates
- **Region:** Middle East
- **Population (approx.):** ~3.8 million
- **Language(s):** Arabic (English widely used)
- **Currency:** UAE Dirham (AED)
- **Main airport:** Dubai International Airport (DXB); Al Maktoum International (DWC)
- **Best time to visit:** November–March
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Dubai is the most populous city in the United Arab Emirates and one of the world's fastest-growing destinations. Once a small fishing and pearling village, it has transformed through trade, tourism, aviation and real estate into a global hub. Dubai is famous for its superlatives, including the world's tallest building, huge shopping malls and artificial islands. Beyond the glamour, it also has traditional souks, historic districts and desert landscapes. The city is multicultural, with most residents coming from abroad.

### Top Attractions

- **Burj Khalifa:** The world's tallest building, with observation decks on levels 124, 125 and 148.
- **Dubai Mall and Dubai Fountain:** A giant mall with an aquarium and an ice rink, and a choreographed fountain show every evening.
- **Palm Jumeirah and Atlantis:** A palm-shaped artificial island with luxury resorts and a water park.
- **Dubai Creek, Al Fahidi and the souks:** The historic district with wind-tower houses, abra boat rides and Gold and Spice Souks.
- **Museum of the Future:** A striking torus-shaped building dedicated to future technologies.
- **Desert safari:** Dune bashing, camel rides and dinner at a desert camp.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Downtown Dubai is central, near Burj Khalifa. Dubai Marina and JBR offer beaches and waterfront dining, while Deira and Bur Dubai are traditional and budget-friendly. Palm Jumeirah hosts luxury resorts.

### Food & Drink

Dubai offers cuisine from around the world, from street shawarma to celebrity-chef restaurants. Emirati dishes such as machboos and luqaimat are served in heritage restaurants. Friday brunches are a local institution. Indian and Pakistani restaurants in Deira and Karama offer excellent value.

### When to Visit

November to March has warm, sunny weather. Summers are extremely hot and humid. The Dubai Shopping Festival (December–January) offers discounts and events.

### Getting Around

The Dubai Metro connects the airport, Downtown and Marina, and trams serve the Marina area. Taxis and ride-hailing apps are widely used. Abras (water taxis) cross Dubai Creek for a tiny fare.

### Day Trips

Abu Dhabi, with the Sheikh Zayed Grand Mosque and Louvre Abu Dhabi, is about 1.5 hours away. Hatta offers mountain scenery and kayaking. Sharjah has museums and a heritage area.

### Practical Tips

- Dress modestly outside beaches and pools.
- Public drinking is not allowed; alcohol is served in licensed venues.
- Book Burj Khalifa tickets in advance for sunset slots.
- During Ramadan, avoid eating in public during daylight.

## Dublin, Ireland

*A friendly literary capital of Georgian squares, pubs and live music.*

- **Country:** Ireland
- **Region:** Northern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~590,000 (city); ~1.5 million (Greater Dublin)
- **Language(s):** English and Irish
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Dublin Airport (DUB)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Dublin is the capital of Ireland, located on the east coast at the mouth of the River Liffey. Founded by Vikings, the city became a centre of British rule and later the birthplace of the Irish state. Dublin has an outstanding literary heritage, producing writers such as James Joyce, Oscar Wilde, W. B. Yeats and Samuel Beckett. Today it is a youthful, international city and a European hub for tech companies. Its pubs, music and warm hospitality make it a popular city break.

### Top Attractions

- **Trinity College and the Book of Kells:** Ireland's oldest university displays the 9th-century illuminated manuscript and the stunning Long Room library.
- **Guinness Storehouse:** A seven-floor visitor experience about Ireland's famous stout, ending with a pint in the rooftop Gravity Bar.
- **Temple Bar:** A lively cobbled area full of pubs, live music and street performers; prices are higher here.
- **Dublin Castle and Chester Beatty Library:** The seat of British rule for centuries, and a free museum with remarkable manuscripts and art from around the world.
- **Kilmainham Gaol:** A former prison central to Irish revolutionary history, visited by guided tour.
- **St. Patrick's Cathedral and Christ Church Cathedral:** Two medieval cathedrals; Jonathan Swift was dean of St. Patrick's.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The area around St. Stephen's Green and Grafton Street is central and elegant. Temple Bar is convenient but noisy, while Smithfield and Stoneybatter are more local and trendy. Ballsbridge is upscale and quiet, south of the centre.

### Food & Drink

Traditional Irish food includes Irish stew, boxty (potato pancakes), soda bread and a full Irish breakfast. Seafood is excellent, including oysters and Dublin Bay prawns. Pubs serve Guinness, Irish whiskey and craft beers. Dublin has a growing modern food scene with many international restaurants.

### When to Visit

Late spring and summer bring the mildest weather and longest days, though rain is possible year-round. St. Patrick's Day on 17 March is celebrated with a festival and parade, drawing large crowds. Bloomsday on 16 June celebrates James Joyce's Ulysses.

### Getting Around

Central Dublin is very walkable. The Luas tram, buses and DART coastal trains cover the wider area. There is no metro to the airport; express buses and taxis take 30–45 minutes.

### Day Trips

The coastal villages of Howth and Dalkey offer cliff walks and seafood. Glendalough, a monastic site in the Wicklow Mountains, is very scenic. The prehistoric passage tombs of Newgrange in the Boyne Valley are older than the pyramids of Giza.

### Practical Tips

- Book Trinity College and the Guinness Storehouse online in advance.
- Accommodation is expensive; book early.
- Pack a waterproof jacket in every season.
- Take a traditional music session in a pub outside Temple Bar for a more authentic experience.

## Dubrovnik, Croatia

*The walled "Pearl of the Adriatic", a limestone old town above turquoise sea.*

- **Country:** Croatia
- **Region:** Southeastern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~41,000
- **Language(s):** Croatian
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Dubrovnik Airport (DBV)
- **Best time to visit:** May–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Dubrovnik is a walled city on the southern Dalmatian coast of Croatia and one of the Mediterranean's most spectacular destinations. For centuries it was the capital of the independent Republic of Ragusa, a maritime power that rivalled Venice. Its massive stone walls, marble streets and red-tiled roofs form a UNESCO World Heritage Site. The city gained new fame as a filming location for Game of Thrones, representing King's Landing. Because of cruise ships and summer crowds, careful timing makes a big difference to the experience.

### Top Attractions

- **City Walls:** A nearly 2-kilometre walk along the medieval walls offers stunning views of the old town and the sea; go early or late to avoid heat.
- **Stradun (Placa):** The polished limestone main street running through the old town between the Pile and Ploče gates.
- **Rector's Palace:** The Gothic-Renaissance seat of the Ragusan government, now a museum.
- **Mount Srđ Cable Car:** A cable car to the summit above the city, with sunset views and a museum about the 1990s siege.
- **Lokrum Island:** A short ferry ride away, with botanical gardens, peacocks, rocky swimming spots and a monastery.
- **Fort Lovrijenac:** A fortress on a cliff outside the walls, used for summer theatre performances.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Staying inside the old town is atmospheric but expensive and involves many steps. Ploče offers views of the old town and access to Banje Beach, while Lapad and Babin Kuk have beaches, promenades and resort hotels. Gruž, near the ferry port, is practical for island hopping.

### Food & Drink

Dalmatian cuisine focuses on seafood, olive oil and fresh vegetables: try black risotto made with squid ink, grilled fish and oysters from nearby Ston. Peka, meat or octopus slow-cooked under an iron bell, often needs to be ordered in advance. Local wines include Plavac Mali reds from the Pelješac peninsula. Rozata, a custard pudding similar to crème caramel, is a local dessert.

### When to Visit

Late spring and early autumn offer warm sea and fewer crowds. July and August are hot, crowded and expensive, though the Dubrovnik Summer Festival brings theatre and music to historic venues. Winters are mild and quiet, with many businesses closed.

### Getting Around

The old town is pedestrian-only. Local buses connect the old town with Lapad, Gruž and the airport. Parking is scarce and expensive, so avoid driving into the centre.

### Day Trips

Boat trips visit the Elaphiti Islands, and ferries reach Mljet National Park and Korčula. The fortified town of Ston has the longest walls in Europe and famous oysters. Kotor in Montenegro and Mostar in Bosnia and Herzegovina are popular cross-border day trips.

### Practical Tips

- Check the cruise ship schedule and avoid the old town when several ships are in port.
- Buy the Dubrovnik Pass, which includes the city walls and several museums.
- Wear water shoes on rocky beaches.
- Carry water and sun protection on the walls; there is little shade.

## Edinburgh, United Kingdom (Scotland)

*Scotland's dramatic capital of castles, closes and the world's largest arts festival.*

- **Country:** United Kingdom (Scotland)
- **Region:** Northern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~530,000
- **Language(s):** English (Scots and Scottish Gaelic also used)
- **Currency:** Pound Sterling (GBP)
- **Main airport:** Edinburgh Airport (EDI)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September; August for festivals
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Edinburgh, the capital of Scotland, is built on ancient volcanic hills and dominated by a castle on a rocky crag. The medieval Old Town and the elegant Georgian New Town together form a UNESCO World Heritage Site. The city was a centre of the Scottish Enlightenment and is associated with writers such as Sir Walter Scott, Robert Louis Stevenson and J. K. Rowling. Every August it hosts the Edinburgh Festival Fringe, the world's largest arts festival. With its narrow alleys, cosy pubs and dramatic views, Edinburgh is one of Europe's most atmospheric cities.

### Top Attractions

- **Edinburgh Castle:** A fortress with the Scottish Crown Jewels, the Stone of Destiny and the One O'Clock Gun.
- **Royal Mile:** The historic street connecting the castle to the Palace of Holyroodhouse, full of closes, shops and pubs.
- **Arthur's Seat:** An extinct volcano in Holyrood Park offering a short but steep hike with panoramic views.
- **Palace of Holyroodhouse:** The official Scottish residence of the British monarch, associated with Mary, Queen of Scots.
- **National Museum of Scotland:** A free museum covering Scottish history, natural history and world cultures, with a rooftop terrace.
- **Calton Hill:** A hill with monuments and one of the best views of the city, especially at sunset.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Old Town is atmospheric and central, while the New Town offers elegant streets, shops and restaurants. Stockbridge has a village feel, with a Sunday market, and Leith, the port area, is known for seafood restaurants and the Royal Yacht Britannia. Bruntsfield and Marchmont are residential and pleasant.

### Food & Drink

Scotland's national dish is haggis, served with neeps and tatties (turnips and potatoes). Scottish salmon, Cullen skink (smoked haddock soup) and a full Scottish breakfast are popular. Whisky bars offer hundreds of single malts, and the Scotch Whisky Experience introduces the basics. Leith has several highly rated seafood restaurants.

### When to Visit

Summer brings the longest days and the Edinburgh International Festival, Fringe and Royal Military Tattoo in August, when prices peak. Spring and autumn are quieter. Hogmanay, the New Year celebration, is one of the world's biggest street parties.

### Getting Around

The city centre is walkable but hilly. Trams connect the airport with the city centre and Leith, and Lothian buses cover the rest of the city. Trains reach Glasgow in under an hour and London in about 4.5 hours.

### Day Trips

Stirling Castle, Loch Lomond and the Scottish Highlands are popular day trips, with organised tours to Loch Ness and Glencoe. The Forth Bridges at South Queensferry and the seaside town of North Berwick are close by. St Andrews, the home of golf, is about 1.5 hours away.

### Practical Tips

- Book accommodation many months ahead for August.
- Weather changes quickly; dress in layers.
- Many museums, including the National Museum and Scottish National Gallery, are free.
- Take a ghost or underground vaults tour for Edinburgh's dark history.

## Florence, Italy

*The cradle of the Renaissance, filled with masterpieces of art and architecture.*

- **Country:** Italy
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~360,000 (city); ~1 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Italian
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Florence Airport, Peretola (FLR); Pisa International Airport (PSA)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Florence (Firenze), the capital of Tuscany, was the birthplace of the Renaissance and home to the powerful Medici family. The city nurtured geniuses such as Leonardo da Vinci, Michelangelo, Botticelli, Brunelleschi and Dante. Its historic centre is a UNESCO World Heritage Site packed with churches, palaces and museums holding some of the world's greatest art. Florence is compact and walkable, but also very crowded in peak season. Beyond art, it is famous for leather, gold jewellery, Tuscan cuisine and wine.

### Top Attractions

- **Florence Cathedral (Duomo):** The Cathedral of Santa Maria del Fiore with Brunelleschi's dome, Giotto's Bell Tower and the Baptistery; climbing the dome requires a timed ticket.
- **Uffizi Gallery:** One of the world's greatest art museums, with Botticelli's Birth of Venus and works by Leonardo, Raphael and Caravaggio.
- **Galleria dell'Accademia:** Home to Michelangelo's original David.
- **Ponte Vecchio:** A medieval bridge lined with jewellery shops over the Arno River.
- **Palazzo Pitti and Boboli Gardens:** A vast Medici palace with several museums and formal Renaissance gardens.
- **Piazzale Michelangelo:** A hilltop square with the classic panoramic view of Florence, best at sunset.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Duomo and Santa Croce areas are central and full of sights. Oltrarno, across the river, is more authentic, with artisan workshops, piazzas and restaurants, especially around Santo Spirito. San Lorenzo, near the central market and station, is practical and good value.

### Food & Drink

Tuscan food is simple and ingredient-focused: bistecca alla fiorentina (a huge T-bone steak served rare), ribollita (bread and vegetable soup), pappa al pomodoro and lampredotto (tripe sandwiches) from street stalls. The Mercato Centrale has a lively food hall upstairs. Gelato was reportedly developed in Florence, and Chianti wine comes from the hills just south of the city.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are the best seasons, although crowds are present most of the year. Summer is hot and packed, and many locals leave in August. Winter is quieter and cheaper, making it ideal for museum visits.

### Getting Around

The historic centre is best explored on foot, and much of it is closed to private cars (ZTL). A tram connects the airport and the main station, Santa Maria Novella. High-speed trains link Florence with Rome, Milan, Bologna and Venice.

### Day Trips

Pisa and its Leaning Tower, Siena with its shell-shaped Piazza del Campo, and the medieval towers of San Gimignano are classic day trips. The Chianti wine region offers vineyard tours and tastings. Lucca, with intact Renaissance walls you can cycle on, is about an hour away.

### Practical Tips

- Book the Uffizi, Accademia and Duomo climbs well in advance.
- Dress modestly in churches, covering shoulders and knees.
- Avoid driving into the ZTL zones, which result in automatic fines.
- Eat away from the main squares for better prices and quality.

## Florianópolis, Brazil

*"Magic Island", a relaxed city of 40+ beaches, lagoons and surf.*

- **Country:** Brazil
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~540,000
- **Language(s):** Portuguese
- **Currency:** Brazilian Real (BRL)
- **Main airport:** Hercílio Luz International Airport (FLN)
- **Best time to visit:** December–March for beaches; April–May for fewer crowds
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Florianópolis, the capital of the southern Brazilian state of Santa Catarina, is mostly located on Santa Catarina Island. Nicknamed "Floripa" or "Ilha da Magia", it is known for its beaches, lagoons and relaxed lifestyle. The island blends Azorean Portuguese heritage with a modern surf and tech culture. Florianópolis has a high quality of life and is one of Brazil's safest capitals. It is a popular summer destination for Brazilians and Argentinians.

### Top Attractions

- **Lagoa da Conceição:** A lagoon with restaurants, bars and water sports.
- **Joaquina Beach:** Famous for surfing and sand dunes for sandboarding.
- **Praia Mole:** A popular surf beach.
- **Santo Antônio de Lisboa:** A historic Azorean village known for oysters.
- **Hercílio Luz Bridge:** The city's iconic suspension bridge.
- **Campeche Island:** An island with clear water and rock carvings.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Lagoa da Conceição is lively. Jurerê Internacional is upscale, and Campeche is relaxed.

### Food & Drink

Oysters are the local speciality. Seafood, sequência de camarão (shrimp tasting menu) and Brazilian barbecue are popular.

### When to Visit

Summer is the high season. Winters are cool.

### Getting Around

Buses and ride-hailing apps are common. Renting a car helps explore beaches.

### Day Trips

Guarda do Embaú, Garopaba and Praia do Rosa are nearby beaches, with whale-watching season from July to November.

### Practical Tips
