- Book accommodation early for Carnival and New Year.
- Traffic is heavy in summer.
- Use sunscreen.
- Try fresh oysters.

## Gdańsk, Poland

*A Hanseatic port city of colourful gabled houses where the Second World War began.*

- **Country:** Poland
- **Region:** Central Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~470,000 (city); ~1 million (Tricity metro)
- **Language(s):** Polish
- **Currency:** Polish Złoty (PLN)
- **Main airport:** Gdańsk Lech Wałęsa Airport (GDN)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Gdańsk is a historic port city on the Baltic coast of Poland and the largest city of the Pomerania region. As a wealthy member of the Hanseatic League, it developed a rich merchant culture reflected in its ornate facades. The Second World War began here in 1939 at Westerplatte, and the Solidarity movement, which helped bring down communism, was born in the Gdańsk Shipyard in 1980. The city was heavily destroyed in 1945 and carefully rebuilt. Together with Sopot and Gdynia, it forms the Tricity, combining history, beaches and nightlife.

### Top Attractions

- **Long Market and Long Lane (Długi Targ and Ulica Długa):** The Royal Route lined with colourful townhouses, the Neptune Fountain and Artus Court.
- **St. Mary's Basilica:** One of the largest brick churches in the world, with a tower offering sweeping views.
- **European Solidarity Centre:** A modern museum telling the story of the Solidarity movement and the fall of communism.
- **Museum of the Second World War:** A large, powerful museum covering the war from a global and Polish perspective.
- **Motława riverfront and the Crane (Żuraw):** A medieval port crane and a lively riverside promenade.
- **Westerplatte:** The site where the first shots of the Second World War were fired, with a monument and ruins.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Main Town (Główne Miasto) is the most convenient area, close to all main sights. Granary Island (Wyspa Spichrzów) has new hotels and restaurants on the riverside. Oliwa, further north, is green and quiet, with a beautiful cathedral and park.

### Food & Drink

Polish classics such as pierogi, żurek (sour rye soup) and bigos are widely available. Being on the Baltic, Gdańsk offers smoked and fried fish, especially herring and cod. Goldwasser, a herbal liqueur with gold flakes, originated here. Local craft beer is popular in pubs along the Motława.

### When to Visit

Summer is the best time for beaches and outdoor events such as St. Dominic's Fair, one of Europe's largest open-air trade and culture fairs, held from late July to mid-August. Spring and early autumn are pleasant but cooler. Winters are cold and windy, though the Christmas market is charming.

### Getting Around

Gdańsk has trams and buses, and the SKM commuter train links Gdańsk, Sopot and Gdynia. The main sights are walkable. The airport is connected by train (PKM) and bus.

### Day Trips

Sopot, with its long wooden pier and beaches, is just 15–20 minutes away. Malbork Castle, the world's largest brick castle and former seat of the Teutonic Knights, is about 40 minutes by train. The Hel Peninsula and Słowiński National Park, with shifting sand dunes, are further options.

### Practical Tips

- Amber is sold everywhere; buy from reputable shops with certificates.
- Visit museums early, as the Second World War Museum can take several hours.
- The Baltic Sea is cool even in summer.
- Card payments are accepted almost everywhere.

## Geneva, Switzerland

*An elegant international city on a lake, home to diplomacy, watchmaking and Alpine views.*

- **Country:** Switzerland
- **Region:** Western Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~205,000 (city); ~1 million (cross-border metro)
- **Language(s):** French
- **Currency:** Swiss Franc (CHF)
- **Main airport:** Geneva Airport (GVA)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Geneva sits at the southwestern tip of Lake Geneva (Lac Léman), surrounded by the Alps and the Jura mountains. It is one of the world's centres of international diplomacy, hosting the European headquarters of the United Nations, the Red Cross and dozens of international organisations. The city was a centre of the Protestant Reformation under John Calvin and is known for its precision watchmaking and private banking. Geneva is compact, clean and very expensive. Its lakefront, old town and nearby mountains make it a pleasant stop in any season.

### Top Attractions

- **Jet d'Eau:** A giant fountain shooting water about 140 metres into the air, the symbol of Geneva.
- **Old Town and St. Pierre Cathedral:** Cobbled streets, antique shops and a cathedral whose towers offer views of the lake and Alps.
- **Palais des Nations:** The UN's European headquarters, open for guided tours with identification.
- **International Red Cross and Red Crescent Museum:** An engaging museum about humanitarian action.
- **Reformation Wall:** A monumental wall in Parc des Bastions depicting leaders of the Protestant Reformation.
- **CERN:** The European particle physics laboratory near the city offers free exhibitions and some guided tours.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Pâquis area near the station is multicultural and has more affordable hotels and restaurants. The Old Town is historic and charming, while Carouge, a separate town with Italianate architecture, has artisan shops and bistros. The lakeside Quai du Mont-Blanc has luxury hotels.

### Food & Drink

Swiss specialities such as cheese fondue and raclette are widely available, along with perch fillets from the lake. Geneva's own dishes include longeole (a spiced pork sausage) and cardon gratin. Swiss chocolate shops are everywhere, and the vineyards around Geneva produce good white wines. Eating out is expensive, so lunch menus offer better value.

### When to Visit

Summer is ideal for lake swimming and outdoor cafés, with the Fêtes de Genève festival in some years. Spring and autumn are pleasant. Winter is cold but provides quick access to Alpine ski resorts, and the Escalade festival in December commemorates a 1602 victory.

### Getting Around

Public transport is excellent, and hotel guests receive a free Geneva Transport Card. The airport is only about 7 minutes by train from the city centre. Yellow water taxis (Mouettes) cross the lake.

### Day Trips

Chamonix and Mont Blanc in France are about an hour away. Lausanne, Montreux and the Château de Chillon lie along the lake, reachable by train or boat. Annecy, a French lakeside town with canals, is a popular trip.

### Practical Tips

- Ask your hotel for the free public transport card.
- Budget carefully; Switzerland is among the most expensive countries in Europe.
- Swim in the lake at Bains des Pâquis, a local institution.
- Carry your passport for trips to nearby France.

## George Town (Penang), Malaysia

*A multicultural heritage city famous for street art and some of Asia's best street food.*

- **Country:** Malaysia
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~800,000 (city area); ~1.8 million (Penang state)
- **Language(s):** Malay, Hokkien, English, Tamil
- **Currency:** Malaysian Ringgit (MYR)
- **Main airport:** Penang International Airport (PEN)
- **Best time to visit:** December–March
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

George Town, the capital of the Malaysian state of Penang, was founded by the British East India Company in 1786 as a trading port. Its historic centre, a UNESCO World Heritage Site, reflects centuries of Malay, Chinese, Indian and European influences. Shophouses, clan houses, mosques, temples and churches stand side by side. George Town is renowned for its street food, considered among the best in Asia. Its street art, including murals by Ernest Zacharevic, attracts visitors from around the world.

### Top Attractions

- **Street art:** Murals and wrought-iron caricatures throughout the historic centre.
- **Khoo Kongsi:** An ornate Chinese clan house with intricate carvings.
- **Cheong Fatt Tze Mansion (The Blue Mansion):** A restored 19th-century mansion.
- **Penang Hill:** A hill station reached by funicular, with cool air and views.
- **Kek Lok Si Temple:** One of the largest Buddhist temples in Southeast Asia.
- **Clan Jetties:** Waterfront villages on stilts built by Chinese clans.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The heritage zone is the best place to stay, with boutique hotels in shophouses. Gurney Drive offers modern hotels and food courts, while Batu Ferringhi is the beach area.

### Food & Drink

Penang is famous for char kway teow (fried flat noodles), assam laksa (sour fish noodle soup), nasi kandar, cendol and Hokkien mee. Hawker centres like Gurney Drive and New Lane are great for sampling dishes. Nyonya (Peranakan) cuisine blends Chinese and Malay flavours.

### When to Visit

December to March is the driest period. Chinese New Year and Thaipusam are colourful festivals. Rain is possible year-round.

### Getting Around

The heritage centre is walkable, and bicycles or trishaws are fun. Grab is widely used. Free CAT buses serve the central area.

### Day Trips

Penang National Park offers jungle trails and beaches. Langkawi is reachable by ferry or plane. The Cameron Highlands and Ipoh are on the mainland.

### Practical Tips

- Hawker stalls often have specific opening hours; check before going.
- Dress modestly in mosques and temples.
- Bring an umbrella for sudden rain.
- Carry cash for hawker stalls.

## Granada, Spain

*The last Moorish kingdom of Spain, crowned by the magnificent Alhambra.*

- **Country:** Spain
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~230,000
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Federico García Lorca Granada Airport (GRX); Málaga Airport (AGP) is 1.5 hours away
- **Best time to visit:** March–June and September–November
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Granada lies at the foot of the Sierra Nevada mountains in Andalusia and was the last stronghold of Muslim rule in Spain until 1492. Its greatest treasure is the Alhambra, a breathtaking palace and fortress complex that is one of the most visited monuments in Europe. The city combines Moorish heritage, Christian Renaissance architecture and a lively student population. Granada is also known for its tradition of free tapas served with drinks. The views of the Alhambra against the snow-capped Sierra Nevada are unforgettable.

### Top Attractions

- **Alhambra:** A UNESCO-listed complex including the Nasrid Palaces with intricate stucco work, the Alcazaba fortress and the Generalife gardens.
- **Albaicín:** The old Moorish quarter of whitewashed houses and narrow lanes on a hill facing the Alhambra.
- **Mirador de San Nicolás:** The most famous viewpoint for the Alhambra, especially at sunset.
- **Granada Cathedral and Royal Chapel:** The Royal Chapel holds the tombs of Ferdinand and Isabella, the Catholic Monarchs.
- **Sacromonte:** A neighbourhood of cave houses associated with the Roma community and flamenco performances.
- **Carrera del Darro:** A picturesque street along the Darro River beneath the Alhambra.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Centro around the cathedral is convenient for sightseeing and tapas bars. The Albaicín offers charming guesthouses with views but involves steep climbs. Realejo, the former Jewish quarter, has street art and a local feel.

### Food & Drink

In many Granada bars, ordering a drink comes with a free tapa, making tapas-hopping a local tradition. Try habas con jamón (broad beans with ham), tortilla del Sacromonte and piononos, small pastries from nearby Santa Fe. Moroccan-style tea houses (teterías) line Calle Calderería. Plaza Bib-Rambla is known for churros with chocolate.

### When to Visit

Spring is the best season, with blooming gardens and mild temperatures. Summers are very hot, while winters are cold but sunny, and skiing is possible in the Sierra Nevada. Holy Week (Semana Santa) and the Corpus Christi fair are major festivals.

### Getting Around

The centre is walkable, though the Albaicín and Alhambra are uphill; small minibuses serve these areas. The metro-tram links the city with suburbs. Buses and trains connect Granada with Seville, Córdoba and Málaga.

### Day Trips

The Sierra Nevada offers hiking in summer and skiing in winter. The Alpujarras, a region of white villages on the southern slopes of the mountains, is very scenic. The Costa Tropical beaches are about an hour south.

### Practical Tips

- Alhambra tickets sell out weeks or months ahead; book only through the official website.
- Your Nasrid Palaces ticket has a fixed entry time that is strictly enforced.
- Bring your passport or ID to the Alhambra, as names are checked.
- Order drinks at the bar to receive free tapas.

## Guadalajara, Mexico

*The birthplace of mariachi and tequila, with colonial plazas and a creative spirit.*

- **Country:** Mexico
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.4 million (city); ~5.3 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Mexican Peso (MXN)
- **Main airport:** Miguel Hidalgo y Costilla Guadalajara International Airport (GDL)
- **Best time to visit:** October–May
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Guadalajara, the capital of Jalisco, is Mexico's second-largest city. It is considered the heart of Mexican traditions such as mariachi music, tequila and charrería (Mexican rodeo). The historic centre features grand plazas, colonial buildings and murals by José Clemente Orozco. Guadalajara is also a growing technology hub, sometimes called Mexico's "Silicon Valley". It will host matches of the 2026 FIFA World Cup.

### Top Attractions

- **Hospicio Cabañas:** A UNESCO-listed building with Orozco's murals, including "Man of Fire".
- **Guadalajara Cathedral:** A cathedral with twin spires.
- **Tlaquepaque:** A colourful neighbourhood with crafts and mariachi.
- **Mercado San Juan de Dios:** One of Latin America's largest indoor markets.
- **Plaza de los Mariachis:** Live mariachi performances.
- **Chapultepec Avenue:** Nightlife and restaurants.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Centro Histórico, Colonia Americana and Chapultepec are popular.

### Food & Drink

Birria (spiced meat stew), torta ahogada (a sandwich drowned in sauce) and tejuino (fermented corn drink) are local specialities.

### When to Visit

October to May is dry and pleasant. The International Mariachi Festival takes place in late August/September.

### Getting Around

Light rail, buses and ride-hailing apps are available.

### Day Trips

Tequila, the town, offers distillery tours. Lake Chapala is nearby.

### Practical Tips

- Take the Jose Cuervo Express train to Tequila.
- Use ride-hailing apps.
- Try birria for breakfast.
- Check travel advisories.

## Guilin, China

*A dreamlike landscape of karst peaks, winding rivers and rice terraces.*

- **Country:** China
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~5 million (prefecture); ~2 million (urban area)
- **Language(s):** Mandarin Chinese (Zhuang and local dialects also spoken)
- **Currency:** Chinese Yuan Renminbi (CNY)
- **Main airport:** Guilin Liangjiang International Airport (KWL)
- **Best time to visit:** April–May and September–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Guilin, in the Guangxi Zhuang Autonomous Region of southern China, is famous for its dramatic karst scenery of limestone peaks rising abruptly from rivers and rice fields. The landscape has inspired Chinese painters and poets for over a thousand years, and a view of the Li River appears on the back of the 20-yuan banknote. The city itself is pleasant and green, built around rivers and lakes, but most visitors come for the countryside. Guilin is the gateway to the Li River cruise, the backpacker town of Yangshuo and the Longji rice terraces. The region is home to ethnic minorities such as the Zhuang, Yao and Dong, whose villages and traditions add cultural depth to a visit.

### Top Attractions

- **Li River cruise:** A four- to five-hour cruise from Guilin to Yangshuo through some of China's most famous scenery, passing peaks with evocative names such as Nine Horses Fresco Hill.
- **Reed Flute Cave:** A limestone cave filled with stalactites and stalagmites, illuminated by colourful lights, with visitor inscriptions dating back more than 1,200 years to the Tang dynasty.
- **Elephant Trunk Hill:** The symbol of Guilin, a rock formation that resembles an elephant drinking from the Li River.
- **Longji (Dragon's Backbone) Rice Terraces:** Spectacular terraced fields carved into the mountainsides by Zhuang and Yao farmers, with villages such as Ping'an and Dazhai.
- **Two Rivers and Four Lakes:** A scenic loop in the city centre with the Sun and Moon Pagodas, beautifully lit at night and explored by boat.
- **Yangshuo:** A lively town surrounded by karst peaks, ideal for cycling, rock climbing, bamboo rafting and the Impression Liu Sanjie outdoor light show.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Central Guilin near Zhengyang pedestrian street and the Shan and Rong lakes is convenient for sightseeing, dining and transport. Yangshuo, about 1.5 hours away, offers both lively West Street and quiet countryside guesthouses among the peaks. Many travellers spend one night in Guilin and several in Yangshuo or at the rice terraces.

### Food & Drink

Guilin rice noodles (mifen) served with braised meat, peanuts and pickles are the city's signature breakfast. Beer fish, cooked with local beer, tomatoes and chillies, is Yangshuo's famous dish. Stuffed dishes (niang), such as stuffed snails or tofu, are typical of the region. In minority villages, try oil tea (youcha), a savoury tea soup, and bamboo-tube rice.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn offer the most pleasant temperatures and clear views. Summer is hot and rainy, but the rivers are full and the terraces are green. The rice terraces look most spectacular when flooded in spring (May–June) and golden before the harvest in late September and October.

### Getting Around

Guilin is connected by high-speed rail to Guangzhou, Shenzhen, Guiyang and other cities, and Yangshuo has its own station on the high-speed line. Within the city, taxis, buses and DiDi ride-hailing are convenient. In Yangshuo, bicycles and electric scooters are the best way to explore the countryside.

### Day Trips

The Longji Rice Terraces are a full-day trip or an overnight stay. Bamboo rafting on the Yulong River near Yangshuo is a peaceful alternative to the Li River cruise. The ancient towns of Xingping and Huangyao offer traditional architecture and fewer crowds.

### Practical Tips

- Book Li River cruise tickets in advance during holidays, and bring your passport.
- Mobile payments (Alipay, WeChat Pay) are essential; set them up before arrival.
- Pack rain gear and insect repellent in summer.
- Avoid Chinese national holidays, when the Li River and Yangshuo become extremely crowded.

## Hamburg, Germany

*Germany's gateway to the world, a maritime city of canals, warehouses and music.*

- **Country:** Germany
- **Region:** Central Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.9 million (city); ~5.4 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** German
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Hamburg Airport (HAM)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Hamburg is Germany's second-largest city and one of Europe's biggest ports, located on the Elbe River. A former Hanseatic city-state, it still has a proud, independent character and considerable wealth. Hamburg has more bridges than Venice and Amsterdam combined, and its canals, lakes and harbour give it a maritime atmosphere. The city is known for its music scene, where the Beatles played in the early 1960s, its famous red-light district, and the spectacular Elbphilharmonie concert hall. It combines elegance with an edgy nightlife.

### Top Attractions

- **Elbphilharmonie:** A concert hall built on top of an old warehouse, with a free public viewing platform (Plaza) offering harbour views.
- **Speicherstadt:** The world's largest warehouse district, built on oak piles, is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
- **Miniatur Wunderland:** The world's largest model railway exhibition, beloved by adults and children.
- **Port of Hamburg and Landungsbrücken:** Harbour boat tours depart from the historic piers.
- **Reeperbahn and St. Pauli:** Hamburg's famous nightlife district with bars, clubs, theatres and Beatles-Platz.
- **Alster Lakes:** Two lakes in the city centre, surrounded by elegant buildings, perfect for walks and boat trips.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Altstadt and Neustadt are central, with shopping and sights. St. Pauli and the Schanzenviertel are alternative and lively, while Ottensen is trendy with cafés. HafenCity is a modern waterfront district near the Elbphilharmonie, and Blankenese is an upscale riverside village.

### Food & Drink

Hamburg's signature dishes include Fischbrötchen (fish sandwiches), Labskaus (corned beef, beetroot and potatoes, often with a fried egg) and Franzbrötchen, a cinnamon pastry. The Sunday morning Fischmarkt in St. Pauli is an institution, with market criers and live music. The city has excellent seafood and international restaurants.

### When to Visit

Summer is the most pleasant season, with outdoor events like the Hamburger DOM funfair (held three times a year) and harbour festivals. Rain is common year-round. December brings many Christmas markets.

### Getting Around

U-Bahn, S-Bahn, buses and harbour ferries are well integrated; ferry line 62 is a cheap alternative to a harbour tour. The S-Bahn connects the airport with the centre in about 25 minutes. Trains reach Berlin in under two hours.

### Day Trips

Lübeck, the medieval "Queen of the Hanseatic League", is about 45 minutes away and famous for marzipan. The seaside resort of Travemünde and the Baltic coast are close. Lüneburg, with its brick architecture and salt history, is another easy trip.

### Practical Tips

- Book Elbphilharmonie concerts well ahead; the Plaza can be visited with a free timed ticket.
- Arrive early at the Fischmarkt on Sunday (around 5–9:30 am in summer).
- Buy tickets for Miniatur Wunderland online to avoid long queues.
- Pack a light raincoat.

## Hangzhou, China

*A poetic city of West Lake, tea plantations, temples and pagodas.*

- **Country:** China
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~12.5 million (municipality)
- **Language(s):** Mandarin Chinese (Wu dialect locally)
- **Currency:** Chinese Yuan Renminbi (CNY)
- **Main airport:** Hangzhou Xiaoshan International Airport (HGH)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and September–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Hangzhou, the capital of Zhejiang Province, is one of China's most beautiful and historic cities, located at the southern end of the Grand Canal. It served as the capital of the Southern Song dynasty in the 12th and 13th centuries, when it was among the largest and richest cities in the world. A traditional Chinese saying claims "Above there is heaven, below there are Suzhou and Hangzhou." The city's highlight is West Lake, a UNESCO World Heritage Site surrounded by hills, gardens, temples and causeways. Today Hangzhou is also a modern technology hub, home to Alibaba, and hosted the 2023 Asian Games.

### Top Attractions

- **West Lake (Xi Hu):** A scenic lake with the Su and Bai causeways, islands, willow-lined paths and classic views such as "Three Pools Mirroring the Moon".
- **Lingyin Temple:** One of China's largest and most important Buddhist temples, founded in 328 AD, with hundreds of rock carvings at nearby Feilai Feng.
- **Longjing tea villages:** Hills of tea terraces where the famous Dragon Well green tea is grown; visit villages like Meijiawu and Longjing for tastings.
- **Leifeng Pagoda:** A rebuilt pagoda linked to the Legend of the White Snake, with views over the lake at sunset.
- **Hefang Street and Qinghefang:** Historic streets with traditional shops, snacks, herbal pharmacies and teahouses.
- **Grand Canal:** The world's longest artificial waterway, with preserved canal-side neighbourhoods such as Qiaoxi and boat rides.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Staying around West Lake, especially the Hubin area, puts you close to the main sights, shopping and restaurants. Wulin Square is a central commercial area with good transport connections. Quieter hotels are found in the hills west of the lake and near the tea villages.

### Food & Drink

Hangzhou cuisine is known for fresh, delicate and slightly sweet flavours. Famous dishes include West Lake vinegar fish, Dongpo pork (braised pork belly named after the poet Su Dongpo), beggar's chicken baked in clay, and Longjing shrimp stir-fried with tea leaves. Pian'erchuan noodles and scallion pancakes are popular everyday foods. Drinking freshly picked Longjing tea in spring is a highlight.

### When to Visit

Spring brings blooming peach blossoms and the first tea harvest, and autumn offers osmanthus scent and clear weather. Summers are hot and humid, with lotus flowers covering parts of West Lake. Winters are cold and damp, but "broken bridge in snow" is a celebrated scene.

### Getting Around

Hangzhou has a modern metro network, a large public bike-share system and plentiful DiDi taxis. The area around West Lake is best explored on foot or by bike. High-speed trains reach Shanghai in about an hour, and the airport is connected by metro.

### Day Trips

The water towns of Wuzhen and Xitang, with canals and stone bridges, are easy trips. Qiandao Lake (Thousand Island Lake) offers scenic islands, and Moganshan is a mountain retreat with bamboo forests. Shanghai and Suzhou are also close by high-speed rail.

### Practical Tips

- Rent a bike or walk around West Lake early in the morning to avoid crowds.
- Avoid visiting during national holidays, when the lake becomes extremely crowded.
- Carry your passport for train tickets, some attractions and hotel check-in.
- Buy tea directly from farmers in the tea villages, but check quality and price.

## Hanoi, Vietnam

*Vietnam's atmospheric capital of lakes, French colonial architecture and street food.*

- **Country:** Vietnam
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~8.5 million
- **Language(s):** Vietnamese
- **Currency:** Vietnamese Dong (VND)
- **Main airport:** Noi Bai International Airport (HAN)
- **Best time to visit:** October–December and March–April
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Hanoi, the capital of Vietnam, celebrated its 1,000th anniversary in 2010 and blends Vietnamese, Chinese and French influences. The city is built around lakes, with tree-lined boulevards from the French colonial era and a bustling Old Quarter whose streets were once named after the goods sold there. Hanoi is more traditional and reserved than Ho Chi Minh City, yet full of energy, with motorbikes, street vendors and pavement cafés everywhere. It is one of the best street food cities in the world. Hanoi is also the starting point for trips to Ha Long Bay, Ninh Binh and the mountains of Sapa.

### Top Attractions

- **Hoan Kiem Lake:** The heart of the city, with the red Huc Bridge leading to Ngoc Son Temple; on weekend evenings, surrounding streets become pedestrian zones.
- **Old Quarter:** A maze of narrow streets with tube houses, temples, markets and countless food stalls.
- **Ho Chi Minh Mausoleum and Complex:** The embalmed body of Vietnam's revolutionary leader, his stilt house and the One Pillar Pagoda; strict dress codes apply.
- **Temple of Literature:** Built in 1070 and dedicated to Confucius, it housed Vietnam's first national university.
- **Hoa Lo Prison:** A museum in a French colonial prison later used for American POWs, nicknamed the "Hanoi Hilton".
- **Vietnam Museum of Ethnology:** An excellent museum about Vietnam's 54 ethnic groups, with traditional houses in the garden.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Old Quarter is lively and ideal for first-time visitors, with many budget and boutique hotels. The French Quarter, south of Hoan Kiem Lake, is elegant, with the Opera House and upscale hotels. Tay Ho (West Lake) is quieter and popular with expats, with lakeside cafés and restaurants.

### Food & Drink

Pho originated in northern Vietnam, and Hanoi's clear-broth beef pho is famous. Bun cha, grilled pork with rice noodles and herbs, became internationally known after Barack Obama and Anthony Bourdain ate it here. Cha ca La Vong (turmeric fish with dill), banh cuon (steamed rice rolls) and bun rieu (crab noodle soup) are local favourites. Egg coffee (ca phe trung), invented in Hanoi, is a must-try.

### When to Visit

Autumn and spring are the most pleasant times. Summers (May–September) are hot, humid and rainy, while winters (December–February) can be surprisingly cool and grey. Tet, the Lunar New Year, is a festive but quiet time when many businesses close.

### Getting Around

Walking is ideal in the Old Quarter, but crossing the street requires a slow, steady pace. Grab and other ride-hailing apps offer cheap cars and motorbike taxis. Hanoi's metro lines are expanding, and buses are cheap but can be confusing.

### Day Trips

Ha Long Bay and the quieter Lan Ha Bay, with thousands of limestone islands, are best experienced on an overnight cruise. Ninh Binh, called "Ha Long Bay on land", offers boat rides through karst landscapes at Trang An and Tam Coc. Sapa, in the northern mountains, is famous for trekking through rice terraces and ethnic minority villages.

### Practical Tips

- Cross streets slowly and predictably so motorbikes can steer around you.
- Check e-visa requirements before travelling.
- Carry small cash notes for street food and markets.
- Dress modestly at the mausoleum, temples and pagodas.

## Havana, Cuba

*A time-capsule city of colonial architecture, vintage cars and music.*

- **Country:** Cuba
- **Region:** Caribbean
- **Population (approx.):** ~2.1 million
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Cuban Peso (CUP); foreign currency and cards are accepted in some places
- **Main airport:** José Martí International Airport (HAV)
- **Best time to visit:** November–April
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Havana, the capital of Cuba, is famous for its crumbling colonial architecture, 1950s American cars and vibrant music scene. Founded by the Spanish in 1519, it was a key port of the Spanish Empire. Old Havana (La Habana Vieja) and its fortifications are a UNESCO World Heritage Site. The city has faced economic hardship, shortages and power outages in recent years. Visitors should be prepared for limited infrastructure, but the atmosphere remains unique.

### Top Attractions

- **Old Havana:** Restored plazas, colonial buildings and museums.
- **Malecón:** An 8-kilometre seafront promenade.
- **El Capitolio:** A grand building resembling the US Capitol.
- **Plaza de la Revolución:** A square with images of Che Guevara and Camilo Cienfuegos.
- **Fusterlandia:** A neighbourhood decorated with mosaics by artist José Fuster.
- **Morro Castle:** A fortress guarding the harbour entrance.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Old Havana, Centro Habana and Vedado are popular. Casas particulares (private homestays) are common.

### Food & Drink

Cuban dishes include ropa vieja (shredded beef), moros y cristianos (black beans and rice) and tostones. Paladares (private restaurants) offer the best meals. Mojitos and daiquiris are classic cocktails.

### When to Visit

November to April is the dry season. Hurricane season runs from June to November.

### Getting Around

Taxis, including classic cars, are common. The city is walkable.

### Day Trips

Viñales, with tobacco farms, and Varadero's beaches are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Bring enough cash in euros or US dollars; ATMs may be unreliable, and many foreign cards (especially US) do not work.
- US citizens face travel restrictions.
- Expect power and internet outages.
- Stay in casas particulares.

## Helsinki, Finland

*A seaside capital of design, saunas and neoclassical squares.*

- **Country:** Finland
- **Region:** Northern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~680,000 (city); ~1.6 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Finnish and Swedish
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Helsinki-Vantaa Airport (HEL)
- **Best time to visit:** June–August; December–March for winter experiences
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Helsinki, the capital of Finland, sits on a peninsula surrounded by the Baltic Sea and hundreds of islands. Founded by the Swedish king in 1550 and later rebuilt under Russian rule, it has an elegant neoclassical centre and a wealth of Art Nouveau and modern architecture. Finland is known for design, education and consistently ranking among the happiest countries in the world. Saunas are central to Finnish culture, and Helsinki has several spectacular public ones. The city is calm, clean and easy to navigate, with nature always close by.

### Top Attractions

- **Senate Square and Helsinki Cathedral:** The white, green-domed Lutheran cathedral towers over a neoclassical square.
- **Suomenlinna:** A UNESCO-listed sea fortress spread across several islands, reached by a 15-minute public ferry.
- **Market Square and Old Market Hall:** Harbour-side stalls selling fish, berries and souvenirs, and a historic indoor food hall.
- **Temppeliaukio Church (Rock Church):** A church carved directly into solid rock with a copper dome.
- **Oodi Central Library:** A striking modern library with maker spaces, cafés and a terrace.
- **Löyly and Allas Sea Pool:** Modern public saunas where you can dip into the sea, even in winter.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Kluuvi and Kamppi are central and convenient for shopping and transport. Punavuori and the Design District are packed with boutiques and galleries, while Kallio is a bohemian former working-class area with bars. Töölö is residential and close to parks and museums.

### Food & Drink

Finnish cuisine features salmon, reindeer, rye bread and wild berries. Try salmon soup (lohikeitto), Karelian pies (rice-filled rye pastries) and cinnamon buns (korvapuusti). Salmiakki, a salty liquorice, divides opinions. Helsinki has a growing New Nordic restaurant scene and many cosy cafés.

### When to Visit

Summer brings very long days, open-air cafés and island trips. Winter is dark and cold but offers ice-swimming, saunas and a magical atmosphere, especially around Christmas. Spring and autumn are cooler and quieter.

### Getting Around

Helsinki has trams, buses, a metro and ferries operated by HSL, and a train connects the airport with the centre in about 30 minutes. The centre is walkable. Ferries link Helsinki with Tallinn and Stockholm.

### Day Trips

Tallinn in Estonia is about two hours away by fast ferry. Porvoo, Finland's second-oldest town with red riverside warehouses, is an hour by bus. Nuuksio National Park offers forests and lakes close to the city.

### Practical Tips

- Bring a swimsuit for public saunas, although some traditional saunas are nude and gender-separated.
- Buy transport tickets using the HSL app.
- Tap water is excellent.
- Tipping is not expected, but rounding up is appreciated.

## Hiroshima, Japan

*A city of peace and resilience, and the gateway to the sacred island of Miyajima.*

- **Country:** Japan
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.2 million
- **Language(s):** Japanese
- **Currency:** Japanese Yen (JPY)
- **Main airport:** Hiroshima Airport (HIJ)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and October–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Hiroshima is a city in western Japan on the Seto Inland Sea, founded as a castle town in 1589. On 6 August 1945, it became the first city in the world to be destroyed by an atomic bomb, killing tens of thousands instantly and many more in the following months. Rebuilt as a "City of Peace", Hiroshima today is a lively, green city with wide boulevards, rivers and a warm, welcoming atmosphere. The Peace Memorial Park and Museum are among the most moving places in Japan. Nearby Miyajima Island, with its famous "floating" torii gate, is one of the country's three most scenic views.

### Top Attractions

- **Peace Memorial Park:** A large park with memorials, including the Cenotaph, the Flame of Peace and the Children's Peace Monument, decorated with thousands of paper cranes.
- **Atomic Bomb Dome (Genbaku Dome):** The skeletal ruin of a building near the hypocentre, preserved as a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
- **Hiroshima Peace Memorial Museum:** A powerful museum documenting the bombing, its victims and the effects of nuclear weapons.
- **Hiroshima Castle:** A reconstructed castle with a museum on samurai culture and the city's history.
- **Shukkeien Garden:** A beautiful traditional garden with a pond, bridges and teahouses, designed in 1620.
- **Itsukushima Shrine on Miyajima:** A shrine built over the water with a large vermilion torii gate that appears to float at high tide.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The area around Hiroshima Station is convenient for Shinkansen travellers. Staying near the Peace Park, Hondori shopping arcade or Nagarekawa entertainment district puts you close to restaurants and sights. On Miyajima, traditional ryokan inns offer a peaceful overnight experience once the day-trippers leave.

### Food & Drink

Hiroshima-style okonomiyaki is a layered savoury pancake with cabbage, noodles, pork and egg, cooked on a griddle in front of you; Okonomimura is a building filled with okonomiyaki stalls. The region is famous for oysters, served grilled, fried or raw. Tsukemen with spicy dipping sauce is a local noodle favourite. On Miyajima, try grilled conger eel on rice (anago-meshi) and momiji manju, maple-leaf-shaped cakes.

### When to Visit

Spring cherry blossoms and autumn leaves make these seasons the most beautiful. Summer is hot and humid, and the Peace Memorial Ceremony on 6 August, followed by a lantern-floating ceremony, draws many visitors. Winter is mild and a good time for oysters.

### Getting Around

Hiroshima's tram network is the easiest way to get around the city. The Shinkansen connects Hiroshima with Osaka in about 1.5 hours and Tokyo in about four hours. JR trains and ferries link the city with Miyajima in under an hour.

### Day Trips

Miyajima is the essential day trip, with hiking or a ropeway up Mount Misen. Onomichi, a hillside port town, is the starting point of the Shimanami Kaido cycling route across islands. Kurashiki, with its preserved canal district, and Okunoshima, known as "Rabbit Island", are other options.

### Practical Tips

- The JR Pass covers the JR ferry to Miyajima.
- Miyajima charges a small visitor tax to all visitors.
- Check tide times to see the torii gate both "floating" and at low tide.
- Allow at least two hours for the Peace Memorial Museum.

## Ho Chi Minh City, Vietnam

*Vietnam's dynamic economic hub, still widely known as Saigon.*

- **Country:** Vietnam
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~9.5 million (city); over 13 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Vietnamese
- **Currency:** Vietnamese Dong (VND)
- **Main airport:** Tan Son Nhat International Airport (SGN)
- **Best time to visit:** December–April
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Ho Chi Minh City, still commonly called Saigon, is Vietnam's largest city and its economic powerhouse, located in the south near the Mekong Delta. It was the capital of French Cochinchina and later of South Vietnam until the end of the Vietnam War in 1975, after which it was renamed after the revolutionary leader. The city is fast-paced and entrepreneurial, with gleaming skyscrapers, French colonial landmarks, rooftop bars and buzzing markets. Millions of motorbikes fill the streets, creating a vivid, noisy atmosphere. It is also a good base for exploring the Mekong Delta and the Cu Chi Tunnels.

### Top Attractions

- **War Remnants Museum:** A sobering museum about the Vietnam War, with photographs and military equipment.
- **Independence Palace:** The former presidential palace of South Vietnam, preserved as it was in 1975, including bunkers and war rooms.
- **Notre-Dame Cathedral Basilica and Central Post Office:** French colonial landmarks built in the late 19th century; the cathedral has been undergoing restoration.
- **Ben Thanh Market:** A busy market selling food, textiles and souvenirs, with food stalls inside and a night market nearby.
- **Nguyen Hue Walking Street:** A pedestrian boulevard leading to the People's Committee Building, lively in the evenings.
- **Bitexco Financial Tower and Landmark 81:** Skyscrapers with observation decks and bars offering views over the city.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

District 1 is the central tourist area, with most sights, hotels, restaurants and nightlife, including the backpacker hub of Bui Vien Street. District 3 has leafy streets, local restaurants and cafés. Thao Dien, now part of Thu Duc City, is popular with expats and has riverside restaurants and boutiques.

### Food & Drink

Southern Vietnamese cuisine is sweeter and richer in herbs than northern food. Try com tam (broken rice with grilled pork), banh mi, hu tieu (noodle soup), banh xeo (crispy pancakes) and fresh spring rolls. Pho is also everywhere. Vietnamese coffee, especially iced coffee with condensed milk (ca phe sua da), is a daily ritual.

### When to Visit

The dry season from December to April is the best time to visit. The rainy season (May–November) brings short but heavy afternoon downpours. Tet, the Lunar New Year, is festive, though many businesses close for several days.

### Getting Around

Grab and other ride-hailing apps offer cheap car and motorbike rides. The city's first metro line, from Ben Thanh to Thu Duc, opened in December 2024. Traffic is intense, so walking requires care, especially when crossing streets.

### Day Trips

The Cu Chi Tunnels, used by Viet Cong fighters, are about 1.5 hours away. The Mekong Delta offers floating markets, canals, fruit orchards and river life around towns like Can Tho and Ben Tre. Vung Tau is a beach town reachable by fast boat or road.

### Practical Tips

- Keep phones and bags secure; snatch theft from motorbikes happens.
- Cross streets slowly and steadily.
- Carry small cash for markets and street food.
- Check Vietnamese e-visa rules before travelling.

## Hoi An, Vietnam

*A lantern-lit ancient trading port of yellow houses, tailors and riverside charm.*

- **Country:** Vietnam
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~120,000
- **Language(s):** Vietnamese
- **Currency:** Vietnamese Dong (VND)
- **Main airport:** Da Nang International Airport (DAD), about 45 minutes away
- **Best time to visit:** February–July
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Hoi An is a small city on the Thu Bon River in central Vietnam, once one of Southeast Asia's busiest trading ports between the 15th and 19th centuries. Merchants from China, Japan, India and Europe settled here, leaving behind a remarkable blend of architecture. When the river silted up and trade moved to Da Nang, Hoi An was left largely untouched, preserving its old town, now a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Today its mustard-yellow houses, wooden shopfronts and silk lanterns make it one of Vietnam's most atmospheric destinations. Hoi An is also famous for its tailors, cooking classes and nearby beaches.

### Top Attractions

- **Ancient Town:** A beautifully preserved district of merchant houses, assembly halls and temples; an entrance ticket covers access to several historic buildings.
- **Japanese Covered Bridge:** A 16th-century bridge and the symbol of Hoi An, recently restored.
- **Assembly halls:** Ornate Chinese community halls such as the Fujian Assembly Hall, decorated with dragons and incense coils.
- **Lantern-lit riverside:** In the evening, silk lanterns illuminate the streets and visitors float candles on the river from small boats.
- **An Bang and Cua Dai beaches:** Sandy beaches a short bike ride from the old town, with beach bars and seafood restaurants.
- **Tra Que Vegetable Village:** A farming village where visitors can join gardening and cooking experiences.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Staying in or near the Ancient Town gives easy access to sights and restaurants but can be busy. Riverside homestays and villas in Cam Thanh or among the rice fields offer quiet surroundings. An Bang Beach is ideal for travellers who want a beach base with the old town nearby.

### Food & Drink

Hoi An has several unique dishes: cao lau, thick noodles with pork and herbs traditionally made with water from a local well; white rose dumplings (banh bao vac); and com ga, Hoi An chicken rice. Banh mi from famous shops such as Banh Mi Phuong is a must. The central market offers cheap, delicious meals, and cooking classes often include a market tour and basket-boat ride.

### When to Visit

February to July is generally dry and sunny, with the hottest months in June and July. The rainy season from September to December can bring floods to the old town. The full-moon lantern festival, held monthly, turns off electric lights in favour of lanterns.

### Getting Around

The Ancient Town is pedestrian-friendly and restricted for vehicles at certain times. Bicycles are the best way to reach beaches and rice fields. Da Nang Airport is about 45 minutes away by taxi or Grab.

### Day Trips

My Son Sanctuary, a complex of ancient Hindu temples built by the Champa kingdom, is about an hour away. The Cham Islands offer snorkelling and diving. Da Nang, the Marble Mountains and Ba Na Hills are close by.

### Practical Tips

- Order tailored clothes early in your stay to allow time for fittings.
- Buy the Ancient Town ticket; checkpoints may ask for it.
- Check flood warnings in October and November.
- Bargain politely at markets and shops.

## Hong Kong, China (Hong Kong SAR)

*A dazzling skyline, dim sum, harbour views and surprisingly wild mountains.*

- **Country:** China (Hong Kong SAR)
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~7.5 million
- **Language(s):** Cantonese and English (Mandarin widely understood)
- **Currency:** Hong Kong Dollar (HKD)
- **Main airport:** Hong Kong International Airport (HKG)
- **Best time to visit:** October–December
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Hong Kong is a Special Administrative Region of China, located on the southern coast next to Shenzhen. It was a British colony for over 150 years until its handover to China in 1997, and it retains a distinctive mix of Chinese and Western influences. Hong Kong is famous for its spectacular skyline, busy harbour, shopping and food. Beyond the skyscrapers, around three-quarters of its territory is countryside, including mountains, beaches and islands with excellent hiking. It is efficient, safe and easy to navigate.

### Top Attractions

- **Victoria Peak:** Reached by the historic Peak Tram, it offers the city's most famous skyline view.
- **Victoria Harbour and Star Ferry:** A cheap ferry ride across the harbour, with the Symphony of Lights show in the evening.
- **Tian Tan Buddha (Big Buddha):** A giant bronze Buddha on Lantau Island, reached by the Ngong Ping 360 cable car.
- **Temple Street Night Market:** A lively market with street food, fortune tellers and stalls.
- **M+ and the West Kowloon Cultural District:** A world-class museum of visual culture and the Hong Kong Palace Museum.
- **Dragon's Back trail:** A popular ridge hike ending at Big Wave Bay beach.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Central and Sheung Wan are the business and nightlife core, with bars in Lan Kwai Fong and Soho. Tsim Sha Tsui offers harbour views and shopping, while Mong Kok is dense and local. Causeway Bay is a shopping hub, and Sai Kung offers seafood and access to beaches.

### Food & Drink

Dim sum, including har gow and siu mai, is best enjoyed at traditional teahouses and budget spots like Tim Ho Wan. Cantonese roast meats such as char siu and roast goose, wonton noodles and egg tarts are classics. Cha chaan tengs, local cafés, serve milk tea and pineapple buns. Hong Kong has many Michelin-starred restaurants, from street food to fine dining.

### When to Visit

October to December offers clear skies and comfortable temperatures. Summer is hot, humid and the typhoon season. Chinese New Year brings fireworks and festivities.

### Getting Around

The MTR subway is excellent, and the Octopus card works on transport and in shops. Trams, ferries and buses are cheap and scenic. The Airport Express reaches Central in about 25 minutes.

### Day Trips

Macau is about an hour away via ferry or the Hong Kong–Zhuhai–Macau Bridge. Lamma Island and Cheung Chau have seafood restaurants and hiking. Shenzhen is reachable by high-speed train.

### Practical Tips

- Use an Octopus card or contactless payment for transport.
- Check typhoon signals during the summer.
- Carry a light jacket; air conditioning is strong indoors.
- Check entry requirements for mainland China if planning side trips.

## Honolulu, United States (Hawaii)

*Hawaii's capital, home to Waikiki Beach, Pearl Harbor and volcanic landscapes.*

- **Country:** United States (Hawaii)
- **Region:** Oceania / North Pacific
- **Population (approx.):** ~350,000 (city); ~1 million (O'ahu)
- **Language(s):** English and Hawaiian
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Daniel K. Inouye International Airport (HNL)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Honolulu, on the island of O'ahu, is the capital of Hawaii and its largest city. It blends Native Hawaiian heritage, Asian and American influences. Waikiki Beach is famous for surfing and resorts, while Diamond Head crater offers hiking. Pearl Harbor is a historic site of the 1941 attack that brought the US into the Second World War. The island offers beaches, mountains and culture.

### Top Attractions

- **Waikiki Beach:** A world-famous beach for surfing and relaxing.
- **Diamond Head:** A volcanic crater with a hiking trail; reservations required.
- **Pearl Harbor National Memorial:** Including the USS Arizona Memorial.
- **'Iolani Palace:** The only royal palace in the US.
- **Hanauma Bay:** A nature preserve for snorkelling; reservations required.
- **Bishop Museum:** Hawaiian and Pacific history.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Waikiki is the tourist hub. Kaka'ako has street art, and Chinatown has restaurants.

### Food & Drink

Poke, loco moco, spam musubi, shave ice and plate lunches are local favourites.

### When to Visit

The weather is warm year-round. Winter brings big waves on the North Shore.

### Getting Around

TheBus covers the island, and a new rail line (Skyline) is opening in stages. Renting a car helps explore.

### Day Trips

The North Shore, Kailua and the Polynesian Cultural Center are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Book Diamond Head and Hanauma Bay in advance.
- Use reef-safe sunscreen.
- Respect Hawaiian culture and sacred sites.
- Beware of strong currents.

## Istanbul, Türkiye

*A city on two continents, where Byzantine and Ottoman empires left their magnificent mark.*

- **Country:** Türkiye
- **Region:** Southeastern Europe / Western Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~15.7 million
- **Language(s):** Turkish
- **Currency:** Turkish Lira (TRY)
- **Main airport:** Istanbul Airport (IST); Sabiha Gökçen Airport (SAW)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–November
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Istanbul straddles the Bosphorus Strait, with neighbourhoods in both Europe and Asia. As Byzantium, Constantinople and then Istanbul, it served as the capital of the Roman, Byzantine and Ottoman empires for over 1,500 years. The skyline of domes and minarets, the vibrant bazaars and the constant ferry traffic create an unforgettable atmosphere. Istanbul is Türkiye's largest city and its cultural and economic heart. It offers a remarkable mix of history, food, shopping and nightlife.

### Top Attractions

- **Hagia Sophia:** Built as a cathedral in 537, later a mosque, museum and again a mosque, it is one of the world's great buildings; visitors must dress modestly.
- **Blue Mosque (Sultan Ahmed Mosque):** Famous for its six minarets and blue İznik tiles, it is still an active place of worship.
- **Topkapı Palace:** The residence of Ottoman sultans, with courtyards, the Harem and treasury.
- **Grand Bazaar:** One of the largest and oldest covered markets in the world, with thousands of shops.
- **Basilica Cistern:** A vast underground Byzantine water reservoir supported by hundreds of columns, including two Medusa heads.
- **Bosphorus cruise:** Ferries sail between Europe and Asia, past palaces, fortresses and wooden mansions.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Sultanahmet is the historic core with major sights but fewer local restaurants. Beyoğlu, around İstiklal Avenue and Galata, is lively and cosmopolitan. Karaköy is trendy with cafés and galleries, while Kadıköy and Moda on the Asian side offer a relaxed local vibe.

### Food & Drink

Istanbul's cuisine is outstanding: try kebabs, meze, lahmacun, pide and fish sandwiches (balık ekmek) by the Galata Bridge. Breakfast (kahvaltı) is a lavish spread of cheeses, olives, eggs and breads. Baklava, Turkish delight (lokum), Turkish coffee and tea are everywhere. The Spice Bazaar is a colourful place to buy spices and sweets.

### When to Visit

Spring brings tulip season in April, and autumn is warm and pleasant. Summers are hot and busy, and winters are cool and rainy with occasional snow. During Ramadan, some restaurants have different hours, but evenings are festive.

### Getting Around

Istanbul has a growing metro network, trams, funiculars, buses and ferries, and the Istanbulkart card works on all of them. Traffic is heavy, so rail and ferries are often faster. Both airports are far from the centre; allow over an hour.

### Day Trips

The Princes' Islands, where cars are banned, are a relaxing ferry trip away. Bursa, the first Ottoman capital, and the Black Sea villages are other options. Many visitors fly or take a bus onwards to Cappadocia.

### Practical Tips

- Women should carry a scarf to cover their hair in mosques; everyone should cover shoulders and knees.
- Mosques close to tourists during prayer times.
- Haggle politely in bazaars.
- Use the Istanbulkart for all public transport.

## Jaipur, India

*Rajasthan's "Pink City" of palaces, forts and colourful bazaars.*

- **Country:** India
- **Region:** South Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~4 million
- **Language(s):** Hindi and Rajasthani
- **Currency:** Indian Rupee (INR)
- **Main airport:** Jaipur International Airport (JAI)
- **Best time to visit:** October–March
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Jaipur, the capital of Rajasthan, was founded in 1727 by Maharaja Sawai Jai Singh II as one of India's first planned cities. Its old city was painted pink in 1876 to welcome the Prince of Wales, earning it the nickname "Pink City". Jaipur is a UNESCO World Heritage City, known for its palaces, forts, bazaars and crafts such as gemstones, textiles and blue pottery. It forms part of the Golden Triangle with Delhi and Agra. The city is a lively mix of royal heritage and everyday Rajasthani life.

### Top Attractions

- **Amber Fort:** A hilltop fort-palace with mirror-decorated halls, courtyards and views over Maota Lake.
- **Hawa Mahal (Palace of Winds):** A honeycomb-like facade with hundreds of small windows built for royal women to observe street life.
- **City Palace:** The royal residence, with museums, courtyards and decorated gates.
- **Jantar Mantar:** An 18th-century astronomical observatory with giant instruments, a UNESCO site.
- **Nahargarh Fort:** A fort on the ridge above the city, famous for sunset views.
- **Bazaars:** Johari Bazaar for jewellery, Bapu Bazaar for textiles and Tripolia Bazaar for crafts.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The old city is central to the sights but crowded. The C-Scheme and Bani Park areas offer comfortable hotels, while heritage havelis and palace hotels provide a royal experience. Luxury options include the Rambagh Palace.

### Food & Drink

Rajasthani cuisine includes dal baati churma (lentils with baked wheat balls), laal maas (a fiery mutton curry) and ghevar, a honeycomb-like sweet. Pyaaz kachori and lassi at Lassiwala are popular. Many rooftop restaurants offer views of the old city. Vegetarian food is widely available.

### When to Visit

October to March is the best time, with pleasant temperatures. Summer is extremely hot, and the monsoon brings humidity. The Jaipur Literature Festival in January and colourful festivals like Diwali and Holi are major events.

### Getting Around

Auto-rickshaws, taxis and ride-hailing apps (Uber, Ola) are the main ways to get around. The Jaipur Metro has a limited network. Trains and highways connect Jaipur with Delhi (around 4–5 hours) and Agra.

### Day Trips

Abhaneri, with the stepwell Chand Baori, is on the way to Agra. Ranthambore National Park offers tiger safaris. Pushkar, a holy town with a lake and camel fair, is about 2.5 hours away.

### Practical Tips

- Buy a composite ticket covering several monuments.
- Bargain in bazaars and be wary of commission-driven shopping tours.
- Dress modestly at temples.
- Avoid elephant rides at Amber Fort for animal welfare reasons.

## Jakarta, Indonesia

*Indonesia's sprawling megacity of colonial heritage, malls and diverse food.*

- **Country:** Indonesia
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~11 million (city); over 30 million (Greater Jakarta)
- **Language(s):** Indonesian
- **Currency:** Indonesian Rupiah (IDR)
- **Main airport:** Soekarno–Hatta International Airport (CGK)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Jakarta, on the northwest coast of Java, is Indonesia's largest city and its economic centre. Founded as Sunda Kelapa, it became the Dutch colonial capital of Batavia in the 17th century. Jakarta is a vast, energetic metropolis with skyscrapers, shopping malls and notorious traffic. Although Indonesia is moving its capital to Nusantara in Borneo, Jakarta remains the country's economic and cultural hub. Visitors find a diverse food scene, museums and a lively nightlife.

### Top Attractions

- **Kota Tua (Old Town):** The Dutch colonial district around Fatahillah Square, with the Jakarta History Museum and cafés.
- **National Monument (Monas):** A 132-metre tower symbolising independence, with an observation deck.
- **Istiqlal Mosque:** One of the largest mosques in Southeast Asia, located opposite the Jakarta Cathedral.
- **National Museum of Indonesia:** A collection of artefacts from across the archipelago.
- **Taman Mini Indonesia Indah:** A park showcasing the culture and architecture of Indonesia's provinces.
- **Sunda Kelapa Harbour:** An old port with traditional wooden schooners (pinisi).

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Central Jakarta (Menteng, Thamrin) is convenient. South Jakarta (Kemang, Senopati, SCBD) is trendy, with restaurants and nightlife. Kota Tua is historic.

### Food & Drink

Jakarta is a melting pot of Indonesian cuisines: try nasi goreng, satay, soto Betawi, gado-gado and kerak telor, a traditional Betawi egg dish. Street food is everywhere, and malls have large food courts. Padang restaurants serve dishes from West Sumatra.

### When to Visit

The dry season from May to September is best. The rainy season (November–March) can cause flooding. Traffic is heavy all year round.

### Getting Around

The MRT, LRT and TransJakarta bus rapid transit help avoid traffic. Ride-hailing apps (Grab, Gojek) are widely used. The airport train connects Soekarno–Hatta with the city.

### Day Trips

The Thousand Islands (Kepulauan Seribu) offer beaches and snorkelling. Bogor has botanical gardens. Bandung, a cool highland city, is reachable by high-speed train in under an hour.

### Practical Tips

- Allow extra time for traffic.
- Dress modestly at mosques.
- Drink bottled water.
- Use apps to avoid taxi scams.

## Jerusalem, Israel / Palestinian territories (status disputed)

*A holy city for Judaism, Christianity and Islam, layered with 3,000 years of history.*

- **Country:** Israel / Palestinian territories (status disputed)
- **Region:** Middle East
- **Population (approx.):** ~990,000
- **Language(s):** Hebrew and Arabic (English widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Israeli New Shekel (ILS)
- **Main airport:** Ben Gurion International Airport (TLV), about 45 minutes away
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and September–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Jerusalem is one of the oldest cities in the world and holds deep religious significance for Jews, Christians and Muslims. Its walled Old City, a UNESCO World Heritage Site, contains the Western Wall, the Church of the Holy Sepulchre and the Al-Aqsa Mosque compound with the Dome of the Rock. The city's political status is disputed, and it remains at the centre of the Israeli–Palestinian conflict. Beyond the Old City, Jerusalem has museums, markets and neighbourhoods reflecting many cultures. Visitors should check current travel advisories before planning a trip.

### Top Attractions

- **Western Wall:** The holiest place where Jews can pray, part of the retaining wall of the Second Temple.
- **Church of the Holy Sepulchre:** Revered by many Christians as the site of Jesus's crucifixion, burial and resurrection.
- **Temple Mount / Haram al-Sharif:** Home to the Dome of the Rock and Al-Aqsa Mosque; access for non-Muslims is limited to certain hours.
- **Via Dolorosa:** The path believed to have been followed by Jesus to his crucifixion.
- **Yad Vashem:** Israel's national Holocaust memorial and museum.
- **Mount of Olives:** A hill with churches, a Jewish cemetery and panoramic views of the Old City.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Old City is divided into Jewish, Christian, Muslim and Armenian Quarters. West Jerusalem includes Mahane Yehuda Market and modern areas, while East Jerusalem is predominantly Palestinian. The German Colony has restaurants and cafés.

### Food & Drink

Try hummus, falafel, shakshuka and sabich. Mahane Yehuda Market is a food destination by day and nightlife spot by night. Knafeh and ka'ak (sesame bread) are popular. Kosher restaurants close for Shabbat.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are pleasant. Summers are hot, and winters can be cold and rainy. Religious holidays affect opening hours.

### Getting Around

The light rail and buses serve the city. The Old City is walkable. Trains connect Jerusalem with Tel Aviv and the airport.

### Day Trips

The Dead Sea and Masada are popular. Bethlehem, in the West Bank, requires crossing a checkpoint. Tel Aviv is less than an hour away.

### Practical Tips

- Check current travel advisories.
- Dress modestly at religious sites.
- Public transport stops during Shabbat.
- Respect local customs.

## Kandy, Sri Lanka

*Sri Lanka's cultural capital and home of the Temple of the Sacred Tooth Relic.*

- **Country:** Sri Lanka
- **Region:** South Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~125,000
- **Language(s):** Sinhala and Tamil
- **Currency:** Sri Lankan Rupee (LKR)
- **Main airport:** Bandaranaike International Airport (CMB), about 3 hours away
- **Best time to visit:** December–April
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Kandy, set among forested hills in central Sri Lanka, was the capital of the last Sinhalese kingdom until the British took control in 1815. It is a UNESCO World Heritage City and the island's cultural and spiritual heart. The city centres on a picturesque lake and the Temple of the Sacred Tooth Relic, which houses a tooth believed to be the Buddha's. Kandy is known for its traditional dance, botanic gardens and the Esala Perahera festival. It is also the gateway to Sri Lanka's tea country.

### Top Attractions

- **Temple of the Sacred Tooth Relic (Sri Dalada Maligawa):** The most sacred Buddhist site in Sri Lanka, with daily rituals (puja).
- **Kandy Lake:** An artificial lake built in 1807, ideal for walks.
- **Royal Botanic Gardens, Peradeniya:** Gardens with orchids, palms and giant trees.
- **Kandyan cultural dance show:** Performances featuring drumming, dance and fire walking.
- **Bahiravokanda Vihara Buddha Statue:** A large white Buddha overlooking the city.
- **Udawattakele Forest Reserve:** A forest with walking trails and birdlife.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Staying near Kandy Lake is convenient. Hillside hotels offer views and quiet.

### Food & Drink

Rice and curry, hoppers and kottu roti are common. Kandy has many bakeries. Ceylon tea is served everywhere.

### When to Visit

December to April is best. The Esala Perahera, a grand procession with decorated elephants, takes place in July or August.

### Getting Around

Tuk-tuks are the main transport. The train from Kandy to Ella is one of the world's most scenic journeys.

### Day Trips

Sigiriya, Dambulla and the tea plantations of Nuwara Eliya are popular. The Pinnawala Elephant Orphanage is controversial for animal welfare.

### Practical Tips

- Book train seats in advance.
- Dress in white or modest clothing at the temple.
- Remove shoes and hats in temples.
- Accommodation fills up during the Perahera.

## Kathmandu, Nepal

*The gateway to the Himalayas, a valley of temples, stupas and ancient squares.*

- **Country:** Nepal
- **Region:** South Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.5 million (Kathmandu Valley ~3 million)
- **Language(s):** Nepali
- **Currency:** Nepalese Rupee (NPR)
- **Main airport:** Tribhuvan International Airport (KTM)
- **Best time to visit:** October–November and March–April
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Kathmandu, the capital of Nepal, lies in a valley surrounded by the Himalayan foothills. The Kathmandu Valley contains seven UNESCO World Heritage monuments, including ancient royal squares, Hindu temples and Buddhist stupas. The city is chaotic, colourful and spiritual, where cows wander the streets and incense fills the air. It was badly damaged by the 2015 earthquake, but many monuments have been restored. Kathmandu is the starting point for trekking to Everest and the Annapurnas.

### Top Attractions

- **Boudhanath Stupa:** One of the largest stupas in the world, surrounded by monasteries.
- **Swayambhunath (Monkey Temple):** A hilltop stupa with views over the city.
- **Kathmandu Durbar Square:** A historic royal square with temples and palaces.
- **Pashupatinath Temple:** A sacred Hindu temple on the Bagmati River, with open-air cremations.
- **Patan and Bhaktapur Durbar Squares:** Beautiful medieval towns in the valley.
- **Garden of Dreams:** A neoclassical garden in the city centre.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Thamel is the tourist hub, with hotels, restaurants and trekking shops. Boudha and Patan are quieter options.

### Food & Drink

Dal bhat (lentils and rice) is the national dish. Momos (dumplings) are everywhere. Newari cuisine includes spicy snacks and rice beer.

### When to Visit

October to November offers clear mountain views. Spring (March–April) brings rhododendron blooms. The monsoon (June–September) brings rain.

### Getting Around

Taxis and ride-hailing apps (Pathao, InDrive) are available. Traffic and dust are heavy.

### Day Trips

Nagarkot offers Himalayan sunrise views. Bhaktapur and Patan are close. Chitwan National Park and Pokhara are further.

### Practical Tips

- Wear a mask on dusty days.
- Get trekking permits in Kathmandu.
- Walk clockwise around stupas.
- Be respectful at cremation sites.

## Kochi, India

*Kerala's historic spice port, where Chinese fishing nets line the waterfront.*

- **Country:** India
- **Region:** South Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~2.1 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Malayalam
- **Currency:** Indian Rupee (INR)
- **Main airport:** Cochin International Airport (COK)
- **Best time to visit:** October–March
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Kochi (Cochin), in the southern state of Kerala, has been a major spice trading port for centuries, attracting Arab, Chinese, Portuguese, Dutch and British traders. Fort Kochi, the historic district, has colonial buildings, churches, synagogues and art galleries. The city is known for its Chinese fishing nets, Kathakali dance performances and the Kochi-Muziris Biennale. Kochi is a gateway to Kerala's backwaters and hill stations.

### Top Attractions

- **Chinese fishing nets:** Large cantilevered nets operated on the waterfront.
- **St. Francis Church:** The burial site of Vasco da Gama.
- **Mattancherry Palace (Dutch Palace):** Murals depicting Hindu epics.
- **Paradesi Synagogue:** A 16th-century synagogue in Jew Town.
- **Kathakali performances:** Traditional dance-drama.
- **Kochi-Muziris Biennale:** A major contemporary art festival.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Fort Kochi and Mattancherry are historic and charming. Ernakulam is the modern commercial centre.

### Food & Drink

Kerala cuisine includes seafood curries, appam with stew and Karimeen pollichathu (pearl spot fish). Banana chips and coconut dishes are common.

### When to Visit

October to March is best. The monsoon brings heavy rain.

### Getting Around

Ferries connect Fort Kochi with Ernakulam. Auto-rickshaws and taxis are common. The metro serves Ernakulam.

### Day Trips

Alleppey (Alappuzha) backwaters are popular for houseboat stays. Munnar has tea plantations.

### Practical Tips

- Book houseboats in advance.
- Dress modestly.
- Try an Ayurvedic massage.
- Carry insect repellent.

## Kolkata, India

*India's intellectual and cultural capital of colonial grandeur, literature and sweets.*

- **Country:** India
- **Region:** South Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~15 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Bengali (Hindi and English widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Indian Rupee (INR)
- **Main airport:** Netaji Subhas Chandra Bose International Airport (CCU)
- **Best time to visit:** November–February
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Kolkata (formerly Calcutta), the capital of West Bengal, was the capital of British India until 1911. The legacy of that era is visible in grand colonial buildings, wide avenues and institutions such as museums, universities and libraries. Kolkata is often called India's cultural capital, home to Nobel laureate Rabindranath Tagore, filmmaker Satyajit Ray and a passionate literary, theatre and film scene. The city is crowded, humid and chaotic, yet warm and full of character, with yellow Ambassador taxis, hand-pulled rickshaws and trams. Its Durga Puja festival is one of the largest cultural events in the world.

### Top Attractions

- **Victoria Memorial:** A white marble monument and museum dedicated to Queen Victoria, surrounded by gardens.
- **Howrah Bridge:** A massive cantilever bridge over the Hooghly River, one of the busiest bridges in the world.
- **Dakshineswar Kali Temple and Belur Math:** Important Hindu temples on opposite banks of the river, connected by ferry.
- **Indian Museum:** The oldest and one of the largest museums in India, with archaeological and natural history collections.
- **College Street:** A street of bookshops and home to the historic Indian Coffee House, a meeting place for intellectuals.
- **Kumartuli:** The potters' quarter where artisans sculpt clay idols for festivals.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Park Street is central, with restaurants, bars and colonial-era hotels. The area around BBD Bagh has colonial architecture, while Ballygunge and Salt Lake are more residential. Sudder Street is popular with budget travellers.

### Food & Drink

Bengali cuisine is famous for fish, especially macher jhol (fish curry) and shorshe ilish (hilsa in mustard sauce). Kolkata is known for kathi rolls, puchka (spicy water-filled puffs) and Kolkata biryani with potato. Sweets such as rasgulla, sandesh and mishti doi (sweet yoghurt) are a local passion. Chinese-Indian cuisine in Tangra, the city's Chinatown, is also popular.

### When to Visit

November to February is pleasant and dry. Summers are hot and humid, and the monsoon brings heavy rain. Durga Puja in September or October transforms the city with elaborate pandals (temporary temples) and celebrations.

### Getting Around

Kolkata has India's oldest metro, as well as buses, trams, taxis and ferries on the Hooghly. Ride-hailing apps are widely used. Traffic can be heavy, so the metro is often the fastest option.

### Day Trips

The Sundarbans, a mangrove forest and home to the Bengal tiger, is a UNESCO site reached on multi-day boat trips. Shantiniketan, Tagore's university town, is about three hours away. Darjeeling and Sikkim can be reached by overnight train or flight.

### Practical Tips

- Visit during Durga Puja for a unique cultural experience, but expect crowds.
- Ride the historic tram while it still operates.
- Carry cash for small vendors.
- Try sweets from famous shops like K.C. Das and Balaram Mullick.

## Kraków, Poland

*Poland's royal capital, with a magnificent medieval square and a rich Jewish heritage.*

- **Country:** Poland
- **Region:** Central Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~800,000
- **Language(s):** Polish
- **Currency:** Polish Złoty (PLN)
- **Main airport:** Kraków John Paul II International Airport (KRK)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Kraków was the capital of Poland for over five centuries and remains its cultural heart. Unlike Warsaw, it survived the Second World War largely intact, preserving an exceptional old town that was among the first UNESCO World Heritage Sites. The city is home to Jagiellonian University, one of the oldest universities in Europe, where Copernicus studied. Kraków combines royal history, a lively student population, affordable prices and vibrant nightlife in historic cellars. It is also the base for visiting Auschwitz-Birkenau and the Wieliczka Salt Mine.

### Top Attractions

- **Main Market Square (Rynek Główny):** One of the largest medieval squares in Europe, with the Cloth Hall and St. Mary's Basilica, where a bugle call sounds every hour.
- **Wawel Castle and Cathedral:** The former royal residence and coronation cathedral on a hill above the Vistula River.
- **Kazimierz:** The historic Jewish quarter with synagogues, cafés, bars and street art.
- **Schindler's Factory:** A museum on Kraków under Nazi occupation, housed in Oskar Schindler's former enamel factory.
- **Rynek Underground:** A museum beneath the Main Square displaying medieval archaeological finds.
- **Planty Park:** A green belt surrounding the old town where the city walls once stood.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Old Town is central and atmospheric but can be noisy. Kazimierz is lively and trendy, with great restaurants and nightlife. Podgórze, across the river, is quieter and home to the former ghetto and Schindler's Factory.

### Food & Drink

Polish classics include pierogi, żurek served in a bread bowl, bigos and kiełbasa. Obwarzanek, a braided bread ring sold from street carts, is Kraków's signature snack. Zapiekanka, a long open-faced baguette with toppings, is popular in Kazimierz's Plac Nowy. Milk bars (bar mleczny) serve cheap, traditional meals.

### When to Visit

Spring and early autumn offer pleasant weather. Summer is busy with tourists and festivals, including the Jewish Culture Festival in late June/early July. Winters are cold, but the Christmas market on the Main Square is charming.

### Getting Around

The old town is walkable, and trams cover the city well. A train connects the airport with the main station in about 20 minutes. Kraków is well connected by train with Warsaw, Wrocław and Gdańsk.

### Day Trips

The Auschwitz-Birkenau Memorial and Museum, about 1.5 hours away, requires advance booking. The Wieliczka Salt Mine features underground chapels carved from salt. Zakopane, in the Tatra Mountains, offers hiking and skiing.

### Practical Tips

- Book Auschwitz and Wieliczka tickets well in advance, especially in summer.
- Beware of overpriced exchange offices in the old town.
- Many museums offer free entry on one day a week.
- Bars and clubs in cellars can be crowded on weekends.

## Kuala Lumpur, Malaysia

*A modern, multicultural capital of twin towers, street food and tropical greenery.*

- **Country:** Malaysia
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~2 million (city); ~8.8 million (Greater KL)
- **Language(s):** Malay (English widely spoken; Chinese and Tamil also common)
- **Currency:** Malaysian Ringgit (MYR)
- **Main airport:** Kuala Lumpur International Airport (KUL)
- **Best time to visit:** May–July and December–February
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Kuala Lumpur, the capital of Malaysia, grew from a tin-mining settlement at the confluence of the Klang and Gombak rivers in the 19th century. Today it is a modern city of skyscrapers, including the iconic Petronas Twin Towers, once the world's tallest buildings. KL is a melting pot of Malay, Chinese and Indian cultures, reflected in its mosques, temples, festivals and food. The city is affordable, with excellent shopping, street food and green spaces. It is a major transport hub for Southeast Asia.

### Top Attractions

- **Petronas Twin Towers:** The 88-storey towers with a skybridge and observation deck; KLCC Park below has fountain shows.
- **Batu Caves:** A Hindu temple complex in limestone caves, reached by 272 colourful steps, with a giant golden statue of Lord Murugan.
- **Merdeka Square:** The site where Malaysian independence was declared, surrounded by colonial buildings.
- **Central Market and Chinatown (Petaling Street):** Markets for crafts, food and souvenirs.
- **KL Tower (Menara KL):** A telecommunications tower with an observation deck and skybox.
- **Islamic Arts Museum Malaysia:** A museum with Islamic art from around the world.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

KLCC is central and upscale, near the Petronas Towers. Bukit Bintang is the shopping and nightlife district, and Chinatown offers budget hotels and street food. Bangsar is trendy, with restaurants and cafés.

### Food & Drink

Nasi lemak, coconut rice with sambal, anchovies and peanuts, is Malaysia's national dish. Street food includes satay, char kway teow, roti canai and laksa. Jalan Alor is famous for night food stalls. Mamak restaurants, run by Indian Muslims, are open late.

### When to Visit

KL is hot and humid year-round, with frequent afternoon rain. Festivals such as Hari Raya, Chinese New Year, Thaipusam and Deepavali are colourful. Malaysia has two monsoon seasons, but rain rarely lasts long in KL.

### Getting Around

KL has an integrated rail network (LRT, MRT, monorail and KTM). The KLIA Ekspres train connects the airport with KL Sentral in about 30 minutes. Grab is widely used and affordable.

### Day Trips

Batu Caves is easy to reach by train. Putrajaya, the administrative capital, has impressive mosques and bridges. Malacca, a UNESCO heritage city, and the Cameron Highlands are longer trips.

### Practical Tips

- Dress modestly at mosques and temples.
- Carry an umbrella for sudden showers.
- Use Grab to avoid taxi overcharging.
- Visit the Petronas Towers skybridge early and book tickets online.

## Kyoto, Japan

*Japan's former imperial capital of temples, gardens and geisha districts.*

- **Country:** Japan
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.4 million
- **Language(s):** Japanese
- **Currency:** Japanese Yen (JPY)
- **Main airport:** Kansai International Airport (KIX), about 75 minutes away; Osaka Itami (ITM)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and October–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Kyoto served as Japan's imperial capital for more than 1,000 years, from 794 until 1868.
