It is the country's cultural heart, with around 1,600 Buddhist temples, 400 Shinto shrines, traditional wooden townhouses and exquisite gardens. Seventeen sites in and around Kyoto are UNESCO World Heritage Sites. The city is famous for its geisha (geiko and maiko), tea ceremonies, kaiseki cuisine and seasonal beauty, especially cherry blossoms and autumn leaves. Kyoto is very popular, so planning helps avoid crowds.

### Top Attractions

- **Fushimi Inari Taisha:** A shrine famous for thousands of vermilion torii gates winding up a mountain; visit early morning.
- **Kinkaku-ji (Golden Pavilion):** A Zen temple covered in gold leaf, reflected in a pond.
- **Kiyomizu-dera:** A wooden temple on a hillside with a large terrace offering views.
- **Arashiyama:** A district known for its bamboo grove, Tenryu-ji temple and monkey park.
- **Gion:** The traditional geisha district with wooden teahouses and lantern-lit streets.
- **Ryoan-ji and Ginkaku-ji:** A famous rock garden and the Silver Pavilion along the Philosopher's Path.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Downtown Kyoto near Shijo and Kawaramachi is convenient for shopping and dining. Gion and Higashiyama are atmospheric, with traditional inns, while the area around Kyoto Station is practical for transport. Staying in a ryokan (traditional inn) is a memorable experience.

### Food & Drink

Kyoto cuisine includes kaiseki (multi-course seasonal dining), yudofu (tofu hot pot), obanzai (Kyoto-style home cooking) and matcha sweets. Nishiki Market, "Kyoto's Kitchen", offers pickles, snacks and street food. Kyoto is also known for sake from the Fushimi district. Vegetarian Buddhist cuisine (shojin ryori) is served at temples.

### When to Visit

Spring cherry blossoms (late March–early April) and autumn leaves (November) are the most beautiful but crowded seasons. Summer is hot and humid, with the Gion Matsuri festival in July. Winter is cold but quiet.

### Getting Around

Kyoto has buses, two subway lines and trains, but buses can be crowded. Walking and cycling are good options. The Shinkansen connects Kyoto with Tokyo in just over two hours.

### Day Trips

Nara, with its deer and giant Buddha, is under an hour away. Osaka is 15–30 minutes by train. Uji is known for matcha tea and the Byodo-in temple, and Himeji Castle is reachable by Shinkansen.

### Practical Tips

- Visit major sights early in the morning.
- Respect geisha and do not photograph them without permission; photography is banned on some private streets in Gion.
- Use an IC card (ICOCA) for transport.
- Book accommodation early for peak seasons.

## La Paz, Bolivia

*The world's highest seat of government, set in a dramatic Andean canyon.*

- **Country:** Bolivia
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~760,000 (city); ~2.3 million with El Alto
- **Language(s):** Spanish, Aymara and Quechua
- **Currency:** Bolivian Boliviano (BOB)
- **Main airport:** El Alto International Airport (LPB)
- **Best time to visit:** May–October (dry season)
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

La Paz is Bolivia's seat of government (Sucre is the constitutional capital) and, at around 3,650 metres, one of the highest cities in the world. It fills a deep canyon, with the city of El Alto on the plateau above and the snow-capped peak of Illimani on the horizon. La Paz has a strong indigenous Aymara culture, visible in markets, festivals and the traditional dress of cholitas. The city is chaotic and fascinating, with steep streets and colourful markets. It is a base for trips to Lake Titicaca, the Death Road and the Uyuni salt flats.

### Top Attractions

- **Mi Teleférico:** The world's largest urban cable car network, offering spectacular views across the city.
- **Witches' Market (Mercado de las Brujas):** Stalls selling herbs, potions and llama foetuses used in Aymara rituals.
- **Plaza Murillo:** The main square with the cathedral and government palace.
- **Calle Jaén:** A well-preserved colonial street with museums.
- **Valle de la Luna:** Eroded clay formations on the edge of the city.
- **Cholita wrestling:** A popular show in El Alto featuring Aymara women wrestlers.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The historic centre near Sagárnaga Street is popular with travellers. Sopocachi is a pleasant area with cafés, and the Zona Sur is lower, warmer and more upscale.

### Food & Drink

Salteñas, juicy baked empanadas, are a morning favourite. Api (a hot purple corn drink) with buñuelos, and sandwiches de chola are street foods. Restaurant Gustu helped launch Bolivia's modern gastronomy.

### When to Visit

The dry season from May to October brings clear skies but cold nights. The rainy season (November–March) affects some roads.

### Getting Around

The cable car network is the best way to get around. Minibuses and taxis are common.

### Day Trips

The Death Road mountain bike ride, Lake Titicaca and Tiwanaku ruins are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Acclimatise to the altitude slowly.
- Choose reputable Death Road operators.
- Carry cash.
- Dress in layers.

## Las Vegas, United States (Nevada)

*The entertainment capital of the world, with casinos, shows and desert adventures.*

- **Country:** United States (Nevada)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~660,000 (city); ~2.3 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Harry Reid International Airport (LAS)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and September–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Las Vegas, in the Mojave Desert of Nevada, is famous for its casinos, resorts, entertainment and nightlife. Founded as a railway town in 1905, it boomed after gambling was legalised in 1931 and the Hoover Dam was built nearby. The Las Vegas Strip is lined with themed mega-resorts replicating Paris, Venice, New York and ancient Egypt. Beyond gambling, Las Vegas offers world-class shows, dining and concerts. It is also a gateway to the Grand Canyon and other national parks.

### Top Attractions

- **The Strip:** A four-mile boulevard of resorts, casinos and attractions.
- **Bellagio Fountains:** A free choreographed water show.
- **Sphere:** A giant spherical venue with immersive concerts and shows.
- **Fremont Street Experience:** Downtown's LED canopy and historic casinos.
- **Shows:** Cirque du Soleil productions, magic shows and residencies by top musicians.
- **High Roller:** One of the world's tallest observation wheels.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Strip is the heart of the action. Downtown is more affordable, with a retro feel.

### Food & Drink

Las Vegas has celebrity-chef restaurants and legendary buffets.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are pleasant. Summers are extremely hot.

### Getting Around

The Monorail, buses and ride-hailing apps are available. Walking the Strip takes longer than it looks.

### Day Trips

The Grand Canyon, Hoover Dam, Red Rock Canyon and Valley of Fire are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Check for resort fees when booking.
- Stay hydrated.
- Set a gambling budget.
- Book shows in advance.

## Lima, Peru

*South America's gastronomic capital, perched on cliffs above the Pacific.*

- **Country:** Peru
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~10 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Peruvian Sol (PEN)
- **Main airport:** Jorge Chávez International Airport (LIM)
- **Best time to visit:** December–April
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Lima, the capital of Peru, was founded by Francisco Pizarro in 1535 as the "City of Kings" and became the centre of Spanish power in South America. Its historic centre is a UNESCO World Heritage Site with colonial churches and balconies. Modern Lima sits on cliffs above the Pacific Ocean, with parks and surf beaches below. The city is renowned for its food, with several restaurants ranked among the world's best. It is the main entry point to Peru.

### Top Attractions

- **Historic Centre:** Plaza Mayor, the cathedral and the Monastery of San Francisco with catacombs.
- **Miraflores and the Malecón:** Clifftop parks and ocean views.
- **Barranco:** A bohemian district with colourful houses and street art.
- **Larco Museum:** Pre-Columbian art, including erotic pottery.
- **Huaca Pucllana:** An adobe pyramid in Miraflores.
- **Magic Water Circuit:** Illuminated fountains.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Miraflores and Barranco are the most popular areas.

### Food & Drink

Ceviche, lomo saltado, ají de gallina and causa are classics. Pisco sours are the national cocktail. Restaurants like Central and Maido are world-famous.

### When to Visit

Summer (December–April) is sunny. Winter brings grey skies (garúa).

### Getting Around

Taxis and ride-hailing apps are common. The Metropolitano bus runs through the city.

### Day Trips

Pachacamac ruins and the Palomino Islands (sea lions) are nearby.

### Practical Tips

- Book top restaurants well in advance.
- Use apps for taxis.
- Watch belongings.
- Try ceviche at lunch.

## Lisbon, Portugal

*A sun-drenched city of seven hills, historic trams, tiled facades and fado.*

- **Country:** Portugal
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~550,000 (city); ~2.9 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Portuguese
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Humberto Delgado Airport (LIS)
- **Best time to visit:** March–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Lisbon, the capital of Portugal, is one of Europe's oldest cities, set on hills overlooking the wide Tagus River near the Atlantic. It was the starting point for the great Portuguese voyages of discovery in the 15th and 16th centuries. After a devastating earthquake in 1755, much of the centre was rebuilt in an elegant grid, while older districts kept their medieval layout. Lisbon has become one of Europe's most popular destinations thanks to its mild climate, friendly atmosphere, food and nightlife. Its miradouros (viewpoints), azulejo tiles and tram 28 are iconic.

### Top Attractions

- **Belém Tower and Jerónimos Monastery:** Masterpieces of Manueline architecture linked to the Age of Discovery, both UNESCO-listed.
- **Alfama:** Lisbon's oldest neighbourhood, with narrow lanes, laundry-hung balconies and fado houses.
- **São Jorge Castle:** A Moorish castle on a hilltop with panoramic city views.
- **Tram 28:** A historic yellow tram rattling through Graça, Alfama and Baixa; it is often very crowded.
- **Praça do Comércio and Baixa:** A grand riverside square leading to the elegant downtown grid.
- **LX Factory and MAAT:** A creative hub in a former industrial complex and a modern art and technology museum.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Baixa and Chiado are central and convenient. Bairro Alto is known for nightlife, and Príncipe Real is chic, with boutiques and gardens. Alfama and Graça offer traditional atmosphere, while Cais do Sodré is lively and close to the Time Out Market.

### Food & Drink

Pastel de nata, a custard tart, is a must; the famous version in Belém is called pastel de Belém. Bacalhau (salted cod) is prepared in many ways, and grilled sardines are a summer classic. Bifanas (pork sandwiches), seafood and ginjinha, a cherry liqueur, are popular. Portuguese wines such as vinho verde are excellent value.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal, with warm weather and fewer crowds. Summers are hot and busy; June brings the Santo António festival with street parties and grilled sardines. Winters are mild but rainy.

### Getting Around

Lisbon has a metro, trams, buses, funiculars and ferries; the Viva Viagem card works on all of them. The metro connects the airport to the centre in about 25 minutes. The hills are steep, so comfortable shoes are essential.

### Day Trips

Sintra, with fairy-tale palaces such as Pena Palace and Quinta da Regaleira, is 40 minutes by train. Cascais and Estoril offer beaches and seaside towns. Óbidos, a walled medieval village, and Évora, with a Roman temple, are also popular.

### Practical Tips

- Wear shoes with good grip; the limestone pavements are slippery.
- Board Tram 28 at its terminus (Martim Moniz) or take it early to avoid queues.
- Watch out for pickpockets on trams.
- Restaurants may place appetisers (couvert) on the table; they are not free, so decline if you don't want them.

## Ljubljana, Slovenia

*A small, green, car-free capital full of riverside cafés and dragons.*

- **Country:** Slovenia
- **Region:** Central Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~295,000
- **Language(s):** Slovenian
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Ljubljana Jože Pučnik Airport (LJU)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Ljubljana, the capital of Slovenia, is a charming, compact city on the Ljubljanica River. Its old town combines Baroque buildings, Art Nouveau facades and the distinctive work of architect Jože Plečnik, whose designs are UNESCO-listed. The city centre is largely car-free, and it was named European Green Capital in 2016. With a large student population, Ljubljana has a lively café culture and a relaxed pace. It is an ideal base for exploring Lake Bled, the Julian Alps and Slovenia's caves.

### Top Attractions

- **Prešeren Square and Triple Bridge:** The heart of the city, with a pink Franciscan church and Plečnik's three bridges.
- **Ljubljana Castle:** A hilltop castle reached by funicular, with views to the Alps.
- **Dragon Bridge:** Decorated with bronze dragons, the symbol of the city.
- **Central Market:** Plečnik's riverside market colonnade, with open-air stalls and Open Kitchen street-food Fridays in warmer months.
- **Tivoli Park:** A large park stretching from the city centre to wooded hills.
- **Metelkova:** An autonomous cultural centre in former army barracks, covered with street art.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Most visitors stay in the old town or around the Ljubljanica riverbanks. Trnovo is quiet and green, with a village feel. The area near the train station is practical and a little cheaper.

### Food & Drink

Slovenian cuisine mixes Alpine, Mediterranean and Balkan influences. Try kranjska klobasa (Carniolan sausage), štruklji (rolled dumplings), jota (bean and sauerkraut soup) and potica, a rolled nut cake. Slovenia produces excellent wines, including orange wines. Riverside cafés are perfect for coffee or a glass of wine.

### When to Visit

Late spring and summer are best, with outdoor festivals and riverside life. Autumn is colourful and pleasant. December brings festive lights and markets.

### Getting Around

The centre is walkable and bike-friendly, with the BicikeLJ bike-share. Electric shuttles (Kavalir) carry people around the car-free zone. The airport is about 25 km away, reached by bus or shuttle.

### Day Trips

Lake Bled, with its island church and cliffside castle, is under an hour away, and Lake Bohinj is quieter and wilder. Postojna Cave and Škocjan Caves are spectacular underground systems, and Predjama Castle is built into a cliff. Piran, on the Adriatic coast, is a Venetian-style seaside town.

### Practical Tips

- Walk or cycle; cars are not needed in the centre.
- Book Postojna Cave in summer to guarantee your preferred time.
- Tap water is excellent and free-flowing fountains are common.
- Slovenia uses a vignette system on motorways if you rent a car.

## London, United Kingdom (England)

*A global capital of history, culture, theatre and diversity.*

- **Country:** United Kingdom (England)
- **Region:** Northern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~8.9 million (Greater London); ~14 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** Pound Sterling (GBP)
- **Main airport:** Heathrow (LHR), Gatwick (LGW), Stansted (STN), Luton (LTN), London City (LCY)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

London, the capital of the United Kingdom, is one of the world's great cities, with a history stretching back to Roman Londinium. It was the centre of the British Empire and remains a global hub of finance, culture, fashion and education. London is enormously diverse, with hundreds of languages spoken and neighbourhoods representing cultures from around the world. Many of its world-class museums are free. From royal palaces and ancient pubs to cutting-edge architecture and West End theatre, London offers endless options.

### Top Attractions

- **Tower of London:** A historic fortress, royal palace and prison housing the Crown Jewels and guarded by the Beefeaters.
- **British Museum:** A free museum with treasures such as the Rosetta Stone, Egyptian mummies and the Parthenon sculptures.
- **Westminster Abbey, Houses of Parliament and Big Ben:** The political and ceremonial heart of the country.
- **Buckingham Palace:** The monarch's London residence, with the Changing of the Guard ceremony and summer tours of the State Rooms.
- **Tate Modern and the South Bank:** A modern art museum in a former power station, along a riverside walk with Shakespeare's Globe and the London Eye.
- **Natural History Museum and V&A:** Free museums in South Kensington covering dinosaurs, design and decorative arts.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Westminster and the West End are central, close to theatres and shopping. Covent Garden and Soho are lively, while Kensington and Chelsea are elegant. Shoreditch and Hackney are trendy with street art and nightlife, and areas like Southwark and Borough are great for food and river walks.

### Food & Drink

London's food scene is one of the world's most diverse. Try a traditional Sunday roast, fish and chips, or a full English breakfast, and visit markets like Borough Market, Camden Market and Maltby Street. Brick Lane is famous for curry and bagels, and Chinatown for dim sum. Afternoon tea in a hotel is a classic experience.

### When to Visit

Late spring and summer offer the best weather and long evenings, with events such as Trooping the Colour and Wimbledon. Autumn and winter are cooler and rainier, but Christmas lights and markets add charm. Rain is possible any time of year.

### Getting Around

The Underground (Tube), Overground, Elizabeth line, buses and DLR cover the city, and contactless cards cap daily fares. The Elizabeth line and Heathrow Express connect Heathrow with central London. Walking between central sights is often faster than the Tube.

### Day Trips

Windsor Castle, Oxford, Cambridge, Bath and Stonehenge are popular day trips. Brighton offers a seaside escape, and the Warner Bros. Studio Tour – The Making of Harry Potter is north of London. Canterbury and Dover's white cliffs are to the southeast.

### Practical Tips

- Use contactless payment instead of buying paper tickets.
- Stand on the right on escalators.
- Many major museums are free; donations are welcome.
- Book West End shows in advance or look for same-day discount tickets at the TKTS booth in Leicester Square.

## Los Angeles, United States (California)

*The entertainment capital of the world, with beaches, Hollywood and diverse neighbourhoods.*

- **Country:** United States (California)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~3.8 million (city); ~13 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English (Spanish widely spoken)
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Los Angeles International Airport (LAX)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and September–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Los Angeles is the second-largest city in the United States, sprawling across a basin between mountains and the Pacific Ocean. It is the global centre of the film and television industry, with Hollywood as its symbol. LA is incredibly diverse, with large Latino, Asian and other communities, reflected in its food and neighbourhoods. The city offers beaches, museums, hiking and a sunny climate. It will host the 2028 Summer Olympics.

### Top Attractions

- **Hollywood:** The Walk of Fame, TCL Chinese Theatre and Hollywood Sign.
- **Griffith Observatory:** Views of the city and the Hollywood Sign.
- **Santa Monica and Venice Beach:** The pier, boardwalk and beach culture.
- **Getty Center:** An art museum with gardens and views.
- **Universal Studios Hollywood:** A film studio and theme park.
- **LACMA:** The largest art museum in the western US.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Santa Monica, West Hollywood, Silver Lake and Downtown LA are popular bases.

### Food & Drink

LA is famous for tacos, Korean BBQ, In-N-Out Burger and diverse cuisines.

### When to Visit

The weather is pleasant year-round. Summer can be hot inland.

### Getting Around

LA is car-oriented. The Metro rail system is expanding.

### Day Trips

Malibu, Disneyland, Joshua Tree and Santa Barbara are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Allow time for traffic.
- Check wildfire conditions.
- Use sun protection.
- Book theme parks in advance.

## Luang Prabang, Laos

*A tranquil UNESCO town of golden temples, saffron-robed monks and Mekong sunsets.*

- **Country:** Laos
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~60,000 (town)
- **Language(s):** Lao
- **Currency:** Lao Kip (LAK)
- **Main airport:** Luang Prabang International Airport (LPQ)
- **Best time to visit:** November–March
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Luang Prabang, located at the confluence of the Mekong and Nam Khan rivers in northern Laos, was the royal capital of the Lao kingdom. Its old town, a UNESCO World Heritage Site, is a charming blend of Buddhist temples and French colonial buildings. The town is peaceful, with a slow pace of life and dozens of monasteries. Every morning, monks collect alms along the streets in a centuries-old tradition. The Laos–China Railway has made the town much easier to reach.

### Top Attractions

- **Wat Xieng Thong:** The most magnificent temple in Luang Prabang, with sweeping roofs and mosaics.
- **Mount Phousi:** A hill with a golden stupa and sunset views over the town and rivers.
- **Alms-giving ceremony (Tak Bat):** Monks walk silently at dawn to receive rice from locals.
- **Royal Palace Museum:** The former royal residence, including the Prabang Buddha statue.
- **Kuang Si Falls:** Turquoise waterfall pools about 30 km from town, with a bear rescue centre.
- **Night Market:** A market selling handicrafts and textiles.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The old town peninsula is the best place to stay, with guesthouses and boutique hotels. Riverside accommodation offers views of the Mekong.

### Food & Drink

Lao food includes larb (minced meat salad), sticky rice, khao soi (a local noodle soup different from the Thai version) and Mekong river weed (khai paen). The night market food alley offers cheap buffets. French bakeries serve baguettes and coffee.

### When to Visit

November to March is dry and pleasant. The hot season follows until May, and the rainy season (June–October) makes the landscape lush. Lao New Year (Pi Mai) in April is celebrated with water fights.

### Getting Around

The town is walkable, and bicycles are available. Tuk-tuks take visitors to sights outside town. Trains connect Luang Prabang with Vientiane and Kunming in China.

### Day Trips

Kuang Si Falls and Tad Sae Falls are popular. The Pak Ou Caves contain thousands of Buddha statues and are reached by boat. Slow boats travel to Huay Xai on the Thai border over two days.

### Practical Tips

- Observe the alms-giving respectfully and quietly; don't use flash or block the monks.
- Dress modestly in temples.
- Carry cash; ATMs can be unreliable.
- Book train tickets in advance.

## Lyon, France

*France's gastronomic capital, with Roman ruins, Renaissance lanes and secret passageways.*

- **Country:** France
- **Region:** Western Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~520,000 (city); ~2.3 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** French
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Lyon–Saint-Exupéry Airport (LYS)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October; December for the Festival of Lights
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Lyon is France's third-largest city, set at the confluence of the Rhône and Saône rivers. Founded by the Romans as Lugdunum, it became the capital of Gaul and later a centre of silk weaving and banking. Its historic centre, including the Renaissance Vieux Lyon, is a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Lyon is widely considered the gastronomic capital of France, with legendary chefs like Paul Bocuse and traditional bouchons. It is less touristy than Paris and has a relaxed, sophisticated atmosphere.

### Top Attractions

- **Vieux Lyon:** One of Europe's largest Renaissance districts, with cobbled streets and hidden passageways called traboules.
- **Basilica of Notre-Dame de Fourvière:** A lavish 19th-century basilica on a hill with views over the city and, on clear days, Mont Blanc.
- **Roman theatres of Fourvière:** Ancient amphitheatres still used for summer concerts, next to the Lugdunum museum.
- **Presqu'île:** The peninsula between the rivers, with grand squares such as Place Bellecour and Place des Terreaux.
- **Musée des Confluences:** A futuristic museum of science and anthropology at the meeting point of the rivers.
- **Croix-Rousse:** The former silk workers' hill, with traboules, murals and a village feel.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Presqu'île is central and ideal for first-time visitors. Vieux Lyon is atmospheric, while Croix-Rousse is bohemian and local. The Confluence district is modern, and Part-Dieu is practical for train travel.

### Food & Drink

Bouchons are traditional Lyonnais restaurants serving hearty dishes like quenelles (fish dumplings in sauce), andouillette, salade lyonnaise and saucisson. Les Halles de Lyon Paul Bocuse is a gourmet market. Praline tart (tarte aux pralines) is a local pink dessert. Wines from nearby Beaujolais and the Côtes du Rhône are served everywhere.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal. Summers are warm and lively with outdoor festivals such as Les Nuits de Fourvière. The Fête des Lumières (Festival of Lights) in early December transforms the city with light installations and attracts millions of visitors.

### Getting Around

Lyon has a metro, trams, buses and funiculars up Fourvière hill. The Rhônexpress tram connects the airport with Part-Dieu station in about 30 minutes. TGV trains reach Paris in about two hours.

### Day Trips

The Beaujolais wine region with its golden-stone villages is just north of the city. Pérouges, a medieval hilltop village, is under an hour away. Annecy and the Alps, and Vienne with Roman ruins, are also popular.

### Practical Tips

- Book popular bouchons in advance, especially on weekends.
- Look for the "Authentique Bouchon Lyonnais" label.
- Book hotels months ahead for the Festival of Lights.
- The Lyon City Card includes transport and museum entry.

## Macau, China (Macau SAR)

*A blend of Portuguese heritage, Chinese culture and glittering casinos.*

- **Country:** China (Macau SAR)
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~690,000
- **Language(s):** Cantonese and Portuguese (Mandarin and English widely used)
- **Currency:** Macanese Pataca (MOP); Hong Kong Dollars widely accepted
- **Main airport:** Macau International Airport (MFM)
- **Best time to visit:** October–December
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Macau, a Special Administrative Region of China, was a Portuguese colony for over 400 years until 1999. Its historic centre, a UNESCO World Heritage Site, features Portuguese churches, cobbled squares and Chinese temples. Macau is also the world's largest gambling centre, with huge casino resorts on the Cotai Strip. The territory's unique Macanese culture combines Portuguese and Chinese influences. It is densely populated and one of the world's wealthiest places per capita.

### Top Attractions

- **Ruins of St. Paul's:** The facade of a 17th-century church, Macau's most famous landmark.
- **Senado Square:** A square with wave-patterned Portuguese tiles and colonial buildings.
- **A-Ma Temple:** One of Macau's oldest temples, dedicated to the goddess of seafarers.
- **Macau Tower:** An observation tower with the world's highest commercial bungee jump.
- **Cotai Strip:** Casino resorts such as The Venetian and Galaxy Macau, with shows and shopping.
- **Taipa Village:** Colonial houses, restaurants and bakeries.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The historic centre is best for culture. Cotai has large resorts, and Taipa and Coloane are quieter.

### Food & Drink

Macanese cuisine includes minchi (minced meat with potatoes), African chicken and Portuguese egg tarts. Pork chop buns and almond cookies are popular snacks. Portuguese restaurants serve bacalhau and wine.

### When to Visit

October to December has pleasant weather. The Macau Grand Prix is held in November. Summers are hot, humid and the typhoon season.

### Getting Around

Free casino shuttle buses run between the ferry terminals and resorts. Macau has a light rail system. Ferries and the Hong Kong–Zhuhai–Macau Bridge connect Macau with Hong Kong.

### Day Trips

Coloane Village is a quiet escape. Zhuhai in mainland China is across the border.

### Practical Tips

- Use free casino shuttles for transport.
- Carry some patacas; change is often given in local currency.
- Hong Kong dollars are accepted nearly everywhere.
- Check visa rules for mainland China.

## Madrid, Spain

*Spain's energetic capital of art museums, royal grandeur and late nights.*

- **Country:** Spain
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~3.4 million (city); ~6.8 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Adolfo Suárez Madrid–Barajas Airport (MAD)
- **Best time to visit:** March–June and September–November
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Madrid, the capital of Spain, lies on a high central plateau and is one of Europe's liveliest cities. It became the royal capital in 1561 and grew into a city of grand boulevards, palaces and plazas. Madrid is home to one of the world's great concentrations of art, with the Prado, Reina Sofía and Thyssen-Bornemisza museums forming the "Golden Triangle of Art". The city is famous for its nightlife, tapas culture and passionate football. Madrileños eat late, stay out late and enjoy life on the streets.

### Top Attractions

- **Prado Museum:** One of the world's finest art museums, with masterpieces by Velázquez, Goya, El Greco, Bosch and Titian.
- **Reina Sofía Museum:** Modern and contemporary art, including Picasso's Guernica.
- **Royal Palace:** The official residence of the Spanish royal family, one of Europe's largest palaces, open for visits.
- **Retiro Park:** A large park with a boating lake, the Crystal Palace and gardens, a UNESCO site together with the Paseo del Prado.
- **Plaza Mayor and Puerta del Sol:** Historic squares in the heart of the city.
- **Santiago Bernabéu Stadium:** Home of Real Madrid, with stadium tours and a museum.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Sol and the Barrio de las Letras are central and close to museums. Malasaña and Chueca are trendy and lively, with bars and boutiques; Chueca is also the LGBTQ+ hub. La Latina is famous for tapas and the Sunday El Rastro flea market, while Salamanca is upscale and elegant.

### Food & Drink

Madrid's classics include cocido madrileño (a chickpea and meat stew), bocadillo de calamares (fried squid sandwich) and churros with thick hot chocolate, famously served at Chocolatería San Ginés. Tapas-hopping along Cava Baja in La Latina is a local tradition. Mercado de San Miguel is a popular gourmet market. Vermouth and caña (small beer) are common drinks.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are pleasant and lively. July and August are very hot, and many locals leave the city. Winters are cold but sunny. The San Isidro festival in May celebrates the city's patron saint.

### Getting Around

Madrid has one of Europe's most extensive metro systems, and the airport is connected by metro and express buses. The centre is walkable. High-speed AVE trains connect Madrid with Barcelona, Seville, Valencia and other cities.

### Day Trips

Toledo, a medieval city of Christian, Muslim and Jewish heritage, is about 30 minutes away by high-speed train. Segovia has a Roman aqueduct and a fairy-tale Alcázar. El Escorial monastery and Ávila's medieval walls are also popular.

### Practical Tips

- The Prado and Reina Sofía offer free entry during the last hours of the day, but lines can be long.
- Dinner is typically served from 9 pm onward.
- Watch for pickpockets in Sol and on the metro.
- Many shops close for a few hours in the afternoon outside central areas.

## Málaga, Spain

*Picasso's sunny birthplace and the gateway to the Costa del Sol.*

- **Country:** Spain
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~590,000
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Málaga–Costa del Sol Airport (AGP)
- **Best time to visit:** March–June and September–November
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Málaga is a port city on Spain's Costa del Sol and one of the oldest cities in Europe, founded by the Phoenicians about 3,000 years ago. Once seen mainly as an airport for beach resorts, it has reinvented itself as a cultural destination with over 30 museums. Pablo Picasso was born here, and the city now hosts the Picasso Museum and a branch of the Pompidou Centre. Málaga enjoys around 300 days of sunshine a year, urban beaches and a lively old town. It is also a great base for exploring Andalusia.

### Top Attractions

- **Alcazaba:** An 11th-century Moorish fortress-palace with gardens and fountains overlooking the city.
- **Gibralfaro Castle:** A hilltop castle connected to the Alcazaba, offering the best views of Málaga and its bullring.
- **Picasso Museum Málaga:** Over 200 works by the artist, housed in a Renaissance palace.
- **Málaga Cathedral:** Nicknamed "La Manquita" (the one-armed lady) because its second tower was never completed.
- **Roman Theatre:** Remains of a 1st-century BC theatre at the foot of the Alcazaba.
- **Muelle Uno and Malagueta Beach:** A modern waterfront promenade and the city's main beach.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The historic centre is walkable and full of bars, but can be noisy. Soho is an arts district with murals, and La Malagueta is ideal for beach lovers. El Palo, further east, is a former fishing village known for seafood restaurants.

### Food & Drink

Try espetos, sardines grilled on skewers over open fires on the beach. Fried fish (pescaíto frito), gazpacho and ajoblanco (a cold almond and garlic soup) are local favourites. The Atarazanas Market is a colourful food hall. Sweet Málaga wine is served at historic bars like Antigua Casa de Guardia.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal, and winters are mild enough for sightseeing. Summer is hot and busy on the beaches. Holy Week (Semana Santa) processions are among Spain's most impressive, and the Feria de Málaga in August is a massive street party.

### Getting Around

The centre is walkable, and a train connects the airport with the city centre in about 12 minutes. Buses and a metro serve the wider area. High-speed trains reach Madrid in under three hours.

### Day Trips

Ronda, a dramatic town on a gorge, and the Caminito del Rey, a spectacular cliffside walkway, are popular trips. Granada, Córdoba and Seville are reachable by train or bus. White villages such as Frigiliana and Mijas and beach towns like Nerja are close.

### Practical Tips

- Book tickets for the Caminito del Rey well in advance.
- Many museums are free on Sunday afternoons.
- Try a "mitad" coffee, a local way of ordering coffee with milk.
- Book accommodation early for Holy Week and August.

## Manaus, Brazil

*A city in the heart of the Amazon rainforest, with a famous opera house.*

- **Country:** Brazil
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~2.1 million
- **Language(s):** Portuguese
- **Currency:** Brazilian Real (BRL)
- **Main airport:** Eduardo Gomes International Airport (MAO)
- **Best time to visit:** June–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Manaus, the capital of Amazonas state, lies near the confluence of the Rio Negro and the Solimões (Amazon) River. During the rubber boom of the late 19th century, it became one of the richest cities in Brazil. The Teatro Amazonas opera house symbolises that era. Today Manaus is the main gateway to the Amazon rainforest. It is a busy, humid city.

### Top Attractions

- **Teatro Amazonas:** A grand opera house built with European materials.
- **Meeting of the Waters:** The dark Rio Negro and sandy Solimões flow side by side.
- **Municipal Market Adolpho Lisboa:** A market inspired by Les Halles in Paris.
- **Amazon lodges:** Jungle lodges for wildlife and canoe trips.
- **MUSA:** A botanical garden with a tower.
- **Ponta Negra Beach:** A river beach.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The historic centre and Ponta Negra are popular.

### Food & Drink

Amazonian fish like tambaqui and pirarucu, açaí and tacacá are local specialities.

### When to Visit

June to November is drier, while the high-water season offers flooded forests.

### Getting Around

Taxis and apps are common. Boats connect with river towns.

### Day Trips

Jungle lodges, Anavilhanas Archipelago and river tours.

### Practical Tips

- Use insect repellent.
- Check vaccination requirements (yellow fever).
- Book lodges in advance.
- Stay hydrated.

## Manchester, United Kingdom (England)

*The birthplace of the Industrial Revolution and a powerhouse of football and music.*

- **Country:** United Kingdom (England)
- **Region:** Northern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~570,000 (city); ~2.9 million (Greater Manchester)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** Pound Sterling (GBP)
- **Main airport:** Manchester Airport (MAN)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Manchester is a major city in northwest England, often described as the world's first industrial city thanks to its role in the cotton trade in the 19th century. Its red-brick mills and warehouses have been transformed into apartments, bars and cultural spaces. Manchester is famous for football, with Manchester United and Manchester City, and for music, from Joy Division and The Smiths to Oasis. It has a progressive, independent spirit and a vibrant student population. The city is a good base for exploring northern England.

### Top Attractions

- **Old Trafford and Etihad Stadium:** Stadium tours and museums for Manchester United and Manchester City.
- **Science and Industry Museum:** Tells the story of Manchester's industrial past, located on the site of the world's oldest surviving passenger railway station.
- **John Rylands Library:** A stunning neo-Gothic library with rare books and manuscripts.
- **Manchester Museum and Whitworth Art Gallery:** University museums of natural history, archaeology and art.
- **Northern Quarter:** A creative district of independent shops, street art, record stores and bars.
- **National Football Museum:** An interactive museum dedicated to the history of football.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The city centre and Northern Quarter are lively and convenient. Ancoats is a trendy former mill district with excellent restaurants. Spinningfields is modern and business-oriented, while Didsbury and Chorlton are leafy suburbs with cafés.

### Food & Drink

Manchester's food scene has grown impressively, with food halls like Mackie Mayor and Hatch. The Curry Mile in Rusholme is famous for South Asian cuisine. Traditional options include Eccles cakes, Manchester tart and pub classics. The city has a thriving craft beer scene.

### When to Visit

Late spring and summer offer the best weather, though rain is frequent year-round. The Manchester International Festival is held in summer in odd-numbered years, and football season runs from August to May. Christmas markets are popular in December.

### Getting Around

Manchester has an extensive Metrolink tram network and free city-centre buses on some routes. Trains connect the airport with Piccadilly station in about 15–20 minutes. London is about two hours away by train.

### Day Trips

The Peak District National Park offers hiking and villages such as Castleton. Liverpool, home of the Beatles, is less than an hour away by train. The Lake District and York are also reachable.

### Practical Tips

- Book stadium tours in advance, especially on match weeks.
- Bring a raincoat or umbrella.
- Many museums are free.
- Check football fixtures; hotel prices rise on match days.

## Manila, Philippines

*A vibrant, chaotic capital of Spanish colonial heritage, malls and warm hospitality.*

- **Country:** Philippines
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.8 million (city); ~14 million (Metro Manila)
- **Language(s):** Filipino and English
- **Currency:** Philippine Peso (PHP)
- **Main airport:** Ninoy Aquino International Airport (MNL)
- **Best time to visit:** December–April
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Manila, the capital of the Philippines, lies on Manila Bay on the island of Luzon. Founded as a Spanish colonial city in 1571, it became a key hub of the Manila galleon trade between Asia and the Americas. The city was heavily damaged during the Second World War, but its walled historic district, Intramuros, retains Spanish-era churches and fortifications. Metro Manila is a huge, congested megacity made up of several cities, including Makati, Taguig and Quezon City. Filipinos are known for their warmth, humour and love of karaoke, and English is widely spoken.

### Top Attractions

- **Intramuros:** The walled Spanish city, with Fort Santiago, cobbled streets and restored colonial houses; explore by bike or horse-drawn kalesa.
- **San Agustin Church:** The oldest stone church in the Philippines and a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
- **Rizal Park (Luneta):** A large park with a monument to national hero José Rizal.
- **National Museum of the Philippines:** Free museums of fine arts, anthropology and natural history.
- **Binondo:** The world's oldest Chinatown, famous for street food tours.
- **Manila Bay sunset:** The bay is famous for its sunsets, viewed from the Baywalk.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Makati is the business district, with hotels, malls and nightlife, while Bonifacio Global City (BGC) in Taguig is modern and walkable. Ermita and Malate are near historic sites but grittier. Pasay has casino resorts near the airport.

### Food & Drink

Filipino cuisine includes adobo (meat braised in vinegar and soy sauce), sinigang (sour soup), lechon and kare-kare (oxtail in peanut sauce). Halo-halo, a colourful shaved ice dessert, is perfect in the heat. Binondo offers dumplings and Chinese-Filipino specialities. Jollibee, the local fast-food chain, is a national favourite.

### When to Visit

The dry season from December to April is best. The rainy season (June–November) brings typhoons and flooding. Christmas celebrations in the Philippines start as early as September.

### Getting Around

Traffic is notoriously heavy. The LRT and MRT rail lines help, and Grab is widely used. Jeepneys and tricycles offer a local experience.

### Day Trips

Tagaytay, on a ridge overlooking Taal Volcano, offers cooler air. Corregidor Island, a Second World War site, is reached by ferry. Las Casas Filipinas de Acuzar is a heritage resort of restored colonial houses.

### Practical Tips

- Allow extra time for traffic, especially to the airport.
- Use Grab rather than street taxis.
- Watch your belongings in crowded areas.
- Check typhoon forecasts during the rainy season.

## Marseille, France

*France's oldest city, a gritty, sun-soaked Mediterranean port with dramatic calanques.*

- **Country:** France
- **Region:** Western Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~870,000 (city); ~1.9 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** French
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Marseille Provence Airport (MRS)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Marseille was founded by Greek sailors around 600 BC, making it France's oldest city. It is the country's second-largest city and its main Mediterranean port, a multicultural crossroads with strong North African and Mediterranean influences. The city has an authentic, sometimes rough-edged character, very different from the glamour of the Riviera. Its regeneration in recent years, including new museums and waterfront areas, has made it a vibrant cultural destination. Marseille is also the gateway to the stunning Calanques National Park.

### Top Attractions

- **Vieux-Port (Old Port):** The historic harbour lined with cafés, fish stalls and boats, the centre of city life.
- **Notre-Dame de la Garde:** A basilica on a hill offering panoramic views; its golden Virgin statue watches over the city.
- **MuCEM:** The Museum of European and Mediterranean Civilisations, connected by a footbridge to Fort Saint-Jean.
- **Le Panier:** The oldest neighbourhood, with narrow streets, colourful houses and artisan shops.
- **Calanques National Park:** Limestone cliffs and turquoise coves reached by boat or hiking trails.
- **Château d'If:** The island fortress made famous by Alexandre Dumas's The Count of Monte Cristo.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Vieux-Port area is central and convenient. Le Panier is charming, while Cours Julien is bohemian, with street art and bars. The Corniche and Vallon des Auffes offer seaside views, and the southern districts give access to beaches.

### Food & Drink

Bouillabaisse, a rich fish stew, is Marseille's most famous dish, traditionally served in two courses; authentic versions are expensive. Panisse (chickpea fritters), navettes (boat-shaped biscuits) and pastis, an anise-flavoured aperitif, are local favourites. The Noailles market offers North African and Mediterranean produce.

### When to Visit

Late spring and early autumn offer warm weather ideal for hiking and swimming. Summer is hot, and access to the Calanques may be restricted due to fire risk. The strong mistral wind can blow at any time of year.

### Getting Around

Marseille has a metro, trams and buses. The airport is linked to Saint-Charles station by shuttle bus. TGV trains reach Paris in about three hours.

### Day Trips

Cassis, a picturesque fishing port, is a gateway to the Calanques. Aix-en-Provence, with its fountains and markets, is about 30 minutes away. Avignon, Arles and the Luberon villages are also popular.

### Practical Tips

- Check Calanques access conditions in summer; some trails close on high-risk days, and reservations are required for Sugiton in peak season.
- Keep valuables secure in crowded areas.
- For bouillabaisse, choose restaurants that follow the traditional charter.
- Wear sturdy shoes for coastal hikes.

## Medellín, Colombia

*The "City of Eternal Spring", famed for innovation, flowers and mountain views.*

- **Country:** Colombia
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~2.6 million (city); ~4 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Colombian Peso (COP)
- **Main airport:** José María Córdova International Airport (MDE)
- **Best time to visit:** December–March and June–August
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Medellín, the capital of Antioquia, lies in the Aburrá Valley surrounded by green mountains. Once notorious as the home of Pablo Escobar's cartel in the 1980s and 1990s, the city has transformed itself through urban innovation, including cable cars and escalators connecting hillside neighbourhoods. Its mild climate earns it the nickname "City of Eternal Spring". Medellín is now a popular destination for travellers and digital nomads. Paisas, as locals are called, are known for their friendliness and entrepreneurial spirit.

### Top Attractions

- **Comuna 13:** A once-dangerous neighbourhood transformed by outdoor escalators, street art and hip-hop; best visited with a local guide.
- **Metrocable:** Cable cars integrated into the metro, offering views and access to Parque Arví.
- **Plaza Botero:** A square with 23 bronze sculptures by Fernando Botero, next to the Museum of Antioquia.
- **Parque Arví:** A nature reserve with trails and markets above the city.
- **Pueblito Paisa:** A replica of a traditional Antioquian village on Cerro Nutibara.
- **Museo Casa de la Memoria:** A museum about the region's armed conflict.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

El Poblado is upscale, with hotels and nightlife, while Laureles is more local and relaxed. Envigado is residential.

### Food & Drink

Bandeja paisa, a hearty platter of beans, rice, meat, chicharrón, egg, plantain and arepa, is the regional dish. Arepas, empanadas and fresh juices are everywhere. Coffee from the region is excellent.

### When to Visit

The weather is mild year-round. The Flower Festival (Feria de las Flores) in August is a highlight.

### Getting Around

The metro, tram and cable cars are efficient. Taxis and ride-hailing apps are common.

### Day Trips

Guatapé, with the Rock of Guatapé (El Peñol) and colourful town, is a popular trip. Coffee farms and Santa Fe de Antioquia are nearby.

### Practical Tips

- Avoid glorifying Pablo Escobar; locals find narco-tourism offensive.
- Use ride-hailing apps.
- Be cautious with dating apps and drinks; scams targeting foreigners occur.
- Keep valuables hidden.

## Mendoza, Argentina

*Argentina's wine capital at the foot of the Andes and Aconcagua.*

- **Country:** Argentina
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~120,000 (city); ~1.1 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Argentine Peso (ARS)
- **Main airport:** Governor Francisco Gabrielli International Airport (MDZ)
- **Best time to visit:** March–April (harvest) and October–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Mendoza, in western Argentina, is the heart of the country's wine industry, producing most of its Malbec. The city was rebuilt after an earthquake in 1861 with wide streets, plazas and irrigation channels (acequias) that keep trees green in the dry climate. Mendoza is surrounded by vineyards with the Andes as a backdrop. It is a base for wine tours, adventure sports and trips to Aconcagua, the highest mountain outside Asia. The city has a relaxed, sunny atmosphere.

### Top Attractions

- **Wineries in Luján de Cuyo, Maipú and the Uco Valley:** Tastings and vineyard lunches.
- **Plaza Independencia:** The main square.
- **Parque General San Martín:** A large park with a lake and hill views.
- **Cerro de la Gloria:** A monument to the Army of the Andes.
- **Aconcagua Provincial Park:** Views of the 6,961-metre peak.
- **Potrerillos:** A reservoir for rafting and kayaking.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The city centre and Chacras de Coria are popular.

### Food & Drink

Asado (barbecue) and empanadas pair perfectly with Malbec. Many wineries offer gourmet lunches.

### When to Visit

The harvest festival (Vendimia) takes place in March. Spring and autumn are ideal.

### Getting Around

Buses and taxis are available. Bikes are popular in Maipú for winery tours.

### Day Trips

Uco Valley, Aconcagua and the Andes road to Chile are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Book winery visits in advance.
- Don't drink and drive; hire a driver or tour.
- Carry cash.
- Protect yourself from the sun.

## Mexico City, Mexico

*A vast, vibrant capital of Aztec ruins, murals, museums and street food.*

- **Country:** Mexico
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~9.2 million (city); ~22 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Mexican Peso (MXN)
- **Main airport:** Benito Juárez International Airport (MEX); Felipe Ángeles International (NLU)
- **Best time to visit:** October–May
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Mexico City (Ciudad de México, CDMX) is built on the ruins of Tenochtitlan, the Aztec capital, which stood on an island in a lake. It is one of the largest cities in the world and the political, cultural and economic heart of Mexico. The city has world-class museums, colonial architecture, leafy neighbourhoods and a legendary food scene. Artists such as Frida Kahlo and Diego Rivera left a lasting legacy. It is a co-host of the 2026 FIFA World Cup, with matches at the Estadio Azteca.

### Top Attractions

- **Zócalo and the Historic Centre:** The main square, the Metropolitan Cathedral and the National Palace with Rivera's murals.
- **Templo Mayor:** Ruins of the main Aztec temple.
- **National Museum of Anthropology:** One of the world's finest museums of pre-Columbian art, including the Aztec Sun Stone.
- **Frida Kahlo Museum (Casa Azul):** Frida Kahlo's home in Coyoacán.
- **Chapultepec Park and Castle:** A vast park with museums.
- **Teotihuacan:** An ancient city with the Pyramids of the Sun and Moon.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Roma and Condesa are trendy, Polanco upscale, and Coyoacán bohemian.

### Food & Drink

Tacos al pastor, tamales, chilaquiles, mole and churros are staples. Pujol and Quintonil are world-famous restaurants.

### When to Visit

October to May is dry. The Day of the Dead (early November) is spectacular.

### Getting Around

The metro is cheap but crowded. Ride-hailing apps are safe and convenient.

### Day Trips

Teotihuacan, Puebla, Xochimilco and Cholula are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Take it easy at altitude (2,240 m).
- Use apps for taxis.
- Drink bottled water.
- Book Frida Kahlo Museum tickets in advance.

## Miami, United States (Florida)

*A sun-soaked city of Art Deco, beaches and Latin American culture.*

- **Country:** United States (Florida)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~450,000 (city); ~6.1 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English and Spanish
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Miami International Airport (MIA); Fort Lauderdale (FLL)
- **Best time to visit:** November–April
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Miami, at the southeastern tip of Florida, is a major centre of finance, culture and tourism with strong Latin American and Caribbean influences. Miami Beach is known for its Art Deco Historic District and nightlife. Little Havana reflects the Cuban exile community. Miami is also an art hub, hosting Art Basel Miami Beach. It is a gateway to the Everglades and the Florida Keys.

### Top Attractions

- **South Beach and Ocean Drive:** Beaches and Art Deco buildings.
- **Wynwood Walls:** Outdoor street art.
- **Little Havana:** Cuban culture on Calle Ocho.
- **Vizcaya Museum and Gardens:** An Italian Renaissance-style villa.
- **Pérez Art Museum Miami:** Contemporary art.
- **Everglades National Park:** Wetlands with alligators.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

South Beach, Brickell, Wynwood and Coconut Grove are popular.

### Food & Drink

Cuban sandwiches, stone crabs and Latin American cuisine are highlights.

### When to Visit

Winter is pleasant. Hurricane season runs from June to November.

### Getting Around

Metrorail, Metromover (free) and buses. A car is useful.

### Day Trips

The Florida Keys and Everglades.

### Practical Tips

- Use sunscreen.
- Check hurricane forecasts.
- Book early during Art Basel.
- Try Cuban coffee.

## Milan, Italy

*Italy's capital of fashion, design and finance, home to The Last Supper.*

- **Country:** Italy
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.4 million (city); ~3.2 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Italian
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Milan Malpensa (MXP), Linate (LIN), Bergamo Orio al Serio (BGY)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Milan is the capital of Lombardy and Italy's economic powerhouse, known worldwide for fashion, design, finance and football. It has a long history as a Roman capital and later as a powerful duchy under the Visconti and Sforza families. While less romantic than Rome or Venice, Milan offers world-class art, magnificent architecture and stylish shopping. It is a dynamic, international city with excellent restaurants, bars and nightlife. Milan is also the gateway to Lake Como and the Italian Lakes.

### Top Attractions

- **Milan Cathedral (Duomo):** A spectacular Gothic cathedral; walk on its rooftop among spires and statues.
- **The Last Supper:** Leonardo da Vinci's mural in the refectory of Santa Maria delle Grazie; tickets must be booked well in advance.
- **Galleria Vittorio Emanuele II:** A 19th-century glass-roofed shopping arcade with luxury boutiques.
- **Sforza Castle:** A castle housing several museums, including Michelangelo's final sculpture, the Rondanini Pietà.
- **Teatro alla Scala:** One of the world's most famous opera houses, with a museum.
- **Navigli:** Canals lined with bars and restaurants, famous for aperitivo.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The area around the Duomo is central and convenient. Brera is artistic and elegant, with galleries and cafés. Navigli is lively in the evening, while Porta Nuova and Isola are modern districts with the Bosco Verticale towers.

### Food & Drink

Milan's specialities include risotto alla milanese (saffron risotto), cotoletta alla milanese (breaded veal cutlet) and ossobuco. Panettone, a sweet Christmas bread, originated here. Aperitivo culture is strong, with cocktails like the Negroni sbagliato and Campari spritz often served with snacks.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are best. Summer is hot and humid, and many businesses close in August. Milan Fashion Week and Milan Design Week (Salone del Mobile, April) fill hotels quickly.

### Getting Around

Milan has an efficient metro, trams and buses. The Malpensa Express connects the main airport with the city in about 50 minutes. High-speed trains reach Rome, Florence, Venice and Turin.

### Day Trips

Lake Como, with towns like Bellagio and Varenna, is about an hour away. Bergamo's upper town and Lake Maggiore with the Borromean Islands are also popular. Verona and Turin are easy trips by high-speed train.

### Practical Tips

- Book The Last Supper tickets months in advance; slots are limited and tours are only 15 minutes.
- Validate transport tickets before travel.
- Avoid visiting in mid-August when many places are closed.
- Aperitivo typically runs from about 6 to 9 pm.

## Montevideo, Uruguay

*A relaxed, safe capital of beaches, Art Deco buildings and grilled meat.*

- **Country:** Uruguay
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.4 million (city); ~1.9 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Uruguayan Peso (UYU)
- **Main airport:** Carrasco International Airport (MVD)
- **Best time to visit:** November–March
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Montevideo, the capital of Uruguay, lies on the Río de la Plata. It is known for its relaxed lifestyle, long waterfront promenade (Rambla) and safety compared with other capitals in the region. The Ciudad Vieja has colonial and Art Deco buildings. Uruguay is progressive, with legal cannabis and same-sex marriage. Locals love mate tea and football.

### Top Attractions

- **Ciudad Vieja:** The old town with colonial buildings.
- **Rambla:** A long promenade along the water.
- **Mercado del Puerto:** A market with parrillas (grills).
- **Teatro Solís:** A historic theatre.
- **Palacio Salvo:** An iconic building.
- **Football Museum at the Estadio Centenario:** Site of the first World Cup in 1930.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Pocitos, Punta Carretas and Ciudad Vieja are popular.

### Food & Drink

Asado and chivito (a steak sandwich) are national dishes.

### When to Visit

Summer is ideal for beaches. Carnival in Montevideo is the world's longest.

### Getting Around

Buses and taxis are common.

### Day Trips

Colonia del Sacramento and Punta del Este.

### Practical Tips

- Carry cash and cards.
- Try mate.
- Visit during Carnival.
- Sunday is quiet.

## Montreal, Canada (Quebec)

*A bilingual city of European charm, festivals, bagels and joie de vivre.*

- **Country:** Canada (Quebec)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.8 million (city); ~4.3 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** French (English widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Canadian Dollar (CAD)
- **Main airport:** Montréal–Trudeau International Airport (YUL)
- **Best time to visit:** May–October
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Montreal, on an island in the St. Lawrence River, is the largest city in Quebec and the second-largest French-speaking city in the world after Paris. Founded in 1642 as Ville-Marie, it has a historic old town with cobblestone streets and grand churches. The city is known for its creative culture, festivals, food and nightlife. Montreal blends North American energy with European style. Its underground city (RÉSO) connects shopping centres and metro stations, useful in winter.

### Top Attractions

- **Old Montreal (Vieux-Montréal):** Cobblestone streets, the Old Port and historic buildings.
- **Notre-Dame Basilica:** A Gothic Revival church with a stunning blue interior and the AURA light show.
- **Mount Royal:** A park designed by Frederick Law Olmsted, with views over the city.
- **Montreal Museum of Fine Arts:** A major art museum.
- **Jean-Talon and Atwater Markets:** Food markets with local produce.
- **Plateau Mont-Royal:** A neighbourhood of colourful houses with outdoor staircases.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Old Montreal is historic, the Plateau and Mile End are bohemian, and Downtown is central.

### Food & Drink

Poutine (fries with cheese curds and gravy), Montreal-style bagels (from St-Viateur and Fairmount) and smoked meat sandwiches (Schwartz's) are icons. Maple syrup treats are everywhere.

### When to Visit

Summer is packed with festivals, including the Montreal International Jazz Festival and Just for Laughs. Winters are cold and snowy.

### Getting Around

The metro is efficient. BIXI bike-share is popular.

### Day Trips

Quebec City, the Eastern Townships and Mont-Tremblant are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Greet with "Bonjour" – most people switch to English if needed.
- Dress warmly in winter.
- Tipping 15–20% is expected.
- Book festivals accommodation early.

## Mumbai, India

*India's city of dreams, home to Bollywood, colonial architecture and seaside promenades.*

- **Country:** India
- **Region:** South Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~21 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Marathi and Hindi (English widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Indian Rupee (INR)
- **Main airport:** Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj International Airport (BOM)
- **Best time to visit:** November–February
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Mumbai (formerly Bombay), the capital of Maharashtra, is India's financial capital and most populous city. Originally a group of seven islands, it was developed by the British into a major port. The city combines Victorian Gothic and Art Deco architecture, bustling markets, luxury hotels and vast informal settlements. Mumbai is home to Bollywood, the world's most prolific film industry. It is fast-paced and energetic, yet also known for its spirit of resilience.

### Top Attractions

- **Gateway of India:** An arch monument on the waterfront built to commemorate a royal visit in 1911.
- **Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj Terminus:** A spectacular Victorian Gothic railway station and UNESCO site.
- **Marine Drive:** A seaside promenade known as the "Queen's Necklace" for its lights at night.
- **Elephanta Caves:** Rock-cut temples dedicated to Shiva on an island reached by ferry.
- **Dhobi Ghat:** A giant open-air laundry.
- **Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj Vastu Sangrahalaya:** The city's main museum, formerly the Prince of Wales Museum.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Colaba is the tourist hub near the Gateway of India. Fort and Kala Ghoda have colonial buildings and galleries, and Bandra is trendy, with cafés and bars. Juhu has beaches and Bollywood homes.

### Food & Drink

Mumbai's street food includes vada pav (potato fritter in a bun), pav bhaji, bhelpuri and pani puri. Seafood, especially Malvani and Koli dishes, is excellent. Irani cafés serve bun maska and chai. Mumbai has a thriving restaurant and bar scene.

### When to Visit

November to February is the most pleasant. The monsoon (June–September) brings heavy rain. Ganesh Chaturthi, in August or September, is celebrated with grand processions.

### Getting Around

Local trains are fast but extremely crowded at rush hour. The metro network is expanding, and taxis, auto-rickshaws (in the suburbs) and ride-hailing apps are available. Traffic is heavy.

### Day Trips

Elephanta Caves are a short ferry ride away. Kanheri Caves are in Sanjay Gandhi National Park. The hill stations of Lonavala and Matheran offer cooler air.

### Practical Tips

- Avoid local trains at rush hour.
- Monsoon floods can disrupt travel.
- Carry cash for street food.
- Use ride-hailing apps for convenience.

## Munich, Germany

*Bavaria's capital of beer gardens, baroque churches and Alpine views.*

- **Country:** Germany
- **Region:** Central Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.5 million (city); ~6 million (metro region)
- **Language(s):** German
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Munich Airport (MUC)
- **Best time to visit:** May–October; late September–early October for Oktoberfest
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Munich (München), the capital of Bavaria, is one of Germany's wealthiest and most attractive cities. It is known for its traditional Bavarian culture, including beer halls, lederhosen and the world-famous Oktoberfest. The city was the seat of the Wittelsbach dynasty, which left behind palaces, museums and royal gardens. Munich is also a major centre of technology and industry, home to BMW and Siemens. With the Alps close by, it combines urban sophistication with easy access to nature.

### Top Attractions

- **Marienplatz and the New Town Hall:** The central square, where the Glockenspiel re-enacts historic events daily.
- **English Garden:** One of the world's largest urban parks, with beer gardens and surfers riding the Eisbach wave.
- **Nymphenburg Palace:** A grand baroque summer palace of the Bavarian rulers.
- **Residenz:** The former royal palace in the city centre, with lavish rooms and a treasury.
- **Deutsches Museum:** One of the world's largest museums of science and technology.
- **Hofbräuhaus:** The most famous beer hall in the world, with oompah music and long tables.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Altstadt is central and walkable. Schwabing is lively, with a student population and cafés near the English Garden, while Glockenbachviertel is trendy and LGBTQ+-friendly. Maxvorstadt is the museum district, and Haidhausen is charming and residential.

### Food & Drink

Bavarian cuisine includes Weisswurst (white sausage, traditionally eaten before noon), pretzels, roast pork, Obatzda (a cheese spread) and Schweinshaxe (pork knuckle). Beer gardens allow guests to bring their own food if they buy drinks. The Viktualienmarkt is a traditional food market. Munich's six major breweries supply Oktoberfest.

### When to Visit

Summer is ideal for beer gardens and lakes. Oktoberfest runs for about 16–18 days, usually from mid/late September to the first Sunday in October. December brings traditional Christmas markets, and winter offers easy access to Alpine skiing.

### Getting Around

Munich has an efficient U-Bahn, S-Bahn, tram and bus network. The S-Bahn connects the airport with the centre in about 40 minutes. The city centre is compact and walkable, and cycling is popular.

### Day Trips

Neuschwanstein Castle, the fairy-tale castle of King Ludwig II, is about two hours away. The Dachau Concentration Camp Memorial Site is a short train ride away. Salzburg, Garmisch-Partenkirchen and the Zugspitze, and lakes such as Tegernsee are also popular.

### Practical Tips

- Book Oktoberfest accommodation many months ahead.
- Book Neuschwanstein tickets in advance.
- Beer steins at Oktoberfest are one litre; pace yourself.
- Shops are closed on Sundays.

## Muscat, Oman

*A serene Arabian capital of white buildings, forts and rugged mountains by the sea.*

- **Country:** Oman
- **Region:** Middle East
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.5 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Arabic (English widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Omani Rial (OMR)
- **Main airport:** Muscat International Airport (MCT)
- **Best time to visit:** October–April
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Muscat, the capital of Oman, lies between the Gulf of Oman and the rugged Al Hajar Mountains. Unlike its flashier Gulf neighbours, Muscat has preserved a low-rise skyline of white buildings and traditional architecture. The city has a long maritime history, and Oman once controlled an empire stretching to Zanzibar. Muscat is calm, clean and welcoming, with forts, souks and beaches. It is an excellent base for exploring Oman's deserts, wadis and mountains.

### Top Attractions

- **Sultan Qaboos Grand Mosque:** A magnificent mosque with a giant chandelier and a huge hand-woven carpet; open to non-Muslims in the morning, except Fridays.
- **Mutrah Souq and Corniche:** A traditional market for frankincense, silver and textiles along a waterfront promenade.
- **Royal Opera House Muscat:** An elegant opera house with performances from around the world.
- **Al Alam Palace and Old Muscat:** The ceremonial palace flanked by Portuguese-era forts Al Jalali and Al Mirani.
- **National Museum of Oman:** Exhibits on Omani history and culture.
- **Bait Al Zubair:** A museum of Omani heritage.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Mutrah and Old Muscat are historic. Qurum and Shatti Al Qurum have beaches and hotels, and Al Mouj is a modern marina development.

### Food & Drink

Omani cuisine includes shuwa (slow-cooked marinated lamb), majboos and halwa (a sticky sweet). Omani coffee (kahwa) is flavoured with cardamom and served with dates. Seafood is excellent.

### When to Visit

October to April is pleasant. Summers are extremely hot. Ramadan affects opening hours.

### Getting Around

Taxis and ride-hailing apps are common. Renting a car is ideal for exploring Oman. The city is spread out along the coast.

### Day Trips

Wadi Shab and Bimmah Sinkhole are popular swimming spots. Nizwa, with its fort and souk, is about 1.5 hours away. The Wahiba Sands desert offers camping.

### Practical Tips

- Dress modestly, especially in mosques and souks.
- Friday is the holy day.
- Carry water when hiking in wadis.
- Respect local customs.

## Naples, Italy

*The chaotic, passionate birthplace of pizza, beneath the shadow of Vesuvius.*

- **Country:** Italy
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~910,000 (city); ~3 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Italian (Neapolitan dialect widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Naples International Airport, Capodichino (NAP)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Naples is southern Italy's largest city and one of the oldest continuously inhabited cities in the world, founded by Greek colonists. Its historic centre is a UNESCO World Heritage Site, full of churches, palaces and underground passages. Naples is loud, crowded and full of life, with a strong local identity, street culture and devotion to football and saints. It is the birthplace of pizza and home to some of Italy's best street food. The city is also the gateway to Pompeii, Herculaneum, the Amalfi Coast and the islands of Capri and Ischia.

### Top Attractions

- **National Archaeological Museum:** One of the world's most important archaeological museums, with treasures from Pompeii and Herculaneum.
- **Spaccanapoli and the historic centre:** A long, straight street cutting through the old town, lined with churches, shops and street vendors.
- **Naples Underground (Napoli Sotterranea):** Tunnels, cisterns and Greek-Roman ruins beneath the city.
- **Cappella Sansevero:** Home to the Veiled Christ, an astonishing marble sculpture.
- **Castel dell'Ovo and the waterfront:** A seaside castle and promenade with views of Vesuvius.
- **Via San Gregorio Armeno:** A street famous for workshops making nativity scenes.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The historic centre is atmospheric and central. Chiaia is elegant, with shops and seafront views, while Vomero is residential, on a hill with panoramic views. The Quartieri Spagnoli are lively and full of murals, including shrines to Diego Maradona.

### Food & Drink

Neapolitan pizza, with a soft, puffy crust, is the city's pride; classic pizzerias include Da Michele and Sorbillo. Street foods include pizza fritta (fried pizza), cuoppo (paper cones of fried seafood) and sfogliatella pastries. Babà al rum is a local dessert. Espresso in Naples is strong and taken standing at the bar.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal. Summer is hot but great for trips to the coast and islands. Religious festivals include the Feast of San Gennaro, when the saint's blood is said to liquefy.

### Getting Around

Naples has a metro, funiculars and buses, though traffic is chaotic. The Circumvesuviana train connects Naples to Pompeii, Herculaneum and Sorrento. Ferries depart for Capri, Ischia, Procida and the Amalfi Coast.

### Day Trips

Pompeii and Herculaneum, Roman towns buried by Vesuvius in AD 79, are unmissable. Hiking to the crater of Mount Vesuvius is possible with a timed ticket. The Amalfi Coast, Capri and the Royal Palace of Caserta are other popular trips.

### Practical Tips

- Watch your belongings in crowded areas and on public transport.
- Book Vesuvius crater tickets online.
- Cross streets confidently; scooters rarely stop.
- The Campania ArteCard offers transport and museum discounts.

## Nara, Japan

*Japan's first permanent capital, famous for friendly deer and a giant bronze Buddha.*

- **Country:** Japan
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~350,000
- **Language(s):** Japanese
- **Currency:** Japanese Yen (JPY)
- **Main airport:** Kansai International Airport (KIX); Osaka Itami (ITM)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and October–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Nara was Japan's capital from 710 to 784, during a period when Buddhism and culture flourished. It contains some of Japan's oldest and largest temples, many of which are part of a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Nara is famous for its free-roaming sika deer, considered sacred messengers of the gods. The city is compact and relaxed, making it a popular day trip from Kyoto or Osaka. Staying overnight allows visitors to enjoy its temples in quieter hours.

### Top Attractions

- **Todai-ji:** A temple housing a 15-metre bronze Buddha in one of the world's largest wooden buildings.
- **Nara Park:** A large park where over 1,000 deer roam freely and bow for deer crackers.
- **Kasuga Taisha:** A Shinto shrine famous for thousands of lanterns.
- **Kofuku-ji:** A temple with a five-storey pagoda.
- **Isuien Garden:** A beautiful traditional garden.
- **Naramachi:** A historic merchant district.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Staying near Nara Park or Kintetsu Nara Station is convenient.

### Food & Drink

Kakinoha-zushi (sushi wrapped in persimmon leaves) and narazuke (pickles) are local specialities. Mochi from Nakatanidou is famous for its fast pounding.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are beautiful. Lantern festivals take place in February and August.

### Getting Around

Most sights are walkable from the stations. Kintetsu and JR trains connect Nara with Kyoto and Osaka.

### Day Trips

Horyu-ji, one of the world's oldest wooden buildings, is nearby. Yoshino is famous for cherry blossoms.

### Practical Tips

- Deer can nibble or butt; be careful with food.
- Visit early to avoid crowds.
- Buy deer crackers only from official vendors.
- Combine Nara with Kyoto or Osaka.

## Nashville, United States (Tennessee)

*"Music City", the home of country music, honky-tonks and hot chicken.*

- **Country:** United States (Tennessee)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~700,000 (city); ~2 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Nashville International Airport (BNA)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Nashville, the capital of Tennessee, is known worldwide as the centre of country music. The Grand Ole Opry radio show, broadcast since 1925, made the city famous. Nashville has a vibrant live music scene across genres. The city has grown rapidly and is popular for bachelor and bachelorette parties. It also has Civil War history and a growing food scene.

### Top Attractions

- **Broadway honky-tonks:** Bars with live music all day and night.
- **Grand Ole Opry:** The legendary country music show.
- **Country Music Hall of Fame and Museum:** The history of country music.
- **Ryman Auditorium:** The "Mother Church of Country Music".
- **The Parthenon:** A full-scale replica of the Athenian temple in Centennial Park.
- **Johnny Cash Museum:** A museum dedicated to the life and music of the "Man in Black".

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Downtown, The Gulch, East Nashville and 12 South are popular.

### Food & Drink

Nashville hot chicken, barbecue and Southern comfort food are local favourites.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal. The CMA Fest takes place in June.

### Getting Around

A car or ride-hailing is useful. Downtown is walkable.

### Day Trips

The Jack Daniel's Distillery, Franklin and Memphis are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Book Opry tickets in advance.
- Try hot chicken with caution.
- Tip musicians.
- Book hotels early for events.

## New Orleans, United States (Louisiana)

*The birthplace of jazz, famous for Mardi Gras, Creole food and French Quarter charm.*

- **Country:** United States (Louisiana)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~360,000 (city); ~1.3 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Louis Armstrong New Orleans International Airport (MSY)
- **Best time to visit:** February–May and October–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

New Orleans, on the Mississippi River in Louisiana, is one of the most unique cities in the United States. Founded by the French in 1718, it later came under Spanish rule, giving it a distinctive Creole culture. The city is the birthplace of jazz and is famous for music, festivals and food. Hurricane Katrina devastated the city in 2005, but it has rebuilt. Its motto "Laissez les bons temps rouler" (Let the good times roll) sums up its spirit.

### Top Attractions

- **French Quarter:** Historic streets with wrought-iron balconies, Jackson Square and St. Louis Cathedral.
- **Frenchmen Street:** Live jazz clubs.
- **Garden District:** Grand mansions and Lafayette Cemetery.
- **National WWII Museum:** A major museum on the Second World War.
- **Bourbon Street:** A famous party street.
- **Swamp tours:** Tours of the bayous.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The French Quarter, Marigny and Garden District are popular.

### Food & Drink

Gumbo, jambalaya, po'boys, beignets from Café du Monde and crawfish boils are local classics.

### When to Visit

Mardi Gras (February/March) and Jazz Fest (April/May) are major events. Summers are hot and humid.

### Getting Around

Streetcars, walking and ride-hailing apps.

### Day Trips

Plantations along River Road and swamp tours.

### Practical Tips

- Book early for Mardi Gras and Jazz Fest.
- Stay aware of your surroundings at night.
- Try beignets.
- Hurricane season runs June–November.

## New York City, United States (New York)
