*The city that never sleeps – skyscrapers, Broadway, museums and endless energy.*

- **Country:** United States (New York)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~8.3 million (city); ~19.5 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English (hundreds of languages spoken)
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** John F. Kennedy (JFK), LaGuardia (LGA), Newark Liberty (EWR)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

New York City is the largest city in the United States and one of the world's great cultural and financial capitals. It consists of five boroughs: Manhattan, Brooklyn, Queens, the Bronx and Staten Island. Founded as New Amsterdam by the Dutch, it became the gateway for millions of immigrants through Ellis Island. New York is a global centre of art, fashion, theatre, media and finance. Its iconic skyline and diverse neighbourhoods make it endlessly fascinating.

### Top Attractions

- **Statue of Liberty and Ellis Island:** Symbols of freedom and immigration.
- **Central Park:** An 843-acre park in the heart of Manhattan.
- **Times Square and Broadway:** Bright lights and theatre.
- **Metropolitan Museum of Art:** One of the world's largest art museums.
- **Empire State Building, Top of the Rock and One World Observatory:** Iconic views.
- **9/11 Memorial and Museum:** Twin reflecting pools on the footprints of the Twin Towers and a museum on the 2001 attacks.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Midtown, Greenwich Village, SoHo, the Lower East Side and Williamsburg in Brooklyn are popular.

### Food & Drink

New York pizza, bagels, pastrami sandwiches and diverse cuisines are everywhere.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal. Christmas is festive.

### Getting Around

The subway runs 24/7; OMNY contactless payment works.

### Day Trips

The Hudson Valley, the Hamptons and Philadelphia.

### Practical Tips

- Walk on the right; keep moving.
- Book Broadway tickets in advance or use TKTS.
- Tip 18–20%.
- Use the subway rather than taxis.

## Nice, France

*The sunny capital of the French Riviera, with a famous seafront promenade.*

- **Country:** France
- **Region:** Western Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~345,000 (city); ~1 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** French
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Nice Côte d'Azur Airport (NCE)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Nice is the largest city on the French Riviera (Côte d'Azur), set on the Baie des Anges between the Alps and the Mediterranean. It belonged to the Kingdom of Sardinia until 1860, and its Italian heritage is visible in its colourful architecture and cuisine. In the 19th century, Nice became a winter resort for European aristocracy, and it later attracted artists such as Matisse and Chagall. Its historic centre and the Promenade des Anglais are UNESCO-listed as "Nice, winter resort town of the Riviera". The city is an ideal base for exploring the Riviera.

### Top Attractions

- **Promenade des Anglais:** A 7-kilometre seafront promenade perfect for walks, cycling and sunsets.
- **Vieux Nice (Old Town):** Narrow lanes, baroque churches and the Cours Saleya flower and food market.
- **Castle Hill (Colline du Château):** A park with a waterfall and panoramic views over the bay and old town.
- **Matisse Museum:** A museum in Cimiez dedicated to Henri Matisse, who lived in Nice for many years.
- **Marc Chagall National Museum:** A collection of Chagall's biblical works.
- **Promenade du Paillon:** A long urban park with fountains linking the old town and the modern city.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Vieux Nice is atmospheric and central, while the area around Place Masséna and the Promenade is convenient. The Port area (Lympia) is lively with restaurants, and Cimiez is quiet and residential, home to museums and Roman ruins.

### Food & Drink

Niçoise cuisine includes salade niçoise, socca (a chickpea pancake), pissaladière (onion tart with anchovies) and pan bagnat (a tuna sandwich). Petits farcis are stuffed vegetables. Local Bellet wine is produced in the hills above the city. Rosé wine from Provence is a summer staple.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are pleasant and less crowded. Summer is hot and busy, with beach life in full swing. The Nice Carnival in February is one of the world's major carnivals, with flower battles.

### Getting Around

Nice has a modern tram network linking the airport, city centre and port. Buses and trains connect Nice with other Riviera towns cheaply. The centre is walkable.

### Day Trips

Monaco and Monte Carlo are 20–30 minutes away by train. Èze, a medieval hilltop village, and Villefranche-sur-Mer are close. Antibes, Cannes, Menton and Saint-Paul-de-Vence are also easy trips.

### Practical Tips

- Nice's beaches are pebbly; bring water shoes or rent a sunbed at a private beach.
- Use the regional trains to explore the Riviera cheaply.
- Book accommodation early for summer and Carnival.
- Many museums in Nice are free or cheap with a city pass.

## Oaxaca, Mexico

*Mexico's culinary and cultural heart, with mole, mezcal and indigenous traditions.*

- **Country:** Mexico
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~270,000 (city); ~700,000 (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish and indigenous languages (Zapotec, Mixtec)
- **Currency:** Mexican Peso (MXN)
- **Main airport:** Oaxaca International Airport (OAX)
- **Best time to visit:** October–April
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Oaxaca de Juárez, the capital of the state of Oaxaca in southern Mexico, is a colonial city surrounded by mountains and indigenous communities. Its historic centre, a UNESCO World Heritage Site, is filled with colourful buildings and churches. Oaxaca is famous for its food, especially mole and mezcal, as well as crafts and festivals. The Zapotec ruins of Monte Albán overlook the city. Oaxaca is one of Mexico's most culturally rich destinations.

### Top Attractions

- **Templo de Santo Domingo de Guzmán:** A baroque church with a gilded interior.
- **Monte Albán:** Ancient Zapotec city on a mountaintop.
- **Mercado 20 de Noviembre:** A market with grilled meats.
- **Hierve el Agua:** Petrified waterfalls.
- **Mezcal distilleries:** Tours in Santiago Matatlán.
- **Guelaguetza:** An indigenous dance festival in July.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Centro and Jalatlaco are charming.

### Food & Drink

Mole negro, tlayudas, chapulines (grasshoppers) and chocolate are local specialities.

### When to Visit

October to April is dry. The Day of the Dead is spectacular.

### Getting Around

The centre is walkable.

### Day Trips

Hierve el Agua, Mitla and craft villages.

### Practical Tips

- Book Day of the Dead accommodation early.
- Drink mezcal slowly.
- Try chapulines.
- Respect local customs.

## Orlando, United States (Florida)

*The theme park capital of the world.*

- **Country:** United States (Florida)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~320,000 (city); ~2.8 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English (Spanish widely spoken)
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Orlando International Airport (MCO)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and September–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Orlando, in central Florida, is one of the most visited cities in the United States thanks to its theme parks, including Walt Disney World, Universal Orlando and SeaWorld. The city grew rapidly after Disney World opened in 1971. Beyond the parks, Orlando has lakes, gardens and a growing food scene. It is a family-friendly destination with countless hotels and attractions. The Kennedy Space Center is about an hour away.

### Top Attractions

- **Walt Disney World:** Four theme parks (Magic Kingdom, EPCOT, Hollywood Studios and Animal Kingdom) and two water parks.
- **Universal Orlando Resort:** Universal Studios, Islands of Adventure and Epic Universe, which opened in 2025.
- **SeaWorld Orlando:** Marine-themed park with rides.
- **Kennedy Space Center:** NASA's launch site, with rockets and exhibits.
- **ICON Park:** Home to The Wheel, a giant observation wheel.
- **Lake Eola Park:** A downtown park with swan boats.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Lake Buena Vista, International Drive and Kissimmee are near the parks. Downtown and Winter Park are more local.

### Food & Drink

Theme parks have themed restaurants, while Disney Springs offers dining and shopping. Cuban and Puerto Rican cuisine is common.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are pleasant. Summers are hot and stormy, with hurricane season from June to November.

### Getting Around

A car is useful. Disney and Universal offer transport for hotel guests.

### Day Trips

Kennedy Space Center, Cocoa Beach and the springs are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Buy park tickets online in advance.
- Use park apps for wait times and ride reservations.
- Stay hydrated.
- Visit on weekdays.

## Osaka, Japan

*Japan's kitchen, a fun-loving city of street food, neon and nightlife.*

- **Country:** Japan
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~2.8 million (city); ~19 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Japanese (Kansai dialect)
- **Currency:** Japanese Yen (JPY)
- **Main airport:** Kansai International Airport (KIX); Osaka Itami (ITM)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and October–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Osaka is Japan's third-largest city and the commercial heart of the Kansai region. Historically a merchant city, it is known for its outgoing, humorous people and love of food, summed up by the phrase "kuidaore" – eat until you drop. The city combines modern skyscrapers, neon-lit entertainment districts and historic landmarks such as Osaka Castle. Osaka hosted the World Expo in 2025. It is a convenient base for day trips to Kyoto, Nara, Kobe and Himeji.

### Top Attractions

- **Osaka Castle:** A reconstructed castle with a museum, surrounded by a park with cherry blossoms.
- **Dotonbori:** A neon-lit canal district famous for street food and the Glico Running Man sign.
- **Universal Studios Japan:** A theme park with Super Nintendo World and The Wizarding World of Harry Potter.
- **Shinsekai and Tsutenkaku Tower:** A retro neighbourhood known for kushikatsu (fried skewers).
- **Kuromon Market:** A food market known as "Osaka's Kitchen".
- **Umeda Sky Building:** An observatory with a floating garden and city views.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Namba and Shinsaibashi are lively and central. Umeda is a business and shopping hub, and Tennoji is convenient for transport.

### Food & Drink

Osaka is famous for takoyaki (octopus balls), okonomiyaki, kushikatsu and kitsune udon. Dotonbori and Kuromon Market are good places to sample street food.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are the best seasons. Summer is hot and humid, and winter is mild.

### Getting Around

Osaka has an extensive subway and train network. The Nankai and JR lines connect Kansai Airport with the city.

### Day Trips

Kyoto, Nara, Kobe and Himeji are easy day trips. Mount Koya is a sacred mountain with temple stays.

### Practical Tips

- Use an ICOCA card for transport.
- Book Universal Studios in advance.
- Try food in Dotonbori at night.
- Avoid rush hour on trains.

## Oslo, Norway

*A green fjord-side capital of Viking ships, bold architecture and outdoor life.*

- **Country:** Norway
- **Region:** Northern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~720,000 (city); ~1.6 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Norwegian
- **Currency:** Norwegian Krone (NOK)
- **Main airport:** Oslo Airport, Gardermoen (OSL)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Oslo, the capital of Norway, sits at the head of the Oslofjord, surrounded by forested hills. Founded around 1040, it was later rebuilt after a fire and renamed Christiania before returning to its original name in 1925. Today it is a modern, prosperous city with striking waterfront architecture, excellent museums and easy access to nature. Locals love the outdoors, hiking in the forests of Nordmarka and skiing in winter. Oslo is expensive, but many attractions are free or included in a city pass.

### Top Attractions

- **Oslo Opera House:** A marble-and-glass building sloping into the fjord; visitors can walk on its roof.
- **MUNCH Museum:** A tall waterfront museum with one of the largest collections of Edvard Munch's work, including versions of The Scream.
- **Vigeland Sculpture Park:** Over 200 sculptures by Gustav Vigeland in Frogner Park.
- **Bygdøy Peninsula:** Home to museums such as the Fram Museum, Kon-Tiki Museum and the Norwegian Folk Museum.
- **Akershus Fortress:** A medieval castle and fortress overlooking the harbour.
- **National Museum:** The largest art museum in the Nordic countries.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The city centre and Aker Brygge are convenient and close to the waterfront. Grünerløkka is a trendy area with cafés, vintage shops and bars. Frogner is elegant and residential, while Bjørvika is a modern district with the Opera House and MUNCH.

### Food & Drink

Norwegian food focuses on seafood, especially salmon, cod and shrimp. Traditional dishes include fårikål (mutton and cabbage stew) and brunost (brown cheese). Oslo has a strong New Nordic dining scene and food halls like Mathallen. Eating out is expensive, so lunch deals and bakeries offer good value.

### When to Visit

Summer brings long days, fjord swimming and outdoor festivals. Spring and early autumn are cooler but pleasant. Winter is cold and dark, but ideal for cross-country skiing and cosy cafés; the Holmenkollen ski jump is a landmark.

### Getting Around

Oslo has an efficient metro, trams, buses and ferries run by Ruter, and tickets can be bought via app. The Flytoget express train reaches the airport in about 20 minutes. Many sights are walkable.

### Day Trips

Fjord cruises and island-hopping by public ferry are easy options. Holmenkollen and the forests of Nordmarka are reachable by metro. Fredrikstad, with its fortified old town, is about an hour away.

### Practical Tips

- The Oslo Pass includes free entry to many museums and public transport.
- Alcohol is expensive; stronger drinks are only sold at Vinmonopolet stores.
- Card payments are accepted almost everywhere.
- Pack layers; weather can change quickly.

## Palermo, Italy

*Sicily's vibrant capital of Arab-Norman splendour, markets and street food.*

- **Country:** Italy
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~630,000 (city); ~1.2 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Italian (Sicilian widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Falcone Borsellino Airport (PMO)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Palermo, the capital of Sicily, has been shaped by Phoenicians, Greeks, Romans, Arabs, Normans, Spanish and Bourbons over nearly 3,000 years. Its Arab-Norman churches and palaces form a unique UNESCO World Heritage Site. The city is chaotic and intense, with bustling markets, crumbling palaces and magnificent churches. Palermo has undergone a cultural renaissance and was named Italian Capital of Culture in 2018. It is famous for having some of the best street food in Europe.

### Top Attractions

- **Palermo Cathedral:** A striking building blending Norman, Gothic, Moorish and Baroque styles, with rooftop views.
- **Palatine Chapel:** A jewel of Arab-Norman art inside the Norman Palace, covered in golden mosaics.
- **Monreale Cathedral:** Just outside the city, famous for its dazzling Byzantine mosaics.
- **Quattro Canti:** A baroque crossroads with ornate facades in the heart of the old city.
- **Markets of Ballarò, Vucciria and Capo:** Noisy, colourful markets with food stalls and vendors shouting their offers.
- **Capuchin Catacombs:** Crypts with thousands of mummified bodies, a macabre but fascinating site.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The historic centre is atmospheric, with neighbourhoods such as Kalsa and Albergheria. Via Maqueda and Via Vittorio Emanuele are central pedestrian streets. Politeama and Libertà are modern and elegant, with shopping and theatres.

### Food & Drink

Street food includes arancine (fried rice balls), panelle (chickpea fritters), sfincione (Sicilian pizza) and pane con la milza (spleen sandwich). Cannoli and cassata are famous desserts, and granita with brioche is a typical summer breakfast. Pasta con le sarde (with sardines and wild fennel) reflects Arab influences.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are pleasant, while summers are very hot. Winter is mild, making Palermo a good year-round destination. The Feast of Santa Rosalia in July is the city's biggest festival.

### Getting Around

The historic centre is walkable. Buses and taxis are available, and a train and buses connect the airport with the city. Renting a car is useful for exploring Sicily, but driving in Palermo is chaotic.

### Day Trips

Mondello, a beach resort, is just 30 minutes away. Cefalù, with a Norman cathedral and beach, is an hour by train. The Greek temples of Segesta and the medieval town of Erice are also popular.

### Practical Tips

- Watch belongings in crowded markets.
- Many churches require modest dress.
- Try a street food tour to navigate the markets.
- Driving in the centre is restricted by ZTL zones.

## Panama City, Panama

*A modern skyline, a colonial old town and the world-famous canal.*

- **Country:** Panama
- **Region:** Central America
- **Population (approx.):** ~1 million (city); ~2 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Panamanian Balboa (PAB) and US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Tocumen International Airport (PTY)
- **Best time to visit:** December–April
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Panama City, the capital of Panama, is a major financial and transport hub, located at the Pacific entrance of the Panama Canal. Founded in 1519, the original city was destroyed by the pirate Henry Morgan in 1671, and a new city was built nearby, now called Casco Viejo. Today Panama City has a gleaming skyline of skyscrapers, a restored colonial quarter and rainforest within city limits. The US dollar is used alongside the balboa. It is a gateway to the San Blas Islands and Bocas del Toro.

### Top Attractions

- **Panama Canal and Miraflores Locks:** Watch ships pass through the locks.
- **Casco Viejo:** A UNESCO-listed colonial quarter with plazas, churches and rooftop bars.
- **Biomuseo:** A colourful museum designed by Frank Gehry about biodiversity.
- **Amador Causeway:** A road connecting islands with views of the skyline.
- **Metropolitan Natural Park:** Rainforest in the city.
- **Panamá Viejo:** Ruins of the original city.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Casco Viejo, Marbella and Punta Pacífica are popular.

### Food & Drink

Sancocho (chicken soup), ceviche and patacones are popular.

### When to Visit

December to April is the dry season.

### Getting Around

The metro and ride-hailing apps are available.

### Day Trips

San Blas Islands, Gamboa Rainforest and Portobelo.

### Practical Tips

- US dollars are used.
- Book San Blas trips with Guna-run operators.
- Use sunscreen.
- Check canal schedules.

## Paris, France

*The City of Light, a global icon of art, fashion, food and romance.*

- **Country:** France
- **Region:** Western Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~2.1 million (city); ~12.4 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** French
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Charles de Gaulle (CDG), Orly (ORY); Beauvais (BVA) for some low-cost flights
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Paris, the capital of France, is one of the most visited cities in the world and a symbol of art, culture and elegance. Its history stretches from Roman Lutetia through medieval Gothic cathedrals, royal palaces and the French Revolution to Baron Haussmann's grand 19th-century boulevards. Paris is home to the Louvre, the world's most visited museum, and landmarks such as the Eiffel Tower and Notre-Dame. The city is divided into 20 arrondissements, each with its own character. Beyond its monuments, Paris is best enjoyed in its cafés, markets, parks and neighbourhoods.

### Top Attractions

- **Eiffel Tower:** Built for the 1889 World's Fair, it offers views from three levels; book tickets online.
- **Louvre Museum:** Home to the Mona Lisa, the Venus de Milo and thousands of other works of art.
- **Notre-Dame Cathedral:** The Gothic masterpiece reopened in December 2024 after restoration following the 2019 fire.
- **Musée d'Orsay:** Impressionist and post-Impressionist masterpieces housed in a former railway station.
- **Montmartre and Sacré-Cœur:** A hilltop village of artists, with the white basilica and views across Paris.
- **Palace of Versailles:** The spectacular royal palace and gardens, about 40 minutes from central Paris.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Le Marais is historic and trendy, with boutiques and falafel shops. Saint-Germain-des-Prés and the Latin Quarter are classic Left Bank areas of cafés and bookshops. Montmartre offers village charm, while Canal Saint-Martin and Belleville are hip and multicultural.

### Food & Drink

Paris is a paradise for food lovers, from croissants and baguettes to fine dining. Try steak frites, French onion soup, crêpes, macarons and cheese from a fromagerie. Bistros serve classic dishes, and markets like Marché des Enfants Rouges offer diverse options. Wine bars and cafés are central to Parisian life.

### When to Visit

Spring and early autumn are the most pleasant seasons. Summer can be hot and busy, and many Parisians leave in August. Winter is cool and grey, but the city's lights and holiday displays are beautiful.

### Getting Around

The Paris Métro is extensive and efficient, complemented by RER trains, buses and trams. RER B connects CDG Airport with the city centre. Walking and cycling with the Vélib' bike share are great ways to explore.

### Day Trips

Versailles, Giverny (Monet's gardens), and the Château de Fontainebleau are popular day trips. Disneyland Paris is about 40 minutes by RER. Reims, in the Champagne region, is 45 minutes away by TGV.

### Practical Tips

- Book major attractions like the Eiffel Tower, Louvre and Versailles online in advance.
- Many museums are free on the first Sunday of certain months, or for EU residents under 26.
- Say "Bonjour" when entering shops and cafés.
- Beware of pickpockets at tourist sites and on the Métro.

## Philadelphia, United States (Pennsylvania)

*The birthplace of American independence, with history, murals and cheesesteaks.*

- **Country:** United States (Pennsylvania)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.55 million (city); ~6.2 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Philadelphia International Airport (PHL)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Philadelphia, founded by William Penn in 1682, is where the Declaration of Independence and the US Constitution were signed. It served as the nation's capital during the Revolutionary era. Today Philly is a vibrant city of historic sites, museums, neighbourhoods and food. It has one of the largest public mural programmes in the world. 2026 marks the 250th anniversary of American independence, with major celebrations in the city.

### Top Attractions

- **Independence Hall and Liberty Bell:** Where the Declaration and Constitution were signed.
- **Philadelphia Museum of Art:** Famous for its "Rocky Steps".
- **Reading Terminal Market:** A historic food market.
- **Eastern State Penitentiary:** A historic prison, once home to Al Capone.
- **Barnes Foundation:** A collection of Impressionist art.
- **Mural Arts tours:** Guided walks and trolley tours of the city's thousands of public murals.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Old City, Center City, Rittenhouse Square and Fishtown are popular.

### Food & Drink

Philly cheesesteaks, soft pretzels and roast pork sandwiches.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal.

### Getting Around

SEPTA subway, buses and trolleys; the city is walkable.

### Day Trips

Valley Forge, Lancaster County (Amish country) and New York City.

### Practical Tips

- Book Independence Hall tickets.
- Try cheesesteaks from several places.
- Check 250th anniversary events.
- Walk to see murals.

## Phnom Penh, Cambodia

*Cambodia's riverside capital of royal palaces, markets and a poignant recent history.*

- **Country:** Cambodia
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~2.3 million
- **Language(s):** Khmer
- **Currency:** Cambodian Riel (KHR); US Dollars widely used
- **Main airport:** Techo International Airport (KTI), which replaced Phnom Penh International (PNH) in 2025
- **Best time to visit:** November–March
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Phnom Penh, the capital of Cambodia, sits at the confluence of the Mekong, Tonlé Sap and Bassac rivers. Once called the "Pearl of Asia", it was admired for its French colonial architecture and elegant boulevards. The city was emptied by the Khmer Rouge in 1975, and its museums and memorials document the tragic genocide that followed. Today Phnom Penh is a fast-growing city with a lively riverfront, markets, cafés and a growing arts scene. It offers a mix of history, culture and everyday Cambodian life.

### Top Attractions

- **Royal Palace and Silver Pagoda:** The residence of the King of Cambodia, with ornate throne halls and a pagoda floored with silver tiles.
- **Tuol Sleng Genocide Museum (S-21):** A former school turned Khmer Rouge prison, now a sobering museum.
- **Choeung Ek Killing Fields:** A memorial site outside the city, with a stupa containing thousands of skulls; the audio guide is highly recommended.
- **National Museum of Cambodia:** The world's leading collection of Khmer art and sculpture.
- **Central Market (Phsar Thmei):** An Art Deco market building with a yellow dome.
- **Sisowath Quay:** The riverside promenade, lively in the evenings.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Riverside area is central, with hotels, restaurants and bars. BKK1 is a leafy upscale district popular with expats, and the area around the Russian Market (Tuol Tom Poung) is trendy.

### Food & Drink

Cambodian cuisine includes fish amok (a curry steamed in banana leaves), lok lak (stir-fried beef), and kuy teav (noodle soup). Num banh chok, rice noodles with fish curry, is a common breakfast. The Russian Market and night markets offer street food.

### When to Visit

November to March is dry and relatively cool. April is very hot, and the rainy season runs from May to October. The Water Festival in November features boat races on the river.

### Getting Around

Tuk-tuks and ride-hailing apps like PassApp and Grab are common. The city is spread out, but central areas are walkable. Buses connect Phnom Penh with Siem Reap and Kampot.

### Day Trips

Oudong, the former royal capital, has hilltop stupas. Silk Island (Koh Dach) offers weaving villages. Kampot and Kep, with pepper plantations and crab markets, are a few hours away.

### Practical Tips

- US dollars are widely accepted, with change often given in riel.
- Dress modestly at the Royal Palace (shoulders and knees covered).
- Watch bags and phones on tuk-tuk rides due to snatch theft.
- Visit Tuol Sleng and Choeung Ek with time to reflect.

## Phuket, Thailand

*Thailand's largest island, with beaches, nightlife and gateway to the Andaman Sea.*

- **Country:** Thailand
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~420,000 (registered); much higher including migrants and seasonal residents
- **Language(s):** Thai
- **Currency:** Thai Baht (THB)
- **Main airport:** Phuket International Airport (HKT)
- **Best time to visit:** November–April
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Phuket is Thailand's largest island, located in the Andaman Sea off the country's southwest coast. It became wealthy through tin mining and rubber, and Phuket Town still reflects this with Sino-Portuguese shophouses. Today tourism dominates, with beaches ranging from party hubs to quiet coves. Phuket is a base for island-hopping to the Phi Phi Islands, Phang Nga Bay and the Similan Islands. It has resorts for every budget.

### Top Attractions

- **Patong Beach:** Phuket's busiest beach, famous for nightlife on Bangla Road.
- **Phuket Old Town:** Colourful Sino-Portuguese buildings, cafés and a Sunday walking street market.
- **Big Buddha:** A 45-metre white marble statue on a hilltop with views.
- **Wat Chalong:** Phuket's most important Buddhist temple.
- **Promthep Cape:** A popular sunset viewpoint at the island's southern tip.
- **Kata and Karon Beaches:** Family-friendly beaches with good swimming.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Patong is lively, while Kata and Karon are more relaxed. Kamala and Surin are upscale, and Mai Khao is quiet, near the airport. Phuket Town offers culture and food.

### Food & Drink

Southern Thai food is spicier and influenced by Chinese and Malay cuisines. Try moo hong (braised pork belly), Phuket-style noodles and seafood. Night markets like Naka Market offer street food.

### When to Visit

November to April is the dry season, with calm seas. The monsoon (May–October) brings rough seas, and some islands close. The Vegetarian Festival in autumn features dramatic processions.

### Getting Around

Public transport is limited; taxis, tuk-tuks and ride-hailing apps are common. Scooter rental is popular but risky.

### Day Trips

Phi Phi Islands, James Bond Island in Phang Nga Bay and the Similan Islands are popular. Krabi and Khao Lak are reachable by road or boat.

### Practical Tips

- Negotiate taxi and tuk-tuk fares in advance, or use apps.
- Heed red flags on beaches during the monsoon.
- Wear a helmet if renting a scooter.
- Book island tours with reputable operators.

## Porto, Portugal

*A hilly riverside city famous for port wine, azulejos and bridges.*

- **Country:** Portugal
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~230,000 (city); ~1.7 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Portuguese
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Francisco Sá Carneiro Airport (OPO)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Porto is Portugal's second-largest city, built on steep hills along the Douro River near the Atlantic coast. It gave its name to Portugal and to port wine, which is aged in cellars across the river in Vila Nova de Gaia. Porto's historic centre, with its colourful houses, churches covered in blue-and-white tiles and baroque architecture, is a UNESCO World Heritage Site. The city is authentic, affordable and increasingly popular. It is also the gateway to the Douro Valley wine region.

### Top Attractions

- **Ribeira:** The riverside district with colourful houses, restaurants and views of the Dom Luís I Bridge.
- **Dom Luís I Bridge:** A double-deck iron bridge; walk across the top level for spectacular views.
- **Port wine cellars:** Tours and tastings at historic lodges such as Graham's, Taylor's and Sandeman in Vila Nova de Gaia.
- **São Bento Station:** A railway station with a hall decorated with around 20,000 azulejo tiles.
- **Livraria Lello:** A famous neo-Gothic bookshop with an ornate staircase; entry requires a ticket.
- **Clérigos Tower:** A baroque bell tower with panoramic views of the city.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Ribeira and Baixa are central and close to sights. Cedofeita and the Miguel Bombarda arts district have galleries and shops, while Foz do Douro is an upscale seaside area. Vila Nova de Gaia, across the river, offers views and port lodges.

### Food & Drink

The francesinha is Porto's famous sandwich, filled with meat, covered in melted cheese and a beer-tomato sauce. Tripas à moda do Porto (tripe stew) is a traditional dish. Seafood, bacalhau and pastel de nata are widely available. Port wine comes in styles such as tawny, ruby and white; a white port and tonic is a popular aperitif.

### When to Visit

Spring and early autumn are ideal. Summer is warm but less hot than Lisbon, and the São João festival on the night of 23 June is a huge street party with fireworks and plastic hammers. Winter is mild but rainy.

### Getting Around

Porto has a metro and historic trams, but the centre is walkable, though very hilly. The metro connects the airport with the centre. Funiculars help with steep climbs.

### Day Trips

The Douro Valley, with terraced vineyards and quintas, is a highlight; visit by train, boat or tour. Guimarães, the birthplace of Portugal, and Braga, with the Bom Jesus sanctuary, are close. Aveiro, known as the "Venice of Portugal", is about an hour away.

### Practical Tips

- Wear comfortable shoes for steep, cobbled streets.
- Book port cellar tours in advance in summer.
- Try a francesinha at a local café rather than touristy spots.
- The Porto Card offers transport and museum discounts.

## Prague, Czech Republic (Czechia)

*The "City of a Hundred Spires", a fairy-tale capital of Gothic and Baroque beauty.*

- **Country:** Czech Republic (Czechia)
- **Region:** Central Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.4 million
- **Language(s):** Czech
- **Currency:** Czech Koruna (CZK)
- **Main airport:** Václav Havel Airport Prague (PRG)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Prague, the capital of the Czech Republic, is one of Europe's most beautiful cities, with a remarkably well-preserved historic centre on the Vltava River. It was the seat of Holy Roman Emperors and a major centre of culture and learning in the Middle Ages. Prague's skyline of spires, towers and domes has earned it the nickname "City of a Hundred Spires". The city is famous for its beer, classical music and the legacy of writers like Franz Kafka. It is very popular, so exploring early or late helps avoid crowds.

### Top Attractions

- **Prague Castle:** One of the largest castle complexes in the world, including St. Vitus Cathedral and Golden Lane.
- **Charles Bridge:** A 14th-century stone bridge lined with baroque statues, best visited at sunrise.
- **Old Town Square and Astronomical Clock:** The medieval heart of the city, with the famous 15th-century clock.
- **Josefov (Jewish Quarter):** Historic synagogues and the Old Jewish Cemetery.
- **Malá Strana:** A charming baroque district below the castle, with palaces and gardens.
- **Petřín Hill:** A park with a lookout tower resembling the Eiffel Tower and views over the city.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Old Town and Malá Strana are central and scenic but busy. Vinohrady is elegant and residential, with cafés and parks, and Žižkov is known for its many pubs. Karlín is a trendy, redeveloped area with restaurants.

### Food & Drink

Czech cuisine is hearty: try svíčková (beef in cream sauce with dumplings), goulash, roast pork with sauerkraut and fried cheese (smažený sýr). Trdelník, a chimney cake sold everywhere, is not traditionally Czech but popular with tourists. Czech beer, including Pilsner Urquell and Budvar, is among the best in the world and very affordable.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal. Summer is warm and crowded, and winter is cold but magical at Christmas markets. The Prague Spring music festival takes place in May and June.

### Getting Around

Prague has an excellent metro, tram and bus network. Buses and the Airport Express connect the airport with the city. The historic centre is walkable, though cobblestones and hills can be tiring.

### Day Trips

Kutná Hora, with the Sedlec Ossuary decorated with human bones, is about an hour away. Český Krumlov is a fairy-tale town in South Bohemia. Karlštejn Castle and the Terezín Memorial are also popular.

### Practical Tips

- Avoid exchange offices with bad rates; use ATMs of major banks and decline dynamic currency conversion.
- Use official taxis or ride-hailing apps.
- Validate transport tickets.
- Visit Charles Bridge early in the morning for fewer crowds.

## Puerto Vallarta, Mexico

*A Pacific resort town of jungle-clad mountains, cobblestones and sunsets.*

- **Country:** Mexico
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~300,000
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Mexican Peso (MXN)
- **Main airport:** Licenciado Gustavo Díaz Ordaz International Airport (PVR)
- **Best time to visit:** November–April
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Puerto Vallarta is a resort city on Banderas Bay on Mexico's Pacific coast, surrounded by the Sierra Madre mountains. Once a small fishing village, it became famous after the 1964 film The Night of the Iguana. Its old town has cobbled streets, white houses and a seafront Malecón. Puerto Vallarta is known for its LGBTQ+-friendly atmosphere and beaches. It is a base for whale watching, snorkelling and jungle tours.

### Top Attractions

- **Malecón:** A seaside promenade with sculptures.
- **Our Lady of Guadalupe Church:** A church with a crown-topped tower.
- **Zona Romántica:** Restaurants, bars and Los Muertos Beach.
- **Los Arcos:** Rock formations for snorkelling.
- **Yelapa:** A village reachable by boat.
- **Whale watching:** Humpback whales visit Banderas Bay from December to March.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Zona Romántica, Centro and Marina Vallarta are popular.

### Food & Drink

Seafood tacos, pescado zarandeado and raicilla (a local agave spirit).

### When to Visit

November to April is dry. Summer is hot and rainy.

### Getting Around

Buses, taxis and water taxis.

### Day Trips

Sayulita, Yelapa and the Marietas Islands.

### Practical Tips

- Book Marietas trips in advance (limited permits).
- Use sunscreen.
- Carry cash.
- Check hurricane forecasts.

## Quebec City, Canada (Quebec)

*A fortified French-speaking city with a European old town.*

- **Country:** Canada (Quebec)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~550,000 (city); ~850,000 (metro)
- **Language(s):** French
- **Currency:** Canadian Dollar (CAD)
- **Main airport:** Québec City Jean Lesage International Airport (YQB)
- **Best time to visit:** June–October; February for Winter Carnival
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Quebec City, the capital of Quebec, was founded by Samuel de Champlain in 1608, making it one of the oldest European settlements in North America. It is the only fortified city north of Mexico with its walls intact. Old Quebec is a UNESCO World Heritage Site. The city is predominantly French-speaking and has a European feel. It is famous for the Château Frontenac and its Winter Carnival.

### Top Attractions

- **Château Frontenac:** A grand hotel overlooking the St. Lawrence River.
- **Old Quebec and Petit-Champlain:** Charming streets.
- **Citadelle of Quebec:** A star-shaped fortress.
- **Plains of Abraham:** A historic battlefield park.
- **Montmorency Falls:** Waterfalls higher than Niagara.
- **Quebec Winter Carnival:** One of the world's largest winter festivals, with an ice palace, parades and snow sculptures in February.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Old Quebec and Saint-Roch are popular.

### Food & Drink

Poutine, tourtière (meat pie) and maple syrup treats.

### When to Visit

Summer and autumn are pleasant. Winters are cold but festive.

### Getting Around

The old town is walkable. A funicular connects the upper and lower town.

### Day Trips

Montmorency Falls, Île d'Orléans and Charlevoix.

### Practical Tips

- Dress warmly in winter.
- Learn basic French phrases.
- Book early for Carnival.
- Wear comfortable shoes.

## Quito, Ecuador

*A high-altitude capital with one of the best-preserved colonial centres in the Americas.*

- **Country:** Ecuador
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~2.8 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish (Kichwa also spoken)
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Mariscal Sucre International Airport (UIO)
- **Best time to visit:** June–September
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Quito, the capital of Ecuador, lies in an Andean valley at about 2,850 metres, making it one of the highest capital cities in the world. Its historic centre was one of the first two sites declared UNESCO World Heritage Sites in 1978. The city is surrounded by volcanoes, including the active Pichincha. Quito is located just south of the equator. It is the main gateway to the Galápagos Islands and the Amazon.

### Top Attractions

- **Historic Centre:** Colonial churches, plazas and monasteries.
- **La Compañía de Jesús:** A church with a gold-covered interior.
- **Basílica del Voto Nacional:** A Gothic basilica with towers you can climb.
- **TelefériQo:** A cable car up Pichincha volcano.
- **Mitad del Mundo:** The equator monument.
- **El Panecillo:** A hill with a statue of the Virgin.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The historic centre, La Floresta and La Mariscal are popular.

### Food & Drink

Locro de papa (potato soup), empanadas and hornado.

### When to Visit

June to September is drier.

### Getting Around

The Quito Metro opened in 2023. Taxis and apps are available.

### Day Trips

Otavalo market, Cotopaxi National Park and Mindo cloud forest.

### Practical Tips

- Acclimatise to the altitude.
- Use apps for taxis.
- Bring layers.
- Be cautious at night.

## Reykjavik, Iceland

*The world's northernmost capital and a base for volcanoes, glaciers and northern lights.*

- **Country:** Iceland
- **Region:** Northern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~140,000 (city); ~240,000 (capital region)
- **Language(s):** Icelandic (English widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Icelandic Króna (ISK)
- **Main airport:** Keflavík International Airport (KEF)
- **Best time to visit:** June–August for midnight sun; September–March for northern lights
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Reykjavik, the capital of Iceland, is small, colourful and creative, home to most of the country's population. It is surrounded by dramatic landscapes shaped by volcanoes, glaciers and geothermal activity. The city has a lively arts and music scene, cosy cafés and geothermally heated swimming pools. Most visitors use Reykjavik as a base for exploring Iceland's natural wonders. Iceland is expensive, but its scenery is unlike anywhere else in Europe.

### Top Attractions

- **Hallgrímskirkja:** A striking church inspired by basalt columns, with a tower offering city views.
- **Harpa Concert Hall:** A glass concert hall on the harbour with a shimmering facade.
- **Sun Voyager:** A steel sculpture resembling a Viking ship on the waterfront.
- **Laugavegur:** The main shopping street, with cafés, bars and boutiques.
- **National Museum of Iceland:** An overview of Icelandic history from settlement to the present.
- **Sky Lagoon and geothermal pools:** Swimming in outdoor thermal pools is a key part of Icelandic culture.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Downtown (101 Reykjavik) is compact and convenient. The Old Harbour has restaurants and whale-watching tours. Staying slightly outside the centre can be cheaper.

### Food & Drink

Icelandic cuisine features lamb, fish and skyr, a thick dairy product. Hot dogs from Bæjarins Beztu Pylsur are famous. Traditional dishes such as fermented shark (hákarl) are an acquired taste. Seafood soup and lamb soup are warming staples.

### When to Visit

Summer offers the midnight sun and access to the highlands. Winter brings long nights and the chance to see the northern lights, but daylight is limited. Weather changes quickly year-round.

### Getting Around

Reykjavik is walkable, with a bus network for longer trips. The Flybus connects Keflavík Airport with the city in about 45 minutes. Renting a car is popular for exploring Iceland, but check road conditions carefully.

### Day Trips

The Golden Circle includes Þingvellir National Park, the Geysir geothermal area and Gullfoss waterfall. The Blue Lagoon is near the airport. The South Coast offers waterfalls, black-sand beaches and glacier hikes.

### Practical Tips

- Check weather and road conditions at safetravel.is and road.is.
- Tap water is clean and free.
- Book the Blue Lagoon in advance.
- Aurora forecasts help plan northern lights hunts.

## Riga, Latvia

*The Baltic's largest city, famous for Art Nouveau architecture and a medieval old town.*

- **Country:** Latvia
- **Region:** Northern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~610,000
- **Language(s):** Latvian
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Riga International Airport (RIX)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Riga, the capital of Latvia, lies on the Gulf of Riga at the mouth of the Daugava River. Founded in 1201, it became a major Hanseatic trading city. Its historic centre is a UNESCO World Heritage Site, known for its medieval old town and the largest collection of Art Nouveau buildings in the world. Riga has a lively cultural scene, with opera, music and festivals. It is affordable and a good base for exploring the Baltic region.

### Top Attractions

- **Old Town (Vecrīga):** Cobbled streets, churches and merchant houses, including the House of the Blackheads.
- **Art Nouveau District:** Elizabetes and Alberta streets have ornate facades by architects such as Mikhail Eisenstein.
- **Riga Central Market:** One of Europe's largest markets, housed in former Zeppelin hangars.
- **St. Peter's Church:** A church with a tower offering views of the city.
- **Freedom Monument:** A symbol of Latvian independence.
- **Latvian National Museum of Art:** A collection of Latvian and Baltic art.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Old Town is central and compact. The Quiet Centre (Klusais centrs) has Art Nouveau buildings and cafés. Āgenskalns, across the river, is a quieter area with wooden houses.

### Food & Drink

Latvian cuisine includes rye bread, grey peas with bacon, smoked fish and potato pancakes. Riga Black Balsam, a herbal liqueur, is a local speciality. The Central Market offers smoked fish and local produce. Restaurants range from traditional to modern Nordic-style.

### When to Visit

Summer brings long days and outdoor festivals, including Midsummer (Jāņi) celebrations in June. Winters are cold and dark, but Christmas markets are charming. Spring and autumn are mild.

### Getting Around

Riga is walkable, with trams and buses for longer trips. Bus 22 connects the airport with the centre. Trains reach Jūrmala and other nearby towns.

### Day Trips

Jūrmala, a beach resort with wooden villas, is 30 minutes away. Sigulda, in the Gauja National Park, offers castles and hiking. Rundāle Palace is a baroque palace designed by Rastrelli, the architect of St. Petersburg's Winter Palace.

### Practical Tips

- Riga's old town is compact; one or two days cover the main sights.
- Some restaurants in the Old Town target tourists with high prices.
- Try Riga Black Balsam mixed with blackcurrant juice.
- Card payments are widely accepted.

## Rio de Janeiro, Brazil

*The "Marvellous City" of beaches, mountains, samba and Carnival.*

- **Country:** Brazil
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~6.2 million (city); ~13 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Portuguese
- **Currency:** Brazilian Real (BRL)
- **Main airport:** Rio de Janeiro/Galeão International Airport (GIG); Santos Dumont (SDU)
- **Best time to visit:** September–March (but beach weather most of the year)
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Rio de Janeiro is one of the world's most spectacularly located cities, set between granite mountains, lush rainforest and the Atlantic Ocean. It was the capital of Brazil until 1960 and the seat of the Portuguese royal court in the early 19th century. Rio is famous for Carnival, samba, bossa nova and its beach culture. The city's landscapes are a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Inequality is visible, with favelas on the hills next to wealthy neighbourhoods.

### Top Attractions

- **Christ the Redeemer:** The giant statue on Corcovado mountain, one of the New Seven Wonders.
- **Sugarloaf Mountain:** Cable cars to the summit.
- **Copacabana and Ipanema beaches:** Famous beaches.
- **Selarón Steps:** Colourful tiled stairway in Lapa.
- **Tijuca National Park:** Urban rainforest.
- **Maracanã Stadium:** Legendary football stadium.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Ipanema, Leblon, Copacabana and Santa Teresa are popular.

### Food & Drink

Feijoada (black bean stew), churrasco, pão de queijo and açaí. Caipirinha is the national cocktail.

### When to Visit

Carnival takes place in February or March. Summers are hot.

### Getting Around

The metro, buses and ride-hailing apps.

### Day Trips

Petrópolis, Ilha Grande and Paraty.

### Practical Tips

- Don't carry valuables on the beach.
- Use apps for taxis.
- Book Carnival early.
- Avoid favela tours without reputable guides.

## Riyadh, Saudi Arabia

*Saudi Arabia's rapidly transforming capital of mud-brick heritage and modern towers.*

- **Country:** Saudi Arabia
- **Region:** Middle East
- **Population (approx.):** ~7.5 million
- **Language(s):** Arabic (English widely used in business)
- **Currency:** Saudi Riyal (SAR)
- **Main airport:** King Khalid International Airport (RUH)
- **Best time to visit:** November–March
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Riyadh, the capital of Saudi Arabia, lies in the heart of the Arabian Peninsula. It grew from a mud-brick oasis town into a modern metropolis after the discovery of oil. The city is changing rapidly under the Vision 2030 programme, with new entertainment venues, museums and tourism projects. The historic district of Diriyah, the birthplace of the first Saudi state, is a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Tourist visas have made Saudi Arabia accessible to international visitors since 2019.

### Top Attractions

- **Diriyah (At-Turaif):** A restored mud-brick district and UNESCO site, with museums and restaurants in Bujairi Terrace.
- **Kingdom Centre Tower:** A skyscraper with a sky bridge offering views.
- **Masmak Fortress:** A clay fort central to the founding of modern Saudi Arabia.
- **National Museum of Saudi Arabia:** Exhibitions on Arabian history and Islamic culture.
- **Edge of the World (Jebel Fihrayn):** Dramatic cliffs overlooking a vast plain, about 1.5 hours from the city.
- **Boulevard City:** An entertainment zone during Riyadh Season.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Olaya is the business district, with hotels and malls. Diriyah is historic, and the Diplomatic Quarter is green and quiet.

### Food & Drink

Kabsa, spiced rice with meat, is the national dish. Dates and Arabic coffee are served everywhere. Riyadh has a large international restaurant scene.

### When to Visit

November to March is pleasant. Summers are extremely hot. Riyadh Season, a festival of entertainment, runs in winter.

### Getting Around

The Riyadh Metro opened in stages from late 2024. Taxis and ride-hailing apps are widely used. The city is car-oriented.

### Day Trips

Edge of the World and the Red Sand Dunes are popular desert trips.

### Practical Tips

- Dress modestly; women are no longer required to wear an abaya but should cover shoulders and knees.
- Alcohol is prohibited.
- Friday is the holy day.
- Obtain an e-visa before travelling.

## Rome, Italy

*The Eternal City, where nearly 3,000 years of history fill every piazza.*

- **Country:** Italy
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~2.8 million (city); ~4.3 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Italian
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Leonardo da Vinci–Fiumicino (FCO), Ciampino (CIA)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Rome, the capital of Italy, was the centre of the Roman Empire and later of the Catholic Church, leaving an extraordinary legacy of monuments, art and architecture. Ancient ruins sit beside Renaissance palaces, baroque fountains and lively modern streets. Vatican City, an independent state within Rome, is home to St. Peter's Basilica and the Sistine Chapel. Rome is a city to explore slowly, enjoying its piazzas, trattorias and street life. It is busy year-round, especially during Jubilee years and religious holidays.

### Top Attractions

- **Colosseum:** The largest amphitheatre ever built, where gladiators fought; combined tickets include the Roman Forum and Palatine Hill.
- **Roman Forum and Palatine Hill:** The political heart of ancient Rome and the hill where emperors built their palaces.
- **Vatican Museums and Sistine Chapel:** Vast collections culminating in Michelangelo's famous ceiling.
- **St. Peter's Basilica:** The world's largest church, with Michelangelo's Pietà and a dome you can climb.
- **Pantheon:** A remarkably preserved Roman temple with a massive concrete dome, now a church.
- **Trevi Fountain:** A baroque fountain where visitors throw a coin to ensure their return to Rome.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Centro Storico, around Piazza Navona and the Pantheon, is central and walkable. Trastevere is charming, with ivy-covered streets and lively nightlife. Monti is trendy and close to the Colosseum, while Prati near the Vatican is quieter and elegant.

### Food & Drink

Roman classics include carbonara, cacio e pepe, amatriciana and gricia. Supplì (fried rice balls), pizza al taglio and carciofi alla giudia (fried artichokes) in the Jewish Ghetto are must-tries. Gelato and espresso are daily pleasures. Testaccio is known for traditional cuisine and its covered market.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are the best seasons. Summer is very hot and crowded, and many locals leave in August. Easter and Christmas bring special religious events at the Vatican.

### Getting Around

Rome has a limited metro, but buses and trams cover the city. The Leonardo Express train connects Fiumicino Airport with Termini station. Walking is the best way to explore the historic centre.

### Day Trips

Ostia Antica, an ancient Roman port city, is easily reachable by train. Tivoli has Hadrian's Villa and Villa d'Este with its fountains. Orvieto, Florence and Naples are also reachable by train.

### Practical Tips

- Book the Colosseum and Vatican Museums well in advance.
- Dress modestly for churches and the Vatican (shoulders and knees covered).
- Drink from the free "nasoni" water fountains around the city.
- Rome charges an access fee for the Trevi Fountain basin area in some periods; check current rules.

## Salvador, Brazil

*The heart of Afro-Brazilian culture, with a colourful colonial old town.*

- **Country:** Brazil
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~2.4 million
- **Language(s):** Portuguese
- **Currency:** Brazilian Real (BRL)
- **Main airport:** Deputado Luís Eduardo Magalhães International Airport (SSA)
- **Best time to visit:** September–March
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Salvador, the capital of Bahia, was Brazil's first capital (1549–1763) and a major port of the transatlantic slave trade. Today it is the centre of Afro-Brazilian culture, visible in its music, religion (Candomblé), capoeira and food. The historic centre, Pelourinho, is a UNESCO site with colourful colonial buildings. Salvador's Carnival is one of the largest street parties in the world.

### Top Attractions

- **Pelourinho:** Colonial centre with churches and music.
- **Elevador Lacerda:** Connects the upper and lower city.
- **São Francisco Church:** Baroque gold interiors.
- **Mercado Modelo:** Crafts market.
- **Barra:** Beach with a lighthouse.
- **Capoeira performances:** Displays of the Afro-Brazilian martial art that combines dance, acrobatics and music.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Pelourinho, Barra and Rio Vermelho.

### Food & Drink

Acarajé (bean fritters), moqueca baiana (seafood stew).

### When to Visit

September–March is dry; Carnival is huge.

### Getting Around

Taxis and apps.

### Day Trips

Morro de São Paulo and Praia do Forte.

### Practical Tips

- Watch belongings.
- Book Carnival early.
- Try acarajé.
- Respect religious traditions.

## Salzburg, Austria

*Mozart's baroque birthplace, framed by the Alps and The Sound of Music.*

- **Country:** Austria
- **Region:** Central Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~155,000
- **Language(s):** German
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Salzburg Airport W. A. Mozart (SZG); Munich Airport (MUC) is about 2 hours away
- **Best time to visit:** May–September; December for Christmas markets
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Salzburg is a beautiful baroque city on the Salzach River near the German border, surrounded by Alpine peaks. For centuries it was ruled by powerful prince-archbishops whose wealth from salt mining funded magnificent churches and palaces. Wolfgang Amadeus Mozart was born here in 1756, and music remains central to the city's identity. The historic centre is a UNESCO World Heritage Site, and the city was a filming location for The Sound of Music. Salzburg is compact, elegant and ideal for a short stay.

### Top Attractions

- **Hohensalzburg Fortress:** One of the largest medieval castles in Europe, reached by funicular, with panoramic views.
- **Mozart's Birthplace and Residence:** Museums dedicated to the life and work of the composer.
- **Salzburg Cathedral:** A baroque cathedral where Mozart was baptised.
- **Mirabell Palace and Gardens:** Formal gardens featured in The Sound of Music.
- **Getreidegasse:** A narrow shopping street with wrought-iron guild signs.
- **Hellbrunn Palace:** A palace with playful trick fountains, a short trip from the city.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Altstadt (Old Town) on the left bank is the most picturesque area. The right bank around Linzergasse and Mirabell is slightly quieter. Staying outside the centre is cheaper and well connected by bus.

### Food & Drink

Austrian classics include Wiener Schnitzel, Kaiserschmarrn (shredded pancake) and apple strudel. Salzburger Nockerl is a sweet soufflé representing the city's mountains. Mozartkugeln, chocolate-covered marzipan balls, are a famous souvenir. Augustiner Bräu is a monastic brewery with a large beer garden.

### When to Visit

Summer brings the world-famous Salzburg Festival of music and theatre in July and August. Christmas markets and Advent concerts create a festive atmosphere in December. Spring and autumn are quieter and pleasant.

### Getting Around

Salzburg's centre is walkable, and trolleybuses cover the city. Trains connect Salzburg with Vienna (about 2.5 hours) and Munich (about 1.5 hours). The Salzburg Card includes transport and attraction entry.

### Day Trips

Hallstatt, a picturesque lakeside village, is a popular trip, though it is very crowded. The Eagle's Nest near Berchtesgaden, in Germany, and the salt mines of Hallein offer history and views. Werfen has the world's largest ice cave, Eisriesenwelt.

### Practical Tips

- Book Salzburg Festival tickets months ahead.
- The Salzburg Card is good value if visiting several sights.
- Visit Hallstatt early or late to avoid tour groups.
- Many shops close early on Saturdays and all day Sunday.

## Samarkand, Uzbekistan

*A legendary Silk Road city of blue domes, madrasas and Timurid splendour.*

- **Country:** Uzbekistan
- **Region:** Central Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~570,000
- **Language(s):** Uzbek (Tajik and Russian widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Uzbekistani Som (UZS)
- **Main airport:** Samarkand International Airport (SKD)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Samarkand is one of the oldest cities in Central Asia, with a history of over 2,500 years. It flourished as a key stop on the Silk Road and became the capital of Timur (Tamerlane) in the 14th century. His dynasty built magnificent mosques, madrasas and mausoleums decorated with turquoise tiles. Samarkand is a UNESCO World Heritage Site, often called the "Crossroads of Cultures". It is easily reached from Tashkent by high-speed train.

### Top Attractions

- **Registan:** A square framed by three grand madrasas, one of the most spectacular architectural ensembles in the world.
- **Gur-e-Amir:** The mausoleum of Timur, with a ribbed azure dome.
- **Shah-i-Zinda:** An avenue of mausoleums with dazzling tilework.
- **Bibi-Khanym Mosque:** A huge mosque built by Timur.
- **Ulugh Beg Observatory:** Remains of a 15th-century observatory.
- **Siab Bazaar:** A market selling bread, dried fruits and spices.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Staying near the Registan is convenient. Guesthouses in the old town offer hospitality.

### Food & Drink

Plov, rice with meat and carrots, is the national dish. Samarkand bread (non) is famous. Shashlik (grilled meat) and samsa (baked pastries) are common.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal. Summers are hot, and winters are cold.

### Getting Around

Taxis and ride-hailing apps are cheap. Afrosiyob high-speed trains connect Samarkand with Tashkent and Bukhara.

### Day Trips

Shahrisabz, Timur's birthplace, is a few hours away. Urgut has a large market.

### Practical Tips

- Book train tickets in advance.
- Dress modestly at religious sites.
- Carry cash.
- Visit the Registan at night for the light show.

## San Diego, United States (California)

*America's finest city, with beaches, parks and a perfect climate.*

- **Country:** United States (California)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.4 million (city); ~3.3 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English (Spanish widely spoken)
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** San Diego International Airport (SAN)
- **Best time to visit:** Year-round; September–November is especially pleasant
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

San Diego, in southern California near the Mexican border, is known for its mild climate, beaches and relaxed lifestyle. It was the site of the first Spanish mission in California in 1769. The city is home to a large US Navy presence, Balboa Park and the famous San Diego Zoo. San Diego has a strong craft beer scene. It is ideal for outdoor activities year-round.

### Top Attractions

- **Balboa Park:** Museums, gardens and architecture.
- **San Diego Zoo:** One of the world's best zoos.
- **USS Midway Museum:** An aircraft carrier museum.
- **La Jolla Cove:** Sea lions and snorkelling.
- **Old Town San Diego:** History and Mexican food.
- **Coronado Beach:** A wide, sparkling beach in front of the historic Hotel del Coronado.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Gaslamp Quarter, Little Italy, La Jolla and Pacific Beach.

### Food & Drink

Fish tacos, California burritos and craft beer.

### When to Visit

Mild year-round; "June Gloom" brings overcast mornings.

### Getting Around

Trolley and buses; a car is useful.

### Day Trips

Tijuana, Temecula and Anza-Borrego.

### Practical Tips

- Book zoo tickets online.
- Use sunscreen.
- Respect sea lions.
- Try fish tacos.

## San Francisco, United States (California)

*A hilly city of cable cars, the Golden Gate Bridge and tech innovation.*

- **Country:** United States (California)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~810,000 (city); ~7.5 million (Bay Area)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** San Francisco International Airport (SFO); Oakland (OAK)
- **Best time to visit:** September–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

San Francisco is a compact city on a peninsula between the Pacific and San Francisco Bay. It grew during the 1849 Gold Rush and was rebuilt after the 1906 earthquake. The city is famous for its hills, Victorian houses, cable cars and the Golden Gate Bridge. It is a centre of counterculture, LGBTQ+ rights and technology. Nearby Silicon Valley is the global tech hub.

### Top Attractions

- **Golden Gate Bridge:** The iconic bridge.
- **Alcatraz Island:** Former federal prison.
- **Cable cars:** Historic transport.
- **Fisherman's Wharf and Pier 39:** Sea lions and seafood.
- **Golden Gate Park:** Museums and gardens.
- **Chinatown:** One of the oldest in North America.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Union Square, North Beach, the Mission and Hayes Valley.

### Food & Drink

Sourdough bread, clam chowder in bread bowls, Mission burritos and Dungeness crab.

### When to Visit

Autumn is warmest; summers can be foggy and cool.

### Getting Around

Muni, BART and cable cars.

### Day Trips

Napa and Sonoma, Muir Woods and Sausalito.

### Practical Tips

- Bring layers.
- Book Alcatraz early.
- Don't leave valuables in cars.
- Use Clipper card.

## San José, Costa Rica

*Costa Rica's capital and the hub for exploring volcanoes, cloud forests and beaches.*

- **Country:** Costa Rica
- **Region:** Central America
- **Population (approx.):** ~350,000 (city); ~2.2 million (Greater Metropolitan Area)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Costa Rican Colón (CRC); US Dollars widely accepted
- **Main airport:** Juan Santamaría International Airport (SJO)
- **Best time to visit:** December–April (dry season)
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

San José, the capital of Costa Rica, sits in the fertile Central Valley at about 1,150 metres above sea level, surrounded by volcanoes and coffee plantations. It became the capital in 1823 and grew wealthy from the coffee trade in the 19th century, which funded elegant buildings such as the National Theatre. Many travellers pass through quickly on their way to the rainforests and beaches, but the city offers good museums, markets and a lively café and food scene. Costa Rica is known for its commitment to conservation, political stability and the national motto "Pura Vida". San José's central location makes it a practical base for day trips across the country.

### Top Attractions

- **National Theatre of Costa Rica:** An opulent neoclassical theatre from 1897 with marble, gold leaf and painted ceilings, and a café inside.
- **Pre-Columbian Gold Museum:** An underground museum beneath the Plaza de la Cultura, with thousands of indigenous gold artefacts.
- **National Museum of Costa Rica:** Housed in a former military barracks (the Bellavista Fortress) with a butterfly garden; Costa Rica abolished its army in 1948.
- **Central Market (Mercado Central):** A historic market with small sodas (eateries), spices, coffee and crafts.
- **Barrio Amón and Barrio Escalante:** Neighbourhoods with colonial mansions, street art, galleries and trendy restaurants.
- **Jade Museum:** A modern museum with the world's largest collection of American jade.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Barrio Escalante is the city's gastronomic district, with restaurants and craft beer bars. Barrio Amón is historic and central. Escazú and Santa Ana, in the western suburbs, are upscale areas with modern hotels and malls, while Alajuela, near the airport, is convenient for early flights.

### Food & Drink

Gallo pinto, rice and beans often served with eggs, plantains and sour cream, is the national breakfast. Casados, set plates of rice, beans, salad, plantain and meat or fish, are served in sodas at lunchtime. Costa Rican coffee is among the world's best, and many cafés offer single-origin brews. Salsa Lizano is the ubiquitous local sauce.

### When to Visit

The dry season from December to April offers sunny days and is the peak tourist season. The green season (May–November) brings afternoon rains, lush landscapes and lower prices. The Central Valley has a spring-like climate year-round.

### Getting Around

Buses are cheap but can be confusing; Uber and official red taxis are widely used. Traffic can be heavy during rush hours. Shuttle services and domestic flights connect San José with beaches and national parks.

### Day Trips

Poás and Irazú volcanoes, with crater viewpoints, are within 1–2 hours. La Paz Waterfall Gardens, coffee plantation tours (such as Doka Estate or Britt) and white-water rafting on the Pacuare River are popular. Manuel Antonio National Park and Monteverde are longer trips.

### Practical Tips

- Carry small change; colones and US dollars are both widely accepted.
- Avoid walking alone in quiet areas at night and keep valuables hidden.
- Check volcano park reservations online before visiting.
- Pack a light rain jacket, especially in the green season.

## San Juan, Puerto Rico (US territory)

*A colourful colonial city of forts, beaches and salsa rhythm.*

- **Country:** Puerto Rico (US territory)
- **Region:** Caribbean
- **Population (approx.):** ~340,000 (city); ~2 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish and English
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Luis Muñoz Marín International Airport (SJU)
- **Best time to visit:** December–April
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

San Juan, the capital of Puerto Rico, is one of the oldest European-founded cities in the Americas, established by the Spanish in 1521. Its historic district, Old San Juan, has pastel-coloured buildings, blue cobblestone streets and massive fortifications, part of a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Puerto Rico is a US territory, so US citizens do not need a passport, and the US dollar is the currency. The city blends Spanish colonial heritage, Caribbean culture and American influences. It is famous for its beaches, nightlife, salsa and reggaeton music.

### Top Attractions

- **Castillo San Felipe del Morro (El Morro):** A 16th-century fortress guarding the entrance to San Juan Bay, with sweeping ocean views.
- **Castillo San Cristóbal:** The largest fortress built by the Spanish in the Americas, with tunnels and ramparts.
- **Old San Juan:** Colourful streets, plazas, the Cathedral of San Juan Bautista and shops.
- **Condado and Isla Verde beaches:** Urban beaches with resorts and water sports.
- **La Placita de Santurce:** A market square that turns into a lively nightlife spot.
- **Paseo de la Princesa:** A promenade along the city walls.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Old San Juan is historic and charming. Condado is upscale, with beachfront hotels, while Isla Verde is near the airport. Santurce is known for street art and nightlife.

### Food & Drink

Puerto Rican cuisine includes mofongo (mashed fried plantains with garlic), lechón (roast pork), arroz con gandules and alcapurrias (fritters). The piña colada was reportedly invented in San Juan. Local rum is excellent, and Bacardí's distillery is across the bay.

### When to Visit

December to April is the dry season. Hurricane season runs from June to November. The San Sebastián Street Festival in January is a huge celebration.

### Getting Around

Old San Juan is walkable, and free trolleys run in some periods. Uber and taxis are widely used. Renting a car is ideal for exploring the island.

### Day Trips

El Yunque National Forest, the only tropical rainforest in the US National Forest System, is about 45 minutes away. Bioluminescent bays at Fajardo, Vieques and La Parguera glow at night. Culebra's Flamenco Beach is one of the Caribbean's most beautiful beaches.

### Practical Tips

- US citizens do not need a passport.
- Check hurricane forecasts in the rainy season.
- Use reef-safe sunscreen.
- Book bioluminescent bay tours in advance, ideally around the new moon.

## San Miguel de Allende, Mexico

*A colonial highland town of pink spires, art and cobblestone streets.*

- **Country:** Mexico
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~175,000 (municipality)
- **Language(s):** Spanish (English widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Mexican Peso (MXN)
- **Main airport:** León/Bajío International Airport (BJX); Querétaro International Airport (QRO)
- **Best time to visit:** October–April
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

San Miguel de Allende is a colonial town in the state of Guanajuato in central Mexico, located about 1,900 metres above sea level. It played an important role in Mexico's War of Independence, and it is named after Ignacio Allende, one of the independence heroes. Its historic centre, with baroque churches and colourful houses, is a UNESCO World Heritage Site. The town has attracted artists and foreign residents since the mid-20th century, creating a vibrant arts and expat community. San Miguel is often ranked among the world's best small cities by travel magazines.

### Top Attractions

- **Parroquia de San Miguel Arcángel:** A neo-Gothic pink parish church, the town's iconic landmark.
- **El Jardín:** The main square, where locals and visitors gather.
- **Fábrica La Aurora:** A former textile factory turned into an art and design centre.
- **El Charco del Ingenio:** A botanical garden with cacti and canyon views.
- **Mercado de Artesanías:** A market for crafts.
- **Hot springs:** Natural thermal pools near town, such as La Gruta.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

El Centro is the best area to stay. Guadiana and San Antonio are quieter residential areas nearby.

### Food & Drink

The town has a sophisticated restaurant scene, with rooftop bars and Mexican fusion. Street food includes tacos, gorditas and elotes (corn). Local wines from Guanajuato's vineyards are gaining a reputation.

### When to Visit

October to April is dry and pleasant. Festivals include Día de los Muertos and the Alborada in late September.

### Getting Around

The centre is walkable, but the cobblestones and hills require good shoes. Taxis are inexpensive.

### Day Trips

Guanajuato, a colourful colonial city of tunnels, is about 1.5 hours away. The Sanctuary of Atotonilco is a UNESCO-listed church with frescoes. Vineyards and the town of Dolores Hidalgo are nearby.

### Practical Tips

- Wear comfortable shoes.
- Book accommodation early for festivals.
- Evenings can be cool; bring layers.
- Try local wines.

## Santiago, Chile

*A modern capital framed by the Andes, with wineries and ski slopes nearby.*

- **Country:** Chile
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~6.9 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Chilean Peso (CLP)
- **Main airport:** Arturo Merino Benítez International Airport (SCL)
- **Best time to visit:** September–November and March–May
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Santiago, the capital of Chile, lies in a valley between the Andes and the Chilean Coastal Range. Founded by Spanish conquistador Pedro de Valdivia in 1541, it is now one of South America's most modern and prosperous cities. The snow-capped Andes provide a dramatic backdrop on clear days. Santiago has excellent museums, a growing food scene and lively neighbourhoods. It is the gateway to Chile's wine valleys, ski resorts, Valparaíso and further destinations such as Patagonia and the Atacama Desert.

### Top Attractions

- **Cerro San Cristóbal:** A hill in the Parque Metropolitano with a funicular, cable car and a statue of the Virgin Mary, offering views over the city.
- **Plaza de Armas:** The historic main square, with the Metropolitan Cathedral and the Central Post Office.
- **La Moneda Palace:** The presidential palace, with a changing of the guard ceremony and a cultural centre below.
- **Museum of Memory and Human Rights:** A moving museum on the Pinochet dictatorship (1973–1990).
- **Chilean Museum of Pre-Columbian Art:** A superb collection of indigenous art from across the Americas.
- **Sky Costanera:** The observation deck of the tallest building in South America.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Providencia and Las Condes are safe, modern areas with hotels and restaurants. Lastarria and Bellas Artes are bohemian, with cafés and galleries, while Barrio Italia has boutiques and design shops. Bellavista is known for nightlife and Pablo Neruda's house, La Chascona.

### Food & Drink

Chilean dishes include empanadas de pino (beef, onion, egg and olive), pastel de choclo (corn pie), completos (loaded hot dogs) and seafood from the long coastline. The Mercado Central is famous for seafood. Pisco sour and wine, especially Carménère, are national favourites. Once (afternoon tea) is a Chilean tradition.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal. Summers are hot and dry, and winters are cool, with smog but good skiing. The Fiestas Patrias in September celebrate independence.

### Getting Around

Santiago has an efficient metro system. Buses and ride-hailing apps are common. The airport is about 30 minutes from the centre.

### Day Trips

Valparaíso and Viña del Mar are about 1.5 hours away. The Maipo, Casablanca and Colchagua wine valleys offer tastings. Ski resorts such as Valle Nevado and Farellones are close in winter, and the Cajón del Maipo canyon is good for hiking.

### Practical Tips

- Keep an eye on belongings in crowded areas.
- Book wine tours in advance.
- Check air quality in winter.
- Earthquakes are common; learn basic safety procedures.

## Santo Domingo, Dominican Republic

*The oldest European city in the Americas, with a vibrant colonial zone and merengue rhythms.*

- **Country:** Dominican Republic
- **Region:** Caribbean
- **Population (approx.):** ~1 million (National District); ~3.9 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Dominican Peso (DOP)
- **Main airport:** Las Américas International Airport (SDQ)
- **Best time to visit:** December–April
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Santo Domingo, the capital of the Dominican Republic, was founded in 1496 by Bartholomew Columbus, making it the oldest continuously inhabited European settlement in the Americas. Its Ciudad Colonial is a UNESCO World Heritage Site, with the first cathedral, hospital, university and fortress built by Europeans in the New World. The city is a bustling Caribbean capital with lively music, especially merengue and bachata. While most tourists head to resorts in Punta Cana, Santo Domingo offers history and authentic urban culture. The Malecón seafront boulevard is the city's social heart.

### Top Attractions

- **Ciudad Colonial:** The historic zone with cobbled streets and colonial buildings.
- **Catedral Primada de América:** The first cathedral in the Americas, completed in 1541.
- **Alcázar de Colón:** The palace of Diego Columbus, son of Christopher Columbus.
- **Fortaleza Ozama:** The oldest European military building in the Americas.
- **Los Tres Ojos:** A series of cave lakes in a national park.
- **Malecón:** A seafront promenade with restaurants and nightlife.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Ciudad Colonial is atmospheric. Piantini and Naco are modern upscale areas.

### Food & Drink

La bandera dominicana (rice, beans and meat), mangú (mashed plantains), sancocho (stew) and tostones. Presidente beer and rum are popular.

### When to Visit

December to April is dry. Hurricane season runs June–November. Carnival takes place in February.

### Getting Around

Taxis, ride-hailing apps and a metro system are available. The colonial zone is walkable.

### Day Trips

Boca Chica beach, Juan Dolio and the city of La Romana (Altos de Chavón) are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Use Uber for safety.
- Carry cash.
- Dress modestly in churches.
- Try merengue dancing.

## São Paulo, Brazil

*South America's largest city, a cosmopolitan powerhouse of food, art and nightlife.*

- **Country:** Brazil
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~11.5 million (city); ~22 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Portuguese
- **Currency:** Brazilian Real (BRL)
- **Main airport:** São Paulo/Guarulhos International Airport (GRU); Congonhas (CGH)
- **Best time to visit:** April–May and September–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

São Paulo is the largest city in South America and Brazil's economic engine.
