It is a vast, diverse metropolis shaped by immigrants from Italy, Japan, Lebanon, Portugal, Germany and elsewhere; it has the largest Japanese community outside Japan. While it lacks beaches, São Paulo offers world-class restaurants, museums, nightlife and shopping. The city has a vibrant arts scene, including street art and major cultural institutions. Locals, called Paulistanos, are known for their work ethic and love of food.

### Top Attractions

- **Avenida Paulista:** The city's main avenue, closed to cars on Sundays, with MASP (São Paulo Museum of Art).
- **Ibirapuera Park:** A large park with museums designed by Oscar Niemeyer.
- **Mercado Municipal:** A historic market famous for mortadella sandwiches.
- **Beco do Batman:** An alley covered in street art in Vila Madalena.
- **Liberdade:** The Japanese district.
- **Pinacoteca:** An art museum.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Jardins, Vila Madalena and Pinheiros are popular.

### Food & Drink

São Paulo has thousands of restaurants, with Italian, Japanese and Brazilian cuisines. Try pizza, pastel and feijoada.

### When to Visit

Autumn and spring are pleasant.

### Getting Around

The metro is efficient. Traffic is heavy.

### Day Trips

Santos, Paraty and Campos do Jordão.

### Practical Tips

- Use apps for taxis.
- Watch belongings.
- Book popular restaurants.
- Avoid rush hour.

## Sapporo, Japan

*Hokkaido's capital of snow festivals, beer, ramen and nearby ski resorts.*

- **Country:** Japan
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.95 million
- **Language(s):** Japanese
- **Currency:** Japanese Yen (JPY)
- **Main airport:** New Chitose Airport (CTS)
- **Best time to visit:** December–February for snow; June–August for summer
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Sapporo is the capital of Hokkaido, Japan's northernmost main island. Founded in the 19th century, it has a grid layout and wide streets unlike most Japanese cities. The city hosted the 1972 Winter Olympics and is famous for its Snow Festival. Sapporo is known for its beer, miso ramen and seafood. It is a base for skiing in Niseko and exploring Hokkaido's nature.

### Top Attractions

- **Sapporo Snow Festival:** Giant snow and ice sculptures in February.
- **Odori Park:** A long park in the city centre.
- **Sapporo Beer Museum:** The history of Japan's oldest beer brand.
- **Mount Moiwa:** Views over the city.
- **Susukino:** The nightlife district.
- **Shiroi Koibito Park:** A chocolate factory and theme park.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Staying near Sapporo Station, Odori or Susukino is convenient.

### Food & Drink

Miso ramen, soup curry and Genghis Khan (grilled lamb) are local specialities. Seafood, especially crab and sea urchin, is excellent.

### When to Visit

Winter brings snow and the festival. Summer is cool and pleasant.

### Getting Around

Sapporo has a subway and trams. Trains connect with New Chitose Airport.

### Day Trips

Otaru, a canal town, is 30 minutes away. Niseko is a ski resort. Furano has lavender fields.

### Practical Tips

- Book accommodation early for the Snow Festival.
- Wear warm clothing in winter.
- Try ramen in Ramen Yokocho.
- Get an IC card.

## Sarajevo, Bosnia and Herzegovina

*A city where East meets West, with Ottoman bazaars, Austro-Hungarian avenues and a resilient spirit.*

- **Country:** Bosnia and Herzegovina
- **Region:** Southeastern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~275,000 (city); ~550,000 (metro)
- **Language(s):** Bosnian, Croatian and Serbian
- **Currency:** Convertible Mark (BAM)
- **Main airport:** Sarajevo International Airport (SJJ)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Sarajevo, the capital of Bosnia and Herzegovina, lies in a valley surrounded by mountains. It has long been a meeting point of cultures, with mosques, Orthodox and Catholic churches and synagogues within a short walk of each other. The assassination of Archduke Franz Ferdinand here in 1914 triggered the First World War, and the city hosted the 1984 Winter Olympics. During the 1992–1996 siege, Sarajevo suffered greatly, and memorials recall this history. Today it is a welcoming, affordable city with a vibrant café culture.

### Top Attractions

- **Baščaršija:** The old Ottoman bazaar with copper workshops, the Sebilj fountain and cafés.
- **Gazi Husrev-beg Mosque:** The most important Ottoman mosque in Bosnia.
- **Latin Bridge:** The site of the 1914 assassination, with a small museum nearby.
- **War Tunnel Museum (Tunnel of Hope):** A tunnel dug under the airport during the siege to supply the city.
- **Yellow Fortress:** A viewpoint popular at sunset.
- **Trebević Cable Car:** A cable car to Mount Trebević, with the abandoned Olympic bobsled track.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Staying near Baščaršija or Ferhadija Street is convenient for sightseeing. Marijin Dvor is a more modern area, and the hills around the old town offer guesthouses with views. The city is compact and walkable.

### Food & Drink

Ćevapi, grilled minced meat in flatbread with onions and kajmak, is Sarajevo's signature dish. Burek and other pies (pita) are also popular. Bosnian coffee is served in a copper pot (džezva) with Turkish delight. Sweets such as baklava and tufahija (stuffed apples) are common.

### When to Visit

Late spring through early autumn is ideal. The Sarajevo Film Festival in August is a major event. Winters are cold and snowy, and nearby mountains offer skiing.

### Getting Around

The old town is walkable, and trams run along the main avenue. Taxis are affordable; agree on the fare or use the meter. Buses and trains connect Sarajevo with Mostar.

### Day Trips

Mostar, with its iconic Old Bridge, is about two hours away by train. The Kravica waterfalls and Počitelj village are nearby. Jahorina and Bjelašnica, Olympic ski resorts, offer winter sports and summer hiking.

### Practical Tips

- Dress modestly when visiting mosques.
- Cash is useful in smaller shops.
- Stay on marked paths when hiking, as landmines remain in some rural areas.
- Learn about recent history with a guided tour.

## Savannah, United States (Georgia)

*A Southern charmer of oak-shaded squares, Spanish moss and historic mansions.*

- **Country:** United States (Georgia)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~150,000 (city); ~420,000 (metro)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Savannah/Hilton Head International Airport (SAV)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and October–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Savannah, founded in 1733 by General James Oglethorpe, is Georgia's oldest city and one of the best-preserved historic cities in the United States. Its unique town plan of 22 leafy squares, shaded by live oaks draped in Spanish moss, creates a romantic atmosphere. The city was spared destruction during the Civil War, when General Sherman presented it to President Lincoln as a "Christmas gift". Savannah is known for its antebellum architecture, ghost stories and Southern hospitality. The Savannah College of Art and Design (SCAD) adds a creative, youthful energy.

### Top Attractions

- **Historic District squares:** Walk through squares such as Chippewa, Lafayette and Madison, each with monuments and fountains.
- **Forsyth Park:** A large park with a famous white fountain.
- **River Street:** Cobblestone waterfront with shops and restaurants in old cotton warehouses.
- **Cathedral of St. John the Baptist:** A French Gothic-style cathedral with stained glass.
- **Bonaventure Cemetery:** A hauntingly beautiful cemetery made famous by the book Midnight in the Garden of Good and Evil.
- **Owens-Thomas House & Slave Quarters:** A house museum that presents the lives of both the owners and the enslaved.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Historic District is the best place to stay. The Starland District is artsy and trendy, and Tybee Island offers beaches nearby.

### Food & Drink

Southern cuisine includes shrimp and grits, fried chicken, Lowcountry boil and pecan pralines. Family-style restaurants like Mrs. Wilkes' Dining Room are institutions. Savannah allows open containers of alcohol in plastic cups in the Historic District.

### When to Visit

Spring (with blooming azaleas) and autumn are ideal. Summers are hot and humid. St. Patrick's Day is one of the largest celebrations in the US.

### Getting Around

The Historic District is walkable. A free DOT shuttle and ferry serve downtown. Trolley tours are popular.

### Day Trips

Tybee Island, Hilton Head Island and Charleston (about two hours) are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Take a ghost tour at night.
- Book early for St. Patrick's Day.
- Wear comfortable shoes.
- Carry insect repellent in warm months.

## Seattle, United States (Washington)

*The Emerald City of coffee, tech, music and mountain views.*

- **Country:** United States (Washington)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~760,000 (city); ~4 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Seattle–Tacoma International Airport (SEA)
- **Best time to visit:** June–September
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Seattle, in the Pacific Northwest, lies between Puget Sound and Lake Washington, with Mount Rainier towering on clear days. The city grew as a gateway to the Klondike Gold Rush and later became a hub for aviation (Boeing) and technology (Microsoft, Amazon). Seattle is the birthplace of Starbucks and grunge music, with bands like Nirvana and Pearl Jam. Its surroundings offer forests, mountains, islands and water sports. It has a reputation for rain, though summers are dry and sunny.

### Top Attractions

- **Pike Place Market:** A historic market famous for fish throwing and the original Starbucks.
- **Space Needle:** An observation tower built for the 1962 World's Fair.
- **Chihuly Garden and Glass:** Stunning glass art by Dale Chihuly.
- **Museum of Pop Culture (MoPOP):** A museum of music and pop culture.
- **Kerry Park:** The classic view of the skyline and Mount Rainier.
- **Seattle Waterfront:** A redesigned promenade with the aquarium and ferries.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Downtown, Capitol Hill, Ballard and Fremont are popular.

### Food & Drink

Seafood, especially salmon, oysters and clam chowder, is excellent. Coffee culture is strong. Seattle-style hot dogs with cream cheese are a local quirk.

### When to Visit

Summer is sunny and dry. Autumn to spring is rainy and grey.

### Getting Around

Light rail connects the airport with downtown. Buses and ferries are useful.

### Day Trips

Mount Rainier National Park, Olympic National Park, Bainbridge Island and the San Juan Islands.

### Practical Tips

- Bring a rain jacket.
- Use the ORCA card.
- Take the ferry for views.
- Visit Pike Place early.

## Seoul, South Korea

*A high-tech capital of palaces, K-culture, street food and mountain trails.*

- **Country:** South Korea
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~9.4 million (city); ~26 million (Seoul Capital Area)
- **Language(s):** Korean
- **Currency:** South Korean Won (KRW)
- **Main airport:** Incheon International Airport (ICN); Gimpo International Airport (GMP)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Seoul, the capital of South Korea, has been the country's political and cultural centre for over 600 years since the Joseon dynasty made it its capital in 1394. It is a dynamic megacity where ancient palaces and Buddhist temples sit among skyscrapers, neon-lit shopping districts and cutting-edge technology. Seoul is at the heart of the global popularity of K-pop, K-dramas, Korean beauty and cuisine. The city is surrounded by mountains, with hiking trails accessible by subway, and divided by the Han River. It is safe, efficient and full of energy day and night.

### Top Attractions

- **Gyeongbokgung Palace:** The largest of Seoul's five grand palaces, with a changing of the guard ceremony; entry is free if you wear a hanbok (traditional dress).
- **Bukchon Hanok Village:** A neighbourhood of traditional wooden houses between palaces; visiting hours are restricted to respect residents.
- **Changdeokgung and the Secret Garden:** A UNESCO-listed palace with a beautiful rear garden visited by guided tour.
- **N Seoul Tower:** A tower on Namsan Mountain with views over the city, reached by cable car or on foot.
- **Myeongdong and Insadong:** Shopping districts for cosmetics, street food, crafts and tea houses.
- **DMZ (Demilitarized Zone):** Guided tours to the border with North Korea, including tunnels and observation points; check availability as tours may be suspended.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Myeongdong and Jongno are central and close to palaces. Hongdae is a youthful area with music, clubs and street performers, while Itaewon is international. Gangnam is upscale and business-oriented, and Seongsu is a trendy area of cafés in converted factories.

### Food & Drink

Korean barbecue (samgyeopsal, bulgogi), bibimbap, kimchi stew and fried chicken with beer (chimaek) are must-tries. Gwangjang Market is famous for bindaetteok (mung bean pancakes) and mayak gimbap. Tteokbokki (spicy rice cakes) and hotteok (sweet pancakes) are popular street foods. Soju is the national drink.

### When to Visit

Spring cherry blossoms and autumn foliage are beautiful. Summers are hot, humid and rainy during the monsoon, while winters are cold and dry. Chuseok (harvest festival) and Seollal (Lunar New Year) are major holidays when some businesses close.

### Getting Around

Seoul's subway is extensive, clean and efficient. The T-money card works on subways, buses and in convenience stores. The AREX train connects Incheon Airport with Seoul Station.

### Day Trips

The DMZ, Suwon's Hwaseong Fortress and Nami Island are popular. Bukhansan National Park offers hiking within the city limits. Jeonju's hanok village is reachable by train.

### Practical Tips

- Use Naver Map or KakaoMap; Google Maps navigation is limited.
- Get a T-money card at the airport or convenience stores.
- Tipping is not customary.
- Many restaurants have minimum orders for two people.

## Seville, Spain

*The passionate heart of Andalusia, home of flamenco, orange trees and grand Moorish palaces.*

- **Country:** Spain
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~680,000 (city); ~1.5 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Seville Airport, San Pablo (SVQ)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and October–November
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Seville, the capital of Andalusia, is one of Spain's most romantic cities, known for its flamenco, colourful festivals and Moorish-influenced architecture. It was a major city of Al-Andalus and later the monopoly port for trade with the Americas, bringing immense wealth. The cathedral, the Alcázar and the Archive of the Indies form a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Seville's streets are lined with orange trees, and its plazas are full of life. It is one of the hottest cities in Europe in summer.

### Top Attractions

- **Real Alcázar:** A royal palace blending Mudéjar, Gothic and Renaissance styles, with beautiful gardens.
- **Seville Cathedral and Giralda:** The world's largest Gothic cathedral, with the tomb of Christopher Columbus and a tower you can climb via ramps.
- **Plaza de España:** A monumental semicircular plaza with tiled alcoves representing Spanish provinces.
- **Barrio Santa Cruz:** The former Jewish quarter of narrow lanes and flower-filled courtyards.
- **Metropol Parasol (Las Setas):** A modern wooden structure with a rooftop walkway.
- **Triana:** A traditional neighbourhood across the river known for ceramics and flamenco.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Santa Cruz and the area near the cathedral are central and atmospheric. Triana is authentic and lively, while Alameda de Hércules is trendy, with bars. El Arenal is close to the bullring and the river.

### Food & Drink

Seville is a tapas city: try salmorejo (a thick cold tomato soup), espinacas con garbanzos, pringá, and fried fish. Jamón ibérico and olives are everywhere. Tinto de verano and sherry from nearby Jerez are popular drinks. Late-night tapas crawls are part of local life.

### When to Visit

Spring is the best time, with Holy Week (Semana Santa) processions and the April Fair (Feria de Abril). Summer temperatures often exceed 40°C. Autumn and winter are mild and pleasant.

### Getting Around

The historic centre is walkable, and trams, buses and a metro line serve the city. The airport bus connects with the centre in about 35 minutes. High-speed trains reach Madrid in about 2.5 hours.

### Day Trips

Córdoba, with its Mezquita-Catedral, is 45 minutes away by high-speed train. Cádiz and Jerez de la Frontera, the home of sherry, are also close. The white villages (pueblos blancos) and Ronda are popular by car.

### Practical Tips

- Book the Alcázar and cathedral online in advance.
- Avoid sightseeing in the midday summer heat.
- Watch a flamenco show at a small tablao or peña.
- Book early for Holy Week and the April Fair.

## Shanghai, China

*China's cosmopolitan megacity of colonial boulevards and a futuristic skyline.*

- **Country:** China
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~24.8 million
- **Language(s):** Mandarin Chinese (Shanghainese locally)
- **Currency:** Chinese Yuan Renminbi (CNY)
- **Main airport:** Shanghai Pudong International (PVG); Shanghai Hongqiao International (SHA)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and September–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Shanghai, on the Yangtze River Delta, is China's largest city and its financial centre. It grew from a fishing town into a major port after the First Opium War, when foreign concessions were established. Its Bund waterfront showcases colonial-era buildings, while across the Huangpu River, Pudong's skyline features some of the world's tallest buildings. Shanghai is cosmopolitan, stylish and fast-paced, with world-class dining, shopping and nightlife. It is also a gateway to the canal towns of the Yangtze Delta.

### Top Attractions

- **The Bund:** A riverside promenade lined with colonial buildings, facing Pudong's skyline.
- **Shanghai Tower:** The tallest building in China, with one of the world's highest observation decks.
- **Yu Garden:** A classical Chinese garden from the Ming dynasty, surrounded by a bazaar.
- **French Concession:** Tree-lined streets with villas, cafés and boutiques.
- **Shanghai Museum:** Collections of ancient Chinese art, bronzes and ceramics.
- **Tianzifang and Xintiandi:** Restored shikumen (stone-gate) houses with shops and restaurants.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Bund and People's Square are central. The former French Concession is charming, with cafés and nightlife, and Jing'an is upscale and modern. Pudong is the business district.

### Food & Drink

Shanghai's specialities include xiaolongbao (soup dumplings), shengjian bao (pan-fried buns), hairy crab in autumn and red-braised pork. Street food and dumpling shops are everywhere. The city also has an excellent international dining scene.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are best. Summers are hot, humid and rainy, and winters are damp and cold. Avoid the October Golden Week holiday.

### Getting Around

Shanghai has one of the world's largest metro systems. The Maglev train connects Pudong Airport with the city at over 300 km/h. DiDi is widely used.

### Day Trips

Water towns such as Zhujiajiao, Zhouzhuang and Wuzhen are popular. Suzhou, with classical gardens, and Hangzhou are short trips by high-speed train.

### Practical Tips

- Set up Alipay or WeChat Pay before arrival.
- Many foreign apps are blocked; plan internet access.
- Carry your passport.
- Visit the Bund at night to see the skyline illuminated.

## Siem Reap, Cambodia

*The gateway to Angkor, the world's largest religious monument.*

- **Country:** Cambodia
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~250,000
- **Language(s):** Khmer
- **Currency:** Cambodian Riel (KHR); US Dollars widely used
- **Main airport:** Siem Reap–Angkor International Airport (SAI)
- **Best time to visit:** November–March
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Siem Reap is a town in northwestern Cambodia and the base for visiting the temples of Angkor, the capital of the Khmer Empire from the 9th to 15th centuries. The Angkor Archaeological Park, a UNESCO World Heritage Site, contains hundreds of temples spread across a vast area. Siem Reap itself has grown into a lively town with markets, restaurants, spas and nightlife. It is also a centre for traditional arts and crafts. The new international airport opened in 2023.

### Top Attractions

- **Angkor Wat:** The largest religious monument in the world, famous for sunrise views.
- **Angkor Thom and Bayon:** A walled city with the Bayon temple and its giant stone faces.
- **Ta Prohm:** A temple overgrown by giant tree roots, featured in Tomb Raider.
- **Banteay Srei:** A small temple with exquisite pink sandstone carvings.
- **Phare, the Cambodian Circus:** Performances by artists trained at a social enterprise.
- **Tonlé Sap Lake:** Southeast Asia's largest freshwater lake, with floating villages.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The area around Pub Street and the Old Market is lively. Quieter hotels are found along the river and in the countryside.

### Food & Drink

Fish amok, lok lak and Khmer curries are staples. Street food and night markets offer cheap meals. Many restaurants support training programmes for disadvantaged youth.

### When to Visit

November to March is best. April and May are very hot, and the rainy season brings lush scenery.

### Getting Around

Tuk-tuks are the main way to visit temples. Bicycles and e-bikes are also available.

### Day Trips

Kulen Mountain, Beng Mealea and the floating villages of Tonlé Sap are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Buy Angkor passes at the official ticket office or online.
- Dress modestly for temples; shoulders and knees must be covered.
- Start early to avoid heat.
- Hire a licensed guide.

## Singapore, Singapore

*A clean, green city-state of futuristic gardens, hawker food and multicultural neighbourhoods.*

- **Country:** Singapore
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~6 million
- **Language(s):** English, Malay, Mandarin and Tamil
- **Currency:** Singapore Dollar (SGD)
- **Main airport:** Singapore Changi Airport (SIN)
- **Best time to visit:** February–April (hot and humid year-round)
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Singapore is a small island city-state at the southern tip of the Malay Peninsula. Founded as a British trading post in 1819, it became independent in 1965 and transformed into one of the world's wealthiest and most developed countries. Singapore is known for its efficiency, cleanliness, safety and strict laws. It is a multicultural society with Chinese, Malay, Indian and Eurasian communities. The city combines futuristic architecture with lush gardens and heritage districts.

### Top Attractions

- **Gardens by the Bay:** Futuristic Supertree Grove, Cloud Forest and Flower Dome conservatories, with a nightly light show.
- **Marina Bay Sands:** An iconic hotel with a rooftop SkyPark and infinity pool.
- **Sentosa Island:** Beaches, Universal Studios Singapore and S.E.A. Aquarium.
- **Chinatown, Little India and Kampong Glam:** Colourful neighbourhoods with temples, mosques and markets.
- **Singapore Botanic Gardens:** A UNESCO site with the National Orchid Garden.
- **Jewel Changi Airport:** A shopping and entertainment complex with the world's tallest indoor waterfall.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Marina Bay is central, with luxury hotels. Chinatown and Little India offer affordable accommodation, and Orchard Road is the shopping district. Tiong Bahru is trendy, with cafés.

### Food & Drink

Hawker centres serve affordable local dishes such as Hainanese chicken rice, laksa, chilli crab, satay and char kway teow. Singapore's hawker culture is inscribed by UNESCO. Some hawker stalls have received Michelin recognition.

### When to Visit

Singapore is hot and humid year-round, with frequent rain. Festivals such as Chinese New Year, Deepavali and Hari Raya are celebrated.

### Getting Around

The MRT is efficient and clean. Contactless cards work on public transport. Grab is widely used.

### Day Trips

Pulau Ubin offers rustic island life. Johor Bahru in Malaysia is across the causeway. Bintan and Batam in Indonesia are reached by ferry.

### Practical Tips

- Chewing gum sales are restricted, and littering is fined.
- Drink tap water; it's safe.
- Carry an umbrella for sudden rain.
- Complete the SG Arrival Card before arrival.

## Sofia, Bulgaria

*An affordable capital with golden domes, Roman ruins and a mountain on its doorstep.*

- **Country:** Bulgaria
- **Region:** Southeastern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.3 million
- **Language(s):** Bulgarian
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR) since 1 January 2026 (formerly the Bulgarian Lev, BGN)
- **Main airport:** Sofia Airport (SOF)
- **Best time to visit:** May–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Sofia, the capital of Bulgaria, is one of Europe's oldest cities, founded as the Thracian settlement of Serdica. Its layered history includes Roman ruins, Byzantine churches, Ottoman mosques and communist-era monuments. The city sits at the foot of Mount Vitosha, offering quick access to hiking and skiing. Sofia is affordable, with a relaxed atmosphere, café culture and lively nightlife. It is also a gateway to Bulgaria's monasteries and mountains.

### Top Attractions

- **Alexander Nevsky Cathedral:** One of the largest Orthodox cathedrals in the world, with golden domes.
- **Serdica archaeological complex:** Roman ruins visible in the centre, including streets and buildings.
- **Boyana Church:** A UNESCO-listed medieval church with remarkable 13th-century frescoes.
- **Vitosha Boulevard:** The main pedestrian street, with shops and cafés and views of Mount Vitosha.
- **Church of St. George (Rotunda):** A 4th-century Roman rotunda, one of the oldest buildings in Sofia.
- **National Palace of Culture (NDK):** A large conference centre with a park and fountains.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The city centre is compact and convenient. The area around Vitosha Boulevard is lively, and Lozenets is residential and pleasant. Oborishte, near the cathedral, has cafés and a local feel.

### Food & Drink

Bulgarian cuisine includes shopska salad, banitsa (cheese pastry), kebapche (grilled minced meat) and tarator (cold yoghurt and cucumber soup). Bulgarian yoghurt is famous. Rakia, a fruit brandy, is widely drunk. Mehanas are traditional taverns.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are pleasant. Summers are warm, and winters are cold with skiing nearby. Festivals and outdoor events take place in summer.

### Getting Around

Sofia has a metro that connects the airport with the centre. Trams and buses cover the city. Taxis are affordable; use reputable companies.

### Day Trips

Rila Monastery, Bulgaria's most important monastery, is about two hours away. Plovdiv, one of Europe's oldest cities, is a popular trip. Mount Vitosha offers hiking and skiing.

### Practical Tips

- Bulgarians nod for "no" and shake their heads for "yes", which can be confusing.
- Cyrillic script is used; learn a few letters to read signs.
- Taxi scams exist at the airport; use official taxi desks or apps.
- Bulgaria switched from the lev to the euro in 2026; some older price tags may still show both currencies.

## Split, Croatia

*A lively Adriatic city built inside a Roman emperor's palace.*

- **Country:** Croatia
- **Region:** Southeastern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~160,000
- **Language(s):** Croatian
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Split Airport (SPU)
- **Best time to visit:** May–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Split is Croatia's second-largest city and the main hub of the Dalmatian coast. Its heart is Diocletian's Palace, built by the Roman emperor around AD 300, which is now a living part of the city with shops, homes and bars inside its walls. The palace is a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Split is a gateway to the islands of Hvar, Brač and Vis. It combines history, beaches and nightlife.

### Top Attractions

- **Diocletian's Palace:** A Roman palace complex forming the old town, with the Peristyle, cellars and the Cathedral of St. Domnius.
- **Riva Promenade:** A palm-lined seafront promenade with cafés.
- **Marjan Hill:** A forested hill with trails, viewpoints and beaches.
- **Bačvice Beach:** A sandy city beach famous for the game of picigin.
- **Cathedral bell tower:** Climb for views over the old town and harbour.
- **Gregory of Nin statue:** A large bronze statue; rubbing its toe is said to bring luck.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The old town is atmospheric but noisy in summer. Varoš is a quieter neighbourhood near Marjan, and Bačvice is close to the beach and nightlife. Apartments are common accommodation.

### Food & Drink

Dalmatian cuisine includes grilled fish, octopus salad, pašticada (beef stew) and soparnik (a Swiss chard pie). Seafood is fresh and abundant. Croatian wines, including Plavac Mali, are excellent. Konobas serve traditional dishes.

### When to Visit

Late spring and early autumn are ideal. July and August are hot and crowded, with festivals and nightlife. Winter is quiet.

### Getting Around

The old town is walkable. Ferries and catamarans depart from the harbour to the islands. Buses connect Split with Dubrovnik and Zagreb.

### Day Trips

Hvar, Brač (with Zlatni Rat beach) and Vis are popular island trips. Krka National Park and Trogir are close. The Blue Cave on Biševo is reached by speedboat.

### Practical Tips

- Book ferries in advance in summer.
- Wear water shoes on rocky beaches.
- Some restaurants in the palace are expensive; explore side streets.
- Split can be busy with cruise passengers.

## Stockholm, Sweden

*A capital built on 14 islands, blending royal history, design and nature.*

- **Country:** Sweden
- **Region:** Northern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~990,000 (city); ~2.4 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Swedish (English widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Swedish Krona (SEK)
- **Main airport:** Stockholm Arlanda Airport (ARN)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Stockholm, the capital of Sweden, is spread across 14 islands where Lake Mälaren meets the Baltic Sea. Founded in the 13th century, it has a beautifully preserved old town and a modern, innovative culture. The city is known for design, music and technology, and it hosts the Nobel Prize ceremony every December. Water and green spaces are everywhere, and the archipelago contains around 30,000 islands. Stockholm is clean, safe and expensive.

### Top Attractions

- **Gamla Stan:** The medieval old town with narrow streets, the Royal Palace and Stortorget square.
- **Vasa Museum:** A museum built around a 17th-century warship that sank on its maiden voyage and was salvaged almost intact.
- **Skansen:** The world's oldest open-air museum, showing traditional Swedish life, with a zoo.
- **ABBA The Museum:** An interactive museum dedicated to Sweden's most famous band.
- **Royal Palace:** One of the largest palaces in Europe, with royal apartments and museums.
- **Stockholm City Hall:** The venue for the Nobel Banquet, with a tower offering views.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Gamla Stan is charming and historic. Södermalm is trendy, with vintage shops and bars, while Östermalm is upscale. Djurgården is a green island with museums and parks.

### Food & Drink

Swedish food includes meatballs with lingonberries, gravlax (cured salmon) and pickled herring. Fika, a coffee break with pastries such as cinnamon buns (kanelbullar), is a cherished tradition. Smörgåsbord is a buffet of traditional dishes. Östermalm Market Hall is a gourmet food market.

### When to Visit

Summer brings long days and archipelago trips; Midsummer in June is a major celebration. Winter is dark and cold but festive in December. Spring and autumn are cooler.

### Getting Around

Stockholm has an excellent metro (tunnelbana), whose stations are decorated with art. The Arlanda Express train reaches the airport in about 20 minutes. Ferries connect the islands.

### Day Trips

The Stockholm archipelago offers island escapes such as Vaxholm and Sandhamn. Drottningholm Palace, the royal family's residence, is a UNESCO site. Uppsala, a historic university town, is 40 minutes by train.

### Practical Tips

- Sweden is nearly cashless; cards are accepted almost everywhere.
- The SL app is useful for public transport tickets.
- Many museums are closed on Mondays.
- Alcohol stronger than 3.5% is sold only at Systembolaget stores.

## Taipei, Taiwan

*A friendly capital of night markets, temples, hot springs and mountains.*

- **Country:** Taiwan
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~2.5 million (city); ~7 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Mandarin Chinese (Taiwanese Hokkien widely spoken)
- **Currency:** New Taiwan Dollar (TWD)
- **Main airport:** Taoyuan International Airport (TPE); Songshan Airport (TSA)
- **Best time to visit:** October–April
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Taipei, the capital of Taiwan, is located in a basin surrounded by mountains in the north of the island. It combines Chinese traditions, Japanese colonial heritage and a modern, democratic culture. Taipei is famous for its night markets, bubble tea and friendly people. The city is safe, clean and easy to navigate. Nature is close, with hot springs, hiking trails and tea plantations.

### Top Attractions

- **Taipei 101:** Once the world's tallest building, with an observatory.
- **National Palace Museum:** One of the world's largest collections of Chinese art.
- **Chiang Kai-shek Memorial Hall:** A monument with a changing of the guard.
- **Longshan Temple:** A historic temple.
- **Shilin Night Market:** Taipei's largest night market.
- **Elephant Mountain:** A hike with views of Taipei 101.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Xinyi is modern, Ximending is youthful and Da'an is residential.

### Food & Drink

Beef noodle soup, xiaolongbao at Din Tai Fung, stinky tofu and bubble tea are must-tries.

### When to Visit

Autumn and spring are best. Summers are hot, with typhoons.

### Getting Around

The MRT is efficient. The EasyCard works on transport.

### Day Trips

Jiufen, Beitou hot springs and Yehliu Geopark are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Carry an umbrella.
- Use an EasyCard.
- Try night markets.
- Taipei is very safe.

## Tallinn, Estonia

*A fairy-tale medieval old town with a cutting-edge digital society.*

- **Country:** Estonia
- **Region:** Northern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~460,000
- **Language(s):** Estonian
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Tallinn Airport, Lennart Meri (TLL)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September; December for Christmas market
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Tallinn, the capital of Estonia, has one of the best-preserved medieval old towns in Europe, a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Founded as a Hanseatic trading city, it has towers, walls and cobbled streets that look almost unchanged since the Middle Ages. Estonia is one of the world's most digitally advanced countries, birthplace of Skype. The city blends medieval charm with modern creativity. It is easy to visit from Helsinki by ferry.

### Top Attractions

- **Old Town:** Cobbled streets, Town Hall Square and medieval walls with towers.
- **Toompea Hill:** A hill with viewpoints, Toompea Castle and Alexander Nevsky Cathedral.
- **Kadriorg Palace and Park:** A baroque palace built by Peter the Great, with the KUMU Art Museum nearby.
- **Telliskivi Creative City:** A former industrial area with shops, cafés and street art.
- **Seaplane Harbour:** A maritime museum in a historic seaplane hangar.
- **St. Olaf's Church:** A church whose tower was once among the tallest buildings in the world.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Old Town is central. Kalamaja is a trendy neighbourhood of wooden houses, and Rotermann Quarter is a modern district with shops and restaurants. Kadriorg is green and elegant.

### Food & Drink

Estonian cuisine includes black bread, smoked fish and hearty dishes. Kama, a mix of roasted grain flours, is a traditional food. Vana Tallinn is a local liqueur. Medieval-themed restaurants like Olde Hansa serve historic dishes.

### When to Visit

Summer brings long days and festivals. Winter is cold, but the Christmas market in Town Hall Square is famous. Spring and autumn are quieter.

### Getting Around

The Old Town is walkable. Trams and buses cover the city, and a tram connects the airport. Ferries link Tallinn with Helsinki in about two hours.

### Day Trips

Lahemaa National Park offers bogs, forests and manor houses. Helsinki is an easy ferry trip. Tartu, a university town, is about 2.5 hours away.

### Practical Tips

- Wear comfortable shoes for cobblestones.
- The Tallinn Card includes museum entry and public transport.
- Cruise ship days can make the old town crowded.
- Card payments are accepted almost everywhere.

## Tashkent, Uzbekistan

*Central Asia's largest city, a blend of Soviet boulevards, ornate metro stations and bazaars.*

- **Country:** Uzbekistan
- **Region:** Central Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~3 million
- **Language(s):** Uzbek (Russian widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Uzbekistani Som (UZS)
- **Main airport:** Islam Karimov Tashkent International Airport (TAS)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Tashkent, the capital of Uzbekistan, is the largest city in Central Asia and an important stop on the ancient Silk Road. Much of the old city was destroyed by an earthquake in 1966 and rebuilt in a Soviet style, with wide avenues, parks and monumental buildings. Today Tashkent is modernising quickly, with new business districts, cafés and cultural venues. The city is the main international gateway to Uzbekistan's Silk Road cities of Samarkand, Bukhara and Khiva. It offers a fascinating mix of Islamic heritage, Soviet architecture and warm Uzbek hospitality.

### Top Attractions

- **Tashkent Metro:** Each station is uniquely decorated with chandeliers, mosaics and marble; photography is now permitted.
- **Chorsu Bazaar:** A huge market under a green dome, selling spices, bread, dried fruits and fresh produce.
- **Khast Imam Complex:** The religious centre of the city, with madrasas, mosques and a library holding what is believed to be one of the oldest Qurans in the world, the Uthman Quran.
- **Amir Timur Square and Museum:** A square with a statue of Timur and a blue-domed museum dedicated to his era.
- **State Museum of Applied Arts:** A beautiful mansion displaying Uzbek crafts such as ceramics, embroidery and woodcarving.
- **Tashkent City Park and Magic City:** Modern leisure areas with fountains, restaurants and evening lights.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The city centre around Amir Timur Square has most hotels and restaurants. The Old City near Chorsu Bazaar offers a more traditional atmosphere. Tashkent City is a new business district with modern hotels and towers.

### Food & Drink

Plov is the heart of Uzbek cuisine, and the Central Asian Plov Centre cooks it in giant cauldrons, usually around lunchtime. Other favourites include shashlik (grilled meat skewers), samsa (baked pastries), lagman (hand-pulled noodles) and manti (steamed dumplings). Uzbek bread (non) is considered sacred and should never be placed upside down. Green tea is served with every meal.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are the most pleasant seasons, with blossoms in spring and harvest produce in autumn. Summers are very hot and dry, often above 40°C. Winters are cold but short.

### Getting Around

The metro is cheap and efficient, and ride-hailing apps like Yandex Go are widely used. The Afrosiyob high-speed train connects Tashkent with Samarkand in about two hours and continues to Bukhara. The city is spread out, so walking between areas can take time.

### Day Trips

Samarkand is an easy day trip by high-speed train, though an overnight stay is better. The Chimgan mountains and Charvak Reservoir offer hiking, skiing and lakeside resorts about 1.5 hours away. Bukhara and Khiva are best visited on longer trips.

### Practical Tips

- Many nationalities can enter Uzbekistan visa-free; check current rules.
- Carry cash, as card acceptance is improving but still limited in bazaars.
- Book high-speed train tickets early, as they sell out.
- Dress modestly at religious sites.

## Tbilisi, Georgia

*A charming Caucasus capital of sulphur baths, balconied houses and wine culture.*

- **Country:** Georgia
- **Region:** Caucasus
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.2 million
- **Language(s):** Georgian
- **Currency:** Georgian Lari (GEL)
- **Main airport:** Tbilisi International Airport (TBS)
- **Best time to visit:** May–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Tbilisi, the capital of Georgia, lies in a valley along the Mtkvari (Kura) River, surrounded by hills. Founded in the 5th century by King Vakhtang Gorgasali, it has been a crossroads of Persian, Ottoman, Russian and European influences. The old town is famous for its colourful wooden balconies, churches, synagogues and mosques standing side by side. Tbilisi has a creative, bohemian spirit, with a thriving café, art and techno club scene. Georgian hospitality, food and wine make the city especially memorable.

### Top Attractions

- **Old Tbilisi:** Narrow streets with houses decorated with carved wooden balconies, around Sioni Cathedral and the Clock Tower of the Rezo Gabriadze Theatre.
- **Narikala Fortress:** A 4th-century fortress on a hill, reached by cable car, with views over the city.
- **Abanotubani sulphur baths:** Domed bathhouses fed by natural hot springs, which gave the city its name (tbili means "warm").
- **Holy Trinity Cathedral (Sameba):** One of the largest Orthodox cathedrals in the world.
- **Rustaveli Avenue:** The main boulevard, with theatres, museums and the Parliament.
- **Fabrika:** A former Soviet sewing factory transformed into a hostel, bars and creative space.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Old Tbilisi is atmospheric and close to the main sights. Sololaki has beautiful Art Nouveau buildings, while Vake and Vera are leafy and upscale. Marjanishvili and the area around Fabrika are trendy.

### Food & Drink

Georgian cuisine includes khachapuri (cheese-filled bread, especially the boat-shaped Adjarian version with egg), khinkali (juicy dumplings), badrijani (eggplant rolls with walnut paste) and mtsvadi (grilled meat). Georgia is one of the world's oldest wine regions, with the traditional qvevri clay-vessel method listed by UNESCO. A supra (feast) is led by a toastmaster called a tamada. Churchkhela, strings of nuts dipped in grape juice, is a popular snack.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are the best seasons. Summers are hot, but mountain escapes are close. The grape harvest (rtveli) in September and October is a great time for wine tours.

### Getting Around

Tbilisi has a metro, buses and cable cars. Taxis are cheap, especially via apps like Bolt and Yandex Go. The old town is walkable but hilly.

### Day Trips

Mtskheta, Georgia's ancient capital with Jvari Monastery and Svetitskhoveli Cathedral, is 30 minutes away. The Kakheti wine region, including Sighnaghi, is about two hours away. Kazbegi (Stepantsminda), with Gergeti Trinity Church below Mount Kazbek, is a spectacular mountain trip along the Georgian Military Road.

### Practical Tips

- Many nationalities can stay in Georgia visa-free for up to a year.
- Pace yourself at a supra; toasts are frequent.
- Use Bolt to avoid taxi overcharging.
- Dress modestly in churches; women should cover their heads.

## Tel Aviv, Israel

*A lively Mediterranean city of beaches, Bauhaus architecture and nightlife.*

- **Country:** Israel
- **Region:** Middle East
- **Population (approx.):** ~480,000 (city); ~4 million (Gush Dan metro)
- **Language(s):** Hebrew (English widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Israeli New Shekel (ILS)
- **Main airport:** Ben Gurion International Airport (TLV)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Tel Aviv, founded in 1909 on the sand dunes north of the ancient port of Jaffa, is Israel's cultural and economic centre. Its "White City", with over 4,000 Bauhaus and International Style buildings, is a UNESCO World Heritage Site. The city is known for its beaches, café culture, startups and vibrant nightlife. Tel Aviv–Yafo includes the historic port city of Jaffa, one of the oldest ports in the world. Visitors should check current travel advisories due to the regional security situation.

### Top Attractions

- **Old Jaffa:** An ancient port with stone alleys, galleries, the Jaffa Flea Market and views of Tel Aviv.
- **Tel Aviv beaches:** A continuous stretch of sandy beaches with promenades.
- **White City:** Bauhaus buildings along Rothschild Boulevard and Dizengoff Street.
- **Carmel Market (Shuk HaCarmel):** A bustling market of food and goods.
- **Tel Aviv Museum of Art:** Israeli and international art.
- **Neve Tzedek:** The city's first neighbourhood outside Jaffa, with boutiques and cafés.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The beachfront is ideal for hotels. Neve Tzedek and Florentin are trendy, and Old North is residential.

### Food & Drink

Try hummus, falafel, shakshuka and sabich. Tel Aviv has a strong vegan scene and fine dining.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal. Summers are hot and humid. Pride Week in June is one of the largest in the region.

### Getting Around

Buses, a light rail line and bike-share (Tel-O-Fun) serve the city. Trains connect with the airport and Jerusalem.

### Day Trips

Jerusalem, Caesarea and Haifa are popular. The Dead Sea is further.

### Practical Tips

- Check current travel advisories.
- Public transport is limited on Shabbat.
- Carry sunscreen.
- Expect strict airport security.

## Tokyo, Japan

*The world's largest metropolis, blending ancient temples with neon-lit futurism.*

- **Country:** Japan
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~14 million (city); ~37 million (Greater Tokyo)
- **Language(s):** Japanese
- **Currency:** Japanese Yen (JPY)
- **Main airport:** Haneda (HND); Narita International (NRT)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and October–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Tokyo, the capital of Japan, is the world's largest metropolitan area and one of its most exciting cities. Formerly known as Edo, it became the seat of the Tokugawa shogunate in 1603 and the imperial capital in 1868. The city is a patchwork of distinct neighbourhoods, from traditional temples and gardens to futuristic skyscrapers and pop-culture districts. Tokyo is famous for its safety, efficiency and exceptional food, with more Michelin-starred restaurants than any other city. It can be enjoyed on any budget.

### Top Attractions

- **Senso-ji:** Tokyo's oldest temple in Asakusa, approached through Kaminarimon Gate and Nakamise shopping street.
- **Shibuya Crossing:** The world's busiest pedestrian crossing, with views from Shibuya Sky.
- **Meiji Shrine:** A peaceful Shinto shrine surrounded by forest near Harajuku.
- **Tokyo Skytree:** The tallest tower in Japan, with observation decks.
- **Tsukiji Outer Market and Toyosu Market:** Food markets for sushi and seafood.
- **teamLab Planets and teamLab Borderless:** Immersive digital art museums.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Shinjuku is a hub of nightlife and transport, while Shibuya and Harajuku are youthful and fashionable. Ginza is upscale, Asakusa is traditional, and Akihabara is the centre of anime and electronics.

### Food & Drink

Sushi, ramen, tempura, tonkatsu and yakitori are everywhere. Izakayas are casual pubs, and convenience stores offer surprisingly good food. Depachika (department store food halls) are worth exploring.

### When to Visit

Spring cherry blossoms and autumn leaves are beautiful. Summer is hot and humid, and winter is dry and sunny.

### Getting Around

Tokyo's rail and subway network is extensive. Suica and Pasmo cards work on all transport.

### Day Trips

Nikko, Kamakura, Hakone and Mount Fuji are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Get a Suica or Pasmo card.
- Avoid rush hour.
- Carry cash for smaller establishments.
- Tipping is not customary.

## Toronto, Canada (Ontario)

*Canada's largest and most multicultural city, on the shores of Lake Ontario.*

- **Country:** Canada (Ontario)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~3 million (city); ~6.7 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English (over 200 languages spoken)
- **Currency:** Canadian Dollar (CAD)
- **Main airport:** Toronto Pearson International Airport (YYZ); Billy Bishop (YTZ)
- **Best time to visit:** May–October
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Toronto, the capital of Ontario, is Canada's largest city and its financial and cultural centre. Over half of its residents were born outside Canada, making it one of the most diverse cities in the world. Toronto has a striking skyline dominated by the CN Tower, and neighbourhoods reflecting cultures from around the globe. The city hosts the Toronto International Film Festival (TIFF), one of the world's major film festivals. Its waterfront, islands and parks offer relief from the busy downtown.

### Top Attractions

- **CN Tower:** A 553-metre tower with an observation deck and the EdgeWalk.
- **Royal Ontario Museum:** Natural history and world cultures.
- **Toronto Islands:** Car-free islands with beaches and skyline views.
- **St. Lawrence Market:** A historic food market.
- **Distillery District:** Victorian industrial buildings with shops and restaurants.
- **Kensington Market:** A bohemian neighbourhood.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Downtown, the Annex, Queen West and Yorkville are popular.

### Food & Drink

Peameal bacon sandwiches, butter tarts and diverse cuisines.

### When to Visit

Summer and autumn are pleasant. Winters are cold.

### Getting Around

The TTC subway, streetcars and buses; UP Express connects the airport with downtown.

### Day Trips

Niagara Falls, Niagara-on-the-Lake wineries and Algonquin Park.

### Practical Tips

- Use a PRESTO card or contactless payment.
- Book CN Tower tickets online.
- Bring layers.
- Visit Niagara on a weekday.

## Tulum, Mexico

*Mayan ruins on a Caribbean cliff, cenotes and bohemian beach life.*

- **Country:** Mexico
- **Region:** North America (Caribbean coast)
- **Population (approx.):** ~50,000
- **Language(s):** Spanish (Maya also spoken; English common in tourist areas)
- **Currency:** Mexican Peso (MXN)
- **Main airport:** Tulum International Airport (TQO); Cancún International Airport (CUN) is about 2 hours away
- **Best time to visit:** November–April
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Tulum is a town on the Riviera Maya coast of Mexico's Yucatán Peninsula, famous for its Mayan ruins perched on a cliff above turquoise waters. Once a quiet backpacker destination, it has become a trendy hub for boutique hotels, wellness retreats, beach clubs and eco-chic design. The area is surrounded by jungle, cenotes (natural sinkholes) and the Sian Ka'an Biosphere Reserve. Rapid development has brought concerns about the environment, rising prices and safety, so choosing where to stay is important. A new airport and the Tren Maya railway have improved access.

### Top Attractions

- **Tulum Archaeological Site:** A walled Mayan port city with El Castillo overlooking the Caribbean; arrive at opening time to avoid heat and crowds.
- **Cenotes:** Gran Cenote, Cenote Dos Ojos and Calavera Cenote are ideal for swimming, snorkelling and diving.
- **Sian Ka'an Biosphere Reserve:** A UNESCO-listed reserve with lagoons, mangroves and wildlife, explored by boat.
- **Playa Paraíso and Playa Ruinas:** Beautiful beaches near the ruins.
- **Cobá:** Mayan ruins in the jungle with the Nohoch Mul pyramid, best explored by bike.
- **Tulum National Park (Parque del Jaguar):** Protected coastline with beaches and the ruins.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Hotel Zone along the beach has boutique eco-hotels and beach clubs, but is expensive. Tulum Pueblo (town) is more affordable, with local restaurants. Aldea Zamá and La Veleta are newer residential areas.

### Food & Drink

Tulum offers Mexican and international cuisine, from tacos al pastor in town to high-end restaurants in the jungle. Yucatecan dishes like cochinita pibil and panuchos are local specialities. Fresh seafood and ceviche are popular.

### When to Visit

November to April is the dry season, with pleasant weather. Hurricane season runs from June to November, and sargassum seaweed can affect beaches from spring to autumn.

### Getting Around

Bicycles are a popular way to move between the town, beach and ruins. Taxis are expensive; agree on the fare first. The Tren Maya connects Tulum with Cancún, Playa del Carmen and other Yucatán cities.

### Day Trips

Cobá, Chichén Itzá, Valladolid and Bacalar's Lagoon of Seven Colours are popular. Akumal is known for swimming with sea turtles.

### Practical Tips

- Bring cash in pesos; card payments sometimes carry surcharges.
- Use biodegradable sunscreen in cenotes.
- Check sargassum reports.
- Be cautious at night and follow local safety advice.

## Udaipur, India

*The romantic "City of Lakes", with palaces reflected in shimmering water.*

- **Country:** India
- **Region:** South Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~600,000
- **Language(s):** Hindi and Mewari
- **Currency:** Indian Rupee (INR)
- **Main airport:** Maharana Pratap Airport (UDR)
- **Best time to visit:** September–March
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Udaipur, in the Indian state of Rajasthan, was founded in 1559 by Maharana Udai Singh II as the capital of the Mewar kingdom. It is set around a series of artificial lakes and surrounded by the Aravalli Hills. The city is famous for its palaces, havelis and romantic atmosphere, often called the "Venice of the East". Udaipur was a setting for the James Bond film Octopussy. It is one of India's most popular destinations for weddings.

### Top Attractions

- **City Palace:** A vast palace complex overlooking Lake Pichola, with museums and courtyards.
- **Lake Pichola:** Boat rides with views of palaces and ghats.
- **Jag Mandir:** A palace on an island in the lake.
- **Jagdish Temple:** A large Hindu temple in the old city.
- **Saheliyon-ki-Bari:** A garden with fountains.
- **Monsoon Palace (Sajjangarh):** A hilltop palace with sunset views.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The old city near Lake Pichola has many heritage hotels. Luxury hotels include the Taj Lake Palace.

### Food & Drink

Rajasthani dishes such as dal baati churma and gatte ki sabzi are local favourites. Rooftop restaurants offer lake views.

### When to Visit

September to March is ideal. Summers are hot.

### Getting Around

Auto-rickshaws and taxis are common. The old city is walkable.

### Day Trips

Kumbhalgarh Fort and Ranakpur Jain Temple are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Book lakeside rooms in advance.
- Watch sunset from a rooftop.
- Dress modestly at temples.
- Bargain in markets.

## Ulaanbaatar, Mongolia

*The world's coldest capital and the gateway to Mongolia's vast steppe.*

- **Country:** Mongolia
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.7 million
- **Language(s):** Mongolian
- **Currency:** Mongolian Tögrög (MNT)
- **Main airport:** Chinggis Khaan International Airport (UBN)
- **Best time to visit:** June–August; July for the Naadam Festival
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Ulaanbaatar, the capital of Mongolia, is home to nearly half of the country's population. Originally a mobile Buddhist monastery settlement, it moved many times before settling in the Tuul River valley in the 18th century. The city mixes Soviet-era apartment blocks, modern glass towers, Buddhist monasteries and sprawling ger (yurt) districts on the outskirts. It is often described as the coldest national capital in the world, with winter temperatures regularly below −30°C. Most travellers use Ulaanbaatar as a starting point for adventures in the Gobi Desert, the steppe and the mountains.

### Top Attractions

- **Gandan Monastery:** Mongolia's largest and most important Buddhist monastery, with a 26-metre statue of Avalokiteshvara.
- **Sükhbaatar Square (Chinggis Square):** The central square, with a monumental statue of Chinggis Khaan in front of the Government Palace.
- **Chinggis Khaan National Museum:** A modern museum covering Mongolian history from prehistory through the Mongol Empire.
- **Zaisan Memorial:** A Soviet-era hilltop monument with panoramic views over the city.
- **Choijin Lama Temple Museum:** A beautiful temple complex with Buddhist art and masks.
- **Bogd Khan Winter Palace:** The residence of Mongolia's last king, with a collection of royal artefacts.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Most hotels are in the city centre around Sükhbaatar Square and Peace Avenue, within walking distance of main sights. The Zaisan area in the south is quieter and more upscale. Staying in a ger camp just outside the city is a popular alternative.

### Food & Drink

Mongolian cuisine is based on meat and dairy, reflecting nomadic traditions. Try buuz (steamed dumplings), khuushuur (fried meat pastries) and tsuivan (stir-fried noodles with meat). Airag, fermented mare's milk, is a traditional summer drink. The city also has Korean, Russian and international restaurants.

### When to Visit

Summer (June–August) is the best time to visit, with mild temperatures and green steppe. The Naadam Festival, held around 11–15 July, features wrestling, horse racing and archery. Winters are extremely cold, and air pollution from coal heating can be severe.

### Getting Around

Traffic congestion is heavy, and public buses are crowded, so taxis and ride-hailing apps are common. The city centre is walkable. The Trans-Mongolian Railway connects Ulaanbaatar with Beijing and Russia.

### Day Trips

Gorkhi-Terelj National Park, about 1.5 hours away, offers rock formations, hiking, horse riding and ger stays. The giant stainless steel Chinggis Khaan Equestrian Statue at Tsonjin Boldog is on the way. Khustai National Park is home to wild Przewalski's horses.

### Practical Tips

- Book accommodation early for Naadam.
- Carry warm layers even in summer, as nights can be cold.
- Carry cash for rural trips.
- Watch for pickpockets in crowded areas.

## Ushuaia, Argentina

*The "End of the World", gateway to Tierra del Fuego and Antarctica.*

- **Country:** Argentina
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~80,000
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Argentine Peso (ARS)
- **Main airport:** Malvinas Argentinas International Airport (USH)
- **Best time to visit:** November–March; June–September for skiing
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Ushuaia, the capital of Argentina's Tierra del Fuego province, is often described as the southernmost city in the world. It lies on the Beagle Channel, framed by the snow-capped Martial Mountains. Founded as a penal colony, the town now thrives on tourism, fishing and Antarctic expeditions. Ushuaia is the main departure point for cruises to Antarctica. Its wild landscapes offer hiking, sailing and wildlife.

### Top Attractions

- **Tierra del Fuego National Park:** Forests, lakes and coastal trails at the end of the Pan-American Highway.
- **Beagle Channel cruise:** Boat trips to see sea lions, cormorants and the Les Eclaireurs Lighthouse.
- **End of the World Train (Tren del Fin del Mundo):** A historic railway once used by prisoners.
- **Maritime and Prison Museum:** A museum in the former prison.
- **Martial Glacier:** A hike with views over the channel.
- **Laguna Esmeralda:** A popular hike to an emerald lake.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The town centre along San Martín Street is convenient. Hotels on the hillside offer views.

### Food & Drink

King crab (centolla), Patagonian lamb and hake are local specialities.

### When to Visit

Summer (November–March) has long days. Winter brings skiing at Cerro Castor.

### Getting Around

Taxis and tours. The town is walkable.

### Day Trips

Penguin colonies on Isla Martillo (Oct–March), the national park, and the Harberton ranch.

### Practical Tips

- Weather changes fast; pack layers.
- Book Antarctic cruises early.
- Carry cash.
- Book penguin walks in advance.

## Valencia, Spain

*The birthplace of paella, with futuristic architecture and long sandy beaches.*

- **Country:** Spain
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~800,000 (city); ~1.6 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Spanish and Valencian
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Valencia Airport (VLC)
- **Best time to visit:** March–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Valencia is Spain's third-largest city, located on the Mediterranean coast. Founded by the Romans in 138 BC, it later flourished under Moorish rule and during a 15th-century golden age of trade. The city combines a charming historic centre with the futuristic City of Arts and Sciences and a huge riverbed park. Valencia is famous as the home of paella and for the spectacular Fallas festival. It offers a relaxed lifestyle, great beaches and excellent value.

### Top Attractions

- **City of Arts and Sciences:** A futuristic complex by Santiago Calatrava and Félix Candela, including a science museum, aquarium (Oceanogràfic) and opera house.
- **Turia Gardens:** A 9-kilometre park built in a former riverbed, ideal for walking and cycling.
- **Valencia Cathedral and the Holy Grail:** The cathedral holds a chalice venerated as the Holy Grail, and its Miguelete tower offers views.
- **Central Market:** One of the largest fresh-food markets in Europe, housed in a modernist building.
- **La Lonja de la Seda:** A Gothic silk exchange and UNESCO World Heritage Site.
- **Malvarrosa Beach:** A long sandy beach with seafront restaurants.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

El Carmen is the historic heart, full of street art and bars. Ruzafa is trendy, with cafés and restaurants, and El Cabanyal is a former fishing village near the beach with colourful tiled houses. The Ciutat Vella is convenient for first-time visitors.

### Food & Drink

Paella valenciana is traditionally made with chicken, rabbit and beans, while seafood versions are also popular; it is eaten at lunchtime. Horchata, a sweet drink made from tiger nuts, is served with fartons (long pastries). Agua de Valencia is a cocktail of cava and orange juice. Arroz a banda and fideuà are other rice and noodle dishes.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are pleasant. Fallas, held in mid-March, features giant satirical sculptures that are burned on the final night. Summer is hot and humid but perfect for the beach.

### Getting Around

Valencia has a metro and tram network, and the airport is linked by metro. The city is flat and bike-friendly, with the Valenbisi bike-share. High-speed trains reach Madrid in under two hours.

### Day Trips

Albufera Natural Park, a lagoon surrounded by rice fields, is the birthplace of paella and offers sunset boat rides. Sagunto has a Roman theatre and castle. Xàtiva and Peñíscola are also popular.

### Practical Tips

- Book accommodation early for Fallas.
- Eat paella at lunchtime, as locals do.
- Rent a bike to explore the Turia Gardens.
- The Valencia Tourist Card includes public transport and discounts.

## Valletta, Malta

*A tiny, fortified baroque capital on the Mediterranean.*

- **Country:** Malta
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~6,000 (city); ~480,000 (Malta's urban area)
- **Language(s):** Maltese and English
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Malta International Airport (MLA)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Valletta, the capital of Malta, is one of Europe's smallest capitals, built by the Knights of St. John after the Great Siege of 1565. The entire city is a UNESCO World Heritage Site, filled with baroque churches, palaces and fortifications. Valletta overlooks the Grand Harbour, one of the most beautiful natural harbours in the Mediterranean. Malta's long history includes prehistoric temples, Roman, Arab, Norman and British rule. English is an official language, making it easy for visitors.

### Top Attractions

- **St. John's Co-Cathedral:** A lavish baroque interior with Caravaggio's The Beheading of Saint John the Baptist.
- **Upper Barrakka Gardens:** Gardens with views over the Grand Harbour and a daily noon gun salute.
- **Grandmaster's Palace:** The former seat of the Knights, now the office of the President, with armoury and state rooms.
- **Fort St. Elmo and National War Museum:** A star-shaped fort covering Malta's military history.
- **Three Cities:** Birgu, Senglea and Cospicua across the harbour, reached by traditional dghajsa boats or ferry.
- **Hypogeum of Ħal Saflieni:** A prehistoric underground burial complex; tickets are limited and must be booked well ahead.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Staying in Valletta is atmospheric and quiet in the evenings. Sliema and St. Julian's offer more hotels, restaurants and nightlife, connected by ferry. The Three Cities are charming and less touristy.

### Food & Drink

Maltese cuisine includes pastizzi (flaky pastries with ricotta or peas), rabbit stew (stuffat tal-fenek) and ftira (a stuffed flatbread). Kinnie is a local bitter-orange soft drink. Seafood is excellent, and Cisk is the local beer.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal. Summers are hot and busy, with festas (village feasts) and fireworks. Winters are mild, making Malta a year-round destination.

### Getting Around

Valletta is walkable, and buses connect the capital with the rest of Malta. Ferries link Valletta with Sliema and the Three Cities. The Barrakka Lift connects the waterfront with the city.

### Day Trips

Mdina, the "Silent City", is a medieval walled town in the centre of the island. Gozo, Malta's sister island, is reached by ferry. The Blue Lagoon on Comino is famous for turquoise water.

### Practical Tips

- Book the Hypogeum weeks or months in advance.
- Traffic drives on the left.
- Wear comfortable shoes; Valletta has many steps.
- Dress modestly in churches.

## Valparaíso, Chile

*A bohemian port city of colourful hillside houses, funiculars and street art.*

- **Country:** Chile
- **Region:** South America
- **Population (approx.):** ~300,000 (city); ~1 million (Greater Valparaíso)
- **Language(s):** Spanish
- **Currency:** Chilean Peso (CLP)
- **Main airport:** Arturo Merino Benítez International Airport (SCL) in Santiago, about 1.5 hours away
- **Best time to visit:** October–April
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Valparaíso, on Chile's Pacific coast, was the most important port in South America in the 19th century, before the Panama Canal opened. Its historic quarter, built on 42 hills overlooking the bay, is a UNESCO World Heritage Site. The city is famous for its maze of colourful houses, steep stairways and historic funiculars (ascensores). Valparaíso is a creative, bohemian city with street art everywhere. It was home to poet Pablo Neruda.

### Top Attractions

- **Cerro Alegre and Cerro Concepción:** Colourful hills with street art, cafés and boutique hotels.
- **Ascensores:** Historic funiculars such as Ascensor Reina Victoria.
- **La Sebastiana:** Pablo Neruda's house with views.
- **Street art tours:** Guided walks through the murals covering the city's hills.
- **Plaza Sotomayor and the port:** The historic main square near the harbour, with the Chilean Navy headquarters and boat tours of the bay.
- **Paseo Gervasoni and Paseo Atkinson:** Viewpoints.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Cerro Alegre and Cerro Concepción.

### Food & Drink

Seafood, chorrillana (fries with meat and eggs) and empanadas.

### When to Visit

Spring and summer. New Year's Eve fireworks are famous.

### Getting Around

Walking, funiculars and trolleybuses.

### Day Trips

Viña del Mar, Casablanca Valley wineries.

### Practical Tips

- Watch belongings.
- Wear good shoes.
- Book New Year's early.
- Take a street art tour.

## Vancouver, Canada (British Columbia)

*A spectacular city between mountains and sea, perfect for outdoor lovers.*

- **Country:** Canada (British Columbia)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~700,000 (city); ~2.8 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** Canadian Dollar (CAD)
- **Main airport:** Vancouver International Airport (YVR)
- **Best time to visit:** June–September; December–March for skiing
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Vancouver, on Canada's Pacific coast, is surrounded by mountains, forests and ocean, consistently ranking among the world's most liveable cities. It is a young city, founded in 1886 as the terminus of the Canadian Pacific Railway, built on the traditional territories of the Musqueam, Squamish and Tsleil-Waututh Nations. Vancouver is known for its outdoor lifestyle: you can ski in the morning and kayak in the afternoon. The city has a large Asian community and an excellent food scene. It hosted the 2010 Winter Olympics and is a host city of the 2026 FIFA World Cup.

### Top Attractions

- **Stanley Park:** A 400-hectare park with the seawall, totem poles and forest trails.
- **Granville Island:** A public market, artisan shops and theatres.
- **Capilano Suspension Bridge:** A swaying bridge over a canyon in North Vancouver.
- **Grouse Mountain:** Skiing in winter and hiking (the Grouse Grind) in summer.
- **Gastown:** The historic district with the steam clock.
- **Vancouver Aquarium and Museum of Anthropology:** A leading aquarium in Stanley Park and a renowned museum of First Nations art at the University of British Columbia.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Downtown, Yaletown, Kitsilano and Gastown are popular.

### Food & Drink

Pacific salmon, sushi (the California roll was popularised here), dim sum in Richmond and food trucks.

### When to Visit

Summer is dry and sunny. Winters are rainy but mild, with skiing nearby.

### Getting Around

The SkyTrain connects the airport with downtown. SeaBus and buses are useful.

### Day Trips

Whistler, the Sea-to-Sky Highway, Victoria on Vancouver Island and Squamish.

### Practical Tips

- Bring rain gear from October to April.
- Use a Compass card or contactless payment.
- Book Whistler early.
- Respect Indigenous heritage.

## Varanasi, India

*India's holiest city, where life and death unfold on the ghats of the Ganges.*

- **Country:** India
- **Region:** South Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.5 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Hindi
- **Currency:** Indian Rupee (INR)
- **Main airport:** Lal Bahadur Shastri International Airport (VNS)
- **Best time to visit:** October–March
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Varanasi, also known as Benares or Kashi, is one of the oldest continuously inhabited cities in the world and the spiritual capital of Hinduism. Located on the banks of the Ganges River in Uttar Pradesh, it attracts pilgrims who come to bathe in the sacred waters and seek liberation (moksha). Dying and being cremated in Varanasi is believed to break the cycle of rebirth. The city's ghats, riverside steps, are the stage for rituals, cremations and daily life. Varanasi is intense, spiritual and unforgettable.

### Top Attractions

- **Dashashwamedh Ghat:** The main ghat, where the spectacular Ganga Aarti ceremony takes place every evening with fire, bells and chanting.
- **Manikarnika Ghat:** The main cremation ghat, where funeral pyres burn day and night; photography is not appropriate.
- **Sunrise boat ride on the Ganges:** The best way to see the ghats come to life at dawn.
- **Kashi Vishwanath Temple:** One of the most sacred Shiva temples, with a new corridor linking it to the river.
- **Sarnath:** The place where the Buddha gave his first sermon, just outside the city.
- **Old city lanes:** A maze of narrow alleys with shrines, shops, cows and sweet stalls.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Guesthouses along the ghats offer river views and atmosphere. Assi Ghat is a more relaxed area popular with long-term travellers. Cantonment has larger hotels.

### Food & Drink

Varanasi is famous for its street food: kachori sabzi for breakfast, tamatar chaat, and malaiyo, a frothy winter dessert. Banarasi paan (betel leaf) is legendary, and lassi is served in clay cups. Food is mostly vegetarian, especially near temples.

### When to Visit

October to March is the best time. Dev Deepawali, in November, sees the ghats lit with thousands of lamps. Summers are extremely hot, and the monsoon floods the ghats.

### Getting Around

Cycle-rickshaws and auto-rickshaws are common. The old city is best explored on foot. Boats travel along the ghats.

### Day Trips

Sarnath is close by. Bodh Gaya, where the Buddha attained enlightenment, is a longer trip.

### Practical Tips

- Be respectful at cremation ghats and do not take photos.
- Beware of touts offering "free" tours.
- Dress modestly.
- Take a boat ride at sunrise.

## Venice, Italy

*A unique floating city of canals, palaces and gondolas.*

- **Country:** Italy
- **Region:** Southern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~250,000 (municipality); ~50,000 in the historic centre
- **Language(s):** Italian (Venetian dialect)
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Venice Marco Polo Airport (VCE); Treviso Airport (TSF)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–November
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Venice is built on more than 100 small islands in a lagoon in northeastern Italy, connected by bridges and canals instead of roads. It was the capital of the powerful Republic of Venice for over a thousand years, a maritime empire that dominated trade between Europe and the East. The city is a UNESCO World Heritage Site and one of the most beautiful places in the world. Venice faces challenges from over-tourism and rising water levels. Staying overnight and exploring quieter areas makes a visit much more rewarding.

### Top Attractions

- **St. Mark's Basilica and Square:** The heart of Venice, with a golden Byzantine basilica and the Campanile.
- **Doge's Palace:** The former seat of Venetian government, with the Bridge of Sighs leading to the prisons.
- **Grand Canal:** The main waterway, best seen from a vaporetto (water bus) or gondola.
- **Rialto Bridge and Market:** A famous bridge and a lively fish and produce market.
- **Gallerie dell'Accademia and Peggy Guggenheim Collection:** Venetian masterpieces and modern art.
- **Murano and Burano:** Islands known for glassmaking and colourful houses with lace-making traditions.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

San Marco is central but crowded. Dorsoduro is artistic, with museums and a student atmosphere, and Cannaregio is residential, with the Jewish Ghetto and lively canalside bars. Castello is quieter and authentic.

### Food & Drink

Venetian cuisine includes sarde in saor (sweet-and-sour sardines), risotto al nero di seppia and fegato alla veneziana (liver with onions). Cicchetti, small snacks served in bàcari (wine bars), are best enjoyed with an ombra (small glass of wine). The Spritz is said to have originated in the Veneto region. Tiramisu also comes from the Veneto.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal, though still busy. The Carnival of Venice, with masks and costumes, takes place before Lent. The acqua alta (high water) floods occur mostly in late autumn and winter, though the MOSE barrier now protects the city in major events.

### Getting Around

There are no cars; transport is by foot, vaporetto or water taxi. Buses and Alilaguna boats connect the airport with the city. Walking is the best way to explore, but expect to get lost.

### Day Trips

The islands of Murano, Burano and Torcello are easy by vaporetto. Padua and Verona are close by train. The Prosecco hills and Lido beaches are also popular.

### Practical Tips

- Venice charges an access fee for day-trippers on peak days; check the official rules before visiting.
- Book St. Mark's Basilica and Doge's Palace in advance.
- Buy a multi-day vaporetto pass if you plan to use boats often.
- Avoid restaurants with touts and menus in many languages.

## Vienna, Austria

*An imperial capital of music, coffeehouses and grand palaces.*

- **Country:** Austria
- **Region:** Central Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~2 million (city); ~2.9 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** German
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Vienna International Airport (VIE)
- **Best time to visit:** April–June and September–October; December for Christmas markets
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Vienna, the capital of Austria, was the centre of the Habsburg Empire for centuries and remains one of Europe's most elegant cities. It is famous for classical music, having been home to Mozart, Beethoven, Haydn, Schubert and Strauss. The city's historic centre is a UNESCO World Heritage Site, with the Ringstrasse lined with palaces, museums and parks. Vienna regularly tops global rankings for quality of life. Its coffeehouse culture is recognised by UNESCO as intangible heritage.

### Top Attractions

- **Schönbrunn Palace:** The Habsburg summer residence, with lavish rooms, gardens and the world's oldest zoo.
- **Hofburg Palace:** The former imperial palace, housing the Sisi Museum, Imperial Apartments and the Spanish Riding School.
- **St. Stephen's Cathedral:** A Gothic cathedral with a colourful tiled roof in the city centre.
- **Belvedere Palace:** Baroque palaces with Klimt's The Kiss.
- **Kunsthistorisches Museum:** One of the world's great art museums, with works by Bruegel, Vermeer and Rubens.
- **Vienna State Opera:** One of the world's leading opera houses; cheap standing tickets are available.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Innere Stadt (1st district) is central and historic. Neubau (7th) and Mariahilf (6th) are trendy, with shops and cafés. Leopoldstadt (2nd) includes the Prater park, and Wieden (4th) is near the Naschmarkt.

### Food & Drink

Vienna's classics include Wiener Schnitzel, Tafelspitz (boiled beef), Sachertorte and apple strudel. Coffeehouses like Café Central, Café Sacher and Café Sperl are institutions. Heurigen, wine taverns on the city's outskirts, serve local wine. The Naschmarkt is a popular food market.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are pleasant. Summer brings outdoor concerts and festivals, while the ball season peaks in January and February. Christmas markets in December are among Europe's best.

### Getting Around

Vienna has an excellent U-Bahn, tram and bus network. The City Airport Train (CAT) and S-Bahn connect the airport with the centre. The city is walkable and bike-friendly.

### Day Trips

The Wachau Valley, with vineyards and the Melk Abbey, is a scenic trip. Bratislava is an hour away by train or boat. Salzburg and Budapest are reachable by train.

### Practical Tips

- Buy standing tickets for the State Opera on the day of the performance.
- Book the Spanish Riding School in advance.
- The Vienna City Card offers transport and discounts.
- Many shops are closed on Sundays.

## Vilnius, Lithuania
