*A baroque old town with a bohemian spirit and a self-declared republic of artists.*

- **Country:** Lithuania
- **Region:** Northern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~600,000
- **Language(s):** Lithuanian
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Vilnius International Airport (VNO)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Vilnius, the capital of Lithuania, has one of the largest surviving medieval old towns in Northern Europe, a UNESCO World Heritage Site. It was the capital of the Grand Duchy of Lithuania, once one of the largest states in Europe. The city has a rich Jewish heritage and was known as the "Jerusalem of the North". Vilnius has a creative, youthful vibe and affordable prices. The quirky Užupis district has declared itself an independent republic with its own constitution.

### Top Attractions

- **Old Town:** Baroque churches, cobbled streets and courtyards.
- **Gediminas Tower:** A hilltop tower with views over the city.
- **Vilnius Cathedral and Palace of the Grand Dukes:** Symbols of Lithuanian statehood.
- **Užupis:** An artists' district with a constitution displayed on a wall in many languages.
- **Gate of Dawn:** A city gate with a chapel housing a revered icon of the Virgin Mary.
- **Museum of Occupations and Freedom Fights:** Located in the former KGB headquarters.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Old Town is central and charming. Užupis is bohemian, and Naujamiestis is a revitalised area with cafés. Žvėrynas is a green neighbourhood with wooden houses.

### Food & Drink

Lithuanian cuisine includes cepelinai (potato dumplings stuffed with meat), šaltibarščiai (cold pink beetroot soup) and kibinai (pastries). Rye bread and local beer are staples. Šakotis, a tree-shaped cake, is traditional for celebrations.

### When to Visit

Summer is ideal, with festivals and outdoor cafés. Winter is cold and snowy, with Christmas markets. Spring and autumn are cooler.

### Getting Around

Vilnius is walkable, with buses and trolleybuses for longer distances. A train and buses connect the airport with the centre. Trains reach Kaunas in about an hour.

### Day Trips

Trakai Island Castle, on a lake, is 30 minutes away. Kaunas, Lithuania's second city, has interwar modernist architecture. The Hill of Crosses near Šiauliai is a unique pilgrimage site.

### Practical Tips

- Vilnius is compact; two days cover the main sights.
- Try cepelinai at a traditional restaurant.
- Card payments are widely accepted.
- Check out the Užupis constitution in your own language.

## Warsaw, Poland

*A resilient capital rebuilt from ruins into a dynamic modern city.*

- **Country:** Poland
- **Region:** Central Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.8 million (city); ~3.1 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** Polish
- **Currency:** Polish Złoty (PLN)
- **Main airport:** Warsaw Chopin Airport (WAW); Warsaw Modlin Airport (WMI)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Warsaw, the capital of Poland, was almost completely destroyed during the Second World War, especially after the Warsaw Uprising in 1944. Its Old Town was meticulously rebuilt using paintings and records and is now a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Today Warsaw is a dynamic, fast-growing city with skyscrapers, green parks and a thriving food and culture scene. It was the birthplace of Frédéric Chopin and Marie Curie. The city offers excellent museums that tell the story of Poland's turbulent history.

### Top Attractions

- **Old Town and Royal Castle:** The rebuilt historic centre with the Royal Castle and Market Square.
- **Warsaw Uprising Museum:** An immersive museum about the 1944 uprising against Nazi occupation.
- **POLIN Museum of the History of Polish Jews:** An award-winning museum covering 1,000 years of Jewish history in Poland.
- **Łazienki Park:** A royal park with the Palace on the Isle and free Chopin concerts in summer.
- **Palace of Culture and Science:** A Stalinist skyscraper with a viewing terrace.
- **Wilanów Palace:** A baroque royal residence often called the "Polish Versailles".

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Śródmieście (city centre) is convenient. The Old Town is charming, while Praga across the Vistula River is gritty and creative. Powiśle is trendy, with cafés and riverside bars.

### Food & Drink

Polish classics include pierogi, żurek, bigos and pączki (doughnuts). Milk bars (bar mleczny) offer cheap traditional meals. Warsaw has a growing fine dining and vegan scene. Vodka is traditional, and craft beer is popular.

### When to Visit

Summer is ideal, with outdoor concerts and riverside life. Winter is cold, with Christmas markets. Spring and autumn are mild.

### Getting Around

Warsaw has a metro, trams and buses. Trains connect Chopin Airport with the centre. The city is spread out, but central areas are walkable.

### Day Trips

Żelazowa Wola, Chopin's birthplace, is about an hour away. Kampinos National Park offers forests and dunes. Łódź, with its industrial heritage, is about 1.5 hours by train.

### Practical Tips

- Many museums are free one day a week.
- Use ride-hailing apps for taxis.
- Book the Uprising Museum in advance in summer.
- Card payments are accepted almost everywhere.

## Washington, D.C., United States (District of Columbia)

*The US capital of monuments, free museums and political power.*

- **Country:** United States (District of Columbia)
- **Region:** North America
- **Population (approx.):** ~700,000 (city); ~6.3 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** English
- **Currency:** US Dollar (USD)
- **Main airport:** Ronald Reagan Washington National (DCA); Washington Dulles (IAD); Baltimore/Washington (BWI)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and September–November
- **Continent:** Americas

### Overview

Washington, D.C., the capital of the United States, was founded in 1790 on land along the Potomac River. Planned by Pierre L'Enfant, it features wide avenues, grand monuments and the National Mall. The city is home to the White House, the US Capitol and the Supreme Court. The Smithsonian Institution operates many free world-class museums. Beyond politics, D.C. has diverse neighbourhoods and a thriving food scene.

### Top Attractions

- **National Mall:** Lincoln Memorial, Washington Monument and war memorials.
- **US Capitol:** Tours of the seat of Congress.
- **Smithsonian museums:** Including Air and Space, Natural History and the National Museum of African American History and Culture.
- **White House:** Exterior views; tours by request.
- **Library of Congress:** A beautiful library.
- **Arlington National Cemetery:** The resting place of over 400,000 service members, with the Tomb of the Unknown Soldier and John F. Kennedy's grave.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Georgetown, Dupont Circle, Capitol Hill and Adams Morgan.

### Food & Drink

Half-smoke sausages, Ethiopian cuisine and diverse restaurants.

### When to Visit

Cherry blossoms in spring (late March–early April). Summers are hot and humid.

### Getting Around

The Metro is efficient. Walking and bike-share on the Mall.

### Day Trips

Mount Vernon, Annapolis and Shenandoah National Park.

### Practical Tips

- Museums are free.
- Book Capitol tours online.
- Visit monuments at night.
- Check for events and closures.

## Wrocław, Poland

*A city of islands, bridges and hundreds of tiny bronze dwarfs.*

- **Country:** Poland
- **Region:** Central Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~670,000
- **Language(s):** Polish
- **Currency:** Polish Złoty (PLN)
- **Main airport:** Wrocław Copernicus Airport (WRO)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September; December for the Christmas market
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Wrocław is the historic capital of Silesia in western Poland, built on the Oder River and spread across 12 islands connected by over 100 bridges. It has belonged to Bohemia, the Habsburgs, Prussia and Germany (as Breslau) before becoming Polish after 1945. The city has a beautiful market square and a lively student population. Wrocław is famous for its hundreds of small bronze dwarf statues hidden around the city. It is one of Poland's most attractive and liveable cities.

### Top Attractions

- **Market Square (Rynek):** One of the largest market squares in Europe, with the Gothic Old Town Hall.
- **Ostrów Tumski (Cathedral Island):** The oldest part of the city, with the cathedral and gas lamps lit by hand every evening.
- **Wrocław dwarfs:** More than 600 bronze dwarf statues scattered throughout the city.
- **Panorama of Racławice:** A huge 19th-century panoramic painting of a 1794 battle.
- **Centennial Hall:** A UNESCO-listed reinforced concrete structure, with a multimedia fountain nearby.
- **University of Wrocław:** The baroque Aula Leopoldina and Mathematical Tower.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Old Town is central and lively. Nadodrze is a revitalised area with street art and cafés, and Ołbin is a student district. The riverside areas are pleasant for walks.

### Food & Drink

Polish cuisine is widely available, and the market square has many restaurants. Wrocław has an excellent craft beer scene. Pierogi, żurek and local specialities are served in traditional restaurants. Hala Targowa is a historic market hall.

### When to Visit

Summer is lively with outdoor events. Winter brings one of Poland's best Christmas markets. Spring and autumn are pleasant.

### Getting Around

Wrocław is walkable, with trams and buses. The airport is connected by bus. Trains reach Kraków, Warsaw, Berlin and Prague.

### Day Trips

Książ Castle, one of the largest castles in Poland, is about an hour away. The Churches of Peace in Świdnica and Jawor are UNESCO sites. The Karkonosze Mountains offer hiking and skiing.

### Practical Tips

- Collect dwarf photos using a map from the tourist office.
- Book the Panorama of Racławice in advance.
- Try local craft beers.
- Many restaurants in the Rynek are pricier.

## Xi'an, China

*An ancient capital and the start of the Silk Road, home to the Terracotta Army.*

- **Country:** China
- **Region:** East Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~13 million (municipality)
- **Language(s):** Mandarin Chinese
- **Currency:** Chinese Yuan Renminbi (CNY)
- **Main airport:** Xi'an Xianyang International Airport (XIY)
- **Best time to visit:** March–May and September–November
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Xi'an, the capital of Shaanxi Province, was the capital of 13 Chinese dynasties, including the Qin, Han and Tang. As Chang'an, it was the eastern starting point of the Silk Road and one of the largest cities in the world. The city is famous for the Terracotta Army, discovered by farmers in 1974. Xi'an's well-preserved city walls, Muslim Quarter and pagodas make it a fascinating destination. It is a key stop on any China itinerary.

### Top Attractions

- **Terracotta Army:** Thousands of life-size clay soldiers guarding the tomb of Emperor Qin Shi Huang.
- **City Wall:** The best-preserved ancient city wall in China; rent a bike to ride along the top.
- **Muslim Quarter:** A lively area of food stalls, the Great Mosque and shops.
- **Big Wild Goose Pagoda:** A Tang dynasty pagoda built to house Buddhist scriptures.
- **Bell Tower and Drum Tower:** Landmarks in the centre of the old city.
- **Shaanxi History Museum:** A museum with treasures from Chinese dynasties.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Staying inside the city walls is convenient. The area around the Big Wild Goose Pagoda is lively at night.

### Food & Drink

Xi'an is known for roujiamo (Chinese "burger"), biangbiang noodles, yangrou paomo (lamb soup with bread) and liangpi (cold noodles). The Muslim Quarter is the best place to try street food.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are ideal. Summers are hot, and winters are cold.

### Getting Around

Xi'an has a metro, including a line towards the Terracotta Army area. High-speed trains connect with Beijing and other cities.

### Day Trips

Mount Huashan, known for its dramatic cliffside trails, is reachable by high-speed train. Huaqing Palace is near the Terracotta Army.

### Practical Tips

- Book Terracotta Army tickets in advance with your passport.
- Hire a guide at the site.
- Rent a bike on the city wall.
- Avoid national holidays.

## Yerevan, Armenia

*The "Pink City" of volcanic stone, café culture and views of Mount Ararat.*

- **Country:** Armenia
- **Region:** Caucasus
- **Population (approx.):** ~1.1 million
- **Language(s):** Armenian (Russian widely spoken)
- **Currency:** Armenian Dram (AMD)
- **Main airport:** Zvartnots International Airport (EVN)
- **Best time to visit:** May–June and September–October
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Yerevan, the capital of Armenia, is one of the world's oldest continuously inhabited cities, founded as the fortress of Erebuni in 782 BC. Much of the city was rebuilt in the 20th century using pink volcanic tuff stone, giving it the nickname "Pink City". Yerevan has a lively café culture, excellent museums and warm hospitality. On clear days, Mount Ararat, a national symbol, towers over the city. Armenia was the first country to adopt Christianity as a state religion, in 301 AD.

### Top Attractions

- **Cascade Complex:** A giant limestone stairway with art, the Cafesjian Center and views of Ararat.
- **Republic Square:** The central square with fountains that dance to music in the evening.
- **Matenadaran:** A museum of ancient manuscripts.
- **Armenian Genocide Memorial and Museum (Tsitsernakaberd):** A memorial to the victims of 1915.
- **Vernissage Market:** An open-air market for crafts and souvenirs.
- **Erebuni Fortress:** Ruins of the Urartian fortress.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Kentron district is central and walkable.

### Food & Drink

Armenian cuisine includes khorovats (barbecue), dolma, lavash bread (UNESCO heritage) and ghapama (stuffed pumpkin). Armenian brandy (cognac) and wine are famous.

### When to Visit

Spring and autumn are best. Summers are hot, and winters are cold.

### Getting Around

Taxis via apps like Yandex Go and GG are cheap. The metro is limited.

### Day Trips

Garni Temple, Geghard Monastery, Khor Virap and Lake Sevan are popular.

### Practical Tips

- Many nationalities enter visa-free.
- Visit the Cascade at sunset.
- Try Armenian brandy.
- Dress modestly at churches.

## Yogyakarta, Indonesia

*Java's cultural heart and the gateway to Borobudur and Prambanan.*

- **Country:** Indonesia
- **Region:** Southeast Asia
- **Population (approx.):** ~420,000 (city); ~4 million (special region)
- **Language(s):** Indonesian and Javanese
- **Currency:** Indonesian Rupiah (IDR)
- **Main airport:** Yogyakarta International Airport (YIA)
- **Best time to visit:** April–October
- **Continent:** Asia

### Overview

Yogyakarta, often called Jogja, is the cultural capital of Java and the only Indonesian region still ruled by a sultan. It is a centre for Javanese arts, including batik, gamelan music, shadow puppetry (wayang kulit) and classical dance. The city is also a university town with a young population. Yogyakarta is the base for visiting Borobudur, the world's largest Buddhist temple, and Prambanan, a magnificent Hindu temple complex. It is affordable and friendly.

### Top Attractions

- **Borobudur:** A 9th-century Buddhist temple with hundreds of stupas and relief panels; climbing access is limited.
- **Prambanan:** A 9th-century Hindu temple complex with tall spires, with Ramayana ballet performances.
- **Kraton:** The Sultan's Palace, with cultural performances.
- **Taman Sari:** The Water Castle, a former royal garden.
- **Malioboro Street:** A shopping street with batik and street food.
- **Mount Merapi:** An active volcano with jeep tours.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

Malioboro and Prawirotaman are popular areas.

### Food & Drink

Gudeg, jackfruit stew, is the local speciality. Angkringan street stalls serve cheap snacks and coffee.

### When to Visit

The dry season (April–October) is best.

### Getting Around

Becaks (pedicabs), taxis and ride-hailing apps are common.

### Day Trips

Borobudur, Prambanan and Merapi are essential.

### Practical Tips

- Book Borobudur tickets in advance.
- Dress modestly.
- Bargain in markets.
- Carry cash.

## Zagreb, Croatia

*A relaxed Central European capital of cafés, museums and award-winning Advent markets.*

- **Country:** Croatia
- **Region:** Southeastern Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~770,000
- **Language(s):** Croatian
- **Currency:** Euro (EUR)
- **Main airport:** Franjo Tuđman Airport (ZAG)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September; December for Advent
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Zagreb, the capital of Croatia, is often overlooked in favour of the coast, but it rewards visitors with a charming mix of Austro-Hungarian architecture, café culture and quirky museums. The city has a medieval Upper Town (Gornji Grad) and a 19th-century Lower Town (Donji Grad). Zagreb is compact, green and friendly. Its Advent Christmas market has won awards as one of Europe's best. The city is a good base for exploring Plitvice Lakes and northern Croatia.

### Top Attractions

- **Upper Town:** Medieval streets with St. Mark's Church, known for its colourful tiled roof.
- **Museum of Broken Relationships:** A unique museum displaying objects from failed relationships.
- **Zagreb Cathedral:** A Gothic cathedral with twin spires, undergoing restoration after the 2020 earthquake.
- **Dolac Market:** A lively market with red umbrellas selling fresh produce.
- **Lotrščak Tower:** A tower from which a cannon is fired every day at noon.
- **Mirogoj Cemetery:** A beautiful cemetery with arcades and domes.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Lower Town is central and elegant. The Upper Town is historic and quiet. Tkalčićeva Street is lively with bars and cafés.

### Food & Drink

Croatian cuisine in Zagreb includes štrukli (cheese-filled pastry), purica s mlincima (turkey with pasta) and kremšnita (cream cake). Coffee culture is strong. Local wines and craft beers are popular.

### When to Visit

Spring and summer are pleasant. December brings the Advent market. Winters are cold.

### Getting Around

Zagreb has an extensive tram network. The Upper Town can be reached by the funicular, one of the shortest in the world. Buses connect the airport with the city.

### Day Trips

Plitvice Lakes National Park, with cascading lakes and waterfalls, is about two hours away. Samobor is a charming town known for cream cakes. Varaždin is a baroque town north of Zagreb.

### Practical Tips

- Book Plitvice tickets in advance in summer.
- Enjoy coffee on terraces, a key part of local life.
- The Zagreb Card offers transport and discounts.
- Wear comfortable shoes for the Upper Town.

## Zurich, Switzerland

*A wealthy lakeside city of banks, art and Alpine views.*

- **Country:** Switzerland
- **Region:** Western Europe
- **Population (approx.):** ~440,000 (city); ~1.5 million (metro)
- **Language(s):** German (Swiss German)
- **Currency:** Swiss Franc (CHF)
- **Main airport:** Zurich Airport (ZRH)
- **Best time to visit:** May–September; December–March for skiing trips
- **Continent:** Europe

### Overview

Zurich is Switzerland's largest city and financial centre, located on Lake Zurich with the Alps on the horizon. It combines a medieval old town with modern culture, art and nightlife. The city is famous for banking, watchmaking and a very high quality of life. Zurich was the birthplace of the Dada art movement and has many museums and galleries. It is expensive but clean, safe and efficient.

### Top Attractions

- **Old Town (Altstadt):** Medieval streets on both sides of the Limmat River.
- **Grossmünster and Fraumünster:** The Grossmünster's twin towers and the Fraumünster's Chagall stained-glass windows.
- **Bahnhofstrasse:** One of the world's most exclusive shopping streets.
- **Lake Zurich:** Boat cruises, swimming and lakeside promenades.
- **Kunsthaus Zürich:** An art museum with works by Swiss and international artists.
- **Uetliberg:** Zurich's local mountain with views over the city, lake and Alps.

### Neighbourhoods & Where to Stay

The Old Town is central and charming. Zurich West is a former industrial area with bars and clubs. Seefeld is upscale and close to the lake.

### Food & Drink

Zürcher Geschnetzeltes (sliced veal in cream sauce) with rösti is the local speciality. Cheese fondue and raclette are popular. Swiss chocolate from Sprüngli and Läderach is famous. Eating out is expensive.

### When to Visit

Summer is ideal for lake swimming and outdoor events. Winter brings Christmas markets and easy access to skiing. Street Parade in August is a massive techno festival.

### Getting Around

Zurich has an excellent tram and bus network. Trains reach the airport in about 10 minutes. The Swiss Travel Pass covers transport.

### Day Trips

Lucerne and Mount Pilatus, Rhine Falls, and the Alpine resorts are popular. The mountain Rigi offers views. Appenzell is a traditional village.

### Practical Tips

- Tap water is excellent, and fountains are safe.
- The Zurich Card includes transport and discounts.
- Expect high prices.
- Shops are closed on Sundays.
